The finite-edge, fused/label strict fully-on-device `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` Xyce run with `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5`, frozen output bias, fused preactivation/score/output rails, and stored activation/delta state completed in `82.9s` with the strict contract true. It improved the 300-image diagnostic eval from `9.0%` to `49.33%`, matching the fast-screen 128-update expectation for this knob set while using the efficient deck shape (`auxiliary_algebraic_source_count=0`, `864` output vectors). Corrected finite-edge deck cost was `1,798` estimated transient points and `14,757` total source PWL points. Compact summary/metrics were copied to `results/tables`; generated deck and weight artifacts were removed.
- Tightened the phase-result audit after the sample-edge fix. The summarizer now reports `robust_sample_transitions` and treats target-topology strict evidence with `sample_edge_s=0` as failing the strict target contract, because exact duplicate-time sample steps are correct on tiny decks but can trigger Xyce time-step failures on the 10x10 target deck. Re-running the target strict ranking with this audit keeps the known finite-edge trajectory as the valid evidence chain: `49.33%` at 128 updates, `68.0%` at 256 updates, and `76.1%` at 512 updates, while the new corrected fused/label 64-update smoke remains a compact `19.67%` contract check rather than the best accuracy point.
- Reran the bounded target-topology learning smoke after restoring robust finite sample edges. The corrected 64-update `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` strict fully-on-device Xyce run (`batch_size=1`, one continuous training transient, no Python checkpoint/update between samples, label targets, fused preactivation/score/output rails, stored activation/delta state, frozen output bias, readout scale `0.35`, temperature `3.0`) completed in `44.9s` with the execution contract true. Eval on the 300-image diagnostic slice improved from `9.0%` to `19.67%` (`nontrivial_learning_met=true`), matching the corrected reference expectation for this short horizon and clearing the old one-class `11.33%` failure caused by invalid sample ramps. Deck cost for the corrected finite-edge 64-update run is `902` estimated transient points, `864` output vectors, and `7,371` total source PWL points. Compact summary/metrics were copied to `results/tables`; generated deck and weight artifacts were removed.
- Fixed the `--sample-edge 0` semantics in continuous phase decks. The old PWL compression emitted only `(t, new_value)` at sample changes, which SPICE interprets as a long ramp from the previous sample rather than a sharp step; this explained the one-sample pass / two-sample failure pattern in phase-vs-batch-op reference checks. Zero-edge sample PWL now emits repeated-time step points, and a two-update 4x4 Xyce readout/local check with `--sample-edge 0` recovered direction agreement (`cos=0.9999999993`, sign alignment `1.0`). On the target 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 deck, exact duplicate-time sample steps can still make Xyce hit "time step too small", so generated strict promotion defaults now omit `--sample-edge` and use the finite phase edge instead. A bounded 16-update target-topology reference check with finite 5ps sample edges passed the direction gate (`cos=0.9999999968`, sign alignment `1.0`, wrong signs `0`) and matched phase/op reference eval on the 100-image diagnostic slice (`19%`/`19%`). The corrected 512-update strict preflight cost is back to `59,547` total source PWL points; the earlier `34,943` sharp-edge cost was based on incorrect ramp semantics and should not be treated as valid.
- Fixed a stale SPICE batch-op reference mismatch before using it to judge more fused synapse/neuron experiments. The phase-transient deck already honored `output_bias_update_scale`, `readout_update_scale`, and `local_update_scale`; the batch-op SPICE reference now has the same params and update formulas, and the phase runner passes the selected scales into reference replay. A tiny reference-backed Xyce run with frozen output bias confirmed the bias part of the mismatch is gone (`output_bias_max_abs_diff=0`), while hidden/readout differences remain, so the next correctness target is still phase timing/update semantics rather than output-bias drift. A target-topology preflight also rechecked the safe combined hidden preactivation shape: `hidden_preactivation_mode=inline` removes 32 auxiliary algebraic sources versus node mode (`32 -> 0`) with unchanged temporary state (`84`), transient points (`902` for the 64-update check), output vectors (`864`), and PWL source points (`4,367`). This reinforces the family rule: algebraic read-side fusion is useful, but removing activation/error latch state changes phase semantics and needs separate proof.
- Added an explicit `--score-state-mode stored|inline` experiment for removing the class-score latch capacitors after fused score calculation. Base runner and generated strict promotions keep `stored` by default. `inline` removes `Cscore*`/`Bstore_score*`, rewires output/error paths to the score expression, and reports `score_state_mode` plus the reduced `score_state_count`. Target-topology preflight with the current strict deck drops `temporary_state_count` from `84` to `74` while preserving the strict contract, `864` output vectors, `7,174` transient points, and `34,943` source PWL points. A two-update Xyce smoke matched stored score state to numerical noise (`readout` max diff `1.17e-7`, `local_bias` max diff `6.98e-8`), but the tiny inline run was slower (`0.49s` vs `0.19s`) because it duplicates larger expressions into the output/error equations. Treat score-state inline as a validated experiment knob, not a strict-promotion default yet.
- Added explicit temporary capacitor-state accounting to the phase-transient path and promotion metadata. Summaries now report hidden activation state, hidden/backward delta state, score state, output-delta state, phased gradient-accumulator state, and their `temporary_state_count` total. Focused tests cover direct mode, fused hidden/output-delta modes, and phased zero-pixel gradient elision. The current target-topology strict 512-update preflight (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, fused preactivation/score/output rails, stored hidden activation, stored output delta) reports `temporary_state_count=84` (`32` hidden activations + `32` hidden deltas + `10` score states + `10` output deltas) with the strict contract still true, `864` output vectors, `7,174` transient points, and `34,943` source PWL points. The explicit hidden-activation-inline experiment would reduce temporary state to `52`, but remains non-promotable because the prior tiny Xyce comparison showed non-equivalent dynamics.
- Added an explicit `--hidden-activation-mode stored|inline` experiment to test the more aggressive fused synapse/neuron idea of removing hidden activation latch capacitors. The default remains `stored` in the base runner and generated strict promotions. `inline` removes `Ch*`/`Bstore_h*` and rewrites score/readout-gradient consumers to the local activation expression; summaries, preflights, sweep commands, and result tables now report `hidden_activation_mode` and `hidden_activation_state_count`. A syntax regression caught and fixed the bad `V(<inline expression>)` derivative path. Target-topology preflight with `inline` still meets the strict contract and keeps the same transient/source/vector counts while reporting `hidden_activation_state_count=0`, but a two-update Xyce comparison against the stored-latch branch is not equivalent (`readout` max diff `2.60e-3`, `local_bias` max diff `1.39e-4`). This confirms hidden activation latching is semantically important for the validated phase ordering; latch-free activation is explicit experiment-only for now, not a promotion default.
- Aligned the older phase-variant sweep wrapper with the efficient generated strict deck defaults. It now defaults generated activation/synapse/neuron experiment commands to `--sample-edge 0.0` and `--hidden-preactivation-mode inline`, matching fast-reference strict promotions while still leaving the base phase runner compatibility defaults unchanged. Focused tests passed (`106 passed`).
- Switched fast-reference generated strict-promotion defaults to the cheaper validated deck shape while preserving the base phase-runner defaults. Generated promotions now keep robust PWL phase clocks, but default to `--sample-edge 0.0` and `--hidden-preactivation-mode inline`; callers can still request finite sample edges or node preactivation explicitly. This moves future promotion/cost-projection commands onto the `67.81935` source-PWL/update target-topology shape by default instead of the old `115.6335`/update shape. Focused tests passed (`113 passed`).
- Reprojected the full-objective fast-reference candidate under the latest strict deck knobs, still preflight-only. Baseline finite-sample/node mode remains `280,006` transient points, `864` output vectors, `2,312,670` total source PWL points (`115.6335/update`), and `32` hidden preactivation sources. With `--sample-edge 0`, total source PWL drops to `1,356,387` (`67.81935/update`). Adding `--hidden-preactivation-mode inline` keeps the same PWL/transient/vector counts while reducing hidden preactivation sources `32 -> 0`. The strict fully-on-device contract remains true in all cases; this is a cost baseline, not a long Xyce learning run.
- Completed deck-mode metadata propagation for the recent sample-edge and fused-preactivation knobs. A shared `phase_deck_mode_fields` helper now feeds both preflight-only and completed transient summaries, so runtime JSON includes `sample_edge_s`, `hidden_preactivation_mode`, and `hidden_preactivation_source_count` instead of only the older `phase_clock_mode`/`target_source_mode` fields. Focused tests passed (`90 passed`), and a tiny no-reference Xyce runtime smoke confirmed the completed-run summary reports `sample_edge_s=0.0`, `hidden_preactivation_mode=inline`, `hidden_preactivation_source_count=0`, with the strict fully-on-device contract true; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added an explicit fused hidden-preactivation experiment knob: `--hidden-preactivation-mode node|inline`, plus `--promotion-hidden-preactivation-mode` in fast-reference promotion/cost projection and forwarding through the older phase-variant sweep. The default `node` preserves existing validated decks; `inline` removes the `Bpre_h*` hidden preactivation source family and inlines the same expression into the hidden activation/derivative equations. Summaries and audit tables now report `hidden_preactivation_mode` and `hidden_preactivation_source_count`. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`112 passed`). A target-topology 20k preflight with sharp sample edges showed `node` has `32` hidden preactivation behavioral sources and `inline` has `0`, with the strict contract, transient points, and source-PWL counts unchanged. A tiny Xyce node-vs-inline smoke on the same direct online deck matched final states closely (`local_weights` max diff `1.29e-6`, `output_bias` max diff `2.21e-6`); smoke artifacts were removed. This is the first concrete fused synapse/neuron-style deck simplification, but it is not yet learning evidence.
- Split sample/label/LR-control PWL transition edges from phase-clock edges with an opt-in `--sample-edge` / `--promotion-sample-edge` knob. The default preserves existing decks by using `--edge`, while `--sample-edge 0` emits sharp sample steps and keeps finite phase clocks for update integration. Fast-promotion cost fields, phase preflight summaries, the older phase-variant sweep, and the phase result summarizer now carry `sample_edge_s`. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`110 passed`). On the 20k-sample full-objective target preflight, sharp sample edges keep `280,006` transient points and the strict contract unchanged while reducing sample-source PWL points `1,912,665 -> 956,382` and total source PWL points `2,312,670 -> 1,356,387` (`115.6335 -> 67.81935` points/update). This is a deck/source cost reduction only; no long Xyce run was launched.
- Added explicit cost-rate and budget-pass fields to phase transient preflight and run summaries, then carried them through the phase result summarizer and phase-variant sweep rows. New summaries report per-update transient/source-PWL costs plus `*_budget_met` booleans for transient, source-PWL, sample-source, total-source, and output-vector gates, which makes long-horizon fully on-device candidates easier to compare without hand-dividing raw counts. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`85 passed`). A cheap in-process cost check of the full-objective fast-reference candidate showed the current 20k-sample strict deck at `14.0003` transient points/update and `115.6335` source-PWL points/update with PWL clocks; analytic clocks would reduce source-PWL cost to `95.63325` points/update but remain an opt-in robustness experiment.
- Added full-objective metadata to the single-candidate fast online reference runner and reused the same helper from the fast variant sweep, so narrow NumPy confirmations now explicitly report whether they meet the `10,000` eval / `90%` accuracy fast-reference gate. A focused test passed (`22 passed`). A single target-topology NumPy confirmation for the prior long-horizon candidate (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, `20,000` online samples, full `10,000` eval, `lr=0.08`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5`, hidden `tanh-clipped`, frozen output bias) reached `10.0% -> 90.1%`, so the algorithmic target can clear the full objective in fast reference. Matching strict phase preflight, still without launching Xyce, reports the fully on-device deck would be `280,006` estimated transient points and `2,312,670` total source PWL points with `864` final output vectors. This is candidate evidence and a cost target, not a SPICE training result.
- Completed the phase-variant sweep's bounded-cost surface for activation/synapse experiments. It now forwards `--max-output-vectors` alongside the transient/source/sample/total-source guards and preserves the key source/output cost fields in sweep rows (`max_*`, sample/pixel/target/control counts, and total source PWL/counts). That keeps cheap variant comparisons on the same fully on-device deck budget contract as fast-reference promotions. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`76 passed`).
- Added total emitted source-element accounting beside the existing sample-source and source-PWL budgets. The phase transient runner now records `total_source_count`, accepts `--max-total-sources`, and fails during preflight before launching a simulator when the source-element budget is exceeded. Fast-reference promotion/projection rows, the older phase variant sweep, and the phase result summarizer now carry the same fields, so compact-label behavioral sources, phase clocks, and LR controls are budgeted consistently. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`104 passed`).
- Added per-budget feasibility counts to fast-reference cost-projection summaries. Summary JSON now reports how many projected rows pass each strict SPICE budget gate individually, plus how many fast-reference full-objective candidates are blocked by transient points, source-PWL points, emitted sample-source count, or output-vector count. This makes a missing cost-feasible full-objective candidate diagnosable without opening the CSV. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`103 passed`).
- Aligned the older phase-variant sweep with the compact strict promotion deck by forwarding `--target-source-mode` and defaulting it to `label`. Dry-run/runtime commands now preserve compact label targets instead of silently falling back to per-class target rails, and sweep rows carry the resulting `target_source_mode`. This keeps activation/synapse comparison commands on the same fully on-device input contract and source-cost model as fast-reference promotions. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`103 passed`).
- Completed sample-source budget propagation in the older phase-variant sweep. Dry-run/runtime commands now forward `--max-sample-sources` to the phase transient runner, reject negative budgets at argument validation, and preserve `max_sample_sources` plus `sample_source_count` in sweep result rows. This keeps activation/synapse comparison sweeps on the same bounded deck-size contract as fast-reference promotion screening. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`103 passed`).
- Added an emitted sample-source budget gate for fully on-device phase runs and fast-reference promotion screening. The phase runner now accepts `--max-sample-sources`, fails during preflight before simulator launch when emitted input/target source count exceeds the budget, and records the configured budget in summaries. Fast-sweep strict promotion commands pass the same guard through, promotion/projection rows report `*_sample_source_budget_met`, and candidate selection treats sample-source budget failures like transient/source-PWL/output-vector failures. This makes constant-pixel and compact-label source reductions actionable in bounded screens. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`103 passed`).
- Completed the zero-pixel local-update elision family for phased comparison decks. Constant-zero pixel inputs already removed direct-mode local weight update sources; phased mode now also omits the matching local gradient capacitor, accumulator, apply, and clear source family when the pixel gradient is identically zero. This is a deck-size cleanup for comparison/debug paths, not a new learning result, and it preserves weight capacitors plus any state-decay source. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`102 passed`).
- Propagated emitted-source audit fields through the phase result summarizer and fast-reference strict-promotion metadata. Promotion rows now include sample/pixel source counts, elided DC pixel counts, target source count, and target behavioral source count for both promotion and projection horizons, so candidate screens can see the deck-element effect of constant-pixel elision instead of only unchanged PWL-point budgets. The Markdown phase summary also shows `sample_source_count` and `sample_source_elided_dc_count`; older summaries naturally leave the new elided field blank. Focused no-SPICE tests passed (`101 passed`), and a Markdown summary smoke over existing strict target artifacts still rendered.
- Elided constant pixel voltage sources from continuous phase decks. Pixel sample rails are only consumed by behavioral source expressions, so DC pixel rails can be represented as numeric literals while PWL pixels stay as `Vpix*` sources. Zero-valued literal pixels also skip direct-mode local weight update sources because their gradient is identically zero; persistent weight capacitors and any state-decay path remain intact. The current strict 512-update target preflight still reports the same source-PWL/transient/output-vector budgets (`59,547` PWL points, `7,174` transient points, `864` vectors), but emitted sample sources drop from `101` to `94` by eliding seven DC pixel rails. This was verified with unit/preflight tests only; no large Xyce run was launched.
- Added summary-level cost-projection audit counts to the fast-reference variant sweep: whether projection was enabled, how many rows emitted projection fields, how many projection rows met the budgets, and how many fast-reference full-objective candidates were projection-budget feasible versus infeasible. This makes an absent `best_fast_reference_full_objective_cost_feasible_variant` diagnosable as either "no full-objective NumPy candidate" or "candidate exists but projected strict SPICE cost misses budget." A tiny NumPy-only smoke emitted the new summary fields; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added a cost-feasible full-objective selector to fast-reference variant-sweep summaries. `best_fast_reference_full_objective_cost_feasible_variant` only appears when a row clears the fast-reference full-objective gates and the strict cost-projection transient/source/output-vector budgets, so high-accuracy NumPy candidates that are too expensive under the projected strict deck do not become the recommended long-horizon target. Cost projections now also report source PWL points per update. A tiny NumPy-only smoke emitted the new summary key and per-update cost fields; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added an optional strict phase training-cost projection horizon to the fast online variant sweep. `--cost-projection-updates` can now differ from `--promotion-updates`, so a screen can keep generating a short bounded Xyce promotion command while also estimating the source/transient/output-vector cost of a longer fast-reference trajectory using the same strict on-device deck assumptions. A tiny NumPy-only smoke with `promotion_updates=2` and `cost_projection_updates=4` emitted separate `strict_phase_promotion_*` and `strict_phase_cost_projection_*` fields; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added fast-reference full-objective metadata to the variant sweep. Rows now report whether their NumPy-only final eval satisfies the configured full-eval sample count and accuracy threshold, plus a `best_fast_reference_full_objective_variant` summary key when a candidate clears both gates. The fields are deliberately named `fast_reference_*` and the summary still states that this is not an on-device/SPICE result, so these screens can guide long-horizon candidates without being confused with Milestone D evidence. A tiny NumPy-only smoke emitted the new fields; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Extended the phase-transient result summarizer with explicit milestone/objective audit fields. Strict target rows now distinguish nontrivial learning evidence from final-objective evidence: the current best strict target run (`512` updates, `76.1%` on `1,000` eval) is marked as strict target nontrivial learning, but `full_eval_10k_met=false` and `milestone_d_full_objective_met=false`. This prevents small-eval or sub-90% runs from being mistaken for the final `>90%` full-test objective while still making useful progress evidence easy to sort.
- Added a skipped-if-MNIST-missing regression for the current strict 512-update target-topology preflight shape. It checks the no-simulator path for `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, direct `batch_size=1`, label targets, frozen output bias/no final `y` rails: `7,174` estimated transient points, `864` output vectors, `49,302` sample-source PWL points, `10,245` phase-clock PWL points, and `59,547` total source PWL points. This protects the current bounded-promotion cost model without launching Xyce.
- Added horizon-aware learning summaries to the fast online variant sweep. Summaries now include the best compact candidate at each configured probe update plus earliest configured threshold hits (default `20%/50%/75%/90%`), so cheap NumPy screens can answer whether a 128/256/512-sample SPICE promotion is justified before launching Xyce. A tiny NumPy-only CLI smoke emitted the new `best_probe_variants_by_update` and `learning_threshold_hits` fields; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Trimmed redundant terminal `t_stop` points from sample/control and phase-clock PWL sources. SPICE/Xyce hold the final PWL value after the last point, so the explicit final duplicate was unnecessary. On the current 512-update target-topology strict preflight this drops source PWL points from `59,646` to `59,547` while preserving the same `7,174` transient points and `864` output vectors. A tiny 2-update strict Xyce smoke with PWL clocks and label targets completed with the strict fully-on-device contract true, confirming Xyce accepts the trimmed waveforms. Refreshed the compact efficiency screen artifacts so their generated strict promotion metadata now reports the lower source count.
- Refreshed the compact 8-row target-topology fast-reference screen with the new promotion-efficiency summary fields, still without launching SPICE. In this constant-rate/current-cost neighborhood all variants have the same strict promotion cost (`59,547` source PWL points, `7,174` estimated transient points, `864` output vectors), so `best_promotion_variant` and `best_promotion_efficiency_variant` agree: `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, local scale `1.0`, temperature `3.0`, predicted `9.0% -> 78.0%` at 512 online updates on the 300-image eval slice. This refresh does not justify another Xyce promotion by itself; it confirms the new efficiency reporting is working and that the next meaningful search needs cost-varying candidates or deeper deck/runtime reductions.
- Added `best_promotion_efficiency_variant` to fast online sweep summaries. The existing `best_promotion_variant` remains the accuracy-first, budget-aware recommendation, while the new field selects the budget-feasible row with the best promotion eval gain per 1k source-PWL points. This gives long-horizon screens a cheap way to identify near-term circuit/runtime-efficient candidates for bounded promotion without changing the main selection semantics. A tiny NumPy-only smoke confirmed the new summary key is emitted; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added promotion-efficiency fields to the fast online variant sweep so candidate rows now report `promotion_probe_eval_improvement`, source PWL points per promoted update, and promotion gain per 1k source-PWL/transient points. This does not change the accuracy-first best-promotion selector, but it makes long-horizon target-topology screens easier to triage when two candidates have similar accuracy but different deck/runtime cost. A tiny NumPy-only sweep smoke confirmed the fields appear in both the CSV rows and best/best-promotion summary entries; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Extended the phase-transient result summarizer so source-cost audits now include `control_source_pwl_points` alongside sample and phase-clock PWL points, and derive `total_source_pwl_points` as sample + clock + control for older summaries that lack an explicit total. This closes an audit gap for linear-decay strict candidates, where the LR schedule rail can add source events even when clocks and labels are optimized. A Markdown summary smoke over existing strict target artifacts now shows the new control-source column; current top constant-rate evidence has `0` control-source PWL points, as expected.
- Added LR-control source accounting to strict fast-promotion metadata and tested an analytic alternative for linear-decay schedules. On the 256-update target-topology linear-decay branch with robust PWL phase clocks, analytic LR control would remove the schedule rail's `512` PWL points (`30,638 -> 30,126` total source PWL points) without changing transient/output-vector counts in preflight. A tiny 2-update Xyce runtime smoke failed at the `BLRCTRL` behavioral source with a time-step-too-small error near the second sample boundary, so the analytic LR-control mode was not landed. The useful landed part is the new `strict_phase_promotion_control_source_pwl_points` reporting, which makes schedule-control overhead visible in future screens while generated strict promotions stay on robust PWL LR control.
- Rechecked analytic phase clocks as a bounded deck-cost knob without changing the robust strict-promotion default. A 512-update target-topology preflight with label targets, frozen output bias, and omitted final `y` rails reports `49,396` total source PWL points when `--phase-clock-mode analytic`, versus the current PWL-clock promotion shape's `59,646`; transient points and final output vectors remain `7,174` and `864`. A tiny 2-update 4x4 Xyce smoke executed successfully with analytic clocks and strict fully-on-device contract true (`phase_clock_source_pwl_points=0`, `total_source_pwl_points=56`). However, the existing 128-update analytic-clock tiny-step failure means generated strict promotions should continue to default to PWL clocks until the analytic waveform is made robust at real promotion horizons; analytic remains an explicit opt-in/preflight experiment knob.
- Ran a 162-row fast-reference refinement over local update scale, readout scale, softmax temperature, and learning rate for the next 1024/2048/4096 horizons on the strict target topology branch. The best 1024-update promotion candidate was `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, local scale `1.25`, temperature `2.5`, predicted `79.0%` on the 1,000-image eval slice and budget-feasible for strict promotion (`14,342` estimated transient points, `120,196` source PWL points, generated timeout `1280s`). That is only a modest improvement over the 512-update strict result (`76.1%`) for roughly double the Xyce horizon, so it is not worth promoting blindly. The better long-horizon direction is lower-rate/local-damped: `lr=0.2`, readout scale `0.35`, local scale `0.75`, temperature `2.0`, which reaches `86.3%` by 4096 fast-reference updates while only predicting `76.5%` at 1024. Next useful work should either push this long-horizon family toward >90 in fast reference or reduce strict runtime/source cost enough to make a 2048/4096 promotion practical.
- Fixed strict-promotion command generation so simulator timeouts auto-scale with promotion horizon when `--promotion-timeout 0` is used. The fast sweep now emits `max(240s, 1.25s * updates)` unless an explicit timeout is supplied; the 512-update promotion command therefore uses 640 s instead of the stale 240 s default, matching the observed 463.8 s strict Xyce runtime. Regenerated the 2048-sample/512-promotion fast-screen artifact so future copied commands include the corrected timeout and an explicit `strict_phase_promotion_timeout_s` field.
- Promoted the 512-update fast-screened branch to a strict fully-on-device Xyce run on the target topology: `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, temperature `2.5`, hidden `tanh-clipped`, frozen output-bias writes, direct `batch_size=1`, random init, no reference replay. The run completed in one uninterrupted transient and exactly matched the fast-reference prediction on the 1,000-image eval slice: initial `8.7%`, final `76.1%`, all 10 predicted classes active, strict contract true, no Python checkpointing, 7,174 estimated transient points, 60,606 total source PWL points, and 463.8 s phase time. This is the strongest strict fully-on-device target-topology result so far, moving beyond the previous 256-update `68.0%` result. The next useful step should either preflight/promote a 1024-update candidate or reduce source/deck/runtime cost before attempting longer horizons.
- Ran a cheap 42-row fast-reference screen for the next 512-update promotion horizon on the target topology. The best final 2048-update candidate was lower-rate (`lr=0.2`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5`) at `83.2%` on a 1,000-image eval slice, while the best 512-update promotion candidate was `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, temperature `2.5`, predicted `76.1%` at update 512 and still `81.2%` at update 2048. The generated strict 512 promotion preflight is budget-feasible (`7,174` estimated transient points, `60,606` source PWL points), but its default 240 s timeout is too short given the new 256-update strict run took 221.4 s. Also simplified generated decks by omitting update/accumulator elements for zero-scaled write families; this removes the ten zero-current output-bias write sources from the current frozen-output-bias strict branch. Focused tests passed (`86 passed`), and a two-update Xyce smoke confirmed a frozen-output-bias deck still runs while emitting no `Bupd_ob*` sources.
- Promoted the best constant-rate 256-update candidate from the LR-schedule screen to strict Xyce. The linear-decay candidate (`lr=0.8`, decay to `0.25`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `3.0`) preflighted within budget (`3,591` estimated transient points, `31,120` source PWL points) but timed out at 300 s with the LR-control PWL source, so it is not a good bounded promotion target yet. The nearest constant-rate branch (`lr=0.8`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `3.0`) completed under the same strict fully-on-device contract and exactly matched the fast reference: `9.0% -> 68.0%` on the fixed 300-image eval slice, all 10 predicted classes active, 3,591 estimated transient points, 30,608 total source PWL points, and 221.4 s phase time. This is now the best strict target-topology result in the checkout, improving the previous 256-update strict branch from `64.33%` to `68.0%`.
- Promoted the refined 128-update target candidate from fast reference to a strict fully-on-device Xyce run: `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, direct `batch_size=1`, random init, no reference replay, `tanh` with hidden `tanh-clipped`, frozen output-bias writes, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5`, and `lr=0.5`. A first attempt using analytic phase clocks failed in Xyce with "time step too small" near the end of the transient after about 119 s, while the same candidate with PWL phase clocks completed and exactly hit the fast prediction: `9.0% -> 49.33%` on the 300-image eval slice, strict contract true, no Python checkpointing between samples, 1,798 estimated transient points, 15,086 total source PWL points, and 109.4 s phase time. This is now the best strict 128-update target-topology result, and generated strict promotions default back to PWL clocks for robustness; analytic clocks remain an explicit opt-in preflight/runtime knob rather than the default.

## 2026-05-22

- Generalized the fast online variant sweep so learning-rate schedules are a real grid axis via `--lr-schedules`, with constant LR canonicalized to a single `lr_final_scale=1.0` row because final scale has no electrical effect there. A bounded NumPy-only target-topology screen over 24 variants (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 256 online updates, 300 eval samples, current `tanh`/hidden `tanh-clipped` branch) found that linear decay is slightly better at the final 256-update horizon: best was `lr=0.8`, decay to `0.25`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `3.0`, moving `9.0% -> 69.0%`. For a 128-update Xyce promotion horizon, constant `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.25`, temperature `3.0` still had the best predicted probe (`48.0%`), so LR decay is promising for later horizons but not the next 128-update promotion target.
- Added an opt-in per-update learning-rate schedule to the fast online reference and fully on-device phase-transient runner. The default remains constant `{LR}` with unchanged deck text, while `--lr-schedule linear-decay --lr-final-scale ...` emits a single `lrctrl` PWL control rail that scales all local/readout/output-bias update currents inside the same uninterrupted transient. Fast sweep rows and strict promotion commands now carry the schedule/final-scale fields and include the LR control source in source-PWL budget estimates. Focused regressions passed (`85 passed`), preflight showed the two-update scheduled smoke still met the strict contract with `control_source_pwl_points=4`, and a two-update Xyce/direct smoke accepted the deck in `0.21s` phase time. This is a small control-surface improvement, not new learning evidence.
- Made fast-sweep strict promotion selection budget-aware. `best_promotion_variant` now prefers candidates whose generated strict Xyce/direct promotion fits both the transient-point and source-PWL budgets, falling back to the best predicted promotion accuracy only when no candidate has feasible budget metadata. This keeps the next fully-on-device SPICE promotion choice aligned with the cost gates instead of selecting a high-accuracy candidate that cannot be run under the configured slice.
- Added strict-promotion cost fields to the fast online variant sweep so candidates carry feasibility metadata before launching Xyce. Each row now records the generated strict promotion's phase-clock mode, estimated transient points, sample/phase-clock/total source PWL points, and transient/source budget pass flags, computed with the same schedule and source-complexity helpers used by the phase-transient preflight path. A two-sample fast-sweep smoke emitted the fields for an analytic-clock promotion (`34` estimated transient points, `24` total source PWL points, budget flags true) without running SPICE; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Switched strict fast-reference promotion commands to use analytic phase clocks by default. The general phase-transient runner still defaults to PWL clocks, but generated strict promotions are always Xyce/direct/`batch_size=1`, which is the validated analytic-clock domain and removes avoidable phase-clock PWL burden from future bounded promotions. A 2-update 4x4 Xyce equivalence smoke comparing PWL vs analytic clocks produced the same strict execution contract and closely matched final state (`max_abs=4.8e-6`, RMS `5.7e-7`) while reducing total source PWL points from `110` to `60`; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Made phase-clock mode and total source PWL budget first-class audit fields for fully-on-device phase-transient runs. New summaries now include `total_source_pwl_points`, and the result summarizer derives the same field for older artifacts while exposing `phase_clock_mode` and source-budget columns in the quick Markdown table. A preflight-only check of the long target candidate (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 2048 direct online updates, `lr=0.08`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5`, analytic clocks) stayed within the transient budget (`28,679` estimated points) and reported `198,994` total source PWL points with phase clocks reduced to zero PWL points. This is still preflight evidence only, not a learning run.
- Added an opt-in analytic phase-clock mode for the online phase-transient runner. It keeps the existing PWL clocks as the default, but `--phase-clock-mode analytic` emits bounded continuous Xyce behavioral clocks for direct `batch_size=1` runs, removing phase-clock PWL points from source-complexity budgets without changing input/target PWL handling. Focused tests and a 2-update 4x4 Xyce smoke passed with the strict fully-on-device contract true and `phase_clock_source_pwl_points=0`. The mode is deliberately Xyce-only because ngspice did not accept the same behavioral expression reliably.
- Added a structural `--softmax-error-gate target-margin` option to the local-feature phase-transient and matched batch-op SPICE training paths. The gate computes the one-hot target score minus the strongest non-target competitor using behavioral arithmetic only, then clips a margin deficit into a 0..1 scale before storing softmax class deltas. Competitor maxima are emitted as intermediate behavioral nodes to avoid giant 10-class source expressions. This keeps default behavior unchanged while making the target/competitor margin branch explicit in summaries and result-table audits. Focused regressions passed (`55 passed` across the phase/local-feature and summary tests), and a single-update 4x4 Xyce smoke accepted the deck and matched the batch-op reference (`cos=0.999999999998`, sign alignment `100%`).
- Ran bounded strict target-topology probes for the new target-margin softmax gate on the current best short branch (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, direct `batch_size=1`, random init, no reference replay, `tanh` + full readout feedback, frozen output-bias writes, readout scale `0.25`, temperature `4`, 16 online updates, 100-image NumPy eval). Margin `0.5` matched the ungated result (`5% -> 22%`, `phase_update_l2=1.286`, dominant class `4` at `46%`), while aggressive margin `0.05` reduced movement but hurt accuracy and concentration (`5% -> 19%`, `phase_update_l2=1.167`, class `4` at `62%`). This branch does not fix the class-takeover problem; the next higher-yield target is likely score/readout normalization or a different error transport shape rather than simple target-margin shutoff.
- Added `--softmax-competition-mode normalized-power` with `--softmax-competitor-power` to concentrate the non-target softmax error rail by normalized `y^p` while preserving total target-vs-non-target error balance. A one-update 4x4 Xyce smoke accepted the expression and matched the batch-op reference (`cos=0.999999999954`, sign alignment `100%`). On the same strict target-topology 16-update branch, `p=2` again reached `5% -> 22%`, but class coverage did not improve: final predictions were still only classes `{0,4,8}`, with class `4` at `47%` and `phase_update_l2=1.276`. So focusing non-target competition by probability alone is not the missing class-coverage mechanism.
- Brought the fast NumPy online reference back into equation-level alignment with the phase runner's current direct-update knobs: softmax temperature, softmax negative/error shaping, target-margin gating, readout class centering, output-bias/readout/local update scales, and state decay. On the exact strict target branch (`tanh`, full readout feedback, hidden `tanh-clipped`, clip `2.0`, output-bias frozen, readout scale `0.25`, temperature `4`), the fast reference predicted the observed short-horizon dip and later recovery: at `lr=0.8`, 300-image eval probes were `10.7%, 19.0%, 10.0%, 18.3%, 23.7%, 15.0%, 19.7%, 43.0%, 64.3%, 72.7%` for updates `1..512`, while `lr=0.1` was slower (`46.0%` at update 512). A bounded strict Xyce run at 64 updates exactly followed the fast `lr=0.8` curve through the measured horizon (`9.0% -> 19.7%`, best `23.7%` at update 16, class `1` dominant at `89.7%`, phase time `46.4s`). This validates the updated fast reference as a cheap selector for this branch and argues the next real Xyce spend should skip 64-update repeats and target the predicted 128+ recovery horizon.
- Ran the predicted 128-update strict Xyce recovery probe on the same target-topology branch. It stayed within the fully on-device contract (`batch_size=1`, random init, `reference_mode=none`, one uninterrupted Xyce transient, no Python checkpointing) and matched the fast reference exactly on the 300-image eval slice: `9.0% -> 43.0%`, best/final probe at update 128, `phase_update_l2=13.56`, phase time `84.0s`. Prediction coverage improved from the 64-update collapse: six predicted classes at final, dominant class `2` at `43.3%`, with strong per-class accuracy for labels `0/1/2/9`. This is the strongest current strict fully-on-device target-topology result in this run sequence and validates moving the next bounded Xyce spend to the 256-update horizon rather than more short objective-shaping probes.
- Extended the same strict fully-on-device target branch to 256 online updates. The run again matched the fast reference exactly on the fixed 300-image eval slice and stayed within the strict execution contract: `9.0% -> 64.33%`, best/final probe at update 256, `phase_update_l2=26.48`, phase time `174.7s`. Final prediction coverage reached all 10 classes, with the dominant class down to `21%` and nonzero per-class accuracy across every label. This is now the strongest current random-init, one-transient, target-topology fully-on-device result, and it supports using the fast reference's 512-update prediction as the next bounded Xyce target.
- Added `--eval-backend both` as a calibration mode for the phase-transient runner. It runs the post-transient SPICE eval and the NumPy diagnostic eval on the same extracted state, keeps SPICE as the primary gate value, and writes backend-specific accuracy/difference fields into summaries and probe rows. This lets short runs periodically validate the cheaper NumPy diagnostic before using it for longer no-reference direct trajectories.
- Calibrated the dual-backend diagnostic on a bounded target-topology slice: `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, direct `batch_size=1`, 16 online updates, 100-image eval, `reference_mode=none`, sparse probes, and `--eval-backend both`. SPICE and NumPy evals matched exactly on both initial and final states (`5% -> 12%`, backend absolute diff `0.0`), the run met the fully on-device execution contract, and `phase_update_l2` ended at `0.3641`. This validates using the cheaper NumPy post-transient diagnostic for modest longer no-reference direct probes on this topology/settings, while keeping occasional short SPICE-vs-NumPy calibrations.
- Added a compact phase-transient result summary helper, `experiments/27_summarize_phase_transient_results.py`, so target-topology fully-on-device evidence can be audited from existing JSON artifacts without launching new SPICE runs. The helper exposes topology, update count, eval backend, execution-contract fields, Python update/checkpoint flags, accuracy deltas, backend calibration deltas, update magnitude, and wall time; `--target-topology --contract-only` currently shows the two explicit-contract 16-update target slices and makes older higher-update runs visibly distinguishable because their summaries predate the explicit contract fields.
- Added `--strict-fully-on-device` to the continuous phase-transient runner. The flag fails fast unless a run is random-init, `batch_size=1`, and `reference_mode=none`; summaries now separately report `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met`, `strict_fully_on_device_requested`, `random_init_used`, and `initial_weights_source`, and the compact summary helper can filter with `--strict-contract-only`. This keeps Milestone A reference-replay checks available while making final-goal evidence harder to confuse with checkpoint-continuation or Python replay diagnostics.
- Ran a tiny strict-contract smoke on the target topology: `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, direct `batch_size=1`, random init, `reference_mode=none`, `--strict-fully-on-device`, 4 online updates, and NumPy final diagnostic eval only. The strict contract fields all passed, `phase_update_l2` was `0.2315`, and the run took `17.5s`; accuracy stayed `18% -> 18%`, so this is contract evidence only, not learning evidence. Compact summary/metrics artifacts were copied to `results/tables` and the generated deck/final-weight sidecars were removed.
- Repeated the bounded target slice with the strict contract enabled: `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, direct `batch_size=1`, random init, `reference_mode=none`, `--strict-fully-on-device`, 16 online updates, powers-of-two sparse probes, and NumPy final diagnostic eval. It reproduced the earlier 16-update learning signal under the strict metadata gate (`5% -> 12%`, improvement `+7pp`, `nontrivial_learning_met=true`) with `phase_update_l2=0.3641` and phase time `61.2s`. This is still a small smoke, but it is now both target-topology strict-contract evidence and nontrivial-learning evidence. Compact summary/metrics/probe CSVs were copied to `results/tables`; generated deck and final-weight sidecars were removed.
- Extended the strict target slice to 32 online updates with powers-of-two sparse probes and NumPy probe evals. The strict contract still passed and state movement grew to `phase_update_l2=0.5000`, but final accuracy was only `5% -> 7%` and `nontrivial_learning_met=false`; the probe curve peaked early at `10%` after update 1, then settled around `6-7%` by updates 4-32. This bounded negative result argues against blindly extending the current `lr=0.1` direct trajectory; the next useful branch should retune update scale/objective or use probe curves on shorter runs before spending on longer strict transients.
- Fixed MNIST sample ordering for horizon comparisons. `mnist_index_splits` now draws a full seeded permutation per split and takes prefixes, so `train_samples=16` is the first 16 examples of `train_samples=32` instead of a different random subset. Under the stable-prefix sampler, a strict target 16-update run reached `5% -> 11%`, with probe eval peaking at `13%` after update 8 and ending at `11%`; this restores nontrivial-learning evidence while making future 32/64-update probe curves comparable to the 16-update baseline.
- Added compact train/eval index metadata to phase summaries (`count`, SHA-256 hash, and first 16 indices) so strict horizon runs can prove they share the same sample prefix without storing full datasets. A stable-prefix strict 32-update rerun confirmed the comparable trajectory shape: eval moved `5% -> 11%` at update 16 but fell to `7%` by update 32, while state movement grew from `0.4033` to `0.4832`. The best probe remained update 8 at `13%`, so the current direct `lr=0.1` path appears to over-update after early improvement rather than simply needing longer unrolled training.
- Ran one lower-rate strict 32-update branch on the same stable-prefix target slice, `lr=0.05`. It reduced final state movement to `phase_update_l2=0.2599` but still ended at `7%` accuracy, with probe evals oscillating between `7%` and `11%` after the first update. Lowering the direct update scale alone therefore did not recover the 16-update/early-probe improvement; the next useful branch should inspect objective/error construction or class/order effects rather than only reducing `lr`.
- Added NumPy evaluation diagnostics to phase summaries and probe rows: label histogram, prediction histogram, correct-by-label, per-class accuracy, dominant predicted class/fraction, and unique predicted classes. A tiny strict 4-update target smoke showed the diagnostic is already useful: the final state predicted class `0` for all 50 eval samples (`dominant_pred_fraction=1.0`), while probes showed class-collapse transitions (`all 8` at update 1, mixed 0/8 at update 2, all 0 at update 4). This points toward output-bias/class-competition dynamics or sample-order class bias as an immediate blocker, not merely weak feature updates.
- Added an `--output-bias-update-scale` diagnostic knob to the phase-transient deck. Freezing output-bias writes on the same tiny strict target smoke reduced state movement from `0.2302` to `0.1042` and softened the collapse (`dominant_pred_fraction` fell from `1.0` to `0.84`, with five predicted classes remaining), but final accuracy stayed `6%` and class `0` still dominated. Output-bias writes amplify the collapse, but readout/class-competition dynamics are sufficient to drive most of it.
- Added the matching `--readout-update-scale` diagnostic knob for phase-transient readout-weight writes and exposed it in compact summaries. A tiny strict target smoke with readout writes frozen (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, 4 online updates, 50-image NumPy eval) still collapsed completely to class `0` (`dominant_pred_fraction=1.0`, one predicted class) and ended at `4% -> 6%`, with `phase_update_l2=0.2221`. Compared with output-bias freeze preserving five predicted classes, this points away from readout writes alone and toward output-bias plus hidden/local update dynamics or error construction as the next diagnostic branch.
- Completed the write-family diagnostic surface with `--local-update-scale`, which scales the local feature-weight and local-bias writes independently of readout and output-bias writes. On the same tiny strict target smoke, head-only writes (`local=0`, readout/output-bias enabled) fully collapsed to class `0` (`dominant_pred_fraction=1.0`, `phase_update_l2=0.2147`), while local-only writes (`readout=0`, `output_bias=0`) still biased strongly to class `0` but kept four predicted classes (`dominant_pred_fraction=0.90`, `phase_update_l2=0.0843`). The collapse is therefore not a single bad write branch; the current softmax/error dynamics can drive class takeover through either the class head or local feature adaptation. Next high-yield branch should change the error/competition construction or use the more robust leaky-ReLU/clipped-feedback setup for this strict no-reference path, not just freeze one write family.
- Added compact train-label metadata to phase summaries and the target-topology audit helper: label sequence hash, prefix, histogram, dominant label/fraction, and unique-label count. A short strict no-reference target smoke using the stronger `tanh` + full-readout-feedback branch (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, `lr=0.8`, hidden `tanh-clipped`, 16 online updates, 100-image NumPy eval) met the fully on-device contract and moved `5% -> 15%` (`nontrivial_learning_met=true`) in `17.5s`. The 16-label training prefix was reasonably spread (`[8,0,0,9,8,4,7,1,9,3,3,0,8,4,4,1]`, histogram `[3,2,0,2,3,0,0,1,3,2]`), so the final concentration on predictions `{1,4,8}` and dominant class `4` at `68%` is not explained by a single-class training prefix. The probe path still shows winner jumping (`8 -> 0 -> 1 -> 4`), so the next branch should damp or normalize class competition/error integration rather than rely on sample-order fixes alone.
- Added a softmax non-target error scale and optional mean-centering to both the phase-transient and matched batch-op local-feature training decks. This preserves the default CE-like error at scale `1.0`, while allowing direct tests of weaker non-target suppression with or without zero-sum class-error centering. On the same strict `tanh` + full-feedback 16-update target smoke, uncentered scale `0.25` worsened collapse (`5% -> 7%`, all 100 eval samples predicted class `4`, `phase_update_l2=5.35`). Mean-centering improved that branch but still trailed the default (`5% -> 10%`, class `4` on `92%`, two predicted classes, `phase_update_l2=3.12` versus default `15%`, class `4` on `68%`, three predicted classes). So the immediate class-collapse fix is not simply reducing non-target softmax error; the next branch should try output-bias/readout normalization, margin constraints, or a different target/error construction rather than this negative-scale axis alone.
- Added `--readout-class-centering` to use class-mean-centered effective readout weights in phase-transient scoring/backward feedback, matched batch-op training/eval, and NumPy diagnostics. The expression is parenthesized as `(v_kbc - mean_class(v_*bc))` before multiplying activations or errors, so it is a real effective-transfer normalization while preserving raw capacitor state. On the same strict `tanh` + full-feedback 16-update target smoke, readout centering exactly reproduced the default accuracy and prediction distribution (`5% -> 15%`, class `4` at `68%`, three predicted classes, `phase_update_l2=2.95364`) but increased phase time to `66s`. With CE-like zero-sum class errors, class-common readout mode was therefore already mostly canceled; this is not the collapse source. The next target should be a margin/target construction or output-bias treatment that changes class differences, not class-common readout centering.
- Ran the stronger strict `tanh` + full-readout-feedback target smoke with output-bias writes frozen from random zero bias. This improved the 16-update / 100-eval result from `5% -> 15%` to `5% -> 20%`, reduced state movement from `phase_update_l2=2.95364` to `2.34770`, and kept three predicted classes with class `4` still dominant at `65%`. Output-bias writes were therefore suppressing useful class differences in the current short strict path, but freezing them does not remove the winner-takeover behavior by itself.
- Compared a leaky-ReLU local activation on the same strict target branch with output bias frozen. At `lr=0.8`, leaky ReLU showed a strong early probe (`30%` at update 8) but collapsed to `11%` by update 16 with class `8` at `98%`; at `lr=0.4`, state movement fell to `0.65497`, but the final result was only `10%` with class `0` at `91%`. The activation has real early signal, but simple learning-rate reduction is not enough to stabilize it. For the next short slices, `tanh` + frozen output bias is the better default branch while margin/error normalization remains the higher-yield target.
- Swept readout write scale on the strict `tanh` + full-feedback + output-bias-frozen branch. Reducing `--readout-update-scale` to `0.25` preserved the `5% -> 20%` final accuracy while reducing dominant class share from `65%` to `51%`, increasing predicted classes from three to four, and cutting state movement to `phase_update_l2=1.23076`. Reducing further to `0.1` slightly underfit (`5% -> 19%`, three predicted classes, dominant class `0` at `42%`). A slower readout head therefore dampens class takeover without killing the useful signal; this is a better default stabilization knob than leaky ReLU at the same horizon.
- Added `--softmax-temperature` to the shared local-feature SPICE error path, so both the phase-transient runner and matched batch-op runner divide score rails before the softmax source and report the setting in summaries/audits. On the current best strict target branch (`tanh`, full feedback, output-bias frozen, `readout_update_scale=0.25`), temperature smoothing gave a small monotonic short-run gain: default `5% -> 20%`, temperature `2` gave `5% -> 21%`, and temperature `4` gave `5% -> 22%` while reducing dominant class fraction from `51%` to `44%`. This does not solve class coverage, but it is aligned with the diagnosis that overconfident competition is still limiting the fully on-device path.
- Extended the temperature-4 strict target branch from 16 to 32 online updates. The train-label prefix now covered all 10 classes, but the probe curve still peaked at update 16 (`22%`) and fell to `14%` by update 32, with final predictions spread across five classes but dominated by class `0` at `46%`. Temperature smoothing therefore improves the early winner-takeover shape but does not fix longer-horizon drift. The next high-yield branch should change the update objective or add a state-level normalization/decay mechanism rather than just extending this trajectory.
- Added `--state-decay` as an explicit on-device update/apply-phase current that pulls persistent local weights, local biases, readout weights, and output biases toward zero; the matched batch-op deck applies the same per-update factor to keep reference diagnostics coherent. A strict 32-update target run on the temperature-4 branch with `state_decay=0.01` lowered state movement (`phase_update_l2=1.99 -> 1.54`) but did not improve learning: it kept the same update-16 peak (`22%`) and ended slightly worse (`14% -> 13%`, class `0` at `49%`). Simple global decay reduces motion but is not the missing stabilization; the next branch should be more selective, likely acting on class/objective normalization rather than damping all persistent state.
- Tested selective local-feature damping on the same strict 32-update temperature branch by setting `--local-update-scale 0.5` while keeping readout scale at `0.25` and output-bias writes frozen. It reduced movement further (`phase_update_l2=1.10`) but worsened learning (`12%` final) and collapsed harder to class `0` (`86%`). Together with global decay, this argues that the current failure is not merely too-large local feature motion; the class/objective signal is still steering the state into bad attractors after the update-16 peak.
- Tested whether the non-target softmax error branch is the source of the strict 32-update drift by setting `--softmax-negative-scale 0` on the temperature-4/readout-scale-0.25/output-bias-frozen branch. It was much worse: state movement exploded to `phase_update_l2=7.39`, the best probe was only `15%` at update 2, and the final eval collapsed completely to class `0` at `7%`. The non-target branch is therefore stabilizing this setup; the next objective-level branch should not remove non-target error, but should look at balanced/normalized class-error transport or margin constraints that preserve competition without runaway attractors.
- Tried stronger but zero-sum non-target competition with `--softmax-negative-scale 2 --softmax-error-centering mean` on the same strict 32-update branch. This avoided the target-only state explosion, but still underperformed the default CE-like error: best probe `20%` at update 16, final `11%`, dominant class `5` at `60%`, and `phase_update_l2=2.24`. Both weaker and stronger non-target scaling are worse here; the next objective experiment should change the error shape more structurally, for example margin-gated errors or score normalization before the error source, not just scalar non-target gain.
- Consolidated the fast online reference's NumPy forward/eval helper surface with the phase-transient runner's diagnostic NumPy helpers. The fast reference now imports the shared block extraction, synapse transfer, activation, output activation, and accuracy functions from the phase runner instead of carrying duplicate copies. This reduces the risk that cheap long-horizon calibration drifts away from the final/probe diagnostic equations used for fully on-device Xyce runs.
- Added an explicit post-transient `--eval-backend numpy` diagnostic path to the phase-transient runner while keeping SPICE eval as the default. The NumPy backend reads only the final/probe states already extracted for diagnostics and computes accuracy without generating post-training SPICE eval decks, so it does not affect the one-transient training contract or write weights between samples. This is a runtime knob for longer no-reference direct runs where final accuracy is allowed to be a Python-side diagnostic.
- Ran a bounded target-topology no-reference sparse-probe smoke after switching probes to Xyce `.print`: `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, direct `batch_size=1`, 16 online updates, 100-image eval. The run stayed in one training transient with no Python weight writes/checkpoints, used `output_mode=print`, and populated probe movement at updates 1/2/4/8/16 (`phase_update_l2` ended at `0.3641`). Eval moved from `5%` to `12%` in this small slice (`nontrivial_learning_met=true` by the configured smoke gate), with phase time `49.5s` and eval time `16.9s`. This is not high-accuracy evidence, but it confirms sparse probes scale to the requested topology without measure-card explosion. Compact summary/metrics/probe CSVs were copied to `results/tables`; generated decks and weights were removed.
- Switched Xyce auto output for phase-transient probes from per-state `.measure` lines to sparse `.print TRAN` rows at the requested probe/final timepoints. Xyce accepts comma-separated `OUTPUTTIMEPOINTS`, so no-reference probe runs can now avoid generating `state_size * probe_count` measurement cards while still recovering measured probe states from the same uninterrupted transient. Explicit `--phase-output-mode measure` remains available for measure-based debugging.
- Split the phase-transient summary's execution contract from the reference-matching gate. No-reference direct runs now report explicit fully-on-device execution fields (`single_phase_training_transient`, persistent in-transient weights, no Python weight updates/checkpointing between samples, and `fully_on_device_execution_contract_met`) even though `continuous_transient_contract_met` remains null when batch-op replay is skipped. This makes long no-reference summaries less ambiguous while preserving the stricter reference-alignment gate for Milestone A checks.
- Made the phase-transient runner clean simulator sidecar files (`.prn`, `.mt0`, `.ms0`, `.ma0`) for the decks it owns after parsing final/probe/eval diagnostics. The explicit artifacts referenced by summaries are still preserved, but repeated short Xyce probe runs no longer leave unreferenced print/measure sidecars in `spice/generated`, reducing disk churn while keeping the fully on-device diagnostic workflow intact.
- Added compact probe-curve fields to phase-transient summaries: probe count, final/max phase-state movement, and best probe eval/improvement when probe evals are enabled. The probe CSV remains the detailed artifact, but no-reference direct runs can now be triaged from the summary JSON alone, which is useful for short `batch_size=1` learning-curve slices without large replay runs.
- Added phase-only update magnitude reporting for no-reference continuous transient runs. When `reference_mode=none`, final summaries and probe CSVs now keep `phase_update_l2` populated from the measured capacitor state delta while leaving reference direction/sign fields null. This makes short direct-run probes more useful for diagnosing dead update paths without paying for batch-op replay or violating the no-checkpoint contract.
- Relaxed the phase-transient probe path so `reference_mode=none` can still save measured in-transient phase states and optional phase-only probe evals after the uninterrupted transient finishes. Probe CSV rows now use null reference-comparison fields when the expensive batch-op replay is intentionally skipped, instead of rejecting probes outright. This keeps long direct `batch_size=1` runs within the no-checkpoint contract while giving a cheap learning-curve diagnostic knob for small selected update points.
- Tightened the fully on-device phase-transient metadata after the direct-update branch: deck comments and JSON summaries now describe phased gradient-accumulator state only for `update_mode=phased`, while `update_mode=direct` reports direct per-sample weight updates and avoids implying Python checkpoint ICs between samples. Added regression coverage for the helper and the direct-mode deck comment so future update-mode variants do not inherit stale state descriptions.
- Replaced the legacy Python netlist-normalization dependency with a neutral, spicelib-backed SPICE adapter. The adapter now keeps raw generated circuit bodies textual, owns title/end/control-block rendering, strips ngspice-only control blocks for Xyce, and uses spicelib for simulator executable resolution while preserving SPICENN's output contract (`ngspice -b deck`, `Xyce deck`). A first attempt to use spicelib's raw/log runner directly exposed two incompatible defaults: ngspice rejects top-level `.measure` when `-r` rawfile output is forced, and Xyce `.print` readers stop seeing `.prn` files when `-r` is forced.
- Added in-transient prefix probes to the fully on-device local-feature phase runner. The probes are `.measure` diagnostics inside the same uninterrupted transient, then compared against the post-run batch-op reference per prefix. A 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 Xyce smoke stayed aligned through 10 updates (`cos=0.9999577`, sign alignment `98.57%`). An 8x8 b4 stride2 c2, channel-2 diagnostic showed gradual accumulated drift rather than an immediate wiring error: cosine was `0.99996` at update 4, `0.99978` at 8, `0.99898` at 16, `0.99785` at 32, and `0.99356` at 50. A first 10x10 b4 stride2 c2, channel-2 smoke completed in the target topology but already missed the strict direction gate at 10 updates (`cos=0.99834`, sign alignment `98.97%`), so the next correction should target accumulated analog integration/drift before longer 10x10 learning runs.
- Fixed the accumulated drift source: finite-edge phase clocks have trapezoid area `phase + edge`, while the gradient/apply integrators had been normalizing by `phase`. Normalizing update integrators by `TAREA=phase+edge` restored the continuous-vs-batch-op contract on the target topology: the 10x10 b4 stride2 c2, channel-2, 10-update Xyce smoke improved from `cos=0.99834`, sign alignment `98.97%`, max state error `3.3e-2` to `cos=0.9999999991`, sign alignment `100%`, max state error `3.5e-5`. The 8x8 b4 stride2 c2, channel-2, 50-update smoke similarly improved to `cos=0.9999999964`, sign alignment `100%`, max state error `1.23e-4`.
- Exposed local activation variants in the continuous phase runner so neuron forward/backward choices can be compared under the same fully on-device contract. A 4x4 10-update Xyce screen passed for `tanh`, `relu`, `clipped-relu`, and `diff-clipped-relu`. On the 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 target topology at 5 updates, `tanh` and `diff-clipped-relu` passed with 100% sign alignment, while single-ended `relu`/`clipped-relu` had excellent cosine but only `92.78%` sign alignment. This makes differential/clipped activation handling the stronger next branch for larger robustness/learning experiments.
- Added a phase-variant sweep harness so activation/neuron choices can be run as a reproducible matrix while preserving the underlying runner's `batch_size=1`, one-transient training contract. A compact 10x10 b4 stride2 c2, channel-2, 8-update Xyce sweep over `tanh` and `diff-clipped-relu` clips `0.5/1.0` kept all variants electrically aligned (`cos >= 0.999999998`, sign alignment `100%`, max state error about `2.4e-5`). Accuracy stayed flat at 12% on a 50-image eval after only 8 online samples, so this was a robustness/contract screen rather than a learning result. The harness is now the right place to add broader synapse/neuron forward/backward variants before committing to long 10x10 learning runs.
- Ran the first corrected medium-scale fully on-device target-topology learning probe: 10x10 b4 stride2 c2, channel-2, `diff-clipped-relu`, `batch_size=1`, 50 online updates, Xyce one-transient training, 200-image eval. The run preserved the contract (`continuous_transient_contract_met=true`, direction cosine `0.9999956`, sign alignment `100%`, final eval matched the batch-op reference exactly) and crossed the nontrivial-learning gate: initial eval `12%`, final eval `20%`, improvement `+8%`. This is still far from the >90% objective and only 50 online samples, but it is meaningful learning on the target topology without Python weight checkpointing between samples. Summary/equivalence/probe CSVs were copied to `results/tables/`; generated decks and weight checkpoints were removed.
- Added explicit neuron/backward/synapse variant knobs to the local-feature phase path and its matched batch-op reference: leaky ReLU and softplus forward activations, exact/stored-gate/floor-exact derivative masks, and full/clipped readout-feedback transfer for backward hidden deltas. A 36-row Xyce robustness screen on the target 10x10 b4 stride2 c2, channel-2 topology showed a clear analog-ReLU split: `tanh`, `diff-clipped-relu`, `leaky-relu`, and `softplus` all preserved the fully-on-device contract with 100% sign alignment, while plain single-ended ReLU failed all six rows despite high cosine (`~92.5-97.7%` sign alignment). Softplus had the smallest short-screen state error, but the 50-update learning probe only moved `14% -> 16%`; leaky ReLU with 1% negative slope and clipped backward readout-feedback moved `6.5% -> 32%` on the same 200-image eval while exactly matching the batch-op reference. This makes leaky ReLU the strongest robust analog-ReLU candidate from this slice, with differential clipped ReLU still a good signed bounded alternative.
- Extended the leaky-ReLU/clipped-feedback branch to a 100-sample uninterrupted Xyce transient on the target topology (`batch_size=1`, `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, `lr=0.8`). The run again preserved the fully on-device contract (`continuous_transient_contract_met=true`, direction cosine `0.999999991`, sign alignment `100%`, final eval matched the batch-op reference exactly) and stayed clearly above random, moving a 300-image eval from `8%` to `31%`. Because this is essentially flat versus the 50-update leaky result (`32%` on 200 images), the next high-yield branch is not merely longer `lr=0.8` training; it should test update scale and backward feedback transfer (`readout` vs clipped/sign) on leaky ReLU, or combine leaky forward transport with the differential clipped bounded representation.
- Tested two direct leaky-ReLU branch hypotheses on the target topology. Full readout feedback at the same `lr=0.8`, 50-update, 200-eval setup passed the contract but reached only `22%`, below clipped feedback's `32%`, so the feedback clip is not the immediate learning bottleneck. Doubling clipped-feedback learning rate to `lr=1.6` also passed the contract but dropped to `12.5%`, so the plateau is not fixed by a larger update scale. Added `--eval-probe-updates` diagnostics to evaluate measured in-transient probe states after the uninterrupted transient finishes; a small Xyce smoke confirmed probe CSVs now carry phase/reference eval accuracy without Python checkpointing during training. The next target run should use this to locate the update horizon where leaky/clipped peaks before trying longer or scheduled runs.
- Found a comparability bug in `load_mnist_sequence`: using one RNG stream for both train and held-out sampling made the held-out eval slice depend on `--train-samples`, so 50/64/100-update phase runs were not measuring against the same test subset. Split train/test sampling into independent deterministic streams and added a regression test. Re-running the leaky-ReLU/clipped-feedback probe-eval target branch with stable held-out sampling (`64` online updates, 200 eval images) preserved the contract (`direction cosine 0.999999978`, sign alignment `100%`, eval reference match), but showed weak learning on the fixed slice: initial `9%`, best probe `13.5%` at update 16, final `12.5%` at update 64. This supersedes cross-horizon conclusions drawn only from moving eval slices; future optimization should use stable held-out samples and probably revisit the objective/feature branch rather than simply extending this leaky/clipped setting.
- Re-tested the high-performing batch-op branch's local nonlinearity under the fully on-device phase runner with stable held-out sampling. `tanh` + full readout feedback on the target topology (`64` online updates, 200 eval images, `lr=0.8`) learned better than leaky ReLU: initial `11%`, probes `13.5%` at updates 16/32, final `22%`. Direction still matched the batch-op reference well (`cos=0.9999589`, sign alignment `100%`), but the strict full contract failed because final phase/reference eval differed by one sample (`22.0%` vs `21.5%`) and state error jumped at update 64 (`state_max_abs_diff=0.028`). Reducing the store aggressiveness from `settle_ratio=80` to `20` did not fix the drift (`state_max_abs_diff=0.031`, same eval mismatch). The next branch should keep `tanh` as the learning candidate but target accumulated state drift, likely via timestep/integration strategy or phase scheduling, before attempting longer fully-on-device runs.
- Added effective synapse-transfer variants to the local-feature batch-op and fully on-device phase-transient paths so hidden/readout forward conductance can be swept separately from neuron activation and backward feedback: linear, smooth/tanh-clipped, hard-clipped, and sign/binary transfers. The phase variant sweep now expands hidden/readout synapse modes in addition to activation, derivative, and feedback mode. A tiny non-default Xyce smoke (`tanh`, hidden `tanh-clipped`, readout `hard-clipped`, `synapse_clip=0.25`, 4x4 b2 stride2 c1, two online samples) preserved the continuous contract (`cos=0.9999999998`, sign alignment `100%`, max state error `1.18e-5`). An 8-row target-topology screen (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, four online samples) crossed `tanh`/`leaky-relu` with linear/tanh-clipped hidden synapses and linear/hard-clipped readout synapses; every row preserved the contract with `100%` sign alignment. Tanh rows had lower short-run state error (`~1.8e-5`) than leaky-ReLU rows (`~4.8e-5` to `5.0e-5`), and tanh-clipping hidden synapses slightly improved both activation families. This screen is still a robustness/equivalence screen, not a learning result, but it gives the next long runs a real synapse axis instead of only comparing neuron nonlinearities.
- Ran two 64-update stable-eval target-topology probes to test whether hidden synapse transfer can reduce the `tanh` branch's long-run drift without killing learning. `tanh-clipped` hidden synapses at `synapse_clip=0.25` were very stable (`state_max_abs_diff=3.23e-4`, exact eval/reference match) but underfit, moving only `9.5% -> 13.0%`; the probe curve matched the linear baseline through update 16 and then stalled. Widening the hidden synapse clip to `1.0` was a better compromise: `11.0% -> 20.0%`, exact phase/reference eval match, direction cosine `0.99999956`, sign alignment `100%`, and `state_max_abs_diff=0.00538`. The old linear hidden-synapse tanh branch still learned slightly more (`22.0%`) but failed the strict contract due eval mismatch and much larger drift (`0.0281`). Current best random-init fully-on-device target branch is therefore still tanh/readout learning, with hidden synapse clipping around `1.0` as a promising robustness knob; `0.25` is too restrictive.
- Generalized the phase variant sweep to accept `--synapse-clips` so future runs can scan hidden/readout transfer saturation without hand-editing repeated commands. Tags now include the synapse clip value, avoiding collisions between otherwise identical transfer-mode rows.
- Tested a wider hidden `tanh-clipped` synapse transfer at `synapse_clip=2.0` on the same stable-eval target topology (`64` online updates, `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, `batch_size=1`). This is the strongest random-init fully-on-device target result so far: initial eval `11.0%`, final phase eval `25.5%`, batch-op reference eval `25.5%`, strict contract passed, direction cosine `0.9999849`, sign alignment `100%`, and `state_max_abs_diff=0.0156`. It recovers and exceeds the old linear-hidden tanh learning (`22%`) while still cutting drift nearly in half versus the old linear branch (`0.0281`). The next high-yield experiment is either a slightly wider clip (`3-4`) or a longer run from this `clip=2.0` branch to see whether the post-update-32 jump continues.
- Added a fast NumPy online reference for the current phase-transient update equations, including softmax-output deltas and hidden/readout synapse transfer. This is not SPICE evidence, but it is now a cheap way to choose long-horizon settings before spending Xyce time. The reference reproduced the main qualitative lesson from the SPICE probes: `lr=0.8` can show early jumps but is unstable over thousands of online samples. On the target topology with `tanh`, hidden `tanh-clipped`, `synapse_clip=2.0`, and `batch_size=1`, a 5k-sample fast sweep found `lr=0.1` best among the scanned rates (`88.2%` on 1k eval at 5k samples), while `lr=0.8` fell back to `8.7%` by 5k. Extending `lr=0.1` to 20k online samples reached `90.8%` on a 2k eval slice. This strongly suggests the next fully on-device/Xyce verification should use much lower learning rate and longer horizon, not extend `lr=0.8`.
- Added `--reference-mode none` to the continuous phase-transient runner so long `batch_size=1` Xyce learning probes can skip the expensive repeated batch-op SPICE replay while still running one uninterrupted transient and final diagnostics. The default remains `reference-mode=spice` for Milestone A equivalence checks, and no-reference mode rejects in-transient probe-update comparisons because those currently require reference states. A tiny 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 Xyce smoke completed with `status=continuous_phase_train_no_reference`, null reference artifact fields, null equivalence gates, `op_reference_wall_time_s` effectively zero, and final eval diagnostics still populated (`15% -> 0%` on a 20-image smoke slice). This was a plumbing/contract smoke, not learning evidence.
- Long no-reference target runs exposed a separate Xyce extraction/runtime issue. With top-level `.measure` final-state extraction, `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2 target runs at `lr=0.1` timed out in the main transient at both 256 updates / 500 eval and 128 updates / 300 eval. Switching Xyce no-probe auto mode to sparse `.print TRAN` with `.options output OUTPUTTIMEPOINTS=<final>` fixed final-state extraction and completed the 128-update run. The completed run was one uninterrupted `batch_size=1` transient with no Python checkpoints and no reference replay; it moved a 300-image eval from `10.33%` to `18.33%` (`nontrivial_learning_met=true`) in `816.3s` phase time plus `36.5s` eval. This is Milestone B early-learning evidence on the target topology, not yet a scalable/high-accuracy result. Compact summary/metrics were copied to `results/tables/`.
- Scanned cheaper timing knobs for the strict one-transient target-topology path at the calibrated `lr=0.1`. A 16-update Xyce equivalence probe with `phase=0.5ns`, `gap=0.05ns`, `edge=5ps`, and `settle_ratio=20` preserved the batch-op direction exactly enough for the contract (`cos=0.9999999994`, sign alignment `100%`, `state_max_abs_diff=2.04e-6`, final eval matched reference). Coarsening `--transient-step` from `50ps` to `100ps` and `200ps` kept the same state/eval metrics while reducing phase time from `97.9s` to `72.8s` and `71.9s` for 16 updates.
