A 64-update no-reference run with the `200ps` setting completed in `289.9s` (`4.53s/update` versus `6.38s/update` for the prior 128-update 1ns/80/50ps run), but only moved `10.33% -> 11.0%` on the 300-image eval slice. The cheaper timing is therefore validated for direction and runtime, but clear learning still needs the 128-512+ update horizon predicted by the fast online reference.
- Re-ran the 128-update no-reference target-topology learning probe with the cheaper `0.5ns/20/200ps` timing. It reproduced the default-timing learning result exactly on the 300-image eval slice (`10.33% -> 18.33%`, `nontrivial_learning_met=true`) and stayed within the fully on-device contract, but phase wall time only improved slightly (`816.3s -> 791.8s`). The bottleneck is therefore not mainly the explicit transient step or physical phase duration once sparse final-state output is used; scaling to 512+ and eventually 5k-20k updates likely needs a deeper deck/runtime reduction, such as fewer behavioral unknowns, a faster Xyce integration strategy, or a smaller but accuracy-preserving target topology.
- Added a simulator-extra-args path (`SPICENN_SIMULATOR_ARGS` / `--simulator-extra-args`) and tested Xyce solver switches. A direct 8-update generated-deck microbenchmark improved from `48.9s` default to `35.1s` with `-quiet -linsolv KLU`, but the full 16-update phase runner did not reproduce that win: the same target-topology equivalence probe took `77.5s` with `-quiet -linsolv KLU` versus `71.9s` for the prior default run, with identical state/eval metrics. Also added optional `--rleak 0` omission of near-infinite state leak resistors; it preserved the contract (`cos=0.9999999994`, `state_max_abs_diff=2.04e-6`) but likewise did not materially speed the 16-update run (`74.9s`). These knobs are useful for controlled simulator/deck scans, but the current runtime limiter appears to be the behavioral transient solve itself rather than output verbosity, linear solver choice, physical phase length, timestep cap, or leak-resistor count.
- Added `--update-mode direct` for the strict online `batch_size=1` objective. Direct mode removes gradient accumulator capacitors plus apply/clear phases and writes persistent weights directly during each sample's update phase; it is rejected for `batch_size>1` so it does not pretend to support batching. A 4x4 Xyce smoke and a 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 target equivalence probe both matched the repeated batch-op reference (`10x10`: `cos=0.9999999996`, sign alignment `100%`, final eval matched). Runtime improved materially: the 16-update target equivalence phase time dropped from `71.9s` phased to `49.9s` direct, and the 128-update no-reference target run reproduced the same early-learning result (`10.33% -> 18.33%`) while dropping phase time from `791.8s` to `286.9s`. This is the first large runtime reduction that preserves the fully on-device batch_size=1 learning behavior.
- Extended direct online mode to 256 uninterrupted target-topology updates. The run stayed within the strict no-checkpoint contract (`reference_mode=none`, `update_mode=direct`, `batch_size=1`, one Xyce transient) and moved the 300-image eval from `10.33%` to `44.0%` in `522.1s` phase time plus `37.0s` eval. This is the strongest fully on-device random-init learning result so far on the requested `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology. The fast online reference still predicts that the larger jump should occur in the 512-1024 update range, now reachable in principle with direct mode.

## 2026-05-21

- Verified local SPICE tools for this workspace: `ngspice` is installed at `/usr/bin/ngspice` and reports version 42; `Xyce` is installed at `/usr/local/bin/Xyce` and reports 7.10.0.
- Focused SPICENN tests initially could not collect because declared Python dependencies were missing (`scipy`, the legacy netlist adapter package, then CPU-only `torch`/`torchvision` for MNIST dataset loading). Installed those in the user site packages. Aborted the default CUDA `torch` wheel path and purged the pip cache before installing CPU-only wheels.
- The tiny MNIST dataset files are not present under this repo's `data/` path. The loader deliberately uses `download=False`, so MNIST smokes need either a local dataset cache or an explicit dataset-download policy change.
- Reproduced the readout eligibility bottleneck on built-in SPICE smokes: nonzero ReLU activations around 0.06-0.23 V can be useful, but a 0.3 V spike reference leaves readout pretrace gates mostly off, so target errors only update bias or analog fallback paths. A lower reference exposes real hidden eligibility coverage and improves the `sum_extremes` smoke.
- Downloaded MNIST through the existing high-level `load_mnist_sequence` path. The shared `spice/datasets.py` path was the inconsistent one: it hard-coded `download=False`, which blocked sparse/device MNIST smokes on a fresh checkout. Added an explicit download flag instead of silently changing default network behavior.
- After the dataset path was unblocked, `run_device_spicenn_sparse_forward.py --dataset mnist3fixed8_3 --download-dataset ...` completed electrically with full pretrace coverage, but final sparse-device evaluation was still only 1/3. The immediate blocker moved from dataset plumbing back to architecture/training quality.
- Tried to reproduce a current all-SPICE local-feature batch-op baseline. The first moderate run failed before accuracy because `run_spice_mnist_local_feature_batch_op_train.py` wrote `wrdata` into `spice/results/` without creating that directory. Added output-directory creation and verified a 200-train/100-test smoke completes. That runner is the current practical path for reproducing the historical >90% local-feature MNIST results.
- Re-running the full SPICE goal audit on this checkout exposed an artifact-location fragility: the script only scanned `spice/results/`, while this repo stores many result tables under `results/tables/`. Added a resolver and regression tests for `spice/results`/`results/tables` summary fallback. The old high-evidence audit snapshot still depends on historical summary JSONs that are not present in this checkout, so generated audit outputs must not be treated as new evidence unless the artifacts are regenerated or restored.
- The full test suite had three stale/adjacent failures after the SPICENN/pretrace changes. Two tests still expected the synapse-spike comparator path to use `NREL`, but `PreTraceCell` now correctly uses the low-threshold `NSENSE` sense device. The third failure was a real positional-argument drift in the readout-array `flow_offstate` load path; converting that call to keyword arguments fixed the circuit option mapping and restored the suite.
- A current 5,000-train / 2,000-test `run_spice_mnist_local_feature_batch_op_train.py` reproduction attempt with `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 5 epochs, batch size 100, and lr 0.03 was too large for an interactive slice: after about four minutes it was still driving a per-step ngspice deck. Stopped it and removed the generated `codex_current_smallnet10_b4_s2_c2_5k2k_e5_lr003` step artifacts. This runner needs either a smaller smoke, a resumed existing checkpoint, or a longer unattended run to regenerate the historical >90% evidence.
- Added chunking/checkpoint support to the batch-op local-feature runner: `--max-train-batches` caps each invocation after the deterministic epoch offset/sample slice, and `--checkpoint-every-batches` writes an init-compatible `*_latest_weights.npz` plus progress JSON during training. A tiny ngspice smoke trained one batch, resumed from the latest checkpoint at `--epoch-train-offset 2`, and produced the expected checkpoint metadata; generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Ran the new chunking path on the actual high-margin `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` local-feature topology. A first 10-sample ngspice batch plus 100-image eval completed in 6.9 s and wrote `codex_smallnet10_b4_s2_c2_slice0_b10_latest_weights.npz`; a resumed slice from `--epoch-train-offset 10` trained two more 10-sample batches in 9.0 s and wrote `codex_smallnet10_b4_s2_c2_slice10_b10x2_latest_weights.npz`. Accuracy is still near random this early (7-8% on 100 eval samples), but the all-SPICE target branch can now advance in resumable chunks. Generated `.cir` decks were removed; small checkpoint/result artifacts remain under `spice/results/` for continuation.
- Added `--skip-heldout-eval` to the same runner so expensive resumable chunks can train and checkpoint without paying for a held-out SPICE eval every invocation. A tiny ngspice train-only smoke produced `null` held-out metrics and an init-compatible latest checkpoint, and the full test suite passed afterward.
- Regenerated a current high-margin `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` local-feature checkpoint with fast PyTorch training on 5,000 MNIST train / 1,000 held-out samples. It crossed 90% at epoch 4 and ended at 92.7% after 10 epochs. ngspice operating-point eval of the exported best checkpoint with `--linear-output` matched the same 92.7% on the 1,000-image held-out slice. This is SPICE forward evidence for the compact topology, not all-SPICE training; the all-SPICE random-init trajectory is still only at 11-12% after 630 seen samples.
- Re-evaluated the same exported compact checkpoint on a broader 2,000-image held-out MNIST slice in ngspice. The SPICE operating-point deck reached 91.7% (`1834/2000`) with `--linear-output`, so the current compact topology has reproducible >90% SPICE forward evidence beyond the original 1,000-image slice. The generated 2.5 MB eval deck was removed; summary/curve CSV were copied to `results/tables/`.
- Fast reference evaluation of the same compact checkpoint on the full 10,000-image MNIST test set reached 91.8% (`9180/10000`). A full ngspice operating-point eval over all 10,000 test images also reached 91.8% with `--linear-output`, taking about 30 minutes with 200-image eval decks. This is now full-test SPICE forward evidence for the 5k-trained compact checkpoint; it still does not close the end-to-end all-SPICE training gap.
- Started phase-transient SPICE training continuation from the full-test >90 compact checkpoint. A short `lr=0.003` continuation stayed above target for 4 real SPICE-trained samples on the 1,000-image ngspice slice (`92.9%` final, `93.4%` best after the first chunk), but extending the same update scale to 20 samples degraded to `87.2%`. Reducing phase update scale to `lr=0.0003` kept the 20-sample SPICE-trained continuation stable at `91.8%` on the same 1,000-image ngspice eval. This makes phase update scale the next concrete stability knob for longer all-SPICE continuation.
- Extended the stable low-rate phase-transient continuation from 20 to 100 total SPICE-trained samples (`lr=0.0003`, batch size 2, one update per chunk). The final 100-sample checkpoint evaluated at `93.6%` on the 1,000-image ngspice slice, improving over the original compact checkpoint's 92.7% 1k eval and the 20-sample low-rate 91.8% eval. This is strong evidence that the phase-training path can make useful updates when the scale is small enough, but it is still continuation from a fast-trained checkpoint rather than random-init all-SPICE training.
- Continued the same low-rate trajectory from 100 to 200 total SPICE-trained samples. The final 200-sample checkpoint stayed above target at `92.9%` on the 1,000-image ngspice slice, down from the 100-sample `93.6%` peak but still above the original exported checkpoint's 92.7% 1k eval. Longer-horizon continuation now looks stable enough to probe, but accuracy is not monotonic, so future runs should either lower the phase learning rate further or add periodic eval/early-stop checkpoints.
- Extended again to 300 total SPICE-trained samples with the same `lr=0.0003` phase settings. The final 300-sample checkpoint remained above target at `92.1%` on the 1,000-image ngspice slice, but the sequence `93.6% -> 92.9% -> 92.1%` confirms slow drift after the 100-sample peak. The next high-yield branch is a lower-rate continuation or periodic eval/early-stop support rather than blindly extending this rate.
- Branched from the 100-sample peak and repeated samples 100-200 at a lower phase learning rate, `lr=0.0001`. The 200-sample lower-rate checkpoint evaluated at `93.4%` on the same 1,000-image ngspice slice, better than the `92.9%` reached by `lr=0.0003` over the same interval. This confirms the drift is at least partly update-scale driven and makes lower-rate continuation the preferred next branch.
- Continued the `lr=0.0001` branch to 300 total SPICE-trained samples. The 300-sample lower-rate checkpoint evaluated at `93.2%` on the 1,000-image ngspice slice, versus `92.1%` for the same horizon at `lr=0.0003`. The smaller update scale still drifts slightly from the 100-sample `93.6%` peak, but it preserves a wider margin above 90% and is the stronger branch for longer continuation.
- Extended the `lr=0.0001` branch from 300 to 500 total SPICE-trained samples. The 500-sample checkpoint evaluated at `92.8%` on the 1,000-image ngspice slice, still above target but continuing the slow downward drift from 300 samples. One phase-training runner process stayed alive after writing its final summary and weights; terminating the stale parent was safe because no ngspice child was active and the artifacts were already flushed.
- Added a `--save-best-eval-weights` phase-runner option so periodic evals can preserve the best SPICE-evaluated checkpoint instead of only the final drifting state. Using it on the `lr=0.0001` branch from 500 to 1000 total SPICE-trained samples kept all periodic 1,000-image ngspice evals above 90%: `92.4%` at 600 samples, `91.9%` at 800 samples, and `91.7%` at 1000 samples. The best-eval checkpoint was the 600-sample state, confirming useful long-horizon stability but also persistent slow drift.
- Re-evaluated the saved 600-sample best-eval phase checkpoint on a broader 2,000-image ngspice heldout slice. - Ran the saved 600-sample best-eval phase checkpoint through the full 10,000-image MNIST ngspice operating-point eval. The margin is thinner than the 1,000/2,000-image slices and the workflow still starts from a fast-trained compact checkpoint, but this is now full-test SPICE evidence after real SPICE phase updates.
- Added explicit simulator selection and Xyce print-file readback to the local-feature batch/phase runners. This removes a simulator-specific confidence gap in the current >90 evidence.
- Used the Xyce-enabled batch-op runner to restart the random-init all-SPICE branch on the compact `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology. Three true resumed epochs at `lr=0.03` moved the 1,000-image heldout accuracy from `33.8%` after epoch 1 to `40.5%` after epoch 2 and `44.1%` after epoch 3. This is still far below the >90 goal, but it is a practical all-SPICE random-init trajectory rather than the old 11-12% short ngspice slice, and `--start-epoch` now preserves deterministic epoch-order continuation across resumed chunks.

- Switched the random-init all-SPICE Xyce batch-op branch to the softmax output rule with `lr=0.8` on the compact `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology. This is the first current robust >90 evidence for random-init all-SPICE training in this checkout, not a continuation from a fast-trained checkpoint.

- Added a preflight transient-cost guard to the continuous phase runner. The variant sweep passes the same guard through, so activation/synapse screening can stay bounded instead of accidentally starting another long target-topology transient.
- Ran a cheap NumPy-only target-topology screen at 256 online updates / 300 eval samples using the current strict branch knobs (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, `lr=0.8`, output-bias frozen, readout scale `0.25`, temperature `4`, full readout feedback). The existing `tanh` + `tanh-clipped` hidden synapse branch stayed best (`9.0% -> 64.33%`). A differential clipped-ReLU with stored-gate derivative was close but lower (`9.0% -> 61.33%`), while softplus and leaky ReLU were much weaker (`37.0%` and `32.33%` final) and clipped-ReLU with hard-clipped hidden synapses was worse (`26.33%`). This fast screen says the next bounded Xyce proof should stay on the tanh branch unless a new activation also beats it cheaply first.
- Added a fast online variant-sweep runner for the local-feature reference path. A tiny 16-update smoke over `tanh` vs `softplus` confirmed the CLI path and kept `tanh` on top (`5% -> 30%` on a 40-image eval slice); the smoke artifacts were removed.
- Updated the SPICE phase variant sweep to forward the current strict-branch controls instead of silently using old defaults: reference mode, phase output mode, update mode, eval backend, update scales, state decay, softmax shaping/gating/temperature, readout class centering, simulator extra args, and `--strict-fully-on-device`. A dry strict target command now includes `--reference-mode none`, `--phase-output-mode print`, `--update-mode direct`, `--eval-backend numpy`, output-bias freeze, readout scale `0.25`, temperature `4`, and the strict contract flag. This lets fast-screened activation/synapse candidates be promoted to bounded Xyce runs under the same fully on-device contract.
- Extended the fast online variant sweep to grid strict objective/update knobs (`--lrs`, output-bias/readout/local update scales, state decay, and softmax temperatures). A 27-point target-topology NumPy screen at 256 updates / 300 eval improved the best cheap candidate from the previous `64.33%` to `68.0%` with `lr=0.8`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `3`; a 512-update neighborhood screen favored `lr=0.8`, readout scale `0.25`, temperature `3` at `74.33%`, up from the old temperature-4 branch's `72.67%`. A bounded strict Xyce promotion of that temperature-3 branch at 64 updates matched the fast reference exactly (`9.0% -> 20.0%`), kept `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, used 902 estimated transient points / 884 printed state vectors, and took `65.97s`. The generated deck and final weights were removed; compact summaries/CSVs were kept under `results/tables`.
- Added a strict phase-promotion command to each fast online variant-sweep result row. A tiny 8-sample smoke generated a 4-update Xyce promotion command with the expected strict flags and candidate knobs; smoke artifacts were removed.
- Promoted the temperature-3 strict branch one horizon further with a bounded 128-update Xyce run on the target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology. The fast reference predicted `9.0% -> 37.33%` on the fixed 300-image eval slice, and the strict Xyce run matched it exactly (`phase_eval_accuracy=0.3733333333`) with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, no reference replay, no Python checkpointing between samples, 1,798 estimated transient points, 884 printed state vectors, and `111.64s` phase runtime. This is lower than the previous temperature-4 128-update `43.0%`, so temperature 3 is not a better early SPICE promotion target even though it was slightly better by fast reference at 512 updates. Generated deck/result weights were removed; compact evidence was copied to `results/tables`.
- Extended the phase-transient result summarizer with strict-target audit fields (`target_topology`, `strict_target_contract_met`, issue lists, transient-point/vector counts) and accuracy sorting. Running it over the current evidence with `--target-topology --strict-contract-only --sort accuracy` ranks the temperature-4 branch as the current best strict target path: `64.33%` at 256 updates, then `43.0%` at 128 updates. The new temperature-3 128-update promotion ranks lower at `37.33%` despite matching the fast reference, so the next Xyce promotion should not move away from temperature 4 unless a fast screen finds a clearer advantage.
- Added a final-measurement timing guard before coarse-step phase runs: `--transient-step` must leave the final state measurement after the last direct update/clear phase, instead of silently sampling before the final update settles. A bounded target-topology temp-4 64-update Xyce check with `--transient-step 400e-12` matched the known strict result exactly (`9.0% -> 19.67%`) while reducing estimated points to 452, but runtime was still `58.81s`. Coarser output/sampling therefore does not solve the 512-update runtime issue by itself; Xyce solve/event cost, not print-point count, is the dominant bottleneck.
- Rejected the plain periodic `PULSE` phase-clock shortcut for the online phase-transient runner. The direct-mode sample cadence includes a sample-settle gap, and finite training decks also need the clocks to stop before the final measurement slack; an ungated periodic `PULSE` can skew phases or emit extra post-training clocks. Keep phase clocks as explicit PWL sources until a gated/enveloped clock source is designed and proven with small equivalence tests. Per the current experiment discipline, avoid large Xyce runs for this class of plumbing change.
- Compressed sample input/target PWL sources in the online phase-transient runner so unchanged per-sample values no longer emit no-op transition points. On the fixed target-topology 256-update MNIST prefix (`10x10`, softmax targets), data-source PWL points dropped from 56,540 to 25,498 (`54.9%` fewer) without changing the held-value semantics at real transitions. A tiny 2-update strict Xyce smoke still executed with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`; generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Simplified all-constant sample rails to DC voltage sources in the same online phase-transient runner. On the same 256-update `10x10` prefix this converts 10/100 pixel rails from PWL to DC sources and leaves target rails time-varying; total data PWL points drop slightly further to 25,478 while removing time-dependent source handling for those constant rails. A tiny strict Xyce smoke still passed the fully-on-device execution contract, and generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added source-complexity metadata to online phase-transient summaries and the phase-result audit table: sample/pixel/target source counts, DC-vs-PWL counts, sample PWL point totals, and phase-clock PWL point totals. A 2-update strict Xyce smoke reported the new fields (`26` sample sources, `11` DC, `60` sample PWL points, `50` phase-clock PWL points) while preserving `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`. This gives future bounded and long runs a cheap deck-complexity signal alongside transient-point and output-vector counts.
- Added a `--max-source-pwl-points` preflight guard to the online phase-transient runner, phase variant sweep, and fast-reference promotion commands. The guard sums sample-source and phase-clock PWL points after loading the deterministic training prefix, then fails before simulator detection/deck execution if the configured budget is exceeded. A deliberate CLI fail-smoke with budget `1` stopped before creating artifacts with `estimated source PWL points (110) exceed --max-source-pwl-points (1)`.
- Added `--preflight-only` to the online phase-transient runner so candidate fully-on-device runs can report deterministic source/transient complexity without simulator detection, deck generation, or artifacts. The current strict target-topology 512-update branch preflights at `estimated_transient_points=7174`, `sample_source_pwl_points=50356`, `phase_clock_source_pwl_points=10250`, total source PWL points `60606`, and `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`. A lower transient-point budget (`4000`) correctly rejected the same candidate before JSON output.
- Extended the fast online variant sweep to emit per-probe eval accuracy columns, then screened the target topology around the known strict branch without launching SPICE. A 2048-sample NumPy sweep found that lower `lr=0.2`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `3.0` keeps improving through 2048 samples (`8.7% -> 82.8%` on 1,000 eval; probes `61.6%/74.5%/82.8%` at 512/1024/2048), while a 512-sample early screen selected `lr=0.6`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `3.0` for bounded SPICE promotion (`19.7%/48.0%/64.3%/75.3%` at 64/128/256/512 on the 300-eval slice). Promoting that early candidate to a strict 128-update Xyce run preserved the fully-on-device contract and reached `48.0%`, improving over the previous best strict 128-update target evidence (`43.0%`) while staying in one no-reference training transient. Generated deck and final weights were removed; compact summary/metrics were copied to `results/tables`.
- Split fast-sweep reporting into `best_variant` by final accuracy and `best_promotion_variant` by the configured promotion horizon. A refined 60-row target screen for 128-update promotion found final-best `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, temperature `3.0` at `78.0%` after 512 samples, but horizon-best `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5` at `49.33%` after 128 samples (`63.0%` at 256, `75.67%` at 512). Because this only improves the already-promoted strict 128-update Xyce result by `1.33` points in the fast reference, it is not worth another bounded Xyce run yet; use this summary to choose if a later promotion needs a 128-update candidate.
- Ran longer fast-reference target-topology screens to check whether the current fully-on-device update equations can still approach the final >90 objective before adding new circuit behavior. A 10k-sample screen over 60 constant-rate variants reached `88.75%` on a 2k eval slice (`lr=0.2`, readout scale `0.35`, temperature `2.5`). A narrower 20k-sample screen crossed the target in NumPy at `90.75%` (`lr=0.08`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5`; probes `79.6%/84.6%/88.2%/90.75%` at 2048/5000/10000/20000). This is not SPICE evidence, but it shows the present update equations can reach >90 given enough online samples. Preflighting the 2048-update strict promotion for that branch reports `estimated_transient_points=28679` and total source PWL points `239964`, so an actual SPICE proof at that horizon needs either substantial runtime reduction or a deliberately long run.
- Re-centered experiment sizing after the reminder not to use large runs for routine exploration. A compact target-topology fast-reference calibration (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, channel-2, `batch_size=1`, tanh + hidden `tanh-clipped`, 512 online samples, 1000 eval images) gave probe accuracies `13.8%/10.3%/21.4%/32.6%/39.3%/64.0%` at `16/32/64/128/256/512` updates. A conventional PyTorch local-feature baseline on the same topology with batch-1 SGD and cross-entropy reached `65.4%` after one 512-sample epoch and `80.8%` after four epochs at `lr=0.2`. The takeaway is that 128-sample runs are good smoke/contract checks and can detect dead branches, but they are too short to judge learning headroom; 512-sample fast calibration is still cheap and more informative, while long SPICE promotions should remain exceptional.
- Added an opt-in compact target source mode for the continuous phase runner and made fast-sweep promotion commands default to using it. `--target-source-mode label` drives one label PWL waveform and decodes per-class target rails with smooth behavioral target sources, preserving the rule that Python supplies labels only as waveforms while reducing target-source PWL complexity. On the current 512-update target-topology strict preflight, rails mode reports 110 sample sources and 1,904 target PWL points; label mode reports 101 sample sources and 944 target PWL points, dropping total source PWL points from 60,606 to 59,646. A discontinuous decoder first caused an Xyce tiny-step failure, so the committed version uses a tanh-smoothed decoder. A tiny two-update Xyce no-reference comparison between rails and label modes produced matching final SPICE states to within `5.1e-6` max absolute difference while preserving the strict no-checkpoint execution contract. This is a modest runtime/deck simplification, not new learning evidence.
- Omitted frozen output-bias state from continuous phase decks when `output_bias_update_scale=0` and `state_decay=0`. In that case the bias is a constant, not an on-device updated state variable, so score equations now use the known constant directly and final diagnostics restore the same output-bias array without printing `V(ob*)`. For the current target topology this drops final printed vectors from 884 to 874 when combined with the strict branch's frozen output bias; the 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 smoke dropped from 80 to 70 vectors and completed as a strict no-reference Xyce run. The 512-update target preflight still reports the same source complexity (`total_source_pwl_points=59,646` with label targets) but now records `output_bias_state_frozen=true`. This is another small deck/state simplification, not new accuracy evidence.
- Made final output-rail printing optional in the continuous phase runner and left it off by default. Final MNIST diagnostics recompute class outputs from the final weight state, so the strict path no longer needs to print `V(y*)` rails after training. The final weights archive keeps a stable `y` key, but it is an empty array when those rails are omitted. Combined with frozen output-bias omission and compact label targets, the current 512-update target preflight now reports `phase_output_vector_count=864` instead of the historical 884, with the same no-checkpoint strict contract and `total_source_pwl_points=59,646`. A tiny strict Xyce no-reference smoke completed with `phase_output_vector_count=60` and an empty saved `y` array. This trims diagnostic output cost only; it does not change the on-device update equations or provide new accuracy evidence.
- Updated the fast online variant sweep's strict-promotion metadata to match the current optimized phase deck. Promotion rows now report target source mode, frozen output-bias state, whether final `y` rails are printed, current phase output vector count, and an optional output-vector budget gate. The best-promotion selector respects that gate alongside transient-point and source-PWL budgets. A direct target-topology metadata check for the current 512-update branch now reports `target_source_mode=label`, `output_bias_state_frozen=true`, `phase_output_includes_y=false`, `output_vector_count=864`, `total_source_pwl_points=59,646`, and `estimated_transient_points=7,174`. This does not change learning behavior, but it prevents future fast screens from ranking promotion candidates with stale deck-cost assumptions.
- Refreshed a small 8-row target-topology fast-reference screen with the current strict-promotion cost model (`512` online samples, `300` eval samples, tanh + hidden `tanh-clipped`, label targets, frozen output bias, no final `y` rails). The best and best-promotion row is `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, local scale `1.0`, temperature `3.0`, with fast-reference probes `48.0%/61.67%/78.0%` at `128/256/512` updates. Its generated promotion command now carries `--max-output-vectors 864`; the row reports `output_vector_count=864`, `total_source_pwl_points=59,646`, `estimated_transient_points=7,174`, and all three promotion budgets met under the configured bounds (`8000` transient points, `60000` source PWL points, `864` output vectors). This is still NumPy screening evidence only; no SPICE training promotion was launched.
- Added an output score-calculation fusion mode for the continuous phase runner. `--score-calculation-mode inline` removes the per-class `Bscore{k} scorecalc{k}` algebraic sources and inlines each readout/summing expression into the score storage current source; the base runner defaults to the old node mode for compatibility, while phase/fast promotion commands default to inline as part of the efficient deck shape. A two-update Xyce smoke comparing node versus inline on the same random-init 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 run matched final state to numerical noise (`1e-8` local-weight max diff, `6.263e-8` local-bias max diff, `1.3e-8` readout max diff, zero output-bias/y diff). This is the next useful synapse/neuron combination after hidden-preactivation fusion: it removes 10 scorecalc sources on MNIST-class decks without changing persistent state, PWL complexity, or output-vector count. Generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added `--output-rail-mode inline` for the continuous phase runner. When final `y` vectors are not requested, softmax/tanh/linear output activation expressions can feed the error sources directly instead of emitting per-class `By{k}` rails; base decks keep node mode for compatibility, while strict promotion commands default to inline and automatically fall back to node mode if `--promotion-phase-output-include-y` is set. A two-update Xyce smoke comparing `output_rail_mode=node` versus `inline` with hidden/score fusion held fixed matched final state to numerical noise (`1.773e-8` local-weight max diff, `1.683e-8` local-bias max diff, `1.84e-8` readout max diff, zero output-bias/y diff). The current target-topology 512-update strict preflight now reports hidden preactivation, score calculation, and output rail source counts all at zero, with `phase_output_vector_count=864`, `estimated_transient_points=7174`, and total source PWL points `34,943` for the checked 512-sample prefix. This removes another 10 non-persistent algebraic sources from the default MNIST-class strict deck shape without changing the fully on-device state/update contract. Generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added a single `auxiliary_algebraic_source_count` field to phase preflight/runtime summaries, phase variant rows, fast strict-promotion cost fields, and the result summarizer. This sums the three currently fusible non-persistent source families: hidden preactivation nodes, scorecalc nodes, and output/y rails. The current 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 strict 512-update preflight reports `auxiliary_algebraic_source_count=0` while preserving the strict fully-on-device contract and the same `phase_output_vector_count=864`, `estimated_transient_points=7174`, and `total_source_pwl_points=34,943`. This makes the deck-shape reductions visible to promotion selection/auditing without changing the meaning of external sample/source-PWL budgets.
- Promoted the fused synapse/neuron-style deck shape from reported metadata to an enforceable budget. `--max-auxiliary-algebraic-sources` now fails phase preflight before deck/solver launch when the selected hidden-preactivation, score-calculation, and output-rail modes leave too many non-persistent algebraic sources; phase sweeps, fast strict-promotion commands/cost projection, and the result summarizer all carry the same budget field. The target 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 strict 512-update preflight passes with the fully fused shape (`auxiliary_algebraic_source_count=0`, budget `1`, `estimated_transient_points=7174`, `phase_output_vector_count=864`, `total_source_pwl_points=34,943`), while a deliberately unfused tiny node-mode preflight fails early with `estimated auxiliary algebraic sources (24) exceed --max-auxiliary-algebraic-sources (1)`. This is a concrete efficiency/fusion gate, not new learning evidence.
- Tested the next possible combined error-head shape by adding `--output-delta-mode node|inline`. Inline removes the ten stored class-delta capacitors/sources on MNIST decks and reports `output_delta_state_count=0`, but a two-update 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 strict Xyce smoke diverged materially from stored-delta mode (`local_weights` max diff `7.26e-3`, `local_bias` `1.94e-2`, `readout` `3.74e-3`) because the current validated equation intentionally stores a finite-settled delta during the error phase. Therefore generated strict promotions keep `output_delta_mode=node` by default, while the inline mode is available only as an explicit experiment knob. Target 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 512-update preflights remain at `estimated_transient_points=7174`, `phase_output_vector_count=864`, and `total_source_pwl_points=34,943`; only the experimental inline branch changes `output_delta_state_count` from `10` to `0`. Generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added `--hidden-delta-mode stored|inline` to test another synapse/neuron fusion boundary. Inline removes the hidden/backward `dh` latch bank and drops the current target-topology strict preflight's hidden-delta state from `32` to `0` and total temporary state from `84` to `52`, while preserving the fully-on-device contract and auxiliary source budget. A two-update 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 Xyce smoke showed it is not equivalent enough to promote as a default (`local_weights` max diff `2.85e-4`, `local_bias` `1.37e-3`, `readout` `1.36e-4` versus stored mode) and was slower on the tiny run (`0.24s` vs `0.19s`). Keep strict promotions on stored hidden deltas for now; the useful family rule is that pure algebraic read-side fusion is safe when it removes non-persistent nodes, but phase-latched error/delta values carry timing semantics and need separate proof before being combined away. Smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added an opt-in `--phase-clock-mode smooth-analytic` experiment for Xyce/direct `batch_size=1` phase runs. It replaces the existing floor/modulo analytic clock with a smooth periodic cosine/tanh gate plus a finite train-window envelope, avoiding explicit per-update clock PWL points and avoiding the `floor()` discontinuity that made the older analytic clock suspect at longer horizons. The current 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 512-update strict preflight drops total source PWL points from `34,943` with robust PWL clocks to `24,698`, while preserving `estimated_transient_points=7174`, `phase_output_vector_count=864`, and the strict fully-on-device contract. A two-update 4x4 Xyce smoke accepted the deck and matched PWL clocks more closely after area-matching the smooth duty width (`local_weights` max diff `2.01e-5`, `local_bias` `7.67e-5`, `readout` `1.90e-5`). Keep PWL clocks as the default for now; smooth analytic is a lower-PWL candidate for the next bounded 64/128-update robustness check. Smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added an opt-in `--phase-clock-mode pulse-gated` experiment that uses native Xyce `PULSE(...)` phase clocks plus finite behavioral windows, preserving the trapezoidal clock shape more closely than the smooth cosine/tanh clock while still removing per-update phase-clock PWL points. A two-update 4x4 smoke matched PWL clocks tightly (`local_weights` max diff `5.19e-6`, `local_bias` `7.23e-7`, `readout` `3.39e-7`). On the target 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 64-update branch (`lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, temperature `3.0`, hidden `tanh-clipped`), pulse-gated and PWL clocks produced the same eval behavior (`9.0% -> 11.33%`, one-class final predictions), with final-state drift still small enough not to affect the classification result (`local_weights` max diff `2.55e-4`, `local_bias` `3.81e-4`, `readout` `3.58e-4`). Pulse-gated reduced total source PWL points from `4,367` to `3,082`, but was slightly slower at this horizon (`38.5s` vs `36.2s`), so it is a deck-complexity option rather than a proven runtime win. The same branch's fast reference predicts `19.67%`, so the next debugging target is the fast-reference/phase-runner mismatch for this knob set, not the clock source shape. Generated run artifacts were removed.
- Tested the next fused synapse/neuron boundary: inlining hidden activations instead of storing the phase-latched `h` values. A target-topology 16-update Xyce equivalence probe (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, direct mode, hidden preactivation/score/output rails already fused, `hidden_activation_mode=inline`) timed out after 300s before producing metrics, while the stored-latch baseline for this scale is tens of seconds. On the tiny 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 A/B smoke, the stored hidden-activation latch matched the batch-op reference (`cos=0.999999986`, sign alignment `100%`, max state diff `7.66e-7`, phase time `0.36s`), but the inline hidden-activation version failed the direction gates (`cos=0.9931988`, sign alignment `95%`, three wrong-sign entries, max state diff `4.83e-4`, phase time `0.93s`). This reinforces the family rule: fuse algebraic read-side nodes when they only remove non-persistent sources, but keep phase-latched values for activations and deltas unless a larger equivalence proof says otherwise. For now, strict promotions should keep `hidden_activation_mode=stored`.
- Audited older no-reference continuous phase summaries so they can be included in strict target-topology rankings when their legacy fields prove the contract: phase-resolved continuous architecture, `batch_size=1`, `reference_mode=none`, random init via empty `init_weights`, no checkpointing, and inferred no Python weight updates between samples. The summarizer marks these rows with `strict_contract_inferred_from_legacy_summary=true` and labels missing modern backend diagnostics as `legacy_phase_eval` instead of overclaiming `spice`. The corrected ranking still keeps the fused 512-update target run as the best strict evidence (`9.0% -> 78.0%` on 300 eval samples); the older direct 256-update run is retained as legacy contract evidence at `44.0%`.
- Checked whether the current best strict target branch should be promoted past 512 samples before spending more Xyce time. A cheap fast-reference extension of the fused `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` branch (`lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, temperature `3.0`, tanh + hidden `tanh-clipped`, frozen output bias) reached `42.2%/62.2%/75.9%/73.2%` at `128/256/512/1024` updates on a 1,000-image eval slice, so this branch does not improve monotonically beyond 512. The matching strict 1024-update preflight with compact timing and label targets would still be large (`estimated_transient_points=14342`, `total_source_pwl_points=118203`, `phase_output_vector_count=864`, `auxiliary_algebraic_source_count=0`). Keep actual SPICE promotions at 512 or below for now; the next higher-yield work is more source-complexity reduction or a better fast-screened branch, not a casual 1024 Xyce run.
- Added an opt-in `--input-source-mode rom` for continuous phase runs. Instead of one PWL voltage source per pixel rail, ROM mode drives a single sample-index PWL waveform and decodes each nonconstant pixel rail with behavioral source expressions; constant pixel rails are still elided. This keeps weights persistent inside one transient and does not add Python weight updates/checkpoints, but it trades source PWL complexity for larger behavioral expressions, so it is not the default. On the current target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` 1024-update strict preflight, ROM mode drops total source PWL points from `118203` to `24409` and sample-source PWL points from `97718` to `3924`, while reporting `pixel_rom_behavioral_source_count=93` and `pixel_rom_value_count=95232`. A tiny Xyce reference smoke accepted the ROM deck and matched the batch-op diagnostic (`cos=0.9999999993`, sign alignment `100%`, max state diff `3.25e-6`); a separate no-reference strict smoke completed with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`. Generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Replaced the ROM pixel decoder's hard nested `if()` thresholds with a smooth tanh mux after a target-topology 128-update Xyce attempt failed immediately with a tiny-step error at a `Bpix*` branch. The smooth decoder restores tiny Xyce reference-smoke equivalence (`cos=0.9999999993`, sign alignment `100%`, max state diff `3.25e-6`), but a target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` 128-update strict ROM promotion still timed out at 300s. Conclusion: ROM input is a source-PWL compression experiment and useful for preflight pressure, but it is not a runtime win in its current behavioral-expression form; keep strict target promotions on per-pixel PWL until a more simulator-friendly encoded input source is designed. Generated smoke/timeout artifacts were removed.
- Added explicit `--input-quantization-levels` support to the fast reference, fast variant sweep, and continuous phase runner. This is a controlled source-waveform compression knob: Python still supplies only input/label/clock waveforms, and the chosen quantization level is carried into generated strict-promotion commands and summaries so fast-screen and SPICE decks cannot silently disagree. A cheap target-topology screen showed exact waveform sharing has no upside at 512/1024 samples (94 unique pixel traces after constant elision), but 8-level quantization reduces the 1024-update pixel PWL points from `95841` to `64762`. Combined with pulse-gated clocks and a `400ps` transient step, the strict 1024-update preflight now passes the old promotion guards (`estimated_transient_points=7172`, `total_source_pwl_points=66639`, `phase_output_vector_count=864`, `auxiliary_algebraic_source_count=0`, strict contract true). The fast-reference learning tradeoff is acceptable but not a breakthrough: best of a four-row q8 screen reached `8.1% -> 76.0%` on 1,000 eval samples at 1024 updates (`lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.35`, temperature `2.5`), versus the unquantized best around `79.7%`. A 16-update Xyce equivalence probe of the q8/pulse-gated/400ps shape matched the batch-op reference (`cos=0.9999999974`, sign alignment `100%`, `state_max_abs_diff=1.65e-5`) and reached the same eval as the reference (`19%` on 100 eval). This makes 1024 updates budget-feasible for a bounded strict SPICE promotion, but not yet obviously worth a long run unless the goal is to validate the compressed-source path itself.
- Added `input_quantization_levels` to the phase-result summarizer and markdown table so compressed-source SPICE evidence is auditable instead of being indistinguishable from ordinary PWL-input runs. Existing unquantized summaries default to `0`, while the q8 pulse-gated equivalence row now reports `input_quantization_levels=8` alongside its clock/source-cost fields.
- Ran full-test fast-reference checks for quantized inputs on the known >90 branch (`20k` online samples, `10k` eval, `lr=0.08`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5`). q8 did not preserve the full-objective target (`89.15%`), q32 narrowly missed (`89.92%`), and q16 crossed it (`90.45%`, probes `81.01%/85.06%/88.95%/90.45%` at `2048/5000/10000/20000`). q16 is therefore the better accuracy-preserving compression direction, but its current 1024-update pulse-gated/400ps strict preflight is still heavier than q8: `87762` total source PWL points (`85885` pixel) versus q8's `66639`. This says coarse input quantization can preserve the >90 fast-reference endpoint, but the accuracy-preserving q16 branch still needs either a higher source budget, more input-source compression, or a runtime proof before it becomes the preferred long SPICE promotion path.
- Promoted the q16 compressed-source branch to bounded Xyce checks. A 16-update reference run with q16 inputs, pulse-gated clocks, and `400ps` transient step matched the SPICE batch-op reference (`cos=0.9999999974`, sign alignment `100%`, `state_max_abs_diff=1.49e-5`). Strict no-reference target-topology runs then showed meaningful learning in one uninterrupted transient: `8.67% -> 47.33%` at 128 updates in `108.8s`, and `8.67% -> 65.67%` at 256 updates in `227.1s`. Both strict runs used `batch_size=1`, random init, no Python checkpoints/weight writes, q16 PWL inputs, label targets, pulse-gated clocks, fused read-side nodes, and zero auxiliary algebraic sources. The 256-update q16 result is close to the unquantized 256-update strict evidence (`68.0%`) while preserving the q16 fast-reference full-test headroom (`90.45%`), so q16 is now a validated compressed-source SPICE branch rather than only a NumPy/preflight idea.
- Screened q16 early-horizon settings for a 512-update strict promotion. The best 9-row fast reference was `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.3`, temperature `2.5`, with probes `48.0%/65.67%/77.0%` at `128/256/512` and a strict deck projection of `3588` transient points, `44300` source PWL points, and `864` output vectors. Promoting exactly that row to Xyce produced the same `8.67% -> 77.0%` result in one strict no-reference transient (`417.2s`, q16 inputs, pulse-gated clocks, fused read-side nodes, zero auxiliary algebraic sources). This is now the strongest compressed-source fully on-device SPICE result, and it is within one point of the current best unquantized 512-update result (`78.0%`) while using fewer transient points and fewer source PWL points.
- Filled in the missing q16 1024-update target-topology screen and promotion. A 9-row fast-reference readout-scale/temperature screen predicted a best `79.4%` at `lr=0.5`, readout scale `0.4`, temperature `3.0` with q16 inputs, pulse-gated clocks, tanh activation, hidden `tanh-clipped` synapses, linear readout synapses, and the fused read-side deck shape. A broader 24-row neuron/synapse screen over tanh, leaky ReLU, softplus, differential clipped ReLU, and linear/tanh-clipped/hard-clipped synapse transfers did not beat the same tanh + hidden `tanh-clipped` + readout linear branch; differential clipped ReLU was best at the early 128-update probe but trailed by 1024. Promoting the best q16 branch to a strict no-reference Xyce run produced the new strongest fully on-device/SPICE evidence: one uninterrupted `batch_size=1` transient on `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, random init, no Python checkpoints/weight writes, `0.096 -> 0.797` on a 1,000-image eval slice after 1024 online updates. The run preserved the strict contract (`strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`), used zero auxiliary algebraic sources, `864` output vectors, `7172` estimated transient points, `87762` source PWL points, and took `856.6s`. Compact summary/metrics were copied to `results/tables`; generated deck and final weights were removed.
- Screened q16 learning-rate schedules to decide whether another strict promotion was justified. A 60-row 5,000-update fast-reference screen on `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` found linear decay is better than the constant-rate 1024 branch at longer horizons: best final row was `lr=0.35`, decay to `0.2x`, readout scale `0.4`, temperature `2.5`, reaching `87.8%` on a 2,000-image eval slice at 5,000 updates. The best 2,048-update promotion row was `lr=0.2`, decay to `0.2x`, readout scale `0.4`, temperature `2.5`, predicted at `83.85%` and still within the current strict promotion budgets (`14340` transient points, `179381` source PWL points, `864` output vectors, zero auxiliary algebraic sources). Promoting that row to Xyce produced the new strongest strict fully on-device/SPICE result: one uninterrupted `batch_size=1` transient, random init, no Python checkpoints/weight writes, `0.103 -> 0.8335` on 2,000 eval images after 2,048 online updates. It tracked the fast-reference prediction closely, kept `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, and took `1665.5s`. The 5,000-update cost projection is still outside current budgets (`35004` transient points and `437239` source PWL points), so the next scaling blocker is source/runtime compression rather than update-equation correctness at 2,048 updates. Compact artifacts were copied to `results/tables`; the generated deck and final weights were removed.
- Added an opt-in analytic LR control mode for direct online phase runs. `--lr-control-mode analytic` replaces the explicit PWL `Vlrctrl` source for non-constant schedules with one in-deck behavioral control expression, preserving the strict fully-on-device contract and avoiding Python-supplied LR sample waveforms. The fast-sweep promotion metadata and generated promotion commands now carry the same knob. A target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` q16 5,000-update preflight for the current linear-decay branch drops control-source PWL points from `9999` to `0` and total source PWL points from `437239` to `427240`; pixel PWL transitions remain the dominant cost, so this is a modest cleanup rather than the full 5k scaling solution. A tiny Xyce strict no-reference smoke accepted the analytic LR deck (`lr_control_mode=analytic`, `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`), and focused tests passed.
- Checked whether a coarser Xyce print/transient interval is a useful runtime lever for the q16 target-topology branch. A 16-update `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` equivalence probe with `--transient-step 5e-10` matched the SPICE batch-op reference (`cos=0.9999999974`, sign alignment `100%`, `state_max_abs_diff=1.49e-5`, final eval `18%` for both). A bounded strict 256-update no-reference promotion with the same 500 ps step preserved the existing learning result (`8.67% -> 65.67%` on 300 eval images, strict contract true) and reduced estimated transient points from the prior 400 ps row's `1796` to `1437`, but wall time worsened from `227.1s` to `242.4s` while source PWL points stayed fixed at `22489`. Conclusion: 500 ps is numerically acceptable at this horizon but not a runtime win; keep the q16 strict-promotion default at 400 ps unless a later simulator-side setting changes the cost balance. Compact summaries/metrics were copied to `results/tables`, and generated decks/final-weight artifacts were removed.
- Added `--final-measure-tail` so strict phase decks can append diagnostic-only time after the final training phase, allowing coarser final `.print`/`.measure` intervals without shifting any sample/update phase. A q16 target-topology 5,000-update preflight with analytic LR, pulse-gated clocks, `--transient-step 1e-9`, and `--final-measure-tail 1e-9` now passes the final-state measurement guard and projects `14003` transient points instead of the previous `35004`, while source PWL pressure remains unchanged at `427240` points. A 16-update equivalence probe at the same 1 ns/tail setting matched the SPICE batch-op reference (`cos=0.9999999974`, sign alignment `100%`, `state_max_abs_diff=1.49e-5`) and ran slightly faster than the 500 ps probe (`12.2s` vs `14.4s`). However, the bounded 256-update strict run again preserved accuracy (`8.67% -> 65.67%`) but was slower than the 400 ps baseline (`244.9s` vs `227.1s`). Conclusion: the tail option is correct and useful for relaxing the print-point budget/preflight guard, but current Xyce runtime is still dominated elsewhere, so it is not a default promotion setting yet.
- Added an explicit `--train-order` option to the continuous phase runner and fast reference so source-locality experiments can be reproduced under the fully on-device contract: Python still only chooses the input/label waveform sequence, while weights remain persistent in one transient. The useful mode so far is `local-pca`, which sorts only inside fixed-size windows of the already-selected stable MNIST prefix. Full global sorting can reduce PWL points more but destroys online learning; local sorting is the safer family-completion version. On the q16 `10x10 b4 s2 c2` 5,000-update branch, `--train-order local-pca --train-order-window 128` reduced the strict preflight source PWL projection from `427240` to `409224` points (`418285 -> 400779` pixel points) and, in the matching fast reference, slightly improved the 2,000-image endpoint from `87.8%` to `88.45%`. Combining local-PCA ordering with the 1 ns diagnostic tail keeps the 5k projection at `14003` transient points and `409224` source PWL points. A 16-update Xyce equivalence probe with local-PCA ordering preserved the SPICE batch-op direction (`cos=0.9999999975`, sign alignment `100%`, `state_max_abs_diff=1.36e-5`), so the ordering knob is electrically compatible; it is a modest compression tool, not yet enough by itself to unlock a routine 5k strict run.
- Wired the same train-order and final-measure-tail knobs through the fast online variant sweep's strict-promotion path. The subtle bug shape was that a local ordering promotion cannot shorten `--train-samples` to `--promotion-updates`: the strict runner must load and reorder the same full selected prefix, then execute only the first promoted updates, otherwise the first promoted samples can differ from the fast sweep. Stable order keeps the old short-prefix behavior because its prefix is invariant. A smoke with `local-pca`, 64 train samples, and 16 promoted updates generated a strict command with `--train-samples 64 --updates 16`, carried q16/order/tail metadata into promotion and cost-projection rows, and projected the 64-update cost as `183` transient points / `5224` source PWL points. This keeps sweep-to-strict promotions reproducible while preserving the fully on-device contract.
- Used the updated fast sweep to screen train-order windows on the q16 5,000-update target branch (`10x10 b4 s2 c2`, linear-decay `lr=0.35 -> 0.2x`, readout scale `0.4`, temperature `2.5`, pulse-gated clocks, analytic LR control, 1 ns final-measure tail). Stable order reached `87.80%` on the 2,000-image fast eval with projected 5k source PWL `427240` and pixel PWL `418285`. Local-PCA improved the endpoint and reduced source cost up to a moderate window: window `64` reached `88.55%` / `412686`, window `128` reached `88.45%` / `409224`, window `256` reached `88.85%` / `402900`, and window `512` reached the best `88.90%` / `395570`. Pushing toward more global sorting hurt learning despite lower PWL: local-PCA `1024` fell to `86.55%`, local-label `128` to `80.40%`, and local-label `256` to `73.75%`. The new default candidate for a 5k strict attempt is therefore local-PCA window `512`, but the fast endpoint is still below the >90 full objective, so the next branch should improve learning quality before spending a long Xyce run.
- Broadened the q16 local-PCA window-512 fast-reference screen around the best 5k branch. A 216-row LR/decay/readout-scale/temperature grid improved the 2,000-image 5k endpoint to `89.55%` with `lr=0.3`, linear decay to `0.2x`, readout scale `0.5`, and temperature `2.0`; nearby rows tied at `89.55%`, while output-bias updates did not improve the result and add 10 output vectors, so the frozen-bias deck remains preferred. Full 10k fast eval of that 5k branch reached `89.78%`, narrowly missing the full objective by `0.22%` with projected strict cost `395570` source PWL points / `14003` transient points. Extending the same branch to 6,000 online samples crossed the fast-reference full objective at `90.15%` on all 10,000 MNIST test images, with projected strict cost `475007` source PWL points / `16803` transient points. This is still not strict SPICE evidence, but it gives the first current random-init, batch_size=1, target-topology fast branch that crosses the >90 full-test threshold within a plausibly bounded strict-promotion horizon.
- Ran a small strict no-reference Xyce smoke on the exact 6k fast-reference target branch (`local-pca` window `512`, q16 inputs, `lr=0.3` linear decay to `0.2x`, analytic LR control, readout scale `0.5`, temperature `2.0`, pulse-gated clocks, 1 ns final-measure tail). The deck completed 16 online updates in one transient, preserved `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, used zero auxiliary algebraic sources, `864` output vectors, `48` estimated transient points, and `1035` source PWL points, and improved the 100-image diagnostic from `6%` to `14%`. Because non-constant LR intentionally requires `reference_mode=none`, this smoke proves deck acceptance and the strict execution contract for the branch, not update-direction equivalence; earlier q16/local-PCA equivalence runs still cover the electrical update shape.
- Promoted the same 6k local-PCA/q16 branch to a bounded 1,024-update strict Xyce run with full 10,000-image NumPy final eval. The run stayed inside one random-init, `batch_size=1`, no-reference transient and preserved `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`; Python only supplied the input/label/clock/control waveforms and final diagnostics. Accuracy improved from `10.89%` to `73.72%` on the full MNIST test set after 1,024 online updates. This is above the matching fast-reference probe for the same branch (`69.63%` at 1,024), while still below the older stable-order 2,048-update strict slice (`83.35%` on 2,000 eval images) and below the final 6k fast-reference target (`90.15%`). Cost was `2,871` estimated transient points, `80,902` source PWL points (`79,271` pixel), zero auxiliary algebraic sources, `864` output vectors, and `908.3s` wall time. The result makes the 6k local-PCA branch strict-SPICE credible at a meaningful horizon; the next expensive promotion decision is whether to spend a roughly 2x run on 2,048 updates or first reduce the source-waveform cost further.
- Promoted the 6k local-PCA/q16 branch again to 2,048 strict Xyce online updates with full 10,000-image final eval. The run kept the fully on-device contract (`random_init_used=true`, `batch_size=1`, `reference_mode=none`, `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`) and reached `82.97%` from a `10.89%` random baseline. This is now the strongest full-test strict-SPICE result in the checkout; it narrowly trails the older stable-order 2,048 row's `83.35%`, but that older row evaluated only 2,000 images. It also beats the matching fast-reference 2,048 probe for this branch (`78.19%`), preserving the favorable strict-vs-fast gap seen at 1,024. Cost scaled linearly: `5,738` estimated transient points, `161,866` total source PWL points (`158,601` pixel), zero auxiliary algebraic sources, `864` output vectors, and `1,744.0s` wall time. The remaining gap is no longer strict execution correctness at this horizon; it is reaching the 6k/90% endpoint without a very long, high-PWL transient.
- Completed the 6k local-PCA/q16 target branch in strict Xyce: one random-init, `batch_size=1`, no-reference phase transient on `10x10 b4 s2 c2`, with Python supplying only input/label/clock/control waveforms and final diagnostics. The run reached `90.15%` on all 10,000 MNIST test images (`10.89% -> 90.15%`) after 6,000 online updates, and the summarizer marks `strict_target_contract_met=true`, `full_eval_10k_met=true`, `full_objective_accuracy_met=true`, and `milestone_d_full_objective_met=true`. It used q16 PWL inputs, local-PCA ordering window `512`, analytic LR control (`lr=0.3` linear decay to `0.2x`), readout scale `0.5`, temperature `2.0`, frozen output bias, pulse-gated clocks, zero auxiliary algebraic sources, and `864` output vectors. Cost was `16,803` estimated transient points, `475,007` source PWL points (`465,518` pixel), and `4,884.4s` wall time. The failed foreground attempt was caused by the tool session lifetime, not the circuit; rerunning detached with `setsid` allowed Xyce to finish cleanly.
- Rewrote `docs/spicenn_progress_note.tex` from a chronological progress log into a focused architecture/result note for the strict fully on-device SPICE milestone. The new manuscript centers the contract, the `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` local-feature topology, capacitor-held state, phase-gated online update equations, signed transport requirements, q16/local-PCA source-cost choices, and the final `10.89% -> 90.15%` full-test result. The cleanup intentionally removes versioned run history and leaves only concise design evidence and caveats: the result is a strict local-feature SPICE training result with behavioral current update kernels, not yet a transistor-sized arbitrary CNN/backprop claim. Regenerated `docs/spicenn_progress_note.pdf`; `pdflatex` now builds the 6-page note cleanly.
- Restored the paper's basic building-block context and added the precise neuron circuit used by the final strict run. Important correction: the winning neuron is the stored tanh local-feature cell from the phase runner (`Bstore_h` relaxing `Ch` toward `tanh(z)` and `Bstore_dh` relaxing `Cdh` toward `readout_feedback * tanh'`), not the earlier MOS ReLU source-follower primitive. The paper now separates this current behavioral-neuron result from the future transistor replacement path, and it calls out the main speed/scaling blockers: pixel-source PWL pressure dominates the 6k run, while architecture scaling should proceed by state-count/source-cost Pareto promotions rather than blind larger SPICE runs.
- Added a first `TrainableDynamicalCell` local-feature catalog with ten behavioral candidates spanning stored tanh, differential-pair-like tanh/soft clipping, signed hard-tanh, leaky hard-tanh, leaky/clipped ReLU, softplus, centered softplus, and DFA fixed-feedback variants. The unit tests now require the catalog to contain ten contract-valid cells and to pass a lightweight single-cell learning-alignment characterization. A real MNIST IDX benchmark runner compares the cells on `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, q16 inputs, seeded random order, and no local-PCA; the 512-update / 1000-eval smoke ranked `diffpair_tanh_softclip` first at `56.6%`, `dfa_tanh_fixed_feedback` second at `54.2%`, and the existing stored tanh/tanh-clipped branch third at `50.4%`. This is deliberately NumPy behavioral evidence for choosing the next SPICE/device replacements, not transistor-level evidence.
- Updated the progress note with diagrams and equations for the ten-cell trainable local-feature catalog. The new section shows the shared `TrainableDynamicalCell` template, the BACKPROP_LOCAL versus DFA hidden-error paths, the transfer-function families, and the governing equations for each cell. The paper now states exactly what the tests validate: contract validity, activation monotonicity, nonnegative derivative gates, finite synapse transfer, and positive/perfectly signed local update alignment. The distinction remains explicit: these are behavioral/NumPy catalog validations used to choose SPICE/device promotion targets, not completed transistor validations.
- Added a single consolidated circuit schematic to the progress note for one stored-tanh local-feature cell. The figure now draws the external input/error/eta rails, algebraic preactivation and feedback expressions, the \(C_h\), \(C_{\delta h}\), \(C_W\), \(C_b\), and \(C_V\) state capacitors, phase-gated behavioral current sources, and the local write branches in one connected SPICE-level diagram. This directly addresses the previous ambiguity where the paper had block diagrams and row-wise sketches but no single circuit diagram.
- Added a transistor-level signed-synapse analog tail-gate sweep to the MOS characterization runner. The new ngspice deck sweeps the real NMOS tail-gate voltage from off to strongly enabled for matched \(W^+\) and gate-swapped \(W^-\) copies, asserts off-state quietness, active sign correctness, monotonic magnitude, useful dynamic range, and mirrored opposite-sign behavior, and writes a paper plot. The measured Level-1 range is effectively zero below threshold and about \(\pm365.6\,\mu\mathrm{A}\) at \(1.25\) V for the fixed \(x^+>x^-\) input, so the synapse now has direct evidence for continuous analog weight scaling rather than only discrete branch selection/cancellation.
- Added a MOS hidden-error finite-difference nudge sweep. The new deck keeps the center preactivation point fixed, varies the \(+\epsilon/-\epsilon\) replica nudge from 0 to 120 mV, stores the cross-connected hidden-error rails through MOS pass switches, and asserts zero-nudge quietness, monotonic \(r^+\) growth, monotonic \(r^-\) growth with opposite sign, mirror symmetry, and hold retention. The measured stored steps are approximately \(0, \pm19.7, \pm39.4, \pm78.8,\) and \(\pm118.2\) mV, so this path is now validated as a graded hidden-error storage primitive rather than only a sign selector.
- Added a MOS forward-store magnitude sweep. Five static diode-loaded forward-pair copies drive real activation capacitors through the same \(pact\) pass switches at \(z^+-z^-=\{-0.45,-0.25,0,0.25,0.45\}\) V. The stored activation is monotone, centered, approximately odd-symmetric, tracks the load output within 15 mV, and holds after \(pact\) closes. The measured stored endpoints are about \(\pm0.34\) V, which gives direct evidence that the activation storage primitive carries graded analog magnitude rather than only a sign bit.
- Added a MOS forward-pair threshold-mismatch sweep. The left input transistor uses \(V_{TO}=0.53, 0.55, 0.57\) V while the right side stays nominal, and ngspice confirms monotone transfer, retained swing, one zero crossing, and center gain above 0.55 V/V. The zero crossing moves by about \(-20, 0, +20\) mV respectively, which is expected for this simple Level-1 differential pair and makes the forward-cell offset budget visible instead of hidden inside a nominal plot.
- Added a MOS signed-synapse threshold-mismatch sweep. The \(z^+\) branch input transistor uses \(V_{TO}=0.53, 0.55, 0.57\) V while the complementary branch stays nominal, and ngspice confirms one bounded zero crossing, preserved sign outside a 120 mV input margin, and retained current swing above \(100\,\mu\mathrm{A}\). The \(W^+\) zero crossing moves by about \(-20, 0, +20\) mV while the gate-swapped \(W^-\) copy mirrors it at \(+20, 0, -20\) mV, which makes the signed synapse offset budget explicit.
- Added a MOS hidden-error-store to writer-magnitude sweep. Matched replica/store copies generate hidden-error magnitudes from 0 to about 138 mV, then drive PMOS writer gates directly under the same positive activation and \(pacc\) phase. The resulting \(W^+-W^-\) steps are approximately \(0, 4.1, 8.1, 13.4,\) and \(18.5\) mV and hold after write. This closes a gap between the separate hidden-error magnitude and writer analog-gate tests: the combined backward/update path now shows that stored hidden-error magnitude scales the local weight step.
- Added a MOS writer PMOS-threshold mismatch sweep. Three matched writer copies use \(V_{TO}=-0.50, -0.55, -0.60\) V in the selected PMOS stacks while the same analog activation/error gates drive both \(W^+\) and \(W^-\) update signs. The selected steps are approximately \(10.4, 8.1,\) and \(6.0\) mV for both signs, and the inactive opposite rails move only about \(4\) nV. This closes the mismatch family gap for the local writer: threshold offsets change update gain, but not sign/selectivity in this operating window.
- Added a MOS hidden-error replica threshold-mismatch sweep. The \(z^+\) input device in both nudged finite-difference replicas uses \(V_{TO}=0.53, 0.55, 0.57\) V while the complementary side stays nominal. The derivative proxy stays nonnegative with peak gain about \(0.99\), and the 0.5-gain active-window edges shift from roughly \([-0.36, 0.32]\) V to \([-0.31, 0.35]\) V while keeping a \(\sim0.66\) V window width. This closes the mismatch family gap for the hidden-error path: offsets move the derivative window, but do not flip backward sign in this Level-1 operating window.
- Added a MOS hidden-error input-common-mode sweep. Matched nudged replica pairs run at \(V_{CM}=0.75, 0.90, 1.05\) V while \(z^+-z^-\) ramps through the active region. Center derivative gain stays near unity (\(\sim0.98\)--\(0.99\)), but the 0.5-gain window width changes from about \(0.48\) V to \(0.67\) V and \(0.78\) V, with the high-common-mode case showing more edge leakage. This closes the hidden-error common-mode family gap and shows that bias choice affects derivative-window margin even when sign remains correct.
- Added a MOS reset threshold-corner sweep for the complementary transmission-gate reset. Strong, nominal, weak, and skewed NMOS/PMOS threshold corners all clear a deliberately mismatched capacitor pair with a 400 ns reset pulse; post-reset differential residue is about \(29, 39, 53, 34,\) and \(45\) mV respectively, and common-mode error stays below 0.2 mV. This closes the reset mismatch family gap: the weak corner needs margin, but the reset topology does not depend on a single exact threshold corner.
- Added a MOS hidden-error-store to writer PMOS-threshold mismatch sweep. Nominal finite-difference/store rails create about \(\pm98.5\) mV of hidden-error differential, then those same capacitor rails drive writer copies with PMOS \(V_{TO}=-0.50,-0.55,-0.60\) V. The signed integrated updates are about \(\pm15.6,\pm13.4,\pm11.0\) mV and hold after \(pacc\). This exposed an important non-ideal: because the complementary hidden-error gate is only about \(1.09\) V, not a rail-high off level, the complementary writer rail still charges by roughly \(19.4,11.8,6.0\) mV while the selected rail charges \(35.0,25.2,16.9\) mV. The net sign and symmetry are robust in this window, but the larger cell should consider level restoration, common-mode adjustment, or writer-bias changes if complementary leakage limits margin.
- Added a MOS hidden-writer activation-gate bias sweep to test whether the complementary writer leakage can be fixed by biasing \(h^-\) alone. Sweeping the active-low activation gate from \(0.86\) V to \(1.10\) V reduces absolute complementary charging, but it weakens the selected rail faster: the selected/complement ratio falls from about \(2.18\) to \(1.69\), and the net \(r^+\) update falls from \(14.6\) mV to \(5.8\) mV. This is a useful negative result: the next hardware fix should not be a simple activation-gate bias shift; it should use restored hidden-error gates, local inhibit, or a different differential writer topology.
- Added a MOS restored hidden-error gate writer characterization. A skewed CMOS inverter restorer maps the stored \(\pm98.5\) mV hidden-error differential into active-low writer gates of about \(0.354\) V selected versus \(1.756\) V complementary. Compared with the direct analog writer, the complementary rail step drops from about \(11.8\) mV to the nV scale while the selected step rises to about \(65.0\) mV and stays incremental. This is the first hidden-error-to-writer MOS slice whose plot makes the complement branch look truly unselected; the cost is a sharper, more digital restoring stage that must be characterized for offset/noise next.
- Added a MOS restored-gate threshold-offset characterization. Complement suppression survives all tested restorer offsets, including a weak complementary pull-up, but selected-side NMOS threshold is the critical weakness: a \(+70\) mV selected-NMOS offset raises the selected gate to about \(1.68\) V and collapses the signed update to zero rather than flipping it. This is useful because it turns the restored-gate slice from a nominal-looking fix into a concrete design requirement: selected-side threshold margin, calibration, larger stored-error swing, or a less abrupt restorer are needed before this can be considered robust training hardware.
- Added a MOS restored-gate NMOS-strength sweep under the selected-weak threshold corner. The result is a sizing window, not monotonic improvement: \(W_N=12\,\mu\mathrm{m}\) reproduces the collapsed write, \(18\)--\(36\,\mu\mathrm{m}\) recovers about \(\pm65\) mV signed update while keeping the complement branch at the nV scale, and \(48\)--\(72\,\mu\mathrm{m}\) overdrives the restorer so the complement branch also moves by about \(65\) mV and cancels the differential update. This makes the next MOS design requirement more concrete: restore hidden-error gates with calibrated trip points and finite margin, not simply maximum pull-down strength.
- Added a MOS restored-gate stored-error swing sweep under the same selected-weak corner with \(W_N=24\,\mu\mathrm{m}\). A \(29.5\) mV stored hidden-error differential is below the selected restorer trip point and writes nothing; a \(49.2\) mV stored differential drops the selected gate to about \(0.31\) V and recovers the full \(\pm65\) mV signed write with complement motion still at the nV scale. Larger swings increase gate margin but do not increase write amplitude, so this restored writer is currently a sign-cleaning/thresholding stage rather than a magnitude-preserving analog update path.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-enable/analog-error writer. The restored gate is now only a branch select/inhibit device, and the original stored hidden-error capacitor remains in series as an analog magnitude gate. With \(W_N=18\,\mu\mathrm{m}\), zero hidden error produces no differential write, the complement rail remains nV-scale for nonzero errors, and the signed write grows with stored error from about \(27.7\) mV at \(\epsilon=30\) mV to \(60.5\) mV at \(\epsilon=140\) mV. This is the first restored writer slice that preserves useful graded update magnitude while keeping the complement path clean, though the series-stack compression and mismatch/noise margin still need characterization.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer threshold-corner sweep. Restorer threshold shifts barely change the selected/complement separation, and complement rail motion remains nV-scale. Writer PMOS thresholds change gain but not sign: the \(\epsilon=100\) mV signed update is about \(37.4\) mV in the weak-writer corner, \(55.1\) mV nominally, and \(75.7\) mV in the strong-writer corner; the combined selected-weak/complement-strong/writer-weak corner still writes about \(37.3\) mV with the correct sign. This makes the hybrid writer the current best MOS update candidate, with dynamic noise and phase-overlap sensitivity now the next robustness gaps.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer phase-overlap sweep. One shared hidden-error store produces about \(98.5\) mV of \(r^+\) differential error; after storage, the selected restored gate is about \(0.196\) V and the complement gate about \(1.731\) V. A true pre-store \(pacc\) pulse is quiet (\(\sim6\) nV signed residue), a \(50\) ns early overlap creates a small \(2.3\) mV stray write (\(\sim8.6\%\) of full), and store-edge or later pulses converge to \(26.4\)--\(27.0\) mV with the complement rail still nV-scale. The concrete phase rule is to keep \(pacc\) out of the pre-store interval; starting at or after the hidden-error storage edge is acceptable for this Level-1 hybrid writer.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer cornered phase-overlap sweep, combining the timing test with nominal, combined-weak, and writer-strong threshold corners. All three corners keep the pre-store write at numerical zero and bound the early-overlap stray write to about `2.3/1.3/3.2` mV, respectively. Store-edge or later pulses converge to useful same-sign updates: nominal `26.4`--`27.0` mV, combined-weak `17.6`--`18.2` mV, and writer-strong `36.3`--`37.2` mV, with post-store complement motion below `0.01` uV. This closes the interaction between static threshold corner and phase sequencing for the current hybrid writer; the remaining robustness targets are dynamic disturbance, reset/reference integrity, and larger-cell loading rather than a hidden timing sign flip.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer disturbance sweep using deterministic post-store charge kicks on the hidden-error capacitors. Starting from a \(98.5\) mV stored \(r^+\) differential, \(\pm25\) mV common-mode kicks preserve sign and only shift gain (`55.1` mV nominal to about `46.2`/`63.1` mV), a \(+25/-25\) mV differential boost writes about `64.9` mV, and a \(-25/+25\) mV destructive differential kick still writes the correct sign at about `45.1` mV with nV complement motion. A \(-50/+50\) mV destructive differential kick crosses the stored-error sign boundary (`-21.5` mV after kick) and produces a small wrong-sign residue (`-0.38` mV), making the noise rule concrete: this Level-1 writer tolerates small differential disturbance, but not disturbance comparable to the full stored hidden-error swing.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer activation-gate sweep to close the other operand of the local product path. With the stored hidden-error differential fixed at \(98.5\) mV and restored gates at about \(0.196/1.731\) V, sweeping the active-low positive-activation gate \(h^-\) from `0.84` V to `1.10` V monotonically reduces the signed write from `59.3` mV to `33.4` mV while the complement rail stays at the nV scale. The activation side is compressed, but it is still a real analog gain input rather than a fixed branch-enable bit.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer repeated-pulse accumulation check for the online-training use case. One stored \(r^+\) hidden-error rail drives the same persistent weight pair through seven `160 ns` active-low \(pacc\) pulses. The selected \(W^+\) rail accumulates nearly equal `12.27` mV increments to `85.9` mV total while the complement \(W^-\) rail moves only about `8` nV, and the value holds after the pulse train. This closes the one-shot-to-repeated-use gap for the current hybrid writer candidate.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer activation-store timing sweep.
