Matched writer copies share one stored \(r^+\) hidden-error rail (`98.5` mV differential), one restored branch selector (`0.196` V selected, `1.731` V complementary), and one sampled activation source; only the active-low `pacc` window moves. A pre-sample write is quiet (`~2` nV signed residue), a sample-edge write is partial (`16.7` mV), and overlap/post-sample/settled-gap writes agree at about `24.33` mV with complement-rail motion at the nV scale. This closes the timing gap between sampled activation storage and the restored local writer: `pacc` must wait for the activation storage phase unless the partial edge write is intentionally budgeted.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer `pacc` pulse-width sweep using the sampled activation capacitor rather than a DC activation gate. With one shared stored \(r^+\) rail, one restored branch selector, and one sampled `h^-` cap, active-low `pacc` widths of `40/80/160/320/640 ns` produced signed updates of `2.91/5.59/10.94/21.65/43.06 mV`; the fit over the tested range is essentially linear (`R^2 ~= 1.0`) and the complement rail stays at the nV scale. The sampled activation cap moved only about `1.8 uV` through the write fanout. This gives the current hybrid writer a concrete transistor-level learning-rate knob instead of relying only on repeated fixed-width pulses.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer four-quadrant product-routing check. The deck stores matched \(e^+\)/\(e^-\) hidden-error rails (`+/-98.5` mV), samples exactly one of `a+`/`a-` active per quadrant through MOS pass gates, and routes the local product as `W+ <- a+e+ + a-e-`, `W- <- a+e- + a-e+`. The four measured signed updates are `+22.99/-22.99/-22.99/+22.99 mV` for `a+e+`, `a+e-`, `a-e+`, `a-e-`, and the inactive rail moves only about `3 nV`. This closes the signed-activation gap in the hybrid writer: it is no longer only a single-ended positive-activation update slice.
- Added a MOS hybrid restored-writer local-bias update check. The full local-feature cell needs \(db \propto e\), not only \(dw \propto ae\). A three-PMOS-stack bias writer uses the same stored/restored \(e^+\)/\(e^-\) rails but no activation operand: \(e^+\) moves `B+` by `34.54 mV` with `B-` at about `3 nV`, and \(e^-\) symmetrically moves `B-` by `34.54 mV` with `B+` quiet. Both signed bias states hold after `pacc`. This closes the bias-write primitive gap for the current hybrid writer ladder.
- Added a MOS read-gated/guarded activation-store aperture comparison for the integrated update-to-forward path. The previous TG store improved short apertures but still drooped for long `pact` pulses (`46.588/52.124/52.141/46.246/34.668/37.163 mV` for `40/80/120/160/240/320 ns`) because the cap remained connected while the forward load collapsed. A series TG controlled by the raw read-valid phase improves the long cases to `40.625 mV`, but still samples part of the read-off transient. A guarded non-overlap valid phase gives `46.577/52.075/52.111/51.355/51.355/51.355 mV`, so the robust topology is not just a bigger TG; it is `pact` AND a separately timed read-valid store gate that disconnects before the load droops.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store timing sweep on the same integrated update-to-forward path. With a long `320 ns` `pact` pulse and guard high-times of `60/100/130/150/180/220 ns`, the preactivation stays valid at about `52.066 mV`, while the stored activation samples `50.275/52.654/51.355/48.570/40.624/34.940 mV`. The useful plateau starts earlier than expected; the guard can close soon after capture, but it must close before the forward-load collapse. The best timing in this sizing is around `100`--`130 ns`, and `180+ ns` begins to track droop.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store pass-gate threshold-corner sweep. With the same long `320 ns` `pact` pulse and nominal `130 ns` guard window, strong/nominal/N-weak/P-weak/both-weak guard pass devices all keep the read preactivation at `52.066 mV` and store `51.884/51.355/51.361/51.266/51.274 mV` after correcting the sweep to target the second, guard-controlled TG rather than the first `pact` TG. The old near-flat `0.021 mV` conclusion was a fixture bug; the real guard-switch corner span is about `0.62 mV`, still much smaller than the clock-skew loss but not zero.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store clock-skew sweep. Holding the guard width at `130 ns` and shifting the window by `-120/-80/-40/0/+40/+80/+120 ns` stores `32.260/48.780/52.517/51.355/42.401/32.238/28.899 mV` while the read preactivation stays near `52.066 mV`. The useful skew window is asymmetric: early skew is tolerated until the `pact` overlap becomes too short (`-40 ns` is still full, `-80 ns` is modestly low), but late skew immediately tracks forward-load droop (`+40 ns` already loses about `9 mV`). The design rule is to bias the guard early rather than late and close it before the read path collapses.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store control-edge slew sweep. With the guard plateau fixed at `3.18--3.31 us` and the long `320 ns` `pact`, guard rise/fall edges of `5/10/20/40/80/120 ns` store `51.536/52.415/51.355/50.611/49.346/46.358 mV`, while preactivation remains near `52.066 mV`. The first hypothesis was that edge rate would be a major hidden limiter; instead ordinary tens-of-ns slew is fine, and only a very slow `120 ns` fall starts to sample the read-path collapse. Absolute guard-window placement remains the dominant timing constraint.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store read-valid fall sweep. With the guard fixed at `3.18--3.31 us`, moving the read fall start through `3.26/3.30/3.33/3.36/3.40/3.44 us` stores `57.604/51.926/51.355/51.355/51.355/51.355 mV`. The failed hypothesis was that early read fall would simply undercharge; instead it can oversample a read-turnoff transient while the guard is still open. Once the guard has disconnected the store, later read fall timing no longer changes the stored activation.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store read-valid rise sweep. With the guard fixed at `3.18--3.31 us` and read fall at `3.36 us`, delaying the read rise end through `2.72/3.00/3.10/3.18/3.24/3.28 us` stores `51.355/34.123/23.346/14.559/7.923/3.489 mV`. The rise side is simpler than the fall side: late read validity monotonically undercharges because the preactivation is not settled inside the guard window. The guard should open only after read has already settled.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store no-reset hold check. With the same nominal guard and long `pact`, but with the reset pulse disabled after capture, the stored activation samples `51.35455/51.35444/51.35431/51.35419/51.35407 mV` at `3.575/4.50/5.50/6.50/7.45 us`; total drift is only about `-0.48 uV` while preactivation remains at `52.066 mV`. In this Level-1 model and leakage setup, the guarded store's immediate problem is timing, not hold leakage.
- Added a corrected MOS guarded activation-store width sweep on the guard-controlled TG. Scaling the nominal `96u/240u` guard pair to `0.125x/0.25x/0.5x/1.0x/1.5x` stores `51.078/51.255/51.425/51.355/51.365 mV`, with the read preactivation fixed near `52.066 mV`. The width spread is only about `0.35 mV`, so the nominal switch is already oversized enough for Level-1 capture; the real design pressure is phase timing and skew, not simply making the guard switch wider.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store guard-start sweep that fixes guard close at `3.31 us` and moves only the guard rise end through `3.10/3.14/3.18/3.22/3.26/3.29 us`. The stored activation is `51.359/51.359/51.355/49.183/40.449/26.159 mV`, with read preactivation fixed at `52.066 mV`. This isolates the start-side part of the skew result: opening guard before `pact` is harmless because the series `pact` TG still blocks the store, but opening after nominal cuts charge time and quickly undercharges the activation capacitor.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store off-isolation stress. After one nominal capture and with reset disabled, the deck applies later read pulses, `pact`-only pulses while guard is low, and guard-only/read+guard pulses while `pact` is low. The preactivation side moves substantially under that stress (`52.066 -> 64.749/69.981/69.981/53.486/20.843/6.105 mV`), but the stored activation remains `51.35455/51.35444/51.35437/51.35431/51.35422/51.35415/51.35406 mV` at `3.575/4.50/5.05/5.50/6.25/6.75/7.45 us`, only `-0.49 uV` total drift. This validates off-state isolation under later control activity, not just quiet hold leakage.
- Added a signed MOS guarded activation-store hold check. The existing negative update/read-reuse deck now routes through the same `pact`+guard TG stack as the positive deck, with reset disabled after capture. Positive and negative preactivation samples mirror exactly to the saved precision (`+/-52.065847/... mV`), and stored activations mirror as `+/-51.354550`, `+/-51.354440`, `+/-51.354310`, `+/-51.354190`, and `+/-51.354070 mV` at `3.575/4.50/5.50/6.50/7.45 us`; saved-sample mirror error is `0 mV`. The guard store is not only good for the positive-only timing cases; it preserves signed differential symmetry in this Level-1 model.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store `pact` edge-slew sweep. With read/guard fixed and the `pact` rise starting at `3.16 us`, rise/fall edges of `5/10/20/40/80/120 ns` store `51.378/51.352/51.355/50.934/48.721/43.040 mV`, with preactivation fixed at `52.066 mV`. The first rough assertion was too aggressive: `pact` edge slew is not as dominant as guard placement, but a very slow `120 ns` edge still loses about `8.31 mV` because `pact` is slewing during much of the valid guard window. Ordinary `5--20 ns` edges agree to tens of microvolts.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store `CSTORE` sweep. With nominal read/`pact`/guard timing and `CSTORE=2/5/10/20/50/100 pF`, the stored activation is `61.070/56.111/51.355/43.865/30.215/20.513 mV`, while preactivation stays near `52.066 mV`. This is a two-sided sizing tradeoff: very small activation caps overshoot due to switch/clock feedthrough, while large caps suppress feedthrough but undercharge in the fixed `130 ns` guard window. The nominal `10 pF` point is a real compromise, not just an arbitrary hold capacitor.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store control-swing sweep. With the read path and nominal timing fixed, `pact` and guard swings of `1.8/1.6/1.4/1.2/1.0/0.8 V` store `51.355/51.336/51.782/58.088/0.000/0.000 mV` while preactivation stays near `52.066 mV`. The result is not just monotone undercharge: `1.4 V` still captures correctly, `1.2 V` is marginal and overshoots from pass-stack feedthrough/slow settling, and `1.0 V` no longer turns on the series TG stack enough to store activation. This gives the phase-driver headroom rule for the current guarded store.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store read-drive sweep. With nominal `pact`/guard timing fixed, read-valid gate drives of `0.75/0.90/1.05/1.15/1.30/1.45 V` produce preactivations of `6.655/22.013/41.643/52.066/59.820/62.056 mV` and stored activations of `8.428/26.991/44.247/51.355/56.009/56.687 mV`. Weak read drive is therefore a signal-path limiter before the store switch: it cannot build the summing/forward state before the guard closes. Stronger read drive helps until the forward/store path saturates around the mid-50 mV range.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store `WREAD` sizing sweep. With nominal read-valid drive and guard timing fixed, read-tail NMOS widths of `6/12/24/48/96/192u` produce preactivations of `17.393/33.678/52.066/61.495/62.753/62.898 mV` and stored activations of `21.209/37.538/51.355/57.040/57.355/57.202 mV`. The read-tail width and read-drive sweeps agree on the limiting mechanism: below nominal, the read path cannot build enough preactivation before the guard closes; above roughly `48--96u`, extra read strength mostly hits the forward/store saturation knee rather than producing a larger held activation.
- Added a MOS guarded activation-store forward-pair/tail width sweep, which corrected the last interpretation. With the same MOS-written `W/B` state, read path, guard timing, and `10 pF` store cap, forward-pair/tail widths of `6/12/24/48/96/192u` keep the read preactivation fixed at about `52.066 mV`, while the forward-load differential rises `12.318/21.020/33.618/51.130/75.728/110.931 mV` and the guarded stored activation rises `12.479/21.184/33.794/51.355/76.077/111.674 mV`. The read-width plateau was therefore a read-preactivation ceiling under the nominal forward gain, not a hard forward/store saturation limit. The forward pair is a real activation-gain knob and needs renewed mismatch/noise/common-mode checks at any larger chosen sizing.
- Added an isolated MOS forward-tail bias sweep after a deliberately attempted shared-`vbias` sweep showed the shared rail also changes the read path (`0.70/0.80 V` underdrive read preactivation and `1.25 V` collapses it). The corrected deck keeps global `vbias=0.95 V` and gives only the crossed forward-pair tail NMOS a private bias source. That keeps preactivation fixed at `52.066 mV`, but the stored activation only moves `49.657/50.752/51.221/51.355/51.653/51.756/51.813 mV` for isolated forward-tail biases of `0.70/0.80/0.90/0.95/1.05/1.15/1.25 V`. Tail bias is therefore a fine trim and isolation rail in this sizing, not the large activation-gain knob; width remains the main way to increase swing.
- Added a MOS `96u` forward-pair robustness check to avoid promoting the wider forward pair from a nominal sweep alone. With the same MOS-written `W/B` state and guard timing, positive hold stores `76.07747/76.07731/76.07713/76.07694/76.07677 mV` over `3.575/4.50/5.50/6.50/7.45 us`, the negative case mirrors it exactly to saved precision, and mirror error stays `0 uV`. The off-isolation stress still moves the preactivation side (`52.066 -> 64.749/69.981/.../6.105 mV`) under later read/phase activity, but the held `96u` activation drifts only about `-0.71 uV`. This does not close mismatch/noise/common-mode for the high-gain candidate, but it proves the larger swing survives signed hold and later control activity in the Level-1 fixture.
- Added a MOS `96u` forward-pair summing-common-mode sweep, which found the first concrete headroom limit for the high-gain candidate. Resetting both summing capacitors to `0.75/0.85/0.90/0.95/1.05 V` keeps the read preactivation positive at `47.585/51.453/52.066/52.222/52.297 mV`, but the forward/store path only captures `0.000/27.139/76.077/88.630/92.774 mV`. The `0.75 V` case has a real differential preactivation but no useful stored activation, and `0.85 V` is partial underdrive. Therefore the `96u` forward pair needs roughly nominal-or-higher summing common mode in this Level-1 fixture; reset/common-mode generation is now an explicit promotion condition before mismatch/noise testing.
- Added a MOS `96u` forward-pair private-tail rebias sweep to check whether the low summing-common-mode failure was just tail overdrive. It was not. With `zcm=0.75 V`, stored activation stays essentially zero for tail biases `0.55/0.65/0.75/0.85/0.95 V`. With `zcm=0.85 V`, the store remains partial (`0.000/24.957/26.456/26.921/27.139 mV`) rather than recovering toward the nominal `76 mV` value. With `zcm=0.90 V`, a too-low `0.55 V` tail bias also kills the otherwise valid readout (`0.000/72.284/74.965/75.729/76.077 mV` across the same bias sweep). The design rule is now sharper: the high-gain 96u forward path needs a summing-common-mode/input-headroom solution, not just a tail-bias trim.
- Added a passive shifted-gate latch characterization for the `96u` forward-pair headroom failure. Instead of driving the large forward-pair gates directly from the low summing caps, the deck resets two `5 pF` gate latches to `0.90 V` and AC-couples the signed summing nodes into them before the crossed MOS pair. With `zcm=0.75 V`, the read preactivation remains about `47.4 mV`, and coupling values of `5/10/20/50 pF` store `43.245/52.154/13.414/0.091 mV`. The useful point is therefore a sizing window, not monotone gain. With the chosen `10 pF` coupling, shifted `zcm=0.75/0.85/0.90 V` stores `52.154/60.215/61.115 mV`, and the negative case mirrors at `-52.154 mV` with sub-`0.3 mV` hold drift. This is the first actual low-common-mode headroom fix candidate, but it now needs mismatch, reset-feedthrough, and repeated/off-isolation checks before promotion.
- Added the shifted-gate latch off-isolation stress. The deck uses the selected `10 pF` coupling into `5 pF` shifted gates at `zcm=0.75 V`, captures `52.153750 mV`, then applies later read-only, `pact`-only, guard-only, and guard+read-only activity while the activation store should remain isolated. The stored samples are `52.153750/52.153640/52.153560/52.153510/52.153410/52.153350/52.153260 mV`, so total drift is only about `-0.49 uV` even while the preactivation side is intentionally disturbed (`47.446 -> 52.972 -> 44.189 -> 26.215 -> 3.263 -> 0.166 mV`). This makes the shifted-gate fix a stronger candidate: the remaining promotion gaps are now reset/feedthrough, mismatch/noise, and repeated-cycle behavior, not basic off-state isolation.
- Added a shifted-gate latch reset/feedthrough stress. The deck deliberately starts the shifted-gate caps from a bad differential state (`zpg=0.35 V`, `zmg=1.45 V`) and compares no-reset behavior against a real MOS reset pulse before read. Without reset, the gate differential is still `-371.522 mV` at `2.70 us` and the activation store overdrives to `362.298 mV`. With reset, the pre-read gate residue is at most `0.118 mV`, the common mode is `0.899993 V`, and the store lands at `57.628 mV`, about `15.9%` of the bad unreset store and close to the nominal initialized shifted-gate `52.154 mV`. Reset charge/feedthrough is therefore still a sizing topic, but the physical reset devices do clear bad initial state and preserve a useful low-common-mode activation.
- Added a shifted-gate latch repeated reset/read/store characterization. Starting from one MOS-written W/B state at low summing common mode (`zcm=0.75 V`), the deck runs three read/forward-store cycles with physical reset pulses between cycles. The read preactivation samples are `46.625/46.823/46.582 mV`, and the stored activations are `49.528/56.585/56.585 mV`; the later cycles are larger because the reset pulse itself adds a small repeatable charge/feedthrough offset to the shifted-gate store. The shifted-gate reset residue between reads is only `0.000466/0.000456 mV`, gate common mode returns to `0.900003 V`, z residue is about `0.019 mV`, h residue is `0`, and W/B drift over repeated reads is `0.003/0.002 uV`. This closes the repeated-cycle promotion gap for the shifted-gate headroom fix; mismatch/noise is the next real gap.
- Added a deterministic shifted-gate offset/noise sweep for the `96u` low-common-mode headroom fix. With nominal shifted gates, the deck stores `52.154 mV`. Common-mode offsets of `-25/+25 mV` on both shifted gates only move the store to `50.092/53.607 mV`. A helpful `-25 mV` gate-differential residue increases the stored activation to `66.787 mV`; destructive `+25/+50 mV` residues reduce it to `37.274/22.219 mV` without flipping sign. This gives the first concrete shifted-gate noise rule: common-mode gate noise is mild in this window, while differential residue directly consumes activation margin and must stay well below the useful shifted-gate differential.
- Added a shifted-gate forward-pair threshold-skew characterization. Matched `-20/+20 mV` input-pair threshold corners keep the low-common-mode shifted store positive (`54.942/45.662 mV`), but one-sided/skewed offsets are severe: `h+` weak leaves only `15.771 mV`, `h-` weak rises to `81.091 mV`, skew `-20/+20 mV` rises to `116.936 mV`, and skew `+20/-20 mV` flips the stored activation to `-19.373 mV` even though the passive gate differential is unchanged at about `-29.916 mV`. Adding helpful gate trim shows the recovery budget: `-25 mV` trim is still negative (`-4.176 mV`), `-50 mV` barely recovers positive sign (`11.022 mV`), and `-75 mV` gives a more useful `26.162 mV`. This is the strongest evidence so far that the shifted-gate fix needs either per-cell offset trim/autozero, larger signal margin, or a less offset-sensitive forward pair before promotion.
- Added a physical split-reset trim characterization for the failing shifted-gate threshold-skew corner. The deck starts the shifted-gate caps from a deliberately bad initial state, uses real MOS reset switches to split reset reference rails, and keeps the same `+20/-20 mV` forward-pair threshold skew that flipped the untrimmed activation. The reset establishes the requested pre-read gate differentials almost exactly (`0/-10/-15/-20/-24.999/-49.998/-74.998/-99.997 mV`) while holding gate common mode at `0.899993--0.899994 V`. The stored activations are `-20.241/-0.766/8.970/18.705/28.438/77.060/125.552/173.790 mV`. Conclusion: physical reset alone does not fix the skew hazard, but a hardware-realistic split reset/autozero rail recovers sign around `-15 mV` and useful margin by `-25 mV`; excessive trim overdrives the activation, so trim is a calibrated offset-cancellation knob rather than free gain.
- Added a signed split-reset balance check across both threshold-skew polarities. The nominal physical-reset store is `57.628 mV`. Untrimmed one-sided threshold corners store `18.653/96.295 mV` for `h+` weak and `h-` weak; signed reset trims of `-20/+15 mV` move them back to `57.510/67.220 mV`. Full opposite skews show the larger problem and the useful symmetry: untrimmed `+20/-20 mV` skew flips to `-20.241 mV`, untrimmed `-20/+20 mV` overdrives to `135.237 mV`, and reset trims of `-35/+35 mV` pull them back to `47.897/67.355 mV`. The split-reset rail is therefore a bidirectional offset-cancellation primitive, not just a one-corner rescue, but it still needs per-cell calibration.
- Added a calibrated shifted-gate repeated-reuse check for the failing `+20/-20 mV` forward-pair threshold skew. The deck uses the same low-common-mode `zcm=0.75 V`, MOS-written `W/B` state, `10 pF` shifted-gate coupling, and a physical split reset, but now reuses the cell for three read/reset/store cycles with `-35 mV` split-reset trim. A pre-reset before the first read is required; without it the first cycle is effectively untrimmed. With the pre-reset, reset samples establish gate differentials of `-34.9986/-34.9995/-34.9995 mV` while holding common mode at `0.899993/0.900003/0.900003 V`, reset `z` stays below `0.064 mV`, and reset `h` is `0`. The three cycles read `z=46.647/46.823/46.582 mV` and store `h=46.985/46.967/46.967 mV`; W/B drift is `0.001/0.000 uV`. This closes the immediate "can calibrated trim be reused without Python poking state" gap for this Level-1 fixture, while leaving calibration strategy and mismatch/noise as the next promotion questions.
- Added a calibrated shifted-gate trim-margin sweep around the `-35 mV` split-reset point. The same skewed repeated-reuse fixture was run at reset trims `-25/-30/-35/-40/-45 mV`. Physical reset samples track the requested trim to within about `0.002 mV` and keep common mode at `0.899993/0.900003/0.900003 V` across cycles. Read preactivation is almost unchanged (`~46.6--46.8 mV`) because the trim acts on the shifted forward-pair gate offset, not on the upstream read. Stored activations are monotone and bounded: cycle means `27.722/37.349/46.973/56.590/66.201 mV`, with per-trim cycle spread only `0.0165--0.0197 mV`. This gives a concrete local calibration-error rule: this fixture tolerates roughly `+/-10 mV` around the selected `-35 mV` trim without sign loss or overdrive, but array-scale trim discovery/distribution and stochastic noise are still open.
- Added a shifted-gate reset-reference perturbation check to avoid assuming ideal reset-rail delivery. The same `zcm=0.75 V`, `96u` forward-pair, `10 pF` shifted-gate, `+20/-20 mV` skew fixture now drives the split reset references with cycle-varying PWL commands. Common-mode commands of `0.88/0.92/0.89 V` sample as `0.909527/0.911545/0.890003 V`, exposing finite reset settling/feedthrough, but the differential trim remains `-34.999/-34.998/-35.000 mV` and the stored activation only changes `47.120/47.119/46.796 mV`. Differential trim jitter is the real sensitivity: requested `-30/-35/-40 mV` samples as `-33.690/-36.409/-39.999 mV` and stores `44.467/49.679/56.585 mV`; requested `-25/-35/-45 mV` samples as `-32.382/-37.819/-44.999 mV` and stores `41.949/52.390/66.196 mV`. The test now asserts bounded sampled common mode, sampled differential inside the calibrated trim window, reset clearing of `z/h`, and activation ordering under differential jitter rather than pretending the physical reset caps track ideal PWL commands exactly.
- Added a shifted-gate threshold-skew versus split-trim law check. The repeated-reuse fixture sweeps opposite forward-pair threshold skews of `10/15/20/25/30 mV` and applies physical split-reset trims of `-15/-25/-35/-45/-55 mV`. The reset switch network samples those trims on the shifted gates as `-14.999/-24.999/-34.999/-44.998/-54.998 mV` on the first reset while holding common mode near `0.899993 V`. Calibrated mean stored activations are almost flat at `46.974/46.973/46.973/46.972/46.972 mV`; a deliberately under-trimmed `30 mV` skew with the old `-35 mV` trim stores only `8.467/8.448/8.448 mV`. This turns the prior "per-cell calibration required" caveat into a sharper rule: the transistor reset primitive can express a skew-scaled offset-cancellation code, but a severe cell needs its own code rather than reusing the nominal `-35 mV` trim.
- Added a signed shifted-gate threshold-skew trim-law check across both skew polarities. The same physical split-reset primitive establishes requested signed trims to within about `0.001 mV` while holding reset common mode near `0.90 V` and clearing `z/h` between cycles. Positive threshold skew with negative trim lands near `46.97 mV` for calibrated `+10/+20/+30 mV` cases, while the opposite skew polarity with positive trim lands near `66.20 mV` for calibrated `-10/-20/-30 mV` cases. The result is therefore bidirectional signed correction, not perfectly symmetric flattening. The under-trim failures bracket the useful band: `+30 mV` skew with only `-35 mV` trim stores `8.454 mV`, while `-30 mV` skew with only `+35 mV` trim over-stores `104.558 mV`. The next calibration question is reducing the residual polarity-dependent gain band, not whether the reset network can apply either trim sign.
- Added a shifted-gate polarity-offset calibration sweep for the severe `30 mV` threshold-skew corners. For `+30 mV` skew, negative trims `-45/-55/-65 mV` store mean activations `27.720/46.972/66.200 mV`; for `-30 mV` skew, positive trims `+55/+65/+75 mV` store `66.207/46.978/27.727 mV`. The reset network tracks all requested trims and keeps common mode near `0.90 V`, so the residual asymmetry is calibratable with an offset code: the opposite-polarity branch needs about `+65 mV`, not the naive signed `+55 mV`, to align with the `-55 mV` reference. This converts the last signed-trim caveat from "polarity-dependent gain remains" into a concrete per-polarity trim-code rule.
- Added a positive-polarity reset-reference perturbation check around the `-30 mV` skew, `+65 mV` calibrated shifted-gate code. Clean reset samples establish `64.998/65.000/65.000 mV` gate differentials and store `46.999/46.967/46.967 mV`. Common-mode commands `0.88/0.92/0.89 V` again settle imperfectly to `0.909527/0.911544/0.890003 V`, but stored activation only moves `47.128/47.130/46.797 mV`. Differential trim jitter dominates: sampled `62.381/67.820/75.000 mV` stores `52.032/41.542/27.716 mV`. This family-completes the earlier negative-trim reset-reference result: both trim signs tolerate common-mode reference error, while differential trim delivery remains the calibration-sensitive rail.
- Added a shifted-gate reset-switch threshold-corner check for both calibrated severe trim polarities. The new deck gives the reset transmission gates explicit strong, nominal, weak, and skewed `VTO` models while reusing the `+30 mV` skew / `-55 mV` trim and `-30 mV` skew / `+65 mV` trim branches. Across all corners, sampled trim error stays below `0.004 mV`, reset common-mode error below `0.009 mV`, and mean stored activation remains `46.971..46.972 mV` for the negative-trim branch and `46.978 mV` for the positive-trim branch. This narrows the remaining shifted-gate risk: ordinary reset TG threshold corners are not the visible limiter in the Level-1 fixture; forward-pair offset calibration and differential trim delivery still are.
- Added a finite reset-reference source-impedance check for the shifted-gate split-reset rails. With nominal reset switches and severe calibrated branches (`+30 mV` skew / `-55 mV` trim and `-30 mV` skew / `+65 mV` trim), `0` and `1 kOhm` source impedance preserve trim within `0.067 mV` and keep stored activation around `47 mV`. At `10 kOhm`, the first short pre-reset pulse already under-delivers: the negative-trim branch samples only `-31.782 mV` on the first reset and stores `1.964/41.967/44.688 mV`, while the positive-trim branch samples `37.563 mV` and stores `97.472/48.589/45.160 mV`. At `100 kOhm` and above the latch returns to the known under-trim failure directions. The reset TGs have device-corner margin, but the trim-reference driver must be low impedance or the reset pulse must be lengthened before this calibration is array-realistic.
- Added ngspice-derived callouts to the hybrid writer activation, product-router, and reset/reuse figures. The activation sweep now exposes the stored hidden error (`98.5 mV`), restored-gate separation (`1.54 V`), positive incremental write range (`33.4--59.3 mV`), gain ratio (`1.78x`), and suppressed complement rail (`0.000 mV`). The four-quadrant product plot now reports symmetric stored errors (`+/-98.5 mV`), selected/complement restored gates (`0.20/1.73 V`), exact activation sampling in this fixture, minimum signed update (`23.0 mV`), zero displayed quadrant spread, and suppressed inactive rail. The reuse plot now reports reset residue/common-mode error near zero, no visible reset disturbance of persistent weight caps, `25.7 mV` first write, `25.7 mV` opposite-sign cancellation, and zero displayed final hold error. This did not change circuit behavior; it makes the existing ngspice pass margins visible in the paper plots.
- Added ngspice-derived callouts to the adjacent hybrid activation-control figures. The activation-store plot now shows the stored hidden error (`98.5 mV`), selected/complement restored gates (`0.20/1.73 V`), active activation sample (`0.920 V`), inactive overwrite (`1.450 V`), active write (`25.7 mV`), zero displayed inactive increment, final drift, and complement step. The activation-timing sweep now makes the required sample-before-write ordering explicit: pre-sample write is `0.00 mV`, sample-edge write is `16.7 mV`, settled writes are at least `24.3 mV`, settled spread is `0.00 mV`, and the complement rail stays at `0.000 mV`. The pacc-width sweep now reports the stored hidden/error gates, sampled activation hold, monotone step range (`2.9--43.1 mV`), minimum width increment (`2.7 mV`), linearity (`R2=1.00000`), and zero displayed complement/selected-dominance error. This keeps the figure captions backed by the same ngspice traces and assertions rather than adding a separate hand-written interpretation.
- Added ngspice-derived callouts to the remaining paper-facing hybrid-writer summary figures: timing-corner, bias-writer, integrated cell-update, and four-quadrant cell-update plots. The regenerated figures now show the same trace-backed margins that the assertions enforce, including selected/complement restored-gate levels, stored error symmetry, inactive-rail suppression, hold error, update/readback magnitudes, pacc timing requirements, and quadrant spread. This did not change circuit behavior; it closes the readability gap for the older hybrid summary plots so the paper figures expose the actual MOS/ngspice evidence instead of requiring readers to trust the captions.
- Added assertion-derived margin callouts to the basic MOS synapse mismatch and common-mode figures. The mismatch plot now reports the bounded zero offset (`20 mV` max under the +/-20 mV threshold-skew fixture), the mirrored gate-swapped W- zero error, retained current swing, and offset span. The common-mode plot now reports peak-current range (`465--486 uA`), minimum sign margin (`315 uA`), centering error (`0.4%` of peak), and W-/W+ mirror error. Regenerated the synapse family through ngspice and inspected the resulting PNGs; this closes a paper-figure readability gap without changing the underlying circuits or thresholds.
- Added assertion-derived callouts to the restored-gate hybrid update-to-forward integration and same-cell reuse plots. The single-sample integration plot now shows stored error, selected/complement restored-gate levels, sampled activation, W/B write magnitudes, pre-read quietness, read preactivation, forward-load/store magnitudes, and W/B read-disturb. The reuse plot now shows first/second signed error stores, reset residue, first W/B writes, opposite-update cancellation residue, reset z/h residue, and cancelled final activation. Reran the full `--only update` ngspice suite; all existing update-family assertions still pass, and the two regenerated PNGs now expose the measured integration margins directly.
- Added assertion-derived callouts to the update-forward quadrant and repeated-read/reuse figures. The quadrant plot now exposes stored error symmetry, selected/complement restored-gate separation, signed W/B update minima and spread, minimum preactivation, stored activation margin, and sign alignment across all activation/error quadrants. The read-reuse plots now show W/B drift, cycle-to-cycle preactivation and activation spread, reset residue, and positive/negative mirror error, making the ngspice-backed reuse margins visible without relying on caption text alone.
- Added ngspice-derived callouts to the shifted-gate reset-reference perturbation figures. The single-cycle `0.80 V` reset-reference plot now reports the nominal `52.0 mV` store, the `+17.2 mV` helpful differential gain, the `17.6 mV` destructive `+10 mV` drop, the `2.6 mV` common-mode store error, and the remaining `16.5 mV` destructive `+20 mV` margin. The multi-cycle negative/positive trim perturbation plots now expose clean trim error (`~0.001--0.002 mV`), common-reference tracking error, differential trim span (`12.6 mV`), common-jitter activation spread (`0.3 mV`), and differential-jitter activation span (`~24 mV`). This keeps the reset-reference calibration story tied to measured SPICE margins rather than visual trend interpretation alone.
- Added trace-derived callouts to the base shifted-gate headroom/coupling, threshold-corner, and physical split-reset trim figures. The headroom plot now reports low-z rescue, partial-z gain, signed mirror error, best coupling, overcoupled drop, and 50 pF collapse. The threshold plot now reports the untrimmed skew flip, trim recovery margins, gate-diff spread, nonhazard minimum, and total h span. The split-reset trim plot now reports first sign recovery, first useful trim, trim tracking error, common-mode error, and overdrive at excessive trim. Regenerated the update-family figures through ngspice and inspected the affected PNGs for readability.
- Added a shifted-gate reset-reference fanout check for the tuned local trim reservoir. The new MOS/ngspice fixture keeps initialized 250 pF split-trim reservoirs, 100 kOhm trim sources, real complementary reset switches, and 5 pF shifted-gate loads at 0.80 V common, then sweeps one reservoir driving 1/2/4/8/16 reset loads. One load keeps trim error below 1.27 mV, four loads stay below the 6 mV trim-error gate, eight loads exceed it at 7.55 mV or more, and sixteen loads reach 13.28/15.61 mV error for the -55/+65 mV trim branches. Common-mode error and first/last load mismatch stay at the displayed zero level, so the limit is differential charge sharing from finite reservoir capacitance rather than reset-switch mismatch. The component rule is now explicit: this 250 pF local reservoir is a roughly four-load trim source unless capacitance or schedule is increased.
- Added a shifted-gate reset-reference reservoir-capacitance sweep to quantify the fanout recovery knob. The same real MOS reset-switch fixture now sweeps 250 pF, 500 pF, and 1 nF local split-trim reservoirs against 4/8/16 shifted-gate loads. The 250 pF case reproduces the eight-load failure (`7.553/8.927 mV` trim error for -55/+65 mV branches), 500 pF recovers eight loads below the 6 mV gate (`4.065/4.804 mV`) but leaves sixteen loads marginal (`7.570/8.946 mV`), and 1 nF recovers sixteen loads (`4.069/4.809 mV`). Common-mode error remains at displayed zero, so the recovery is the expected differential charge-sharing improvement rather than a common-mode artifact. Inspected the generated PNG; the callouts are readable enough for the paper figure.
- Added a cold-start fanout-precharge sweep for the larger shifted-gate trim reservoirs. This family-completes the previous capacitance result: bigger initialized reservoirs recover fanout, but they also need a longer MOS startup-precharge budget. With cold reservoirs, `100 kOhm` sources, nominal startup TGs, real reset switches, and tuned `0.80 V` common mode, the `250 pF / 4 loads` case passes the 6 mV trim-error gate at `20 ns` (`4.307/5.094 mV`), `500 pF / 8 loads` passes at `40 ns` (`4.391/5.194 mV`), and `1 nF / 16 loads` remains marginal at `40 ns` (`9.122/10.779 mV`) but passes at `80 ns` (`4.428/5.236 mV`). First/last reset-load mismatch is below displayed precision, so the plot shows reservoir charging schedule cost rather than fanout imbalance. The figure now plots the actual reservoir differential during the startup TG pulse instead of a mostly empty post-reset trace.
- Added a larger-reservoir startup-precharge strength sweep for the shifted-gate fanout fix. The same cold-reservoir MOS fixture now keeps the short schedule and scales only the temporary startup TG around the nominal `300/900 um` NMOS/PMOS path. At `1x`, `500 pF / 8 loads / 20 ns` still misses the 6 mV gate (`8.243/9.745 mV`) and `1 nF / 16 loads / 40 ns` still misses (`9.122/10.779 mV`). At `2x`, both recover (`4.328/5.119 mV` and `4.404/5.209 mV`), with common-mode error below `6.737 mV` and first/last load mismatch below displayed precision. The lower plot was trimmed to the representative `+65 mV` branch after inspection; the full assertion still covers both trim polarities. This makes the reservoir fanout fix a width-time circuit tradeoff, not an ideal initialization assumption.
- Added a repeated fresh-fanout recharge check for the larger shifted-gate trim reservoirs. The new MOS/ngspice fixture starts the reservoirs cold, applies the recovered `2x` startup-precharge schedules, then uses the same passive reservoir to reset three fresh banks of real `5 pF` shifted-gate loads. The first bank still passes (`500 pF / 8 loads` worst first-cycle error `5.208 mV`; `1 nF / 16 loads` worst `5.296 mV`), but a `1 us` repeated-reset interval fails by the third fresh bank (`13.447/13.660 mV` worst for the two larger cases). Extending recharge to `200 us` recovers the third reset (`4.979/5.585 mV` worst for the positive trim branch, all branches below the `6 mV` gate) with common-mode error below `5.936 mV` and first/last load mismatch below displayed precision. This closes the startup-only blind spot: the larger fanout reservoir is realistic for repeated fresh-load use only with an explicit recharge budget, more local reservoir partitioning, or a lower-impedance trim refresh path.
- Added a trim-refresh source-impedance sweep for the hardest repeated fresh-fanout case (`1 nF` reservoir, `16` fresh loads, `2x` startup precharge). The fixture uses real MOS startup/precharge transmission gates, real MOS reset switches, passive reservoir capacitors, and fresh shifted-gate load banks; the only swept knobs are source impedance and recharge interval. With the original `100 kOhm` refresh source, a `20 us` cadence still fails by cycle 3 (`9.668/11.424 mV` trim error for the `-55/+65 mV` branches). A `10 kOhm` source recovers that cadence (`4.599/5.442 mV`, both below the `6 mV` gate). A `1 kOhm` source at `1 us` is close but still marginal for the positive-trim branch (`5.305/6.264 mV`). This turns the previous "needs recharge or lower impedance" caveat into a concrete circuit tradeoff: the hard 16-load reservoir wants roughly `10 kOhm` at `20 us`, or more partitioning/refresh time if the source must remain weaker.
- Added a local-partitioning sweep for the same hard repeated fresh-fanout reset-reference case. Total reservoir capacitance stays `1 nF` and total fresh shifted-gate load stays `16 x 5 pF`, but the deck splits that into `1/2/4/8/16` local passive reservoirs, each with its own real `100 kOhm` refresh path, `2x` MOS startup precharge, and complementary reset switches. The measured rule is sharper than the hypothesis: `4 x 4` helps but still fails the `20 us` positive-trim third cycle (`7.622 mV`), while `8 x 2` recovers it (`5.834 mV`) and `16 x 1` gives more margin (`4.916 mV`). Partitioning does not rescue a `1 us` cadence: even `16 x 1` leaves `9.654/11.409 mV` third-cycle error for the `-55/+65 mV` branches. This makes partitioning a real locality/area knob for the 20 us regime, not a substitute for lower refresh impedance when the schedule is pushed to 1 us.
- Added a joint partition/local-refresh impedance sweep for the remaining `1 us` repeated fresh-bank reset-reference failure. The fixture keeps total reservoir capacitance at `1 nF`, total load at `16 x 5 pF`, cold passive reservoirs, `2x` MOS startup precharge, and real complementary reset switches; only the number of local partitions and each partition's finite refresh resistor change. The measured 1 us boundary is now concrete: `8 x 2` with `10 kOhm` local refresh is still marginal on the positive-trim branch (`6.990 mV` cycle-3 error), but `8 x 2` with `3 kOhm` passes (`3.768 mV`) and `16 x 1` with `10 kOhm` passes (`4.891 mV`). A single global `1 x 16` reservoir still fails even with a `3 kOhm` source (`9.842 mV`). This closes the earlier "partitioning or lower impedance" ambiguity: the 1 us component needs both local reservoir partitioning and a low enough local refresh path.
- Added a fast-reset refresh-resistance margin sweep around the two recovered `1 us` split-reset reference configurations. The new ngspice fixture keeps the same cold passive reservoirs, `2x` MOS startup precharge, ordinary complementary reset switches, and three fresh shifted-gate load banks, then scales or P/M-skews only the local refresh resistors. `16 x 1 @ 10 kOhm` is a real pass at nominal (`4.886 mV` positive-trim cycle-3 error) and at `1.25x` matched resistance (`5.499 mV`), but it crosses the 6 mV gate at `1.5x` (`6.052 mV`) and fails clearly at `2x` (`6.987 mV`). `8 x 2 @ 3 kOhm` has wider margin and still passes at `2x` (`5.382 mV`). The tested `1.25/0.75` P/M resistance skews stay below `4.854 mV`. This turns the nominal recovery into a component choice: one-load partitions are area/local but resistance-sensitive; two-load partitions with a stronger local refresh path are more robust for the 1 us cadence.
- Added a fast-reset reset-pulse/switch margin sweep for the same two recovered `1 us` split-reset reference configurations. This keeps the local refresh paths fixed (`8 x 2 @ 3 kOhm`, `16 x 1 @ 10 kOhm`) and changes only active reset-pulse width and complementary reset TG sizing. Unlike refresh impedance, reset aperture has comfortable margin in this fixture: `8 x 2` still passes at `40 ns` with nominal reset switches (`4.579 mV` positive-trim cycle-3 error), `16 x 1` passes at `80 ns` nominal (`5.179 mV`), and `120 ns` with half-size reset switches remains below gate (`3.780/4.900 mV` for the two positive-trim branches). This localizes the remaining fast repeated-bank risk to reservoir recharge/refresh impedance rather than the tested reset-switch aperture.
- Added a shifted-gate load-capacitance sweep for the same two recovered `1 us` fast reset-reference configurations. Nominal `5 pF` loads still pass, and heavier loads monotonically consume third-cycle margin. `8 x 2 @ 3 kOhm` has the wider load margin: positive-branch cycle-3 trim error is `3.762 mV` at `5 pF`, `4.645 mV` at `6.25 pF`, `5.506 mV` at `7.5 pF`, and `7.163 mV` at `10 pF`. `16 x 1 @ 10 kOhm` is already at the boundary around `6.25 pF` (`6.003 mV`) and fails by `7.5 pF` (`7.081 mV`). This closes the family gap left by the aperture sweep: fast reset margin is dominated by reservoir recharge/refresh and shifted-gate load, not just switch width or pulse duration.
- Added a combined fast-corner sweep for the recovered `1 us` split-reset reference cells. This keeps the same MOS startup TGs, passive local reservoirs, finite refresh paths, real complementary reset switches, and fresh shifted-gate load banks, but combines load capacitance, refresh-resistance, and reset-aperture stress. The result is stricter than the one-knob sweeps: `8 x 2 @ 3 kOhm` passes nominal (`3.76 mV` worst) and isolated `7.5 pF` load stress, but `7.5 pF + 1.25x` refresh resistance + `80 ns` reset reaches `6.61 mV` and fails the 6 mV gate. `16 x 1 @ 10 kOhm` is already load-limited at `7.5 pF` (`7.08 mV` on the positive branch), and the stronger `1.50x/80 ns` combined corner reaches `9.02 mV` worst case. Isolated fast-reset margins do not compose linearly; the component budget has to be set jointly across load, refresh impedance, partitioning, and reset pulse width.
- Tightened the fully-on-device MNIST result accounting after separating the behavioral 90% reference from the transistor objective. The phase-transient MNIST runner now explicitly reports `learning_device_implementation=behavioral_bsource`, `no_behavioral_learning_devices=false`, and `transistor_or_passive_learning_path=false`; the result summarizer no longer allows a full-objective milestone-D claim unless the run also has realistic stable sample order and transistor/passive learning devices with no behavioral learning math. The existing behavioral phase result remains useful as the phase-schedule/reference target, but cannot satisfy the transistor-level objective gate.
- Ran the first small repeated-sample transistor training smoke after fixing direct script execution for `run_device_sequential_training.py`. The deck uses one ngspice transient, persistent hidden/readout weight capacitors, MOS/passive forward/error/backward/update paths, and no behavioral signal math. The original direct analog-gradient NMOS pull-up exposed a rail-health failure: after a negative sample, the positive readout rail recovered only to about `0.12 V`, below the forward MOS threshold. Replacing readout potentiation with a passive/MOS restored write gate lets the readout signed weight swing back to a valid high rail (`low_then_high` final readout signed `+1.2 V`), but the full smoke still fails output recovery because stale negative readout feedback drives the hidden update negative and kills the hidden activation before the recovered readout can be used. This identifies the next transistor integration target: hidden credit must be gated/delayed/floored, or use a DFA/eligibility-style path, rather than blindly applying exact backprop through a stale wrong-sign readout.
- Added a first transistor-only DFA-style hidden-credit branch to the sequential training smoke. `direct_feedback` computes hidden delta with fixed MOS stacks from `dp/dn`, `act`, and `bwd`, while `exact_backprop` remains available as an explicit comparison mode that gates hidden credit through the current `vwp/vwn` readout rails. The default direct-feedback ngspice smoke now passes all four sequential polarity gates in one transient (`all_error_readout_weight_polarities_pass=true`, `all_output_polarities_pass=true`, `all_hidden_update_polarities_pass=true`), including the prior `low_then_high` recovery case (`hidden_delta_net=+0.150767`, `d_hidden_signed=+0.248223`, `d_out=+0.028043`). The exact-backprop comparison still fails the same case (`hidden_delta_net=-0.150768`, `d_hidden_signed=-0.423845`, output motion essentially zero), which makes stale readout-sign credit a concrete integration failure rather than a tuning guess. This is still a tiny binary smoke, not MNIST learning, but it moves the transistor/passive path toward a viable hidden credit contract without adding behavioral learning devices.
- Added `--assert-pass` to `run_device_sequential_training.py` so the transistor/passive repeated-sample smoke can be used as a promotion gate. The direct-feedback path exits cleanly with `all_device_training_smoke_polarities_pass=true`; the exact-backprop comparison exits nonzero with the expected hidden/output polarity failure. This turns the current Level-1 transistor training rung into an executable regression check for future MNIST-device integration.
- Added `run_device_mnist01_scalar_training.py`, the first real-MNIST data-stream integration runner around the transistor/passive sequential cell. It loads balanced MNIST `0/1` samples, maps each image to the single positive input rail available in the current device cell, runs one uninterrupted training transient with `batch_size=1`, and evaluates before/after with separate forward-only SPICE transients that disable `err/bwd/acc/apply` clocks rather than using a Python forward model. The generated train/eval decks are checked for no behavioral `B` sources and the summary reports the strict/no-checkpoint/no-Python-update contract fields. A `6` train / `6` eval ngspice smoke completed with transistor/passive state changes (`vwp-vwn` rails driven to the restored high state) but no nontrivial learning (`0.5 -> 0.5`, output-active fraction `0.0`). The failure is now localized to forward/output headroom and scalar-cell capacity on real MNIST inputs, not to Python orchestration: the next device MNIST rung needs either a bias/common-mode/headroom fix for eval-visible output, or a small multi-feature/tile cell rather than the one-scalar smoke.
- Promoted the output node from the nominal `NREL` source follower to an explicit low-threshold transistor sense follower option (`output_driver_model=sense`, still no behavioral sources). The headroom probe showed the output summing node was already alive on MNIST01 scalar eval (`score_before` about `52--120 mV` after training), but the nominal output follower suppressed it to displayed zero. Reusing the existing `NSENSE` model for only the output sense device made the same signal observable (`out_after` about `30--96 mV`). With a fixed `40 mV` decision threshold, the 6-train/6-eval MNIST01 scalar ngspice smoke now reports `initial_eval_accuracy=0.5`, `final_eval_accuracy=0.833333`, `final_eval_output_active_fraction=0.666667`, and `nontrivial_learning_met=true`, while preserving `batch_size=1`, one uninterrupted training transient, no Python weight updates/checkpoints, and transistor/passive learning devices. This is Milestone-B-style evidence only for the scalar MNIST01 smoke; the remaining objective gaps are still multiclass topology, real feature capacity, and scaling toward `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`.
- Added `run_device_mnist01_quad_training.py`, the first multi-feature transistor/passive MNIST data-stream runner. It instantiates four independent MOS hidden/update slices from quadrant mean-ink input rails, shares one output summing node and one `dp/dn` error rail, and still uses one uninterrupted `batch_size=1` training transient with no behavioral sources or Python weight updates. The first 4-train/4-eval run proved the deck topology and state movement but did not learn at the initial threshold. A readout-writer width sweep showed the scalar writer was too strong when four feature slices update in parallel; adding an explicit transistor width knob (`readout_apply_scale=0.5`) produced a 6-train/6-eval ngspice smoke with `initial_eval_accuracy=0.5`, `final_eval_accuracy=0.666667`, `final_eval_output_active_fraction=0.5`, and `nontrivial_learning_met=true` at a fixed `100 mV` decision threshold. This is still binary MNIST01 and a four-quadrant toy, but it is the next validated rung beyond the scalar smoke: multiple trainable device slices feeding a shared output/error path under the fully-on-device training contract.
- Generalized the MNIST01 multi-feature transistor/passive runner from a fixed 2x2 quadrant fixture into a configurable row-major grid of independent MOS hidden/update slices, still sharing the same output/error rails and still rejecting behavioral `B` sources. The old 2x2 path is preserved as the default and reproduced the ngspice smoke (`6` train / `6` eval, image `8`, `readout_apply_scale=0.5`) at `0.5 -> 0.666667` with `nontrivial_learning_met=true`. A bounded 3x3 smoke (`4` train / `4` eval, image `9`) also converged and improved `0.5 -> 0.75`, which is the first evidence that this transistor/passive MNIST01 runner is no longer structurally limited to four feature slices. The remaining objective gaps are unchanged: binary MNIST01, mean-ink grid features rather than `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, built-in LEVEL=1 MOS rather than a PDK, and no multiclass/full-MNIST result yet.
- Added `run_device_mnist01_block_training.py`, a transistor/passive block-stride-channel runner that replaces each scalar mean-ink feature with a local raw-pixel feature cell: every block/channel feature owns persistent signed per-pixel hidden weight capacitors, local MOS forward stacks from the raw pixel rails, MOS/passive direct-feedback hidden error storage, readout weight capacitors, and local per-pixel hidden writers. The structural regression emits the actual target topology shape (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2` = `16` blocks, `32` feature cells, `512` trainable hidden pixel-weight capacitors) with no behavioral `B` sources. A bounded real-MNIST ngspice smoke on the small `4x4 b2 stride2 c1` topology ran through the fully-on-device contract but did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5` at the fixed `100 mV` threshold): readout state moved, while held positive eval activations were mostly dead. Increasing hidden forward width to `12u` did not fix that, so the next block-cell circuit target is activation/headroom/feature diversity rather than threshold tuning. This is a topology-scaling rung, not a Milestone-B replacement for the earlier scalar/grid nontrivial-learning evidence.
- Added persistent signed hidden-bias state to the block-stride transistor/passive runner: each feature now has `bhp/bhn` capacitors, MOS bias contribution stacks into `pre`, and local MOS/passive bias writers driven by stored hidden error. This closes a structural gap versus the behavioral local-feature model, where a trainable hidden bias is part of the cell contract. The current `4x4 b2 stride2 c1` ngspice smoke still does not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`), but the failure mode is sharper: default bias gives nonzero but millivolt-scale positive-class activations, moderate bias (`bhp/bhn ~= 0.65/0.10`) remains stable but still random, and aggressive bias (`0.85/0.05`) drives the MOS forward stack into an ngspice timestep failure. This makes the next circuit target a bounded activation/headroom design, likely with a guarded store/restored-gate style activation path or better feature polarity diversity, not a Python-side threshold or scoring change. Also fixed the shared sample PWL source generator to avoid duplicate boundary time points, eliminating ngspice's non-increasing-PWL warnings in the current block smoke.
- Added explicit raw/complement input rail modes to the block-stride runner so multi-channel feature cells can use real supplied differential-like image data rather than only same-polarity raw pixels. In the target `c2` mapping, even channels read `x[p]` and odd channels read `nx[p]`; both are independent PWL input rails, not behavioral complement nodes. Full-swing complement rails made the `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` deck numerically unstable in ngspice at the MOS forward stacks, so the loader now bounds complemented input rails with `--complement-rail-scale` (default `0.5`). With that bound, the `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke is stable under the fully-on-device/no-`B` contract, but still does not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`), matching the stable raw-rail `c2` control. This rules out simple channel polarity diversity as the missing piece; the next block-cell rung needs a more robust activation/readout operating point or a characterized bounded activation store, not just more input rails.
- Added an explicit `--target-polarity` convention to the block-stride runner. `active-high` keeps the old target encoding (`positive_digit -> 1.1 V`), while `active-low` lets Python supply a low target rail for the semantic positive class and evaluates low output as positive; this is a label-voltage convention, not a Python weight update or hidden-state intervention. The active-low `4x4 b2 stride2 c1` smoke still stayed at chance (`0.5 -> 0.5`) and readout motion was tiny, so the block-cell failure is not only that raw-pixel features naturally fire more for digit `1` than digit `0`. The useful diagnosis is now narrower: the block-local hidden-error/readout update path is not producing enough aligned state motion for this topology, even when the task polarity is matched to the existing feature activity.
- Exposed explicit MOS sizing knobs for the block-cell update path: readout-gradient storage width, hidden-error storage width, hidden-update pretrace width, and hidden weight/bias write width. This keeps the experiment in the transistor/passive contract while making update-strength sweeps reproducible instead of hard-coded. A bounded strong-update `4x4 b2 stride2 c1` smoke (`readout_apply_scale=0.8`, `readout_gradient_width=48u`, `hidden_error_width=64u`, `hidden_update_width=24u`, `hidden_weight_write_width=0.5u`) remained stable and drove the first readout rail hard (`readout0_signed_after ~= 1.2 V`, bias0 signed moved by about `42 mV` on the first positive sample), but final eval accuracy was still `0.5 -> 0.5`. Final positive-class eval activations stayed at only `~6--21 mV` while the negative sample with active features produced the only measurable output (`~0.77 mV`, below threshold). This rules out merely undersized writer devices: the next block-cell target is the forward activation/readout operating point and feature selectivity, especially how stored weights/biases produce useful held activations on positive samples.
- Added explicit transistor sizing knobs for the block-cell hidden activation follower and readout-to-score stacks (`hidden_activation_width`, `readout_forward_width`) so the forward operating-point sweeps are reproducible instead of editing deck strings. The characterized wider hidden activation point (`96u`) is stable in integrated `4x4 b2 stride2` MNIST01 smokes, and it exposes the actual failure more clearly: held-out digit-1-like samples can produce `~0.16--0.24 V` feature activations but only `~50 mV` score / `~28 mV` sensed output, while many held-out digit-0 samples keep all useful features nearly off. Scaling the readout-to-score stack from `256u` to `1024u` barely changes that score, so the current limit is not simple readout device width. A `positive_digit=1` 8-sample smoke still stayed `0.5 -> 0.5` with very small weight motion. The next block-cell fix should target feature-cell selectivity/common-mode and the score/output sense topology, not just larger forward/output transistor widths or a lower decision threshold.
- Added a block-runner `phase_time_scale` knob that stretches every sample's reset/forward/error/backward/accumulate/apply windows and all measurement points together while keeping the single continuous transient and no-Python-update contract. This turns timing from a hard-coded assumption into an explicit transistor experiment variable. A `4x` timing smoke with `4x4 b2 stride2 c1`, `hidden_activation_width=96u` stayed stable and moved state much harder than the default timing (`vwp0 -> 1.2 V`, `vwn0 -> ~14 uV`, `bhn0` pulled down by about `89 mV`), but final accuracy remained `0.5 -> 0.5`. The same `4x` timing with `c2` also stayed stable but did not learn; final held-out positives still had no useful score, while a held-out digit-1 sample produced the largest output. Full-swing and `0.75x` complement rails still caused ngspice timestep failures in the forward MOS stack, while `0.6x` was stable but did not change the learning result. This rules out short phase windows as the sole blocker. Longer timing helps writes, but the block cell still needs a better feature/common-mode or score-sense topology to align updates with held-out positives.
- Added a selectable hidden activation transistor model to the block runner (`hidden_activation_model=nrel|sense`) so the ReLU-like activation follower can use the existing low-threshold `NSENSE` device without changing the no-`B` contract. With `hidden_activation_model=sense`, `hidden_activation_width=96u`, and `phase_time_scale=4`, the small `4x4 b2 stride2 c1` smoke raised baseline feature activations from millivolts into roughly `0.10--0.29 V` and produced much stronger first-sample readout/bias motion, but final accuracy still stayed `0.5 -> 0.5`: held-out digit-0 positives remained near-zero score while the strongest held-out digit-1 sample reached about `91 mV` score / `69 mV` output. The `c2` raw/complement version showed the same result: feature rails were healthier (`~0.11--0.29 V`), but final positives scored only `~8 mV` while the held-out digit-1 sample scored `~93 mV`. This rules out activation follower threshold/headroom as the sole blocker. The next real circuit fix needs feature polarity/selectivity that produces positive-class evidence, or a differential/contrastive score path that can learn absence-of-ink evidence instead of only amplifying ink-like features.
- Added `hidden_polarity_init=alternating-channel` for the block runner. This is not a Python update or behavioral helper: it only changes the initial capacitor voltages so every odd channel in a block starts with the hidden input weights swapped (`whp < whn`) while keeping a positive bias, making that channel an inhibitory/absence-style detector on the same MOS forward stack. A `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke with raw rails, alternating hidden polarity, `hidden_activation_model=sense`, and `phase_time_scale=4` stayed stable and preserved the no-`B` contract, but it still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`). The absence channels did respond (`~0.006--0.14 V` depending on sample and block), but the final held-out digit-0 positives scored only `~7--8 mV` while the strongest held-out digit-1 sample scored `~98 mV` / `~76 mV` output. This makes the failure sharper: simple paired ink/absence initialization is not enough. The next architectural step should make the score/error path differential or contrastive so negative evidence can actively train a class decision, instead of relying on one single-ended positive score node to discover absence evidence indirectly.
- Added a transistor/passive differential score path to the block-stride MNIST01 runner. `score_mode=differential` gives the negative readout branch its own `scoren` storage/reset rail, measures `score_net=score-scoren`, senses output with a MOS pull-up/pull-down pair, and feeds `scoren` into the output-error competition as the negative score contribution. The first implementation had an invalid negative readout stack; the corrected stack charges `scoren` from `vdd -> act -> vwn -> fwd -> scoren`, mirroring the positive `score` branch instead of leaving the first internal node dangling. The `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` raw/alternating-polarity/sense/phase4 ngspice smoke converged with no behavioral sources, but still stayed at `0.5 -> 0.5`: `score` and `scoren` separated electrically, yet later positive samples produced large error with weak useful local update coverage, and final positive eval samples had `score_net ~= 20 uV` while one negative sample reached `~51 mV`.
- Added `readout_forward_model=nrel|sense` so the block runner can test the readout-to-score transistor operating point without changing the learning contract. The low-threshold `NSENSE` scoring stack raised score/output common-mode (`score/scoren` around `120 mV` on low-net samples instead of `22 mV`) but did not fix learning in the same bounded smoke: final accuracy remained `0.5 -> 0.5`, positives still had `score_net ~= 20 uV`, and the largest negative sample had `score_net ~= 53 mV`. This rules out a plain readout-threshold/headroom fix for the current block cell. The next target is feature/update alignment: the circuit needs a local mechanism that keeps useful eligibility/pretrace coverage when target error arrives, or a more contrastive feature cell whose positive-class evidence is not silent on the held-out positives.
- Added `learning_activation_gate_model=nrel|sense` plus passive `gvp/gvn` measurements for the block runner's act-gated learning branches. This covers both hidden-error gating (`hdp/hdn <- act * dp/dn`) and readout-gradient storage (`gvp/gvn <- act * dp/dn`) with the same transistor-model choice, avoiding a one-off readout-only knob. In the same `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` differential/readout-sense smoke, the low-threshold learning gates did activate the previously weak pretraces: sample-2 positive error produced `gvp` values around `0.04--0.13 V`, `gvn ~= 0`, and several readout rails saturated upward. The result still failed (`0.5 -> 0.25`) because the stronger update generalized in the wrong direction: final positives stayed at `score_net ~= 39 mV`, while one negative reached `~177 mV`. The block-cell problem is therefore no longer just threshold-starved learning gates; it needs selectivity/normalization/bounded writes or a more contrastive feature cell so positive-sample updates do not simply amplify ink-like negative evidence.
- Added an optional passive readout-weight leak to the block runner: `readout_weight_leak_resistance > 0` connects every `vwp/vwn` capacitor to fixed analog reference rails through resistors, giving the restored MOS writer a hardware regularizer instead of letting readout state stick at the supplies. With `readout_apply_scale=0.05`, differential scoring, readout/learning sense gates, and a `5 MOhm` leak, the same `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke stayed no-`B` and moved readout rails reversibly rather than rail-sticking (`vwp` ended around `0.87 V`, `vwn` around `0.12--0.25 V`). The negative held-out score was reduced from the prior `~177 mV` to `~61 mV`, but final accuracy only recovered to chance (`0.5 -> 0.5`) because positive held-out scores remained near zero. This stabilizes the readout state but does not solve feature selectivity; the next circuit target should be competitive/contrastive feature evidence or a normalizer that suppresses broad ink-like updates while preserving positive-class evidence.
- Added `hidden_polarity_init=random-pixel` for the block runner. This is still just initial capacitor state, not a Python update: each feature starts with a mix of positive and negative per-pixel hidden weights, forcing the local block cells to behave like random contrastive stroke filters instead of duplicated all-ink/all-absence detectors. Structural tests now require every random-pixel feature to contain both polarities. In bounded no-`B` smokes with differential score, sense readout/learning gates, `5 MOhm` readout leak, and `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, random-pixel features were stable but did not learn. With digit `0` positive, final eval stayed `0.5 -> 0.5`; held-out positives had `score_net ~= 0--5 mV`, while one held-out negative still reached `~38 mV`. Reversing the binary task (`1` positive, `0` negative) also stayed at chance. The failure is therefore not only channel polarity or the positive-label convention: the block path needs an in-circuit competitive/normalizing mechanism, or a richer feature topology, so class evidence is not dominated by a few broadly active stroke/ink features.
- Added an optional transistor/passive global activation-competition loop to the block runner. `activation_competition_width > 0` creates a shared `actinh` capacitor, charges it from active features during `fwd`, and uses it to pull down each `act` rail through MOS stacks; no behavioral source or Python-side normalization is involved. A bounded `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke with random-pixel features, differential score, sense readout/learning gates, `5 MOhm` readout leak, and `2u` competition converged under the no-`B` contract. The loop was electrically active: `actinh` rose to `~0.23--0.25 V` on broad/high-activation samples versus `~0.108 V` on ordinary samples, activation sums dropped on the highest positive sample (`~0.858 -> ~0.727` compared with the prior random-pixel smoke), and the problematic held-out negative score dropped from `~38 mV` to `~14 mV`. Accuracy still stayed at chance because held-out positives remained near-zero score. This makes activation competition a useful suppressor/normalizer, but not the missing class-evidence mechanism; the next target should create positive evidence on held-out positives, likely by changing the feature topology or adding a trainable/competitive hidden bias/readout objective rather than only suppressing broad negatives.
- Added an optional trainable signed output-bias circuit to the block runner. `--output-bias` creates persistent `obp/obn` capacitors, MOS score/scoren forward branches, error-driven `gop/gon` pretrace capacitors, and restored MOS/passive writers. The first attempt exposed two real integration bugs: bias updates are not activation-gated and therefore need their own `output_bias_apply_scale`, and output bias must not inherit the readout-weight leak references because that passively drives a neutral bias positive during eval. After adding a separate neutral `output_bias_leak_resistance` with independent refs and preserving the measured `obp/obn` state into final eval, the bounded `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke (`random-pixel`, differential score, sense readout/learning gates, `2u` activation competition, `5 MOhm` readout leak, neutral `5 MOhm` bias leak, `0.1x` bias writer) reached `0.5 -> 1.0` on four held-out MNIST01 samples with no behavioral sources. Final eval outputs were `~0.151/0.109 V` for the two positive digit-0 samples and `~0.088/0.077 V` for the two negative digit-1 samples at the fixed `0.1 V` threshold. This is only a tiny binary smoke, but it is the first block-stride raw-pixel transistor/passive rung showing nontrivial learning under the fully-on-device contract.
- Checked the same output-bias block configuration on a slightly larger `6` train / `6` eval MNIST01 slice. The strict no-`B` contract still passed, but learning did not generalize (`0.5 -> 0.5`, final active fraction `0.667`). Row-level diagnostics show the result is dominated by output-bias state rather than robust image evidence: a positive training sample drove `obp-obn` to `~0.85 V`, the final trained bias ended at `~0.67 V`, and final eval then depended strongly on passive bias decay order. Lowering `output_bias_apply_scale` from `0.1` to `0.03` was not a clean fix; that diagnostic hit an ngspice timestep failure at `on0_0` during training. The next block-cell target should therefore treat output bias as calibration only and improve input-dependent feature/readout evidence, rather than spending more effort tuning a sequence-prior-like bias memory.
- Added output-bias drift diagnostics to the block summary so future smokes report when a nominal learning result is mostly persistent bias state. Summaries now include final train `obp-obn`, first/last/max final-eval bias, eval drift, threshold ratio, and `output_bias_state_drift_warning` when the trained bias is larger than the decision threshold and its passive eval drift is also threshold-scale. This does not change circuit behavior; it makes the objective audit stricter by separating real input-dependent evidence from bias-memory/order effects.
- Added an optional MOS input-residual activation path to the block runner. `hidden_input_residual_width > 0` emits weak per-pixel `x -> fwd -> act` transistor stacks, giving each feature an input-dependent leaky eligibility branch when the learned signed preactivation path is silent; no behavioral source or Python-side state access is introduced. A bounded no-output-bias `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke with `0.25u` residual devices stayed stable and strict no-`B`, and activation coverage improved materially (`act` sums around `0.8--1.2 V` instead of near-dead held positives). It still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`, final active fraction `0.0`): the largest held-out score remained on a digit-1 negative sample (`score_net ~= 41 mV`) while held-out digit-0 positives stayed near zero net score. This rules out dead eligibility alone; the next block-cell circuit target should add contrastive/normalizing readout evidence that penalizes broad ink-like activation without relying on output-bias memory.
- Added an optional transistor score-inhibition branch driven by the existing activation-competition rail. `score_activity_inhibition_width > 0` requires `activation_competition_width > 0`; in differential mode it emits an `actinh -> fwd -> scoren` MOS stack, so broad/common-mode activation contributes negative score without behavioral arithmetic. A bounded no-output-bias `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke with residual activation and `4u` score inhibition stayed stable and strict no-`B`, and it reduced the problematic held-out digit-1 net score from about `41 mV` to `28 mV`. It still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`, final active fraction `0.0`) because the held-out digit-0 positives remained near-zero net score. This is useful but insufficient normalization; the next path likely needs a feature/readout architecture that creates positive evidence for digit-0-like strokes, not only suppresses broad digit-1 ink evidence.
- Tested the residual/score-inhibition block path with alternating raw/complement rails to give odd channels absence evidence. The low-threshold learning-gate variant failed numerically at `mgvp1_d`, while the nominal-threshold learning-gate variant completed under the strict no-`B` contract. Complement rails improved the internal differential score ordering compared with raw-only residual runs: final held-out digit-0 positives reached `score_net ~= 14/13 mV` versus digit-1 negatives at `~12/5 mV`, but the transistor `out` node stayed common-mode dominated and accuracy remained `0.5 -> 0.5`. Added explicit `output_score_pullup_width` / `output_scoren_pulldown_width` knobs and score-net class-margin diagnostics so future runs report both circuit output accuracy and the underlying measured differential score margin. A stronger `48u/96u` output sensor still did not classify; it lowered common-mode output but left the highest-output held-out sample on a digit-1 negative. The next circuit target is therefore a better differential output/readout normalization or stronger feature selectivity, not Python-side score interpretation.
- Added an opt-in score-gated differential output stage: `output_differential_stage=score-gated` gates the pull-up path with a PMOS controlled by `scoren` before the `score`-controlled NMOS, while keeping the `scoren` pull-down transistor path. This is still transistor-only and leaves the learning path unchanged. On the same alternating-complement residual/score-inhibition smoke, the stage ran cleanly under the strict no-`B` contract and preserved the learned internal score margin (`final_eval_score_net_margin ~= +5.35 mV`), but the output remained inverted at the class-mean level (`final out_after` mean `~37.4 mV` for digit-0 positives versus `~39.6 mV` for digit-1 negatives). The useful landed diagnostic improvement is that summaries now report both score-net margins and output-node margins, making this internal/output mismatch visible without manual CSV inspection. The next circuit target should be a real differential comparator/latch or a readout normalization stage that converts small score margins into a rail-level decision.
- Added an opt-in dynamic MOS differential output latch (`output_differential_stage=latched`). The latch adds a complementary `outn` node, cross-coupled PMOS pull-ups, and a `score/scoren` NMOS differential discharge pair gated by `fwd`; no behavioral source or Python-side score computation is used. On the alternating-complement residual/score-inhibition block smoke, the latch converted the previously small internal score margin into rail-level output separation after training: final digit-0 positives reached `out ~= 1.094--1.096 V`, digit-1 negatives reached `~1.075/1.085 V`, and `out_diff` class margin was `~17.7 mV`. With the old `0.1 V` output threshold all final samples were active and accuracy remained `0.5 -> 0.5`; with a fixed high latch-output threshold of `1.09 V`, the same strict no-`B` circuit reached `0.5 -> 1.0` on the bounded 4-train/4-eval MNIST01 slice. This is the first no-output-bias block-stride raw-pixel transistor/passive smoke to show nontrivial learning, but it is still an operating-point result on a tiny binary slice; the threshold must be pre-registered/validated across larger slices, and the topology is still below `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`.
- Reused the same latched-output operating point on a slightly larger 6-train/6-eval MNIST01 slice with the preselected `1.09 V` threshold. The strict no-`B` contract still passed, and final accuracy improved from `0.5` to `0.667` with no output-bias path enabled. The class margins stayed in the right direction (`final_eval_score_net_margin ~= +8.46 mV`, `final_eval_out_after_margin ~= +18.0 mV`, `final_eval_out_diff_margin ~= +21.1 mV`), but the threshold was not robust enough: one held-out negative latched high at `~1.099 V`, one sat barely above threshold at `~1.0901 V`, and one held-out positive was only `~1.092 V`. This supports the latch as the right output-conversion family, but scaling now needs either a larger latch margin or a calibrated transistor reference/comparator, not post-hoc Python thresholding.
- Added explicit passive readout-stack parasitics and opt-in MOS decision latches to the block runner. A uniform `1 TOhm` shunt on readout stack internal nodes did not stabilize the problematic Level-1 MOS stack and could trigger earlier timestep failures, so it remains an opt-in diagnostic. A tiny `0.1 fF` passive capacitor on each `op*/on*` internal readout-stack node fixed the ngspice timestep failure seen in the 6-train/6-eval latched-output smoke while preserving the no-`B` contract and the previous learning result: with no output bias and the preselected `1.09 V` `out_after` threshold, the run completed at `0.5 -> 0.667`, with `final_eval_score_net_margin ~= +9.14 mV`, `final_eval_out_after_margin ~= +18.37 mV`, and `final_eval_out_diff_margin ~= +21.61 mV`. The decision-latch family-completion was less successful: a fixed-reference latch (`ref-latched`) stayed weak or flipped whole-run common mode depending on sizing, and an `out/outn` differential latch (`diff-latched`) railed high because `out > outn` for both classes in the current operating point. This means the stable landed progress is physical readout-stack stabilization; the circuit still lacks a robust transistor decision/reference stage and still relies on a fixed Python-read threshold for accuracy reporting.
- Added a late `dec` clock and two additional transistor decision-stage experiments, then used the failures to redirect toward topology scaling. Clocking the reference latch after the output latch settles removed the obvious race, but the dynamic reference latch still perturbed the high-impedance output operating point.
