A static ratioed inverter was nonintrusive but its Level-1 CMOS trip could not be pushed near the required `~1.09 V` latch threshold. A source-follower shifted inverter had the right isolated DC trip region (`~1.0--1.1 V` with `32u/4u` inverter sizing), but in the integrated high-gain latch deck it again forced the output latch into the low state. Conclusion: the current `out` latch is too sensitive to hang a decision circuit directly on it; the next robust decision path needs an explicit buffer/preamp or should be deferred while improving score/output margin.
- Added an opt-in passive hidden-forward-stack parasitic capacitance, matching the readout-stack stabilization pattern. The first `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` target-topology probe without it failed at the hidden negative stack node `hn9_9_0` around `76 ns`. Adding `0.1 fF` to every `hp*/hn*` internal hidden forward-stack source/drain node let the strict no-`B` target-topology smoke complete for `2` train / `2` eval samples with `hidden_stack_parasitic_capacitance=1e-16` and `readout_stack_parasitic_capacitance=1e-16`. Runtime was high (`~2038 s` wall time), but the topology flag was true and the deck preserved the no-Python-update/no-behavioral-learning contract. Learning was not present yet (`0.5 -> 0.5`), although final output margin moved in the right direction (`final_eval_out_after_margin ~= +168.9 mV`, `final_eval_out_diff_margin ~= +191.4 mV`) while the final score-net margin remained negative (`~-21.8 mV`). This closes the first numerical scaling blocker for Milestone C; the next scaling work needs a faster preflight mode and better target-topology learning alignment.
- Added an opt-in continuous final-eval path for the transistor/passive block MNIST01 runner. `--continuous-final-eval` now emits one train+final-eval deck and one ngspice transient, with per-sample phase scheduling that leaves `fwd`/`dec` active for every sample but disables `err`/`bwd`/`acc`/`apply` on the final-eval tail. This removes the previous Python final-weight extraction/reseed step from that mode (`python_weight_transfer_to_final_eval=false`, `final_weights_used_to_seed_eval=false`) while keeping passive diagnostics. `--skip-initial-eval` can omit the separate baseline deck for faster convergence/preflight checks. A 2-train/2-eval 4x4 smoke completed in ngspice with no behavioral sources, strict contract true for the train+eval transient, and expected no-learning result (`final_eval_accuracy=0.5`); generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Added `--measurement-detail outputs` for continuous transistor/passive block runs. The mode keeps the same circuit, input rails, phase clocks, state capacitors, and MOS write devices, but records only score/output/decision measurements needed for final accuracy instead of every hidden/readout/weight capacitor. It is rejected unless `--continuous-final-eval` is enabled, because compact diagnostics intentionally do not expose measured final weights for Python to reseed a separate eval deck. On a generated `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` target-topology single-sample deck, passive `.meas` statements fell from `1741` to `13` with no behavioral sources. A 2-train/2-eval 4x4 ngspice smoke completed in compact mode with strict contract true, `python_weight_transfer_to_final_eval=false`, `final_weights=null`, and expected no-learning accuracy (`0.5`); generated smoke artifacts were removed.
- Ran the first compact continuous target-topology ngspice probe: `1` train sample followed by `1` final-eval sample in the same `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` transient, with hidden/readout `0.1 fF` stack parasitics, latched differential output, no behavioral sources, no Python final-weight transfer, and output-only diagnostics. It completed in `157.1 s` wall time with strict contract true. The sole eval sample was a negative digit and stayed below threshold (`final_eval_output_active_fraction=0.0`), so the resulting `final_eval_accuracy=1.0` is only a convergence/contract smoke, not learning evidence. This exposed and fixed a reporting bug: when initial eval is skipped, `nontrivial_learning_met` must remain false rather than treating a one-sample final eval above the `0.5` fallback as proof of learning.
- Rechecked the small nontrivial-learning rung under the stronger continuous final-eval contract. The original `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` reconstruction with ink-polarity features and the random-pixel/sense variant both failed (`0.5 -> 0.5`), showing that the old latched-output result depended on the full operating point rather than just the latch. Restoring the useful random-pixel/sense feature family, `5 MOhm` readout leak, wider `48u/96u` output latch devices, `0.25u` input residuals, `2u` activation competition, `4u` score inhibition, and `0.1 fF` readout-stack caps produced positive class margins in one train+final-eval transient with no Python final-weight transfer. With the latch read threshold set to `1.11 V` for this operating point, the strict continuous run reached `0.5 -> 0.75` on the 4-train/4-eval MNIST01 slice (`final_eval_score_net_margin ~= 1.08 mV`, `final_eval_out_after_margin ~= 9.35 mV`, `final_eval_out_diff_margin ~= 11.04 mV`, wall time `34.2 s`). This restores Milestone-B-style evidence for the transistor/passive block path, but it is still tiny binary MNIST01 below the `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` target and still uses a Python-read voltage threshold rather than a robust transistor decision/reference stage.
- Extended the same strict continuous operating point to `6` train / `6` eval samples. The fixed `1.11 V` output-read threshold failed (`0.5 -> 0.5`) because all final outputs landed above threshold, but the final output values were still separable: positives were `1.1300--1.1335 V`, negatives were `1.1228--1.1286 V`, and a threshold near `1.13 V` would classify the slice perfectly. This is not acceptable as headline learning because it is a post-hoc Python threshold, but it is useful circuit evidence: the latch/readout produces separable analog state while the missing block is a robust transistor reference/decision stage. Added passive threshold-window diagnostics to the block summary so future runs report best separability for `out_after`, `out_diff`, `decision_after`, and `decision_diff` without altering the circuit. Two existing transistor decision attempts were rechecked on the same operating point: `ref-latched` at `1.13 V` preserved the output margins but produced only a weak `~0.167 V` decision rail with sub-millivolt separation, while `shift-inverter` again collapsed the output latch low. The next decision circuit should therefore use an explicit high-impedance preamp/level-shift or a calibrated reference path, not a direct latch or the current source-follower inverter hung on `out`.
- Added three transistor/passive decision-stage candidates and an explicit output-latch storage-capacitance knob to turn the previous post-hoc output threshold into a real circuit readout. A diode-load `ref-preamp-latched` stage preserved the learned `out` margin but only produced about `0.3 mV` of decision separation; a `stacked-inverter` stage did not work because the latch output is already near `VDD`, leaving the PMOS side effectively off and classifying every sample high. A dynamic `ref-precharged-latched` stage initially collapsed the high-impedance output latch through read disturbance, even with weak input devices. Increasing the physical output storage caps from `20 fF` to `200 fF` fixed that disturbance: on the strict continuous `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` MNIST01 smoke with one train+final-eval transient, no behavioral sources, no Python final-weight transfer, and no hidden-state intervention, the precharged reference latch reached `0.5 -> 1.0` accuracy with `nontrivial_learning_met=true`. Final margins were `score_net ~= +1.08 mV`, `out_after ~= +9.35 mV`, `out_diff ~= +11.05 mV`, `decision_after ~= +537 mV`, and `decision_diff ~= +1.077 V`. This is still a tiny binary slice below the `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` target, but it removes the immediate Python-read voltage-threshold dependency for the current small rung and identifies output read-disturb capacitance as a real circuit sizing condition.
- Re-ran the previously problematic strict continuous `6` train / `6` eval small slice with the same `200 fF` output storage and `ref-precharged-latched` hardware decision path, using a fixed `1.13 V` reference instead of a Python-read output threshold. The run completed with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, `python_weight_transfer_to_final_eval=false`, `initial_eval_accuracy=0.5`, `final_eval_accuracy=0.8333`, and `nontrivial_learning_met=true`. The output margins were still modest (`out_after ~= +5.95 mV`, `out_diff ~= +7.05 mV`), but the transistor decision stage amplified them to `decision_after ~= +402 mV` and `decision_diff ~= +874 mV`. One held-out sample still misses at the fixed decision threshold, and the score-net margin is negative on this slice, so the next target is not more threshold interpretation; it is stronger score/readout alignment and checking whether this larger output storage cost remains acceptable on the `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` target topology.
- Checked the same `200 fF` output storage and `ref-precharged-latched` decision path on the actual `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` transistor/passive block topology. A `1` train / `1` eval compact continuous smoke completed in `174.3 s` with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, `target_10x10_b4_stride2_c2_topology=true`, no behavioral sources, and no Python final-weight transfer; the single eval sample was only a convergence/contract check. A balanced `2` train / `2` eval target smoke with the small-topology `1.13 V` decision reference also completed (`373.2 s`) and showed strong analog separation (`out_after ~= +167 mV`, `out_diff ~= +189 mV`), but both decision rails stayed below the fixed `0.6 V` read threshold (`final_eval_accuracy=0.5`, `decision_after_margin ~= +93 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~= +170 mV`). Lowering only the transistor reference to `0.95 V` on the same target smoke preserved the strict contract and produced a valid hardware decision (`final_eval_accuracy=1.0`, `decision_after_margin ~= +785 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~= +1.556 V`, wall `362.1 s`). This proves the hardware decision stage scales electrically, but also exposes the next real circuit problem: the decision reference cannot be a hand-picked per-topology Python constant in the final objective. We need an on-circuit/reference-generation or calibration strategy and better score alignment before claiming learning on target topology.
- Replaced the external `Voutref`-only decision-reference path with an opt-in passive resistor-divider source (`--output-decision-ref-source divider`). The emitted deck now uses only `Routref_top`, `Routref_bot`, and a tiny `Coutref` for that mode; no behavioral source or Python-updated state is involved. The same balanced `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, `2` train / `2` eval target smoke with `0.95 V` generated by a `1.2 MOhm` divider matched the previous voltage-source result: `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, `final_eval_accuracy=1.0`, `out_after_margin ~= +167 mV`, `decision_after_margin ~= +785 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~= +1.556 V`, and `379.3 s` wall time. This does not solve adaptive calibration, but it removes the external ideal voltage reference from the successful target-topology decision probe and gives the next reference-centering work a real passive primitive to replace with trim, averaging, or learned reference state.
- Added an opt-in adaptive decision-reference state (`--output-decision-ref-source adaptive`) for the `ref-*` decision stages. The reference is now a physical `Coutref` capacitor initialized by IC, optionally anchored by the same passive divider, and updated only by MOS/passive devices during `apply`: `dp` discharges/lowers `outref`, while `dn` pulls down a PMOS gate and charges/raises `outref`. There is no `Voutref` source, no behavioral element, and no Python update. Unit tests now cover the emitted capacitor/divider/writer devices and the focused block suite passes (`191 passed`). A strict continuous 4x4 `2` train / `2` eval smoke showed the intended sign alignment (`dn>dp` negative sample raised `outref` by `~198 mV`; `dp>dn` positive sample lowered it by `~68 mV`) but did not classify the slice because the learned output margin itself was inverted and both final eval decisions landed high. A conservative `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` `1` train / `1` eval adaptive-reference probe also converged with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, no behavioral sources, and topology flag true (`188.3 s` wall). This is reference-state infrastructure, not a learning claim: the writer needs better gain/centering control and the target topology still needs stronger score/readout alignment before the adaptive reference can replace hand-picked reference points.
- Added an opt-in readout-weighted hidden-credit mode for the transistor/passive block runner (`--hidden-credit-mode readout-weighted`). The new hidden backward branch implements signed transport with four MOS stacks per feature: positive hidden credit gets `dp*vwp + dn*vwn`, negative hidden credit gets `dp*vwn + dn*vwp`, with activation and `bwd` phase gating in each stack. This keeps the old direct-feedback path as the default and requires differential score mode. Focused regressions pass (`193 passed`). Strict continuous hardware-decision smokes at 4x4 still converge under the no-`B`/no-Python-update contract: `4` train / `4` eval remains `0.5 -> 1.0`, and the harder `6` train / `6` eval remains `0.5 -> 0.8333`. A full-diagnostic 1-sample comparison confirmed the new branch is electrically active (`hdp/hdn` changed, e.g. feature 1 direct `~24/31 mV` versus weighted `~31/31 mV`), but raw `vwp/vwn` gate voltages are both high enough that the first weighted version mostly equalizes hidden credit instead of strongly discriminating signed readout influence. The family-completion takeaway is that exact signed hidden feedback needs centered/restored readout-weight gates or a small transistor preamp/comparator per feature; raw capacitor voltages alone are not a high-yield scaling fix.
- Added the family-completion variant of the hidden-credit path: `--hidden-credit-mode readout-restored`. Each feature now has a dynamic transistor latch that compares `vwp`/`vwn` during the `err` phase, stores restored feedback rails on `rvwp`/`rvwn` capacitors, and then uses those rails in the existing four-quadrant hidden-credit MOS stacks during `bwd`. Full-detail runs now measure `rvwp/rvwn` after the error phase. Unit/focused regressions pass (`194 passed`). A 1-sample diagnostic showed the restore latch is active (`rvwp ~= 1.20 V`, `rvwn ~= 0.20--0.24 V` for initially positive readout weights), but hidden credit remained nearly balanced because `dp` and `dn` are both nonzero on that sample; restored weight sign alone is not enough without a sharper/restored error rail. Strict continuous smokes preserved the existing learning rungs: 4x4 `4` train / `4` eval stayed `0.5 -> 1.0`, 4x4 `6` train / `6` eval stayed `0.5 -> 0.8333` with a slightly larger decision-diff margin, and a `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` `1` train / `1` eval target-topology convergence probe completed with strict contract true in `189.3 s`. This lands a real transistor feedback-restoration primitive but points the next alignment work at error-rail sharpening/competition rather than more raw readout-weight gating.
- Added restored output-error rails to the transistor/passive MNIST01 block runner. The new latch precharges `edp/edn`, compares raw `dp/dn` during `err`, and stores full-swing signed learning rails without `B` sources or Python state intervention. A 4x4 1-train/1-eval strict probe showed the intended sharpening: raw `dp-dn = -7.18 mV` became restored `edp-edn = -1.188 V`. Routing those restored rails into every consumer (`--error-signal-mode restored`) was electrically valid but too blunt for the current readout writer: the 4-train/4-eval slice stayed analog-separable but missed the fixed decision threshold (`0.5 -> 0.5`, best-threshold diagnostics `1.0`). This says full-swing restoration is useful for sign transport, but using it as the readout-gradient magnitude loses proportional error information.
- Added `--error-signal-mode restored-hidden` as the higher-yield split: hidden/backward credit uses restored `edp/edn`, while readout gradients, output bias, and adaptive decision-reference writers keep proportional raw `dp/dn`. Focused regressions pass (`196 passed`). Strict ngspice smokes with `readout-restored` feedback and `restored-hidden` error preserve the existing small learning rungs under the no-behavioral/no-Python-update contract: 4x4 b2 stride2 c2, 4 train / 4 eval improved `0.5 -> 1.0` with final decision margin `~0.537 V` and decision-diff margin `~1.077 V`; 6 train / 6 eval improved `0.5 -> 0.8333` with decision margin `~0.403 V` and decision-diff margin `~0.875 V`.
- Checked target-topology convergence with the restored-hidden error path. The direct `hidden_error_width=32u` 10x10 b4 stride2 c2 1-train/1-eval probe failed in ngspice with timestep-too-small near a hidden forward NMOS late in the first training cycle, consistent with restored full-swing hidden credit overdriving the larger array. Reducing only `hidden_error_width` to `8u` let the same target-topology strict continuous probe converge (`strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, `target_10x10_b4_stride2_c2_topology=true`, no behavioral learning devices, no Python final-weight transfer, wall time `213.3 s`). This is a convergence/contract smoke, not learning evidence; the scaling rule is that restored hidden-error sign rails need an explicit gain/sizing knob before larger-topology training runs.
- Added opt-in passive hidden-state and hidden-forward-stack stabilization knobs for the target-topology restored-hidden path: `--hidden-weight-leak-resistance` leaks hidden pixel weights and hidden biases toward explicit reference rails, and `--hidden-stack-shunt-resistance` gives each hidden forward-stack internal source/drain node a weak DC path. These mirror the existing readout leak/shunt/cap characterization style and keep the deck transistor/passive-only. Focused regressions pass (`198 passed`). The first target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 2-train/2-eval restored-hidden smoke without extra stabilization failed after the first update at `~19.03 ns` in a hidden negative forward stack; the same run with `2 MOhm` hidden weight/bias leak failed even in the initial-eval deck at an internal positive-stack node, so strong persistent-state leakage is not a safe default. A `0.1 fF` hidden stack cap and a `1 TOhm` hidden stack shunt each avoided the old fast timestep failure, but both train/final-eval probes timed out (`600 s` and `420 s` respectively) before returning accuracy. The immediate target-topology blocker is now explicit: passive conditioning can move the failure away from singular internal stack nodes, but the current MOS-stack topology becomes too slow for multi-sample target runs unless the forward stack is simplified, better bounded, or simulated with a cheaper physical parasitic recipe.
- Tested whether the target-topology restored-hidden multi-sample problem is primarily hidden-credit overdrive. Reducing only `hidden_error_width` from `8u` to `2u` on the `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` continuous target smoke changed the failure mode in the same useful direction as stack shunts/caps: both 2-train/2-eval and 2-train/1-eval probes passed the old fast second-forward timestep crash and then timed out at `420 s`. This says smaller restored hidden-credit gain improves numerical/physical stability after the first update, but it does not by itself make target multi-sample ngspice runs tractable. The next circuit-level candidate should reduce the number/depth of floating stacked devices in the hidden forward path, rather than adding more weak passive conditioning around the same topology.
- Added an opt-in shared-phase hidden-forward topology for the transistor/passive block runner (`--hidden-forward-topology shared-phase`). The default keeps the old per-pixel phase stack, while the new path shares one positive and one negative `fwd` phase device per feature, leaves only one per-pixel positive/negative internal stack node, and preserves signed positive/negative preactivation transport; hidden-stack shunts/caps now attach to the topology-specific internal nodes. Focused regressions pass (`201 passed`). The revised shared-phase cell preserved the strict small learning rung: `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4 train / 4 eval, `readout-restored` feedback, `restored-hidden` error, hardware reference latch, no behavioral learning devices, no Python final-weight transfer, improved `0.5 -> 1.0` with final decision margin `~0.537 V` and wall time `131.7 s`. It did not solve the target runtime blocker: the compact `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` 1-train/1-eval target probe with `hidden_error_width=8u`, output-only diagnostics, and the same strict contract timed out at `360 s` rather than completing near the old per-pixel 1/1 convergence rung. This is useful negative evidence: sharing the phase devices preserves behavior but is not enough to make target-topology multi-sample ngspice runs tractable. The next scaling candidate should reduce the analog forward graph more substantially, for example by moving from per-pixel series stacks to a current-summed feature/tile primitive or adding a real bounded transconductance/comparator cell that avoids large high-impedance stack forests.
- Added a more aggressive opt-in hidden-forward topology (`--hidden-forward-topology always-on`) that removes the per-pixel `fwd` device entirely. Each signed pixel contribution is now a two-device `input * stored-weight` MOS stack into `pre`, with only one positive and one negative internal stack node per pixel; the feature bias is also direct instead of phase-gated. This is a deliberate physical approximation to test whether the target bottleneck is primarily phase-gated stack depth. Focused regressions pass (`203 passed`). The tiny strict MNIST01 rung still works: `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4 train / 4 eval, no behavioral learning devices, no Python final-weight transfer, improved `0.5 -> 1.0` with final score-net margin `~3.35 mV`, final decision margin `~0.537 V`, and wall time `113.1 s`. The target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` compact 1-train/1-eval probe with `hidden_error_width=8u`, output-only diagnostics, and the same strict contract still timed out at `360 s`. Conclusion: the target runtime problem is not fixed by deleting one phase device per pixel; the array-scale forward/update graph and many always-active high-impedance stack nodes remain too expensive for ngspice. The next useful topology should collapse pixel contributions more radically, e.g. a current-mode summed feature primitive or a tiled crossbar-style transconductor where the simulator sees fewer floating internal nodes per pixel.
- Added a runnable standalone ngspice template for the shared-phase local feature cell (`spice/templates/shared_phase_local_feature_cell_smoke.cir`) after the earlier pasted fragment proved incomplete. The template includes a title line, concrete LEVEL=1 MOS model aliases, voltage/phase/error sources, state capacitors, high-value leak resistors for internal nodes, and concise `.meas` outputs for forward activation, differential score, restored feedback, and hidden-error transport. It avoids behavioral sources and runs directly with `ngspice -b`; the measured smoke values include `pre_forward ~= 312 mV`, `act_forward ~= 180 mV`, `score_forward ~= 145 mV`, restored feedback separation `rvwp/rvwn ~= 1.13/0.027 V`, and `hdp_after_bwd ~= 56 mV`. Added a regression test that runs this exact template when ngspice is available, preventing future non-runnable circuit snippets from drifting into documentation/examples.
- Added a second standalone shared-phase cell template that preserves the longer fragment's original model names (`NMOS`, `PMOS`, `NSENSE`, `NREL`) and includes both positive and negative hidden-error transport rails. The failure mode in the pasted netlist was reproducibility/harness related rather than a parser mystery: the fragment omitted `.model` cards, supplies, phase/error sources, score/state storage, and DC shunts for floating stack nodes; additionally, comments copied with leading indentation can be misread by stricter SPICE parsers because `*` comments are safest in column 1. The new `spice/templates/shared_phase_local_feature_cell_full_smoke.cir` runs directly with `ngspice -b`, reports `pre_forward ~= 312 mV`, `act_forward ~= 181 mV`, `score/scoren ~= 146 mV`, restored feedback `rvwp/rvwn ~= 1.13/0.027 V`, and hidden-error rails `hdp/hdn ~= 57 mV` in this symmetric one-pixel smoke. Added a regression test so both the concise and full templates are executed under ngspice.
- Added an aggressive `--hidden-forward-topology split-rail` option to the block runner. It replaces each per-pixel hidden forward stack with a direct positive branch into `pre` and a direct negative branch into a separate `pren` rail, then lets the activation follower pull up from `pre` and pull down through `pren`; this removes all per-pixel hidden forward stack conditioning nodes while preserving signed preactivation transport. Focused regressions pass. A strict 4x4 b2 stride2 c2 smoke ran much faster than the previous small shared/always-on probes (`~42.7 s`) and preserved the fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract, but it did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`): the analog score/output margins were positive while the hardware decision stage saturated high for all final eval samples. Treat this as scaling/topology evidence, not as a replacement for the current small nontrivial-learning rung.
- Started moving the raw transistor MNIST block deck toward simulator-native hierarchy instead of flat generated element forests. The runner now emits a reusable `.subckt signed_store p n Cp=20f Icp=... Icn=... Rleak=...` and instantiates it for persistent signed hidden pixel weights (`Xwh*`), hidden biases (`Xbh*`), readout weights (`Xvw*`), and optional output bias (`Xob`). This keeps the public state nodes unchanged (`whp/whn`, `bhp/bhn`, `vwp/vwn`, `obp/obn`), so writers and `.meas` diagnostics still attach to the real capacitor state, while removing hundreds of repeated raw cap/leak lines from target-topology decks. Focused block regressions pass (`65 passed`). A tiny 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 continuous ngspice smoke with the hierarchical stores preserved the strict fully-on-device/no-behavioral-learning-device contract and ran through the actual script path; this is a netlist-maintainability refactor, not a new learning claim.
- Rechecked split-rail after the `signed_store` hierarchy on the actual target shape (`10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 1 train / 1 eval, restored-hidden error, readout-restored credit, hardware reference latch, output-only diagnostics). The run completed in one continuous ngspice transient with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, `target_10x10_b4_stride2_c2_topology=true`, no behavioral learning devices, and no Python weight transfer; wall time was `265.3 s`. It is only a convergence/contract smoke: final accuracy on the single eval sample was `0.0`, and `nontrivial_learning_met=false`. This is slightly slower than the earlier per-pixel target 1/1 convergence rung (`~213 s`) but better than the shared/always-on 360 s timeouts, so split-rail is not enough by itself; the next scaling target should be a more substantial current-summed/tiled primitive or a stronger hardware decision/reference centering strategy, not more shallow forward-stack rearrangement.
- Factored the split-rail hidden-forward branch into simulator-native motifs: `split_rail_hidden_pixel`, `split_rail_hidden_bias`, and `split_rail_relu_{nrel,sense}` subcircuits. The generated deck now shows `Xhs*`, `Xhb*`, and `Xrelu_h*` instances rather than raw per-pixel `Mhspos/Mhsneg` and per-feature split activation transistor lines. This is behavior-preserving and still transistor/passive-only; it makes the specific branch that needs the next circuit changes inspectable and replaceable without changing the experiment contract. Focused block regressions pass (`65 passed`), and a 4x4 b2 stride2 c1 split-rail continuous ngspice script smoke preserved the strict fully-on-device/no-behavioral-learning-device contract.
- Added summary diagnostics for adaptive decision-reference state (`outref_final_train`, final-eval drift, min/max, and max absolute error from nominal). This closes a visibility gap: `outref` was in the CSV but not in the headline summary, making reference-centering experiments hard to compare. Re-ran two small 4x4 b2 stride2 c2 adaptive-reference probes on the known separable operating point with weak reference writers (`0.05u`, `0.10u`) and a physical `200 fF` reference cap weakly anchored by the passive divider. Both preserved the strict fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract and retained analog separability (`out_after` and `decision_after` best-threshold diagnostics were 1.0), but neither crossed the fixed hardware decision threshold (`0.5 -> 0.5`). The reference moved in the intended signed direction during training and settled back near nominal during final eval; the miss is now more clearly a decision gain/thresholding problem, not silent Python-side thresholding or uncontrolled reference drift.
- Tested stronger passive centering for the adaptive decision reference on the same 4x4 b2 stride2 c2 restored-hidden/readout-restored operating point. Lowering the nominal physical reference to `1.12 V` and reducing the passive divider resistance recovered nontrivial hardware-decision learning without Python threshold extraction: both `200 kOhm` and `50 kOhm` divider anchors improved `0.5 -> 0.75` with strict fully-on-device contract true and no behavioral learning devices. The `50 kOhm` anchor held final-eval `outref` essentially fixed (`1.120002 V -> 1.120000 V`, max abs error `~2 uV`) while keeping a large decision margin (`decision_after_margin ~= 270 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~= 542 mV`). This is still a tiny binary slice and one miss below the fixed-reference 4/4 best, but it shows that a physical adaptive/reference-centering path can support a real decision rail; the next check is whether this reference operating point transfers to 6/6 and target-shape probes without hand-picking per-run thresholds.
- Promoted the adaptive-reference centering check to the harder 4x4 b2 stride2 c2, 6-train/6-eval small slice. With the same `50 kOhm` passive anchor, `outref=1.12 V` was too low after training and classified every final-eval sample high (`0.5 -> 0.5`, decision margins essentially zero). Raising only the physical nominal reference to `1.13 V` recovered the previous small hardware-decision result while keeping the reference fully circuit-owned: `initial_eval_accuracy=0.5`, `final_eval_accuracy=0.8333`, `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, `outref_final_eval_first=outref_final_eval_last=1.13 V`, `decision_after_margin ~= 403 mV`, and `decision_diff_margin ~= 875 mV`. This is still not target-topology or multiclass, but it removes the earlier weak/adaptive-reference failure mode on the small Milestone-B rung and gives a concrete physical reference-centering setting to try on 10x10 b4 stride2 c2.
- Added deterministic robustness perturbation controls to the transistor/passive MNIST01 block runner: input-DAC rail jitter, phase timing jitter, passive reference-resistor mismatch, and initial capacitor-state mismatch. The perturbations are seeded and reproducible, affect only experiment-supplied rails/passive values/initial state, and do not add behavioral learning devices. Focused regressions pass (`72 passed`). Two mild 4x4 b2 stride2 c2 robustness smokes (`5 mV` input jitter, `20 ps` phase jitter, `1%` passive mismatch, `5 mV` state mismatch, seed 11) both converged with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true` and `no_behavioral_learning_devices=true`. The adaptive-reference hardware decision missed low (`final_eval_accuracy=0.5`, active fraction `0.0`) even though analog outputs remained separable (`out_after_best_threshold_accuracy=1.0`, `decision_diff_best_threshold_accuracy=1.0`). The diff-latched decision variant also failed by saturating both decision rails high (`decision_after_best_threshold_accuracy=0.5`) while `out_after/out_diff` stayed separable. Current robustness lesson: the learning/update path has some margin under mild perturbation, but the absolute/reference decision cell is too finicky; the next robust topology should use a stronger calibrated differential sense/decision primitive or train directly against a differential readout margin rather than continuing to tune `outref`.
- Fixed the transistor/passive MNIST block generator's PMOS source/drain orientation for the family of clocked/precharged/regenerative pullups and PMOS write-pullup stacks. Precharge devices, restored readout-sign latches, restored error latches, dynamic output latches, reference/differential decision latches, adaptive outref raise devices, readout-weight potentiation stacks, and output-bias potentiation stacks now put the charged/storage node on the drain side and `vdd` or the upstream high node on the source side instead of relying on source/drain symmetry. Added a regression that fails if generated PMOS pullups use `vdd` as the drain in the representative block netlist, and updated the standalone shared-phase smoke template's readout-sign latch orientation. Focused regressions pass (`74 passed`). A strict corrected-orientation 4x4 b2 stride2 c2, 4-train/4-eval smoke still converged with `strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`, but the fixed hardware decision remained below threshold (`0.5 -> 0.5`) while analog separability stayed intact (`out_after_best_threshold_accuracy=1.0`, `decision_diff_best_threshold_accuracy=1.0`). This confirms the previous robustness diagnosis under a more physical PMOS orientation: the update/readout path still creates class-separable analog margins, but the decision/centering cell is the fragile missing primitive.
- Added `--forward-phase-mode split-hidden-output` to decouple hidden feature settling from readout scoring. The runner still emits the legacy `fwd` rail for compatibility, but split mode also emits `fwdh` and `fwdo`; hidden pixel/bias/residual paths use `fwdh`, while readout/activation competition/score inhibition/output latch paths use `fwdo`. Focused regressions pass (`76 passed`). A strict 4x4 b2 stride2 c2, 4-train/4-eval smoke with the corrected PMOS orientation and split forward phases completed in one continuous train+final-eval transient under the no-behavioral/no-Python-update contract. It did not recover fixed-threshold hardware accuracy (`0.5 -> 0.5`), but it substantially widened the separable analog margins (`out_after ~= 69.6 mV`, `out_diff ~= 78.4 mV`, `decision_after ~= 38.0 mV`). The diagnosis is now sharper: split clocking improves forward/readout stability, but the decision/reference centering primitive remains the blocker.
- Added an opt-in headline accuracy selector (`--accuracy-signal`) so experiments can explicitly score `out_after`, `out_diff`, `decision_after`, or `decision_diff`; `auto` preserves the old behavior, and differential signals default to a zero threshold. This is not a learning claim by itself, but it prevents confusing absolute-threshold failures with differential-rail failures. Focused regressions pass (`78 passed`). Two strict split-forward 4x4 probes showed the distinction: `decision_diff` sign scoring with the adaptive `1.13 V` reference still failed (`0.5 -> 0.5`) because both classes remained negative (`positive mean ~= -0.713 V`, `negative mean ~= -0.779 V`), and the no-reference `diff-precharged-latched` stage also failed sign scoring by saturating both classes near `+1.2 V` with only `~0.4 mV` separation. A passive `0.95 V` divider reference produced a real circuit decision rail with large class ordering (`decision_after margin ~= 396 mV`, `decision_diff margin ~= 784 mV`) but only `0.75 -> 0.75` headline accuracy on this slice. Conclusion: the readout is separable and split-forward helps, but we still need a circuit-owned centering/auto-zero/reference-training primitive rather than a post-hoc threshold or a hand-picked topology reference.
- Added `--output-decision-ref-source track`, an experimental circuit-owned reference tracker. It emits a real `Coutref` state cap, optional passive divider anchor, and a CMOS pass switch from `out` to `outref` during the existing `apply` phase; no behavioral source or Python update is involved. Focused regressions pass (`79 passed`). On the strict split-forward 4x4 b2 stride2 c2 smoke, a strongly anchored tracker at `0.95 V` behaved like the passive divider (`0.75 -> 0.75`, `outref` final-eval drift under `1 mV`). Starting from the previously bad `1.13 V` reference without an anchor converged but moved the sampled reference the wrong way (`outref_final_train ~= 1.169 V`) and all decision rails stayed below the fixed threshold (`0.5 -> 0.5`). This rules out a naive sample-and-hold tracker as the robust centering fix; the next decision primitive should use a proper auto-zero/precharge comparator or a bounded differential preamp, not direct charge sharing with the late-cycle output rail.
- Added `--output-decision-stage ref-precharged-preamp-latched`, a MOS-only dynamic precharged reference preamp followed by the existing regenerative decision latch. The preamp rails are precharged/equalized during reset, then an NMOS differential pair compares `outref` and `out` during `dec`; the decision latch consumes the preamp rails instead of directly loading the output node. Focused regressions pass. On the strict split-forward 4x4 b2 stride2 c2, 4-train/4-eval smoke with a physical `0.95 V` divider reference, the old single-ended `decision_after > 0.6 V` headline still failed (`0.5 -> 0.5`), but the differential decision rail became genuinely zero-centered (`decision_diff` positive mean `~+249 mV`, negative mean `~-183 mV`, best-threshold accuracy `1.0`). Scoring the circuit's differential decision sign gave `0.75 -> 0.75`; the harder 6-train/6-eval slice also stayed flat (`0.667 -> 0.667`) while preserving a zero-centered differential margin (`~+238 mV` positives, `~-68 mV` negatives). This is a better comparator primitive than the previous direct latch, but it is not yet a nontrivial-learning rung.
- Added `spice/run_shared_phase_cell_robustness.py`, a standalone ngspice robustness/yield harness for the transistor-only shared-phase local-feature cell template. It randomizes supply/input offsets, phase jitter, stored capacitor ICs, restored-readout latch VTO/KP mismatch, optional clock-feedthrough caps, extra dynamic-node capacitance, and temperature, then scores pass/fail on circuit margins (`score-scoren`, `rvwp-rvwn`, `hdp`, and opposite-rail `hdn` leakage). The harness intentionally uses `.meas PARAM` and MOS/passive netlist changes, not behavioral signal buckets. Focused tests pass (`84 passed`). A strict nominal campaign already fails (`0/3` yield): the restored readout latch has large margin (`rvw_margin ~= 1.10 V`) and `hdp` is above `50 mV`, but score margin is only `~0.07 mV` and the wrong hidden-error rail rises almost equally (`hdn ~= 57 mV`). Under mild stress with relaxed score/hdn checks, yield was `18/20`; the failures were `hdp` droop. This makes the next transistor cell target concrete: hidden-error quadrant isolation and score-margin generation must be fixed before expecting noise/jitter to act only as a useful regularizer.
- Finished the hard-gated restored-readout cleanup for the transistor/passive block runner and standalone shared-phase cell. The hidden-credit restored-sign gates now use nominal-threshold `NMOS` devices in `readout-restored-hardgate`, which removes the previous wrong-rail hidden-error leakage (`hdn` effectively zero in the standalone ngspice smoke). Added a separate `--readout-weight-gate-model` knob for the forward readout score stack; `switch` keeps the low-threshold sense devices on activation/phase transport but uses nominal-threshold `NMOS` for the stored readout-weight gates. This fixed the standalone score-margin failure: strict nominal shared-phase robustness moved from `0/3` yield to `3/3`, with `score_margin ~= 145.7 mV`, `rvw_margin ~= 1.10 V`, `hdp ~= 57 mV`, and `hdn ~= 0`. Under mild statistical stress (`20 mV` supply sigma, `10 mV` input sigma, `20 ps` phase jitter, `5 mV` state mismatch, latch mismatch, small feedthrough/parasitic caps), yield was `18/20`; both failures were `hdp` dipping just under the `50 mV` threshold, not sign flips. The same hard-gated readout path on the small strict MNIST01 block slice preserved the no-behavioral/no-Python-update contract and strong analog separability, but did not create new headline learning (`4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4 train / 4 eval, `decision_diff` stayed `0.75 -> 0.75`; best-threshold diagnostics remained `1.0`). Current interpretation: nominal-threshold gating fixes circuit correctness/margins for the local cell, while the MNIST runner is still limited by decision centering and training signal margin rather than by the old low-threshold leakage.
- Added explicit readout-weight initialization controls to the transistor/passive block runner so small MNIST smokes can start from a near-neutral readout instead of the previous favorable `vwp/vwn ~= 0.52/0.25` projection. The new knobs are `--readout-weight-positive-init`, `--readout-weight-negative-init`, and `--readout-weight-init-sigma`; the initialized values are stored as real signed capacitor ICs and still train only through the MOS/passive write path. A strict continuous `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval ngspice run with hard-gated restored credit, hard-gated readout score weights, neutral readout ICs (`0.36/0.34`), and no Python weight transfer improved from `0.5 -> 0.75` on `decision_diff`, with final `score_net` margin `~76.8 mV`, `out_diff` margin `~74.0 mV`, and `decision_diff` margin `~458 mV`. Repeating the run with neutral leak references (`readout_weight_positive_ref=0.36`, `readout_weight_negative_ref=0.34`) kept the same headline improvement while removing the passive drift toward a positive readout prior: only feature 0 moved strongly (`vwp/vwn ~= 0.878/0.235`), while features 1--7 stayed near `0.36/0.34`. This is cleaner Milestone-B-style evidence than the earlier hardgate run because initial eval is chance and the readout separation is created by on-device writes. The remaining miss is not a threshold-only issue; activations show feature 2 responds strongly to the hard negative, but the readout-gradient state barely updates beyond feature 0. Next circuit target: distribute eligibility/readout-gradient writes across active features, or add a current-mode/normalized readout update primitive, rather than tuning the comparator reference.
- Added `--readout-gradient-source {act,pre}` so the readout-gradient storage stack can be gated by the feature's preactivation capacitor instead of only the post-activation storage node. This is still a MOS/passive local write path: it only changes which on-device state node gates `Mgvp*/Mgvn*`, and hidden credit continues to use the activation derivative path. On the same strict neutral-readout/neutral-leak `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke, `readout_gradient_source=pre` preserved the fully-on-device contract and the `0.5 -> 0.75` improvement. It did not solve the missed sample, but it did spread readout depression beyond feature 0: final `vwn` moved from `0.34` to about `0.269` on feature 2, `0.339` on feature 4, and `0.323` on feature 7, while the activation-gated run kept those features essentially unchanged. Added summary diagnostics for readout update participation (`readout_signed_delta_*`, updated-feature count, and participation fraction) so future sweeps can distinguish real multi-feature credit distribution from a single-feature latch-up. Current conclusion: preactivation gating helps distribute small updates but is still not enough; the next topology should store/boost an eligibility trace during the forward phase or normalize the readout update current across active features.
- Added an opt-in readout eligibility state (`--readout-gradient-source eligibility`). Each feature now samples preactivation onto a real `eg*` capacitor during the hidden-forward phase using MOS devices, then uses that capacitor to gate the readout-gradient storage stack. Focused regressions pass, and the strict neutral-readout/neutral-leak `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` run preserved the fully-on-device `0.5 -> 0.75` learning result. The diagnostic was negative for the hoped-for fix: the eligibility cap moved on multiple features (`eg2` reached visible levels on the hard negative sample), but the actual readout write remained dominated by feature 0 (`readout_signed_updated_features_10mv=1`, participation `0.125`). Conclusion: storing eligibility alone is not enough; the readout write primitive needs stronger/normalized eligibility-to-gradient transfer or a current-mode update path so active non-dominant features receive material write current.
- Added optional ngspice source-level transient noise hooks to the block MNIST runner: `--supply-transient-noise-sigma`, `--input-transient-noise-sigma`, `--phase-transient-noise-sigma`, and `--transient-noise-timestep`. These wrap the existing supply, input/target, and phase PWL rails with series `TRNOISE(...)` voltage sources; no behavioral learning devices or Python weight updates are introduced. Unit coverage checks the emitted noise source topology. A small `1` train / `1` eval output-only strict run with mild source noise completed (`strict_fully_on_device_contract_met=true`), validating the mechanism. A full `4` train / `4` eval source-noise run was too slow to keep as the default loop, so TRNOISE should be treated as a circuit characterization/yield stress tool rather than the primary training regularizer.
- Re-ran the neutral-readout preactivation-gated small learning slice with deterministic robustness perturbations (`5 mV` input rail jitter, `20 ps` phase jitter, `1%` passive mismatch, `5 mV` state-IC mismatch, seed 11). It preserved the strict fully-on-device contract and the `0.5 -> 0.75` learning result, with final `decision_diff` margin about `404 mV`, best-threshold diagnostics still `1.0`, and `readout_signed_updated_features_10mv=3/8`. This supports jitter/mismatch as a useful robustness/regularization campaign, but it did not fix the remaining missed sample or remove the need for a better distributed readout-update primitive.
- Re-tested the latch-free direct differential hidden-credit path (`--hidden-credit-mode readout-weighted`) on the strict neutral-readout/neutral-leak `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval slice. This mode uses the stored analog `vwp/vwn` rails directly in the backward credit stacks and does not instantiate the per-cycle `rvwp/rvwn` sign-restoration latch. It preserved the strict fully-on-device contract and matched the current nontrivial learning rung (`0.5 -> 0.75`) with final `decision_diff` margin about `419 mV`, best-threshold diagnostics still `1.0`, and `readout_signed_updated_features_10mv=3/8`. This is an important architecture point: for this small case, the restored sign latch is not required for learning, so the more analog magnitude-preserving path should stay in the candidate set. The caveat remains that near-neutral `vwp/vwn` common-mode can weaken/corrupt hidden credit at larger scale; we need alignment/yield sweeps before promoting it beyond the small slice.
- Added a standalone hidden-credit alignment harness (`spice/run_readout_hidden_credit_alignment.py`) that sweeps stored readout differential voltage and measures `hdp-hdn` for both latch-free `readout-weighted` and `readout-restored-hardgate` circuits. The deck is a transistor/passive smoke with fixed activation/error rails, persistent `vwp/vwn` capacitors, and no behavioral sources. The small and wide sweeps showed a clear near-neutral result: the direct analog path was sign-aligned for every nonzero tested delta down to `10 mV` and had a clean dead zone at zero, while the restored-hardgate motif was weak across the tested range because its "low" restored rail remained above the NMOS hidden-credit gate threshold and charged both `hdp` and `hdn`. At the favorable old smoke point (`vwp/vwn=0.52/0.25`), latch-free credit produced `hidden_credit_margin ~= 263 mV`; restored-hardgate produced essentially zero hidden-credit margin in this isolated fixture. This does not invalidate the integrated hardgate learning rung, but it makes the analog `vwp/vwn` path the better candidate for a magnitude-preserving, less-sensed backward branch and identifies the restored-latch primitive as needing a real low-rail/discharge redesign before it should be trusted near neutral weights.
- Added row-pulsed input support to the block MNIST runner so the split-rail hidden topology can be driven as `row waveform -> differential conductance pair -> pre/pren caps` instead of holding ideal pixel rails for the whole sample. The implementation factors phase-window generation into a shared schedule and can emit transistor-level row drivers (`row_src` voltage source gated through NMOS/PMOS devices by `fwdh/fwdhn`) so the row is high-Z outside the hidden-forward windows except for reset/leak. A first strict `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval MNIST01 smoke with row-pulsed split-rail, latch-free readout-weighted hidden credit, neutral readout ICs, passive divider decision reference, no behavioral sources, and continuous final eval met the fully-on-device contract but did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`). The corrected high-Z row-driver version did produce real final output separation (`out_diff_margin ~= 26.4 mV`) and broad readout participation (`7/8` features moved by >10 mV), but the hardware decision still classified every eval sample high and headline accuracy remained `0.5`. This says the topology is electrically active, but the full MNIST runner still needs bounded/centered local updates and decision centering before it is a useful learning rung.
- Added a standalone row-conductance primitive harness (`spice/run_row_conductance_primitive.py`) to isolate the intended silicon-shaped primitive before spending more large MNIST runtime. It characterizes three transistor/passive operations with capacitor-stored gates used only as prototype conductance state: row-pulsed split-rail forward accumulation, local signed weight movement through update devices, and latch-free analog backward credit through `vwp/vwn`. The first harness run exposed two testbench bugs: the reset pulse was inverted and clamped the row during forward, and hidden credit was measured after the error pulse had ended. After fixing those, ngspice passed all 8 sign cases (`forward: 7 aligned / 1 dead zone`, `update: 4 aligned / 4 dead zone`, `hidden credit: 6 aligned / 2 dead zone`). Representative margins were strong for clear weights (`pre_p-pre_n ~= 305 mV`, hidden-credit margin `~= 408 mV`) and clean at readout neutrality (`hidden_credit_margin ~= 0`). The important caveat is that the current writer is too hard: positive/negative update cases can drive one stored rail near 0 V in a single pulse. Next primitive target is a bounded or normalized local writer, not more 10x10 scaling.
- Re-sized the standalone row-conductance primitive writer from the initial `4u` smoke width to `0.25u`, matching the smaller block-runner writer scale. The primitive still passed all 8 ngspice sign cases, but the update step became much less destructive: the positive update moved signed weight by about `+93 mV` and the negative update by about `-90 mV` instead of rail-slamming a stored side. This is still only a local primitive result, but it gives a better default for the next MNIST integration attempt: preserve row-conductance forward/backward signs while making the local update incremental enough for multiple samples.
- Added an opt-in conductance-row readout forward topology (`--readout-forward-topology conductance-row`) to match the row-pulsed hidden primitive: the runner now creates a switched `actrow` during `fwdo` and lets `actrow` drive `score/scoren` through the stored `vwp/vwn` conductance gates, instead of the old `vdd -> act -> weight -> phase -> score` stack. Focused regressions pass (`104 passed`). A strict continuous `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval row-pulsed smoke with split-rail hidden forward, conductance-row readout, latch-free readout-weighted hidden credit, neutral readout ICs, no behavioral sources, and passive decision reference met the fully-on-device contract but did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`). The new readout is electrically active but too weak for the existing output latch (`final_eval_out_diff_margin ~= 3.46 mV`, decision rail stayed low), and it exposes the unresolved writer issue more sharply: several readout weights moved to high positive signed values (`vwp ~= 1.07 V`, `vwn` as low as `~0.06--0.15 V`). The next integrated circuit target should be a bounded/centered current-mode readout writer or common-mode clamp, not more comparator threshold tuning.
- Added an opt-in apply-phase MOS clamp for stored readout weights (`--readout-weight-apply-clamp-width`) plus readout common-mode diagnostics. The clamp connects `vwp/vwn` to their configured neutral reference rails during `apply`; it is a transistor-level stabilizer, not a Python state update. Focused regressions pass (`105 passed`). On the strict row-pulsed + conductance-row `4x4` smoke, a `0.25u` clamp was too strong: it kept the readout near neutral (`readout_signed_delta_max_abs ~= 7.4 uV`, no features updated) and produced no learning. A weaker `0.025u` clamp allowed broad updates (`7/8` features, signed delta max `~= 408 mV`) and reduced the previous runaway (`mean signed delta ~= 305 mV` versus `~= 791 mV` with no clamp), but still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`) and the output latch remained effectively low. This brackets the problem: simple clamping can control common-mode or allow learning pressure, but not both yet. The next writer should be a differential/common-mode-separated update primitive rather than a clamp fighting an asymmetric pull-up/pull-down writer.
- Added an opt-in bounded-reference readout writer (`--readout-weight-update-topology bounded-ref`) plus a standalone readout-writer primitive harness (`spice/run_readout_writer_primitive.py`). The new writer reuses the local `gvp/gvn` gradient storage and `rgp/rgn` restore nodes, but pulls the readout rails toward finite `vwhi_ref/vwlo_ref` targets (`positive_ref + span`, `negative_ref - span`) instead of toward `vdd/0`. The standalone ngspice primitive passed: positive/negative update signs aligned and common-mode motion stayed bounded (`~10--16 mV`) while the no-error case stayed in the dead zone. In the strict row-pulsed + conductance-row `4x4` integrated smoke, bounded-ref preserved the fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract but still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`). It did substantially tame the readout writer (`readout_signed_delta_max_abs ~= 78.6 mV`, `readout_common_delta_max_abs ~= 36.7 mV`, `7/8` features updated), so the old rail-slamming writer is no longer the immediate failure. The remaining integrated blocker is that conductance-row readout produces only millivolt-level output/decision drive (`final_eval_out_diff_margin ~= 2 mV`, decision rails saturated low), so the next circuit target should be score/readout gain or sensing for the row-conductance topology, not more readout-writer clamping.
- Added actual ngspice-backed pytest coverage for low-level assumptions instead of only checking generated netlist strings. `tests/test_readout_writer_primitive.py` now runs the bounded-reference readout writer deck for positive, negative, and no-error cases and asserts signed update direction plus common-mode bounds. Added `spice/run_score_decision_primitive.py` and `tests/test_score_decision_primitive.py` to isolate direct score/scoren sensing with a precharged regenerative latch, and added the corresponding opt-in block-runner stage (`--output-decision-stage score-diff-precharged-latched`). The primitive SPICE tests proved positive/negative score differentials resolve with the correct decision polarity. They also exposed an important non-guarantee: exact neutral `score == scoren` is not a dead zone in this latch; it resolves to a rail through numerical/device imbalance. Focused regressions that include these ngspice primitive tests pass (`106 passed`). This should be the pattern going forward: subcomponent circuit hypotheses get fast SPICE tests before they are promoted into long MNIST transients.
- Added a low-level conductance-row readout primitive harness (`spice/run_conductance_readout_primitive.py`) and ngspice-backed pytest coverage. This isolates the intended readout operation from MNIST, output latches, and update writers: `actrow` is switched from an activation source, `vwp/vwn` are persistent capacitor-stored gate voltages, and the two MOS conductances charge `score/scoren` capacitors. The default ngspice primitive passed four cases: positive weight produced `score_margin ~= +133.5 mV`, negative weight produced `~= -133.5 mV`, neutral weights stayed near dead zone (`~= 18.9 uV`), and inactive activation stayed quiet (`score_common ~= 26.6 nV`). Focused regressions including this new SPICE-backed harness pass (`125 passed`). This gives a fast approximate guarantee for readout polarity and inactive behavior, and it also shows the conductance-row readout itself can make large score margins in isolation; integrated failures are therefore more likely from upstream activation levels, competition/inhibition loading, multi-feature charge sharing, or downstream sensing/timing rather than the one-synapse readout primitive alone.
- Fixed a row-pulsed integration bug in the binary block runner: when `input_waveform_mode="row-pulsed"`, input rails are intentionally disconnected after the hidden forward phase, but the hidden-weight writer was still gating on the live input rail during `acc/apply`. The runner now samples a per-hidden-pixel `helig{feature}_{pix}` capacitor during hidden forward and uses that held eligibility in the hidden writer only for row-pulsed inputs. A new structural test checks the emitted MOS/capacitor topology, and a two-sample ngspice regression proves the integrated block path works: negative label credit through a positive readout produces `hdn > hdp`, weakens the signed hidden input weight, and reduces the next-cycle activation in one continuous transient. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_device_mnist01_block_training.py::test_row_pulsed_block_hidden_writer_uses_sampled_input_eligibility tests/test_device_mnist01_block_training.py::test_row_pulsed_block_hidden_writer_updates_weight_in_ngspice tests/test_row_conductance_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` (`33 passed`).
- Added primitive-level learning-effect regressions for the row-pulsed hidden-weight cell: after a hidden-credit-driven write, the next forward pass in the same transient must move in the useful direction. Starting from a negative hidden weight (`wp=0.30`, `wn=0.55`), positive output credit through a positive readout conductance makes the cycle-2 forward margin less negative (`~-170 mV -> ~-144 mV`); starting from a positive hidden weight, negative output credit reduces the next forward margin by the same scale. This proves the tested local update is not just capacitor motion but affects subsequent inference under reset/forward sequencing. Focused row primitive regression passes (`29 passed`).
- Added per-cycle output-credit rails to the row-pulsed conductance primitive so continuous batch-1 sequences can change `edp/edn` sample by sample without Python touching weights. The new `cycle_credit_modes` path emits PWL `edp/edn` sources and preserves the hidden-credit-driven writer path, letting one transient run positive, negative, or inactive credit cycles against the same persistent `wp/wn` capacitor state. Ngspice regressions now prove two positive hidden-credit cycles accumulate signed weight movement, a positive-then-negative credit sequence reverses direction on cycle 2, and a later `row=0` sample blocks the second update even when hidden credit is present. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_row_conductance_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` (`29 passed`).
- Connected the row-pulsed conductance primitive's latch-free hidden-credit rails to the local weight writer as an opt-in `writer_error_source=hidden_credit` path. In this mode external `ep/en` are held at zero, `hdp/hdn` gate the existing transistor writer stacks directly, and the apply pulse fires while the backward-credit pulse is still valid; applying after the credit pulse failed because the same conductance devices discharged `hdp/hdn` when `edp/edn` fell. New ngspice regressions prove positive output error through positive readout conductance increases signed hidden weight (`~+28.8 mV`), positive output error through negative readout conductance decreases it (`~-28.8 mV`), neutral readout stays in the dead zone, and `row=0` blocks the update despite valid hidden credit. Focused row primitive tests pass (`24 passed`), and the shared ngspice availability slice passes (`26 passed`).
- Extended the conductance-row readout primitive to test multi-feature summation on shared score capacitors. The ngspice-backed tests exposed the key scaling flaw in the direct floating topology: two identical active branches do not add (`single ~= 133.5 mV`, `two ~= 133.6 mV`) because the score rail is settling toward the row voltage, and an inactive extra row shunts the active score (`inactive-extra ~= 65.8 mV`). A diode-isolated variant blocks the inactive-row shunt (`single ~= inactive-extra ~= 110 mV`) but still does not make equal active rows additive. Adding a low-impedance score load/virtual-ground approximation makes increments visible (`single ~= 2.4 mV`, `two ~= 6.6 mV`) while preserving inactive isolation, at the cost of much smaller voltage signals. Focused SPICE-backed regressions pass (`130 passed`). This is strong evidence that the next integrated readout direction should be current-mode/virtual-ground or sensed-current accumulation, not a floating voltage-mode passive row summing node.
- Integrated the primitive multi-feature readout finding into the block runner as an opt-in `--readout-forward-topology conductance-row-isolated` plus `--readout-score-load-resistance`. The isolated topology inserts per-feature mid nodes and diode-connected devices before the shared `score/scoren` rails, and the score-load knob lets integrated smokes test low-impedance/current-mode-ish accumulation without editing deck strings. Added structural regressions for the emitted topology and validation, while keeping the existing ngspice primitive tests as the actual low-level behavior checks. Focused SPICE-backed regressions pass (`131 passed`). A strict continuous `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval smoke with row-pulsed split-rail hidden forward, isolated conductance-row readout, `10 kOhm` score loads, latch-free readout-weighted hidden credit, and bounded-ref writer met the no-behavioral/no-Python-update contract and updated readout weights broadly (`7/8` features >10 mV), but still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`). The score-net margin improved modestly (`~33 mV` final) and the hardware decision stayed separable but biased; the next target remains a real current-sensing/virtual-ground readout or decision-centering path rather than more floating-node tuning.
- Extended the conductance-row readout primitive with an optional direct score-differential decision latch so the readout/load assumption is tested in one ngspice deck instead of assuming a measured millivolt score survives until a later latch. The first test failed usefully: with a loaded score rail, the score margin decays if the decision phase waits too long. Moving the decision pulse into the valid readout window made the primitive pass. A `30 kOhm` diode-isolated score load gives a useful low-level tradeoff (`single_positive score_margin >30 mV`, `two_positive >1.8x single`, inactive branch still isolated) and directly drives the precharged latch with the correct positive polarity. A strict integrated `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke at `30 kOhm` converged but still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`): final score-net margin was only modestly better (`~36.6 mV`) and the decision polarity remained biased against the positive class. Focused SPICE-backed regressions pass (`134 passed`). Current interpretation: load tuning can create a latchable local primitive, but the integrated blocker is now class-evidence/sign centering, not simply insufficient score-load resistance.
- Added a conductance-mode trainable output-bias forward path for the row-conductance readout family. The low-level ngspice primitive first showed that near-neutral bias gates (`obp/obn ~= 0.39/0.34`) produce no useful centering, while an active but bounded bias (`0.45/0.34`, `8u`) shifts a mixed/cancelled score by about `26 mV` and preserves about `44 mV` of sample-dependent increment. The block runner now has `--output-bias-forward-topology conductance-row` and `--output-bias-forward-width`, so the persistent `obp/obn` capacitors can inject bias through the same row-conductance/load style as the readout instead of the old stacked voltage-mode branch. A strict continuous `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval smoke with row-pulsed split-rail hidden forward, isolated conductance-row readout, `30 kOhm` score loads, conductance-row output bias, latch-free hidden credit, and bounded-ref readout writer met the no-behavioral/no-Python-update contract and improved from `0.5 -> 0.75` on hardware `decision_diff`. Final score-net class margin was `~45.9 mV`; final decision-diff margin was `~1.18 V`; readout participation stayed broad (`7/8` features >10 mV). The caveat is that this is still a tiny binary smoke and output-bias state is materially involved (`obp-obn` final train `~264 mV`, final-eval drift `~50 mV`), so the next checks should be larger slices and bias-drift/order sensitivity before treating it as robust class evidence.
- Re-ran the same conductance-row output-bias configuration on a larger strict `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 6-train/6-eval slice. The fully-on-device contract still passed and headline hardware-decision accuracy improved from `0.5 -> 0.8333`, with final score-net margin `~60.7 mV`, output margin `~24.2 mV`, and decision-diff margin `~1.56 V`. Readout updates remained broad (`7/8` features >10 mV). However, the output-bias drift warning now tripped: `obp-obn` ended training at `~362 mV`, drifted by `~118 mV` across final eval, and the train bias was `3.6x` the nominal decision threshold. The runner now includes `"output-bias state drift is threshold-scale"` in `full_objective_contract_issues` whenever that diagnostic is true, so these bias-assisted wins are not mistaken for clean progress toward robust class evidence. Focused SPICE-backed regressions pass (`137 passed`). Next useful branch: reduce/regularize output-bias dependence or show the result survives order/seed changes and larger eval slices before scaling this topology.
- Added pytest-level ngspice coverage for the row-pulsed differential conductance primitive instead of relying on the harness summary and string checks alone. `tests/test_row_conductance_primitive.py` now writes temporary decks and runs ngspice for three low-level contracts: capacitor-stored weight gates produce the expected `pre_p-pre_n` forward polarity (`+/-305 mV` for clear weights and a quiet inactive row), positive/negative error pulses physically move the stored signed weight by about `+93/-90 mV` while no-error stays in the dead zone, and the latch-free direct `vwp/vwn` hidden-credit path routes `hdp-hdn` with the expected sign (`~+/-195 mV`) while equal readout weights produce a differential dead zone. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_row_conductance_primitive.py -q` passed (`13 passed`). This turns the current row-conductance assumptions into fast SPICE-backed regression tests before larger MNIST transients use the primitive.
- Added sample-order diagnostics to the strict MNIST01 block summary: train/eval digit prefixes, positive-label prefixes, MNIST index prefixes, class balance, unique digit count, and an order hash. Focused regression with the row primitive tests passed (`111 passed`). Re-ran the current best conductance-row/output-bias `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 6-train/6-eval, continuous-final-eval configuration at `output_bias_apply_scale=0.01` on two seeds with the same executable revision. Seed 0 reproduced nontrivial strict learning (`0.5 -> 0.6667`) with train order hash `5bf2d8c737cb1b87`, final score-net margin `48.8 mV`, decision-diff margin `803 mV`, output-bias final train `241 mV`, and eval bias drift `61 mV` below the warning threshold. Seed 1 failed (`0.6667 -> 0.5`) with train order hash `2933f953d8080ff9`, final score-net margin `-18.1 mV`, decision-diff margin only `0.82 mV`, output-bias final train `212 mV`, and eval bias drift `48 mV` below the warning threshold. Both runs kept the fully-on-device/no-Python-update/no-behavioral-learning contract and broad readout participation (`7/8` features >10 mV). Conclusion: the `obscale=0.01` rung is not robust enough to scale; the next circuit step should reduce sequence sensitivity in the output-bias/readout-centering path rather than promoting this exact configuration to larger topologies.
- Added a binary-descent score/error topology as an opt-in circuit primitive and integrated block-runner path. The low-level harness now supports `error_topology=binary-descent`, which emits MOS stacks for `dp ~= targetp*scoren` and `dn ~= targetn*score`, using explicit class-label rails `targetp/targetn`. Ngspice primitive tests pass for clear positive/negative cases, verify a no-label neutral dead zone, and show that this topology fixes the old negative-label/high-`scoren` positive-pressure failure in the raw competition circuit. The block runner now emits `targetp/targetn` PWL rails, validates that binary descent requires differential score mode, and exposes `--error-topology`. Focused regression passed (`156 passed`). A strict seed-1 `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` smoke with the current row-pulsed/conductance-row/output-bias stack preserved the no-behavioral/no-Python-update contract but did not learn (`0.6667 -> 0.5`); readout weights barely moved (`readout_signed_delta_max_abs ~= 0.1 uV`) and output-bias drift was small. This says binary descent removes one sign pathology but also starves the current writer in this operating point, so the next circuit target should be error/readout gain or writer sensitivity, not just the error sign formula.
- Updated `docs/spicenn_progress_note.tex` and rebuilt `docs/spicenn_progress_note.pdf` with a new description and TikZ diagram for the current differential-score / binary-descent error path. The paper now explicitly distinguishes the newer transistor/passive MNIST01 block-runner path from the older high-accuracy behavioral softmax-like reference, documents `score_mode=differential`, `error_topology=binary-descent`, optional `edp/edn` restoration, and latch-free readout-weighted hidden credit, and states that the integrated binary smokes are still not robust learning evidence.
- Added a standalone output score/error primitive harness (`spice/run_score_error_primitive.py`) plus ngspice-backed pytest coverage. The deck isolates the exact transistor/passive target + `score/scoren` -> `dp/dn` competition and optional restored `edp/edn` latch used by the MNIST01 block runner. The tests now assert clear local contracts: target-positive cases drive positive raw error, target-negative with positive score drives negative raw error, and neutral raw rails stay near dead zone without the restore latch. The primitive also exposed two important nonidealities that are now regression-documented: with the default restore width, negative restored error is sign-correct but weak (`edp-edn` only about `-11 mV` in the clear negative case), while a negative target with high `scoren` creates positive raw pressure (`dp-dn` about `+71 mV`) instead of a clean no-error state. Focused low-level SPICE regressions passed (`50 passed`). This makes the next integrated target clearer: normalize or redesign the score/error conversion before treating output-bias/order-sensitive MNIST results as robust.
- Tested the existing circuit-owned output-bias passive centering knob before adding another clamp topology. Tightening `output_bias_leak_resistance` from `5 MOhm` to `1 MOhm` in the same strict `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 6/6, `obscale=0.01` setup reduced bias magnitude and eval drift but did not recover robust learning. On the previously failing seed 1, final accuracy only matched the initial baseline (`0.6667 -> 0.6667`), with score-net margin still wrong-sign (`-14.9 mV`) but decision-diff margin restored to `394 mV`; output-bias final train dropped to `139 mV` and eval drift to `12.6 mV`. On the previously positive seed 0, the stronger leak killed the nontrivial result (`0.5 -> 0.5`) despite preserving a positive score-net margin (`47.3 mV`); decision-diff margin collapsed to `40.7 mV`, output-bias final train dropped to `188 mV`, and eval drift to `34.6 mV`. This says simple stronger bias anchoring damps the sequence-sensitive dynamics but does not solve the underlying score/readout/error centering problem. The next aligned circuit target should be a normalized/centered readout-error or score-sense primitive rather than another output-bias clamp.
- Added weak-error sensitivity coverage to the bounded-reference readout-writer primitive. The new `error_amplitude` knob reproduces the integrated binary-descent operating point where `dp/dn` rails are only tens of millivolts: with `error_amplitude=0.08`, the primitive still stores a positive gradient (`gvp-gvn ~= 58 mV`) but the default `16u` restore pulldown leaves the signed readout update in the dead zone. Increasing only the gradient-restore device to `32u` makes the same weak error produce a useful signed update (`>50 mV`) while keeping common-mode motion bounded (`<50 mV`). The block runner now exposes this as `--readout-gradient-restore-width`, and focused SPICE-backed regressions passed (`109 passed`). In the strict seed-1 binary-descent `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` integrated smoke, `32u` restore fixed writer starvation (`readout_signed_delta_max_abs ~= 27.2 mV`, `5/8` features above `10 mV`) but did not restore learning (`0.6667 -> 0.5`); the final score-net margin stayed wrong-sign (`~-13.9 mV`) while the hardware decision margin was tiny positive (`~3.6 mV`). The blocker has therefore moved from "the current writer never fires" to class-evidence/sign centering in the readout/error path.
- Ran the same strict binary-descent + `32u` readout-gradient-restore configuration on seed 0. The fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract still passed, and the writer again participated broadly (`readout_signed_delta_max_abs ~= 21.0 mV`, `7/8` features above `10 mV`, common-mode max `~=10.5 mV`). Accuracy still did not improve (`0.5 -> 0.5`). Internal score-net class margin was positive (`~39.7 mV`), but the hardware decision-diff margin remained below useful classification robustness (`~36.3 mV` with all final decisions active under the fixed threshold), and output-bias state was smaller/stable (`obp-obn ~= 130.8 mV`, eval drift `~=9.7 mV`). This supports the seed-1 conclusion: stronger restore solves a local writer-sensitivity problem, but the integrated path now needs decision/readout centering or class-evidence normalization rather than still more writer gain.
- Extended the readout-writer primitive with a real restored gradient-gate option so eligibility transfer is tested in ngspice, not only by block-netlist string checks. With a weak direct eligibility gate (`gate_amplitude=40 mV`), the primitive stores only about `19 mV` on `gvp-gvn` and the bounded-reference writer remains in the dead zone. The same weak gate through the MOS restored-gate path (`32u` restore) produces a full eligibility rail, `gvp-gvn >25 mV`, and a bounded signed readout update (`>50 mV`, common-mode `<50 mV`). Focused low-level/block regressions passed (`156 passed`). Two strict 4x4 b2 stride2 c2 integrated smokes then bracketed the result: restored eligibility plus the rail writer preserved nontrivial learning (`0.5 -> 0.75`) and increased final score-net margin to `~262 mV`, but it rail-slammed one negative readout side (`vwn ~= 1.197 V`) and common-mode drift reached `~672 mV`; replacing only the writer with bounded-ref kept common-mode controlled (`max ~=10.7 mV`) but lost headline learning (`0.5 -> 0.5`) despite separable analog best-threshold diagnostics. The next circuit target should combine restored/normalized eligibility transfer with bounded differential write strength; raw rail gain is useful but destructive, while the current bounded writer is too weak or poorly centered for the decision path.
- Swept the existing bounded-reference readout span as the next low-risk way to increase differential write strength without returning to supply-rail writes. The standalone restored-gate writer primitive showed that `readout_weight_update_span=0.34` can produce about `488 mV` signed movement while keeping the high target at `0.70 V` and the low target at `0 V`. In the strict restored-eligibility 4x4 b2 stride2 c2 integrated smoke, the wider span preserved the fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract and kept readout common-mode far below the rail-writer failure (`max common delta ~=82 mV` instead of `~672 mV`), but it still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`): analog `out_after/out_diff` remained best-threshold separable while the selected hardware decision rail collapsed to the same side for every sample. Added `accuracy_signal_centering_diagnostics` so summaries now report fixed-threshold positive/negative margins, all-same-side saturation, and an explicit `"accuracy signal is separable only with a shifted threshold"` contract caveat. Focused block tests passed (`102 passed`), and the broader low-level SPICE regression set passed (`158 passed`). The immediate blocker is now decision/readout centering under bounded writes, not raw eligibility current or signed update amplitude alone.
- Centralized ngspice availability in a shared pytest fixture (`ngspice_path`) so SPICE-backed tests no longer repeat `shutil.which("ngspice")` and local skip logic. Updated the existing low-level ngspice tests to request that fixture, while preserving the same skip behavior on machines without ngspice. The affected SPICE-backed regression slice passed (`160 passed`), and `rg` now shows the ngspice lookup only in `tests/conftest.py`.
- Extended the score-diff precharged decision latch with a physical centering knob. The low-level score-decision primitive now has shifted positive/negative score cases and a `scoren_pulldown_scale` parameter; ngspice proves the default symmetric latch rejects the shifted-positive operating point, while `0.25x` on the `scoren`-driven pulldown resolves that shifted-positive case and still rejects the more negative shifted case. The block MNIST01 runner now exposes the same knob as `--output-decision-scoren-pulldown-scale`, validates it, records it in summaries, and emits the scaled transistor width only in `score-diff-precharged-latched`. Focused tests passed (`108 passed`).
