- Ran the strict restored-eligibility + bounded-reference span-0.34 `4x4 b2 stride2 c2` MNIST01 smoke with the physical score-latch centering knob at `0.25x`. The run preserved the full on-device contract (`batch_size=1`, one train/final-eval transient, no Python weight updates/checkpoints, no behavioral learning devices) and improved hardware `decision_diff` accuracy from `0.5` to `1.0` over four train and four eval samples. Final `score_net` class margin was `66.9 mV`, final `decision_diff` positive/negative means were about `+1.143/-1.151 V`, fixed-threshold worst margin was `1.141 V`, and the shifted-threshold caveat cleared. Readout motion stayed bounded (`readout_common_delta_max_abs ~=80 mV`) but participation was still limited (`3/8` features above `10 mV`). This is a useful circuit-owned decision-centering rung, not a completed architecture: it is binary 4x4 MNIST01 and not yet the `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` target.
- Scaled the same physical score-latch centering configuration from 4/4 to strict 6-train/6-eval MNIST01 on two sample orders. Both runs preserved the fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract and bounded readout common-mode, but neither learned. Seed/order 0 stayed `0.5 -> 0.5`: final `score_net` margin remained positive (`40.6 mV`) and best shifted-threshold `decision_diff` accuracy was `0.8333`, but the fixed-threshold latch margin was wrong (`worst ~= -1.154 V`) and the shifted-threshold caveat returned. Seed/order 1 degraded `0.5 -> 0.3333`: final `score_net` margin was wrong-sign (`-12.7 mV`) and `decision_diff` margin was also wrong-sign (`-754 mV`), with best-threshold accuracy only `0.6667`. Both updated only `3/8` readout features above `10 mV`. Conclusion: the 0.25x latch asymmetry is a real small-slice centering fix, but it is not a robust scaling solution. The next circuit target should be upstream class-evidence normalization/distributed readout update, not more fixed comparator-ratio tuning.
- Added a two-feature ngspice regression deck for the bounded-reference readout writer. The primitive now proves a distribution-specific low-level contract: with the same weak positive error pulse, a direct weak eligibility gate (`40 mV`) stores only about `19 mV` of gradient and leaves the second feature in the update dead zone, while the MOS restored-gate path turns that weak eligibility into a full rail and moves both readout-weight pairs by about `64 mV` signed with bounded common-mode motion (`~32 mV`). This does not solve the 6/6 scaling failure by itself, but it turns the "only a few features update" suspicion into a SPICE-backed subcomponent test and keeps the next integrated work focused on normalization/centering rather than guessing whether weak active features can physically reach the writer.
- Added integrated readout eligibility/gradient participation diagnostics to the block-runner summary. The reducer now reports how many feature rows produce restored eligibility above `0.5 V` and how many produce stored readout-gradient margin above `25 mV`, separately from the final readout-weight movement. A strict continuous `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 2-train/2-eval full-measurement smoke with restored eligibility, bounded-ref span `0.34`, and the score-diff latch centering knob preserved the on-device contract and populated the fields: `readout_eligibility_active_features_0p5v=3/8`, `readout_gradient_active_features_25mv=3/8`, and `readout_signed_updated_features_10mv=3/8`. This moves the current integrated blocker upstream of the bounded writer: the writer can update weak restored features in isolation, but the MNIST block only makes three features eligible/gradient-active in this operating point. The next aligned circuit work should improve hidden feature/eligibility coverage or normalization before retuning the output latch.
- Extended the participation reducer to report analog hidden-activation coverage at a `25 mV` threshold, then compared the old stacked hidden path against the row-pulsed split-rail hidden path. On strict continuous 2-train/2-eval diagnostic smokes, the stacked path had only `1/8` hidden activations above `25 mV` and `3/8` restored-eligibility/gradient/readout updates, while split-rail row-pulsed hidden forward raised those to `7/8` across activation, eligibility, gradient, and readout update participation. A strict continuous 4-train/4-eval split-rail smoke with the same restored-eligibility, bounded-ref span `0.34`, and score-diff latch-centering stack showed nontrivial learning (`0.5 -> 0.75`) with `7/8` readout features updated and positive score/decision margins (`score_net ~= 68 mV`, `decision_diff ~= 1.25 V`). The caveat remains decision centering: the final decision signal was still separable only with a shifted threshold. This makes split-rail row-pulsed hidden forward the better next base than the stacked hidden path, but the next circuit target is centering/normalizing the score-diff decision under broad updates.
- Swept the physical `score-diff-precharged-latched` scoren-pulldown scale on the split-rail 4/4 base using compact continuous SPICE runs. Scales `0.35` and `0.50` both preserved nontrivial learning (`0.5 -> 0.75`) with the same final score margin (`~68 mV`), while `0.75` and `1.0` fell back to `0.5` final accuracy. None removed the shifted-threshold caveat, so pure latch-ratio tuning is not enough. The useful observation is that the reject rail `decisionn` is itself separable for the better scales: at scale `0.35`, positives had `decisionn ~= 1--7 mV`, the ambiguous negative had `decisionn ~=142 mV`, and the clear negative had `decisionn ~=1.18 V`; a low-positive reject threshold around `74 mV` would classify the slice perfectly. Added summary diagnostics for `decisionn_after` and an inverted `decisionn_after_reject_best_threshold_*` window so the next step can be a real second-stage transistor/reference circuit instead of a hidden Python threshold.
- Added decision-time score measurements (`score_at_decision`, `scoren_at_decision`, `score_net_at_decision`) and an opt-in two-phase `score-diff-reject-ref-latched` decision experiment. The low-level ngspice primitive proves the two-phase topology can resolve clear cases: first `dec` resolves score/scoren into pre-decision rails, then `dec2` compares the reject rail to an `outref` reference. In the integrated strict split-rail 4/4 smoke, however, the candidate did not recover learning (`0.5 -> 0.5`). The new timing diagnostics explain why the earlier score/reject interpretation was too optimistic: the latch sees the second forward window, not the earlier `score_net` measurement. Final eval score at decision time was `+70 mV`, `-40 mV`, `+0.14 mV`, and `-119 mV`; the near-zero negative sample resolved like a positive in the first score latch. This means the next real circuit target should not be a fixed reject threshold on the already-regenerated rail; it should produce better score evidence at the decision window or add a deliberate analog dead-zone/reference before regeneration.
- Generalized pytest ngspice availability handling one step further: `tests/conftest.py` now caches discovery once per session, provides the existing `ngspice_path` fixture for tests that need the executable path, registers a `@pytest.mark.ngspice` marker, and collection-skips tests that either request `ngspice_path` or carry the marker when ngspice is unavailable. This keeps low-level SPICE tests boilerplate-free while preserving a direct path value for subprocess-based tests.
- Added a pre-regeneration score-window decision primitive and exposed it as `score-diff-window-latched` in the MNIST01 block runner. The low-level ngspice test uses two physical offset comparisons, `score > scoren + outref` and `scoren > score + outref`, before any final decision interpretation; it resolves clear decision-time rails from the previous failed smoke (`score-scoren ~= +64 mV` and `-119 mV`) while keeping a near-zero `+0.14 mV` case below a `50 mV` decision margin. Focused regressions passed (`117 passed`). A strict continuous split-rail `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval integrated smoke with restored eligibility and bounded-ref readout writes preserved the fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract but did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`). The new stage did not fix the full deck because final score evidence at the decision window was only about `0.50 mV` class margin and `decision_diff` stayed millivolt-scale; the next target is still upstream score-evidence/readout normalization or gain before regeneration, not a later reject threshold.
- Added a transistor/passive shared-shunt normalizer for readout-gradient storage. The low-level readout-writer primitive now has an opt-in `shared-shunt` path that charges a shared `gnorm` capacitor from active restored eligibility during `acc`, then weakly shunts each local `gvp/gvn` gradient storage node. The ngspice regression proves the intended local contract: a single active feature still updates, while four simultaneously active features have their total signed readout update reduced to less than `65%` of the unnormalized case and keep common-mode motion bounded. The block runner exposes the same circuit plus optional large shunts on gradient-stack internal nodes for timestep conditioning. A strict split-rail `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval integrated smoke with `shared-shunt`, `1e12 Ohm` gradient-stack shunts, restored eligibility, bounded-ref writes, and `score-diff-window-latched` preserved the fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract but still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`): final score margin was only `~0.46 mV`, decision-time score margin was `~0.51 mV`, and the hardware decision stayed same-side. The normalizer is therefore useful as a proven subcomponent and diagnostic knob, but this setting is too weak/late to create class evidence in the full deck. The next aligned experiment should normalize or contrast feature/readout evidence earlier, or add current-mode score gain before regeneration, rather than spending more time on final latch thresholds.
- Fixed a stage-default mismatch in the block runner: `score-diff-window-latched` is an offset/dead-zone score comparator, but the CLI inherited the ordinary reference-latch default of `1.09 V`. The runner now uses a `50 mV` default only for the score-window stage while preserving `1.09 V` for ordinary output/reference decision stages, and a structural regression checks both the helper and direct netlist emission. Re-running the compact strict split-rail `2-train/2-eval` diagnostic with the corrected default preserved the on-device/no-behavioral contract but still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`). The corrected stage no longer hid behind an overlarge reference, but the final score differential was positive for both held-out classes (`score_net` means `~115 mV` for positives and `~108 mV` for negatives), so the same-side hardware decision was a real upstream score-polarity failure. This rules out the stale `1.09 V` reference as the main explanation for the score-window failure.
- Added an earlier common-activity suppression primitive, `readout_gradient_normalization=shared-gate-shunt`. Unlike the previous `shared-shunt` mode, this one charges the shared `gnorm` rail from active restored eligibility and uses it to pull down the restored gradient gate before `gvp/gvn` storage. The ngspice primitive test proves a real local dead-zone behavior with tuned physical sizing: a single active feature with a modest `4u` restore still moves the signed readout weight by about `62 mV`, while four common-active features are suppressed to less than `1 mV` total signed update. The block runner exposes the same circuit as an opt-in hook. A strict split-rail `2-train/2-eval` MNIST01 diagnostic preserved the full on-device/no-behavioral contract, and the shunt reduced measured readout-gradient L1 from the prior `~6.28` scale to `~3.21`, but it still did not learn (`0.5 -> 0.5`): seven of eight readout weights still moved by more than `10 mV`, most ended at the bounded extremes (`vwp ~= 0.7`, `vwn ~= 0`), and final score evidence remained positive for both classes. This makes the gate shunt a useful low-level primitive but not enough by itself; the next circuit change needs class-contrastive update direction or a writer that avoids rail-equivalent motion under repeated same-polarity gradients, not only more common-activity suppression.
- Compared compact strict split-rail `2-train/2-eval` diagnostics after switching the score/error path to `error_topology=binary-descent`. This was the larger integrated improvement, not the `shared-gate-shunt` itself. With binary descent and no shunt, readout-gradient L1 fell to `~0.355`, readout-gradient/update participation stayed at `4/8` features, and final score evidence separated only weakly (`score_net` class margin `~2.11 mV`, `decision_diff` margin `~1.19 mV`). Adding `shared-gate-shunt` made the numbers very similar (`~0.329` readout-gradient L1, `4/8` active/update features, `score_net` margin `~2.23 mV`, `decision_diff` margin `~1.26 mV`). Reducing the score-window reference to `1 mV` did not fix headline accuracy because both class means remained positive; only a shifted `decision_diff` threshold separated the two held-out samples. Conclusion: binary descent fixed much of the same-polarity update pathology, but the next aligned circuit target is physical score/readout gain and centering before regeneration, not more common-activity shunting or post-hoc threshold interpretation.
- Added a low-level dynamic score-gain/window decision primitive for the current millivolt-margin blocker. The new `score-diff-gain-window` ngspice deck first precharges `score_amp/scoren_amp` capacitors, discharges them through score/scoren-controlled NMOS devices during the sense phase, then applies the existing two-sided score-window latch to the amplified rails. This is transistor/passive only and is regression-tested on the integrated operating scale: a direct `50 mV` score window rejects a `2.2 mV` raw score margin, while the dynamic gain/window stage resolves that tiny positive case (`score_gain_diff ~= 44 mV`, full decision rail) and still rejects a `90 uV` near-neutral case. The block runner now exposes the same motif as `output_decision_stage=score-diff-gain-window-latched`, with measurements for `score_amp/scoren_amp` and the post-gain window margins. This is not a learning claim yet; it is the next SPICE-backed subcomponent candidate for turning binary-descent's tiny score evidence into a robust physical decision before running long MNIST transients.
- Checked the dynamic score-gain/window stage inside integrated MNIST01 smokes. Two compact binary-descent `2x2` probes were not useful because the slices were trivially separable and produced no readout-gradient or signed readout-weight movement. The active `4x4 b2 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval restored-eligibility + bounded-reference run did move the readout (`3/8` features above `10 mV`, readout-gradient L1 `~=2.74`), but the final score evidence was wrong-sign (`score_net_margin ~= -26.8 mV`, `score_net_at_decision_margin ~= -27.2 mV`) and both hardware decision rails saturated same-side. This separates the primitive from the system blocker: score-gain/window is a valid sign-preserving decision primitive, but integrated learning now needs upstream score/readout evidence generation and centering, not more final latch sensitivity.
- Tried the next upstream hypothesis in a compact integrated probe: split-rail row-pulsed hidden forward plus isolated conductance-row readout with a `30 kOhm` score load and the score-gain/window decision stage. The strict 4x4 `2` train / `2` eval run preserved the no-behavioral/no-Python-update contract and completed in `27.5 s`, but it was only a trivial separability case (`1.0 -> 1.0`, `nontrivial_learning_met=false`). More importantly, the actual score evidence stayed wrong-sign (`final score_net_margin ~= -3.25 mV`) while the bounded readout writer moved `7/8` features and pushed most readout pairs to the bounded extremes (`vwp ~= 0.7`, `vwn ~= 0`). Isolated conductance-row readout plus a finite score load is therefore not by itself the missing score-centering fix; the next candidate should constrain/update readout direction earlier, or add class-contrastive normalization before the readout weights rail-equivalent.
- Added a physical low-conductance floor for the bounded-reference readout writer. The new `readout_weight_update_low_floor` knob changes the low write reference from `negative_ref-span` to `max(negative_ref-span, floor)` and is covered by ngspice: the old strong positive bounded-ref write with `span=0.34` drives the opposite readout half to `~0 V`, while `floor=0.20 V` keeps it above `180 mV` and still produces a large positive signed update. In the same compact split-rail / isolated-conductance-row / gain-window `4x4` `2` train / `2` eval probe, the floor worked electrically (`vwn` stopped at `0.2 V`, readout signed L1 dropped from `~3.09` to `~1.69`) but did not fix learning or score centering: the run remained a trivial `1.0 -> 1.0` case and final `score_net_margin` stayed `~-3.25 mV`. This narrows the blocker again: preventing low-side erasure is useful hygiene, but the current integrated path still needs class-contrastive update direction or score/readout centering before the writer saturates.
- Added a stronger low-level alternating-update readout-writer primitive. The same stored differential readout pair now receives a positive error sample and then a negative error sample in one ngspice transient; the regression proves the bounded-reference/low-floor writer first moves the signed pair positive, then reverses it negative, while keeping both halves above the low floor and common-mode bounded. This rules out a local one-way writer defect: the integrated score failures are coming from the direction/scale/centering of the generated gradients, not because the physical writer cannot reverse a weight pair.
- Added `error_topology=label-descent` to the standalone score/error primitive and the MNIST01 block runner. The primitive is MOS/passive only (`dp ~= targetp`, `dn ~= targetn` during `err`), has ngspice polarity coverage for positive and negative labels, and keeps the no-label neutral case in the dead zone. Focused regressions passed (`169 passed`). In the compact strict split-rail / isolated-conductance-row / gain-window `4x4` `4` train / `4` eval probe with the low-floor `span=0.34` writer, label descent fixed the binary-descent starvation mode (`readout_gradient_active_features_25mv=7/8`, `readout_signed_updated_features_10mv=7/8`, `readout_signed_delta_l1 ~=4.72` versus binary `~1.5e-5`) but overcorrected: most readout pairs hit bounded extremes and the final score margin flipped wrong-sign (`score_net_margin ~= -44.1 mV`). Reducing only the bounded write span to `0.12` kept the on-device/no-behavioral contract, broad updates, and positive final score margin (`~4.72 mV`), with analog `out_after` best-threshold accuracy `1.0`, but the gain/window decision stage still saturated same-side because `score_amp/scoren_amp` remained tied (`score_gain_diff ~= -60 pV`). The current best interpretation is that class-contrastive pressure plus smaller bounded writes is closer than binary starvation or full-span label slam, but the next circuit target must sense/amplify very low absolute score rails before regeneration, not just add more final threshold tuning.
- Added a low-common-mode score decision primitive and found/fixed a sequencing bug in the integrated two-phase decision stages. The old `score-diff-gain-window` uses NMOS score inputs and has essentially zero gain when the final score rails sit near ground; a new `score-diff-low-gain` primitive uses PMOS score inputs, resolves `score-scoren ~= +/-5.87 mV` at low common-mode in ngspice, and is exposed in the block runner as `score-diff-low-gain-latched`. While integrating it, the compact probe exposed that `dec2` overlapped the next sample reset/precharge (`dec2` previously extended past the 16 ns cycle boundary), so decision rails were being measured after reset had driven them same-side high. The schedule now ends `dec2` at `15.95 ns` and measures two-phase decisions at `15.97 ns`; a regression locks this ordering. Focused SPICE-backed regressions pass (`173 passed`). In the strict split-rail / isolated-conductance-row / label-descent / bounded-span-0.12 `4x4` `4` train / `4` eval probe, the low-gain stage preserved the no-Python/no-behavioral contract and turned the former picovolt gain failure into a real separable hardware margin (`final decision_diff_margin ~=10.5 mV`, best shifted-threshold accuracy `1.0`; `8u/12u` latch sizing improved this to `~20.4 mV`). It still did not meet the fixed-threshold accuracy contract (`0.5 -> 0.5`) because the sensed margin is centered around a small positive threshold, not restored to rails. The next aligned circuit target is therefore a physical centering/restoration stage for the low-gain sensed margin, or a readout/error change that produces true negative-class `scoren` evidence, rather than more Python threshold interpretation.
- Added a referenced low-common-mode score decision stage (`score-diff-low-gain-ref-latched`) and a low-level ngspice regression that proves real centering behavior: after PMOS-input score gain, a physical `outref` current makes a `4.7 mV` low-common-mode score margin resolve positive while a smaller `2.0 mV` positive margin and a negative margin resolve negative. The first integrated attempt appeared to fail because the new two-phase stage was still measured at `15.50 ns`, before `dec2` rose at `15.60 ns`; extending the two-phase measurement offset to the new referenced/windowed low-gain stages fixed that harness bug and is now covered structurally. With a passive `0.165 V` divider reference and smaller `8u/12u` decision devices, the strict split-rail / isolated-conductance-row / label-descent / bounded-span-0.12 `4x4` 4-train/4-eval probe preserved the fully-on-device contract and improved from `0.5 -> 1.0` using the fixed zero sign of the physical `decision_diff` rails. Final `decision_diff` means were about `+14.8 mV` for positives and `-6.38 mV` for negatives, worst fixed-threshold margin was `5.65 mV`, `readout_signed_updated_features_10mv=7/8`, and the shifted-threshold caveat cleared. This is still only a binary 4x4 rung, but it gives a SPICE-proven path from low-common-mode score evidence to a centered differential hardware decision without behavioral learning devices or Python threshold fitting.
- Scaled the referenced low-common-mode decision path from 4/4 to strict 6-train/6-eval and isolated a readout-write magnitude issue. Keeping the previous bounded writer span at `0.12` failed (`0.5 -> 0.5`): readout features participated, but most pairs were driven between bounded extremes by each label, so the final weights followed the last few training samples and the held-out score margin collapsed to `~-0.16 mV`. Sweeping only the physical bounded write span recovered the same 6/6 slice at `0.04`, `0.06`, and `0.08`; a full `span=0.06` diagnostic preserved the full on-device/no-behavioral contract and improved `0.5 -> 1.0` with final score-net-at-decision margin `~6.52 mV`, final `decision_diff` margin `~27.7 mV`, fixed zero-threshold worst margin `~5.65 mV`, broad hidden/readout participation (`7/8` active features and updates), and bounded readout common-mode motion (`~134 mV` max). A compact target-shape `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 1-train/1-eval convergence probe with the same `span=0.06` completed in one strict continuous transient (`target_10x10_b4_stride2_c2_topology=true`, no Python weight transfer, no behavioral learning devices) in `~288 s`; it is only a convergence/contract check because the one-sample split was all negative and did not demonstrate learning. The next scaling work should use the smaller write span as the default operating point for balanced target-shape 2/2 or larger probes, then address target runtime and true multiclass/full-MNIST generalization.
- Promoted the smaller bounded-reference readout write span into the block-runner default. The previous generic default (`0.15`) was no longer aligned with the best strict device-level evidence: `0.12` already showed last-label domination on the 6/6 slice, while `0.04--0.08` recovered learning and `0.06` has the strongest diagnostic record so far. The CLI and direct `block_netlist` default now use `readout_weight_update_span=0.06`; the bounded-ref writer regression checks the resulting physical references (`vwhi_ref=0.42 V`, `vwlo_ref=0.28 V`) while retaining explicit larger-span coverage.
- Ran the first balanced target-shape `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 2-train/2-eval strict continuous ngspice transient using the new default bounded span (`0.06`), split-rail row-pulsed hidden forward, isolated conductance-row readout, label descent, and the referenced low-common-mode decision stage. The deck preserved the target topology and full on-device/no-behavioral contract with balanced train/eval labels, but it did not meet nontrivial learning (`0.5 -> 0.5`) after `~508.6 s`. The encouraging part is that analog class evidence improved at target shape: final `score_net_at_decision` margin was `~28.9 mV`, `decision_diff` class margin was `~371.6 mV`, and a shifted `decision_diff` threshold would classify the two held-out samples perfectly. The blocking part is still physical centering: both final `decision_diff` samples remained on the same side of the fixed zero threshold (`worst fixed-threshold margin ~= -101 mV`), so the contract reported `"accuracy signal is separable only with a shifted threshold"`. This makes the next target-shape work a centering/common-mode problem in the low-common decision/readout path, not a failure of strict continuous transient generation or balanced sample scheduling.
- Re-ran the same balanced target-shape `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 2-train/2-eval strict continuous deck with only the physical low-common decision reference changed from `0.165 V` to `0.25 V`. A low-level ngspice regression now locks the mechanism using the measured target-scale unipolar score rails: `score=55.2 mV, scoren=0` resolves positive, while `score=26.3 mV, scoren=0` resolves negative through the same transistor/passive `score-diff-low-gain-ref` primitive. Integrated behavior matched: the run preserved the full on-device/no-behavioral contract, target topology, realistic balanced order, and batch-1 continuous transient, and improved fixed-threshold hardware accuracy from `0.5 -> 1.0` after `~399.7 s`. Final `score_net_at_decision` margin remained `~28.9 mV`; the physical decision produced `decision_diff ~= +58.5 mV` for the positive eval sample and `~-9.75 mV` for the negative eval sample, clearing the shifted-threshold caveat (`worst fixed-threshold margin ~=9.75 mV`). This is the first balanced target-shape nontrivial-learning rung. It is still binary MNIST01 and only two eval samples, so the next work should test whether `0.25 V` is a robust target-shape reference across larger balanced slices/seeds or whether a feature-count/score-scale-normalized reference is needed.
- Scaled the centered target-shape reference from 2/2 to a strict continuous `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, 4-train/4-eval run. The same `0.25 V` passive decision divider, split-rail row-pulsed hidden path, isolated conductance-row readout, label descent, restored eligibility, and bounded-ref span `0.06` preserved the full on-device/no-behavioral contract and again improved fixed-threshold hardware accuracy from `0.5 -> 1.0` after `~878.1 s`. Final `score_net_at_decision` margin was `~26.8 mV`, final `decision_diff` means were `+50.9 mV` for positive eval samples and `-17.0 mV` for negative eval samples, and the worst fixed-threshold margin was `~8.84 mV`; the shifted-threshold caveat stayed clear. This is stronger evidence that the target-shape blocker was a physical score-scale/decision-reference centering issue, not just a one-pair artifact. It remains binary MNIST01 and the runtime is now the immediate scaling pressure: larger target-shape slices should either reduce measurement/deck cost or move toward a feature-count/score-scale-normalized reference before full MNIST claims.
- Added a default-preserving `--tran-step-ps` screening knob to the block runner. The default remains `10 ps`; larger values only change the transient print step, not circuit phase timing, and are recorded in summaries. Focused regressions passed (`178 passed`). A target-shape `4` train / `4` eval screen using `--skip-initial-eval --tran-step-ps 25` preserved the strict train/final-eval continuous transient and final fixed-threshold hardware accuracy (`1.0`) while reducing wall time from the full diagnostic `~878.1 s` to `~340.8 s`. The final score/decision margins were essentially unchanged (`score_net_at_decision` margin `~26.8 mV`, `decision_diff` means `+48.7/-20.4 mV`, worst fixed-threshold margin `~11.7 mV`). Because the initial-eval baseline was intentionally skipped, this screen does not itself demonstrate nontrivial learning; it is evidence that larger target-shape sweeps can use correlated fast-screen settings and reserve full initial/final diagnostics for promoted candidates.
- Used the correlated fast-screen settings on a larger balanced target-shape `8` train / `8` eval slice. The strict train/final-eval continuous transient still preserved the full no-Python-update/no-behavioral contract and final fixed-threshold hardware accuracy stayed at `1.0` after `~855.7 s`. Final `score_net_at_decision` margin increased to `~37.4 mV`, final `decision_diff` means were `+39.6 mV` for positives and `-106.5 mV` for negatives, and worst fixed-threshold margin was `~11.8 mV`; the shifted-threshold caveat remained clear. As with the 4/4 screen, initial eval was skipped, so the summary reports no nontrivial-learning claim. The useful conclusion is that the centered `0.25 V` decision reference survives an 8/8 balanced target-shape screen; the next scaling blocker is now simulator/runtime throughput rather than immediate score-reference collapse on binary MNIST01.
- Promoted the `8` train / `8` eval target-shape result from skipped-baseline screen to a full initial/train/final diagnostic using the same centered `0.25 V` decision path and `--tran-step-ps 25`. The run preserved the target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology, realistic balanced order, strict fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract, and one continuous train/final-eval transient; initial eval was not skipped. Hardware decision accuracy improved from `0.5` to `1.0`, so `nontrivial_learning_met=true`, with final `score_net_at_decision` margin `~37.4 mV`, final `decision_diff` means `+39.6 mV / -106.5 mV`, worst fixed-threshold margin `~11.8 mV`, and no shifted-threshold caveat after `~959.5 s`. The remaining explicit contract issue is scope, not mechanism: this is still binary MNIST01 rather than multiclass/full MNIST.
- Extended the same correlated target-shape fast-screen path to a balanced `10x10 b4 stride2 c2`, `16` train / `16` eval strict continuous transient. The run preserved the full no-Python-update/no-behavioral-learning-device contract, target topology, balanced labels, and fixed-threshold hardware accuracy (`final_eval_accuracy=1.0`) after `~1342.0 s` with `--skip-initial-eval --tran-step-ps 25`. Final `score_net_at_decision` margin stayed high at `~37.3 mV`; final `decision_diff` means were `+26.1 mV` for positives and `-132.5 mV` for negatives, with worst fixed-threshold margin `~5.75 mV` and no shifted-threshold caveat. Because the initial eval was intentionally skipped, this still cannot claim nontrivial learning by itself, but it is the strongest scale-screen evidence so far that the centered `0.25 V` low-common-mode decision path remains electrically centered at target shape. Runtime is now clearly the limiting factor before larger slices or full diagnostics.
- Tried to promote the `16` train / `16` eval target-shape screen to a full initial/train/final diagnostic with the same centered circuit and `--tran-step-ps 25`. The run generated both the initial-eval and train/final-eval decks but ngspice exited nonzero before the script wrote a summary; the old runner error path reported only the stderr tail (`Using SPARSE 1.3 as Direct Linear Solver`) and discarded stdout, so the electrical/simulator cause is not recoverable from this attempt. Added a regression-covered diagnostic improvement to `run_netlist`: nonzero simulator exits now report the deck path, return code, stdout tail, and stderr tail. This does not change circuit behavior, but it makes the next long 16/16 promotion failure actionable instead of opaque.
- Added stage-level telemetry to the block MNIST01 runner for long target-shape diagnostics. The new opt-in `--progress-log` flag prints start/done lines for each SPICE deck (`initial_eval`, `train_final_eval`, `train`, `final_eval`) with sample count, deck path, row count, and elapsed wall time; nonzero exceptions are also wrapped with the failing stage name and deck path. A tiny ngspice smoke verified the CLI path (`initial_eval` and `train_final_eval` progress lines plus `progress_log=true` in the summary), and focused regressions pass (`126 passed` across block-training and sequential-training tests). This is infrastructure rather than new learning evidence, but it removes a practical blocker for retrying the 16/16 full promotion and larger diagnostics.
- Updated `docs/spicenn_progress_note.tex` and the generated PDF with a new "Current Centered Target-Shape Rung" subsection. It now documents the current best device-level binary MNIST01 path (`row-pulsed` split-rail hidden forward, isolated conductance-row readout, `label-descent`, bounded span `0.06`, low floor `0.20 V`, and `score-diff-low-gain-ref-latched` at `0.25 V`) and records the 8/8 full target-shape nontrivial-learning evidence plus the 16/16 skipped-baseline screen caveat. The update built cleanly with `pdflatex` and was pushed to `origin/main` in commit `036f850`.
- Reduced output-only block-training decks further for long target-shape diagnostics. `measurement_detail="outputs"` now emits explicit `save` lists for only the output/decision nodes needed by `.meas`, and no longer emits redundant `.control print out_before/out_after/error_net` commands; `parse_measures` already consumes the `.meas` lines from simulator stdout/stderr. Focused block/sequential regressions pass (`127 passed`). A 16/16 full target-shape promotion using the save-list change is currently running; because it was launched before the print-suppression edit, a retry would be needed for the no-print deck if this run still hits simulator memory pressure.
- The 16-train/16-eval target-shape full promotion completed successfully with the output-only save-list deck. It preserved the target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology, realistic balanced order, strict fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract, and one continuous train/final-eval transient. Initial eval was `0.5`, final eval was `1.0`, so `nontrivial_learning_met=true`; final `score_net_at_decision` margin was `~37.34 mV`, final `decision_diff` means were `+26.1 mV / -132.5 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~=158.6 mV`, worst fixed-threshold margin was `~5.75 mV`, and the shifted-threshold caveat stayed false. Wall time was `~2581.9 s`, with `initial_eval` taking `828.9 s` and `train_final_eval` taking `1750.6 s`. The remaining contract issue is scope only: binary MNIST01, not multiclass MNIST. Compact evidence lives in `results/tables/codex_target10x10_16t16e_outref025_full_tran25_save_summary.json` and the matching CSV.
- Ran a seed/order robustness fast screen for the centered target-shape path using `--seed 1`, `4` train / `4` eval, `--skip-initial-eval`, `--measurement-detail outputs`, `--tran-step-ps 25`, and the committed no-print output-only deck path. The deck preserved the target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology and strict on-device/no-behavioral contract, and final fixed-threshold hardware accuracy was `1.0` after `~350.8 s`. Because initial eval was skipped, this is not a nontrivial-learning claim, but it is useful centering evidence under a different balanced sample order: final `score_net_at_decision_margin ~=46.7 mV`, final `decision_diff` means were `+41.7 mV / -148.4 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~=190.0 mV`, worst fixed-threshold margin was `~39.8 mV`, and the shifted-threshold caveat stayed false. The generated decks and duplicate `spice/results` copies were cleaned; compact evidence lives in `results/tables/codex_target10x10_4t4e_seed1_outref025_skipinit_tran25_summary.json` and the matching CSV.
- Added regression coverage for the shared `ngspice_path` pytest fixture and collection-time skip policy. Tests that request `ngspice_path` or carry the `ngspice` marker are now explicitly covered by a lightweight unit test that verifies they are skipped when the executable is unavailable and left unmarked when it is present. This locks in the requested general mechanism so low-level SPICE-backed tests do not need to repeat local `shutil.which("ngspice")` / `pytest.skip(...)` boilerplate. Focused test: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` (`2 passed`).
- Completed a target-shape random trainable-state screen using `--weight-seed 1`, `4` train / `4` eval, `--skip-initial-eval`, `--measurement-detail outputs`, and `--tran-step-ps 25`. The centered `0.25 V` low-common-mode decision path again preserved the strict fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract and target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology, with final fixed-threshold hardware accuracy `1.0` after `~511.8 s`. Because the initial baseline was skipped, this is not a nontrivial-learning claim, but it is useful evidence that the path survives a different random capacitor/weight initialization: final `score_net_at_decision_margin ~=28.2 mV`, final `decision_diff` means were `+15.2 mV / -118.8 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~=134.0 mV`, worst fixed-threshold margin was `~13.8 mV`, and the shifted-threshold caveat stayed false. Generated decks and duplicate `spice/results` copies were cleaned; compact evidence lives in `results/tables/codex_target10x10_4t4e_wseed1_outref025_skipinit_tran25_summary.json` and the matching CSV. The run also exposed a metadata gap: summaries did not record sample-order seed or weight seed, so the runner now records `seed` and `weight_seed` in future summaries. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_ngspice_availability.py tests/test_device_mnist01_block_training.py::test_summary_records_sample_and_weight_seeds -q` (`3 passed`).
- Ran a mild deterministic perturbation target-shape screen on the same centered path: `5 mV` input rail jitter, `20 ps` phase jitter, `1%` passive mismatch, `5 mV` initial-state mismatch, and `perturbation_seed=11`, with `4` train / `4` eval, `--skip-initial-eval`, `--measurement-detail outputs`, and `--tran-step-ps 25`. The run preserved the strict fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract and target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology, and final fixed-threshold hardware accuracy remained `1.0` after `~435.5 s`. This is still a skipped-baseline robustness screen rather than a nontrivial-learning claim, but it is important margin evidence under simultaneous deterministic stress: final `score_net_at_decision_margin ~=27.9 mV`, final `decision_diff` means were `+133.7 mV / -21.8 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~=155.5 mV`, worst fixed-threshold margin was `~10.6 mV`, and the shifted-threshold caveat stayed false. Generated decks and duplicate `spice/results` copies were cleaned; compact evidence lives in `results/tables/codex_target10x10_4t4e_mildpert11_outref025_skipinit_tran25_summary.json` and the matching CSV.
- Promoted that mild deterministic perturbation target-shape screen to a full initial/train/final diagnostic using the same stress settings (`5 mV` input rail jitter, `20 ps` phase jitter, `1%` passive mismatch, `5 mV` initial-state mismatch, `perturbation_seed=11`). The run preserved the strict fully-on-device/no-behavioral contract, target `10x10 b4 stride2 c2` topology, realistic balanced ordering, and one continuous train/final-eval transient; initial eval was not skipped. Hardware decision accuracy improved from `0.5` to `1.0`, so `nontrivial_learning_met=true` under deterministic stress. Final `score_net_at_decision_margin ~=27.9 mV`, final `decision_diff` means were `+133.7 mV / -21.8 mV`, `decision_diff_margin ~=155.5 mV`, worst fixed-threshold margin was `~10.6 mV`, and the shifted-threshold caveat remained false. Wall time was `~640.9 s` (`initial_eval ~=199.1 s`, `train_final_eval ~=440.5 s`). The only remaining objective issue reported by the summary is scope: binary MNIST01, not multiclass/full MNIST. Generated decks and duplicate `spice/results` copies were cleaned; compact evidence lives in `results/tables/codex_target10x10_4t4e_mildpert11_outref025_full_tran25_summary.json` and the matching CSV.
- Ran a tiny 3-class MNIST fixed8 direct-flow device smoke to start closing the multiclass gap. The path is still the older dense random-hidden runner, not the current target-shape block topology, but it exercises per-class score rails, one-vs-rest error rails, and transistor/passive readout writes in one ngspice transient. On `mnist3fixed8_6` it improved score accuracy from `0.167 -> 0.667` and output accuracy from `0.333 -> 0.833` in `~22.2 s`, with readout weights moving (`max_abs_total_readout_weight_signed_delta_v ~=53.3 mV`). The result is explicitly not a success rung yet: final score/output margins were still negative (`~-40.9 mV` score, `~-6.16 mV` output), and common-mode readout movement was too large relative to signed movement (`readout_common_to_signed_delta_ratio ~=5.08`). Added summary labeling/mirroring so these multiclass direct-flow runs no longer describe themselves as binary-only experiments and leave compact evidence in `results/tables/codex_mnist3fixed8_6_random_pmos_smoke_summary.json`. The next promising direction is to port the centered target-shape binary readout/decision discipline into a generalized multiclass output head rather than continuing to tune this legacy direct-flow branch in isolation.
- Refactored the strongest centered target-shape decision primitive (`score-diff-low-gain-ref`) into a prefixable emitter and wired the binary block runner to use the shared primitive implementation. This is scaffolding for a K-class output head: each class can now own independent `score/scoren`, gain, and `decision/decisionn` nodes without hand-renaming the latch/preamp devices. Added a low-level ngspice regression that instantiates two prefixed class-local copies in one deck with the target-scale unipolar score cases (`55.2 mV` resolves positive, `26.3 mV` resolves negative against `outref=0.25 V`), proving the reusable primitive resolves independently and has no hidden single-output node collision. Full score-decision primitive regressions pass (`19 passed`). This does not yet make the target-shape trainer multiclass; it removes one concrete single-output assumption from the output/decision primitive we need to duplicate per class.
- Added a standalone class-local differential output-head primitive for the next multiclass rung. The ngspice deck instantiates three class-local readout weight pairs (`c{i}_vwp0/c{i}_vwn0`), one-hot `targetp/targetn` label-descent rails, transistor/passive gradient storage, bounded-ref readout writes, and class-local differential score rails. With class 1 as the target, the SPICE run updates the target signed readout weight up by `~60.0 mV`, pushes both non-target signed readouts down by `~80.0 mV`, and then produces a target score-net winner margin of `~6.76 mV` during the post-update forward window. Focused regression `tests/test_multiclass_output_head_primitive.py` passes (`3 passed`) and actually calls ngspice through the shared `ngspice_path` fixture. This is still a primitive, not MNIST training, but it proves the class-local output/write machinery needed before duplicating the centered binary block output head across K classes.
- Added a continuous class-local multiclass output-head sequence runner. It runs initial eval, train, and final eval in one uninterrupted transient; Python supplies only fixed feature rails, one-hot class label rails, and phase rails, while every class/feature readout weight remains a capacitor pair updated by transistor/passive bounded-ref writers. A low-level ngspice regression proves the circuit learns a 3-class one-hot sequence from chance/tie behavior to perfect final eval with positive score margins. On a balanced real `mnist3fixed8_12` output-only slice (`6` train / `6` eval, `8` fixed features), the same circuit did not improve (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final min margin `~-8.46 mV`); reducing non-target rail strength to `0.25` or `0.0` also failed (`0.333 -> 0.167`). This is useful negative evidence: class-local capacitor writes and continuous state retention work, but simple full/weak one-vs-rest label-descent on fixed8 features is not enough for MNIST3. The next circuit/algorithm target is a score-dependent multiclass error rail or normalized/competitive class update, not more single-sample output-head plumbing.
- Added an experimental score-gated nontarget update mode to the continuous multiclass output-head sequence. The deck now can run an early class-local forward window, retain the score caps as physical error modulators, reset score rails after update, and then run the post-update scoring window. This keeps the error modulation inside the transistor/passive deck rather than using Python-derived error values. The ngspice one-hot sequence regression still passes, but the balanced `mnist3fixed8_12` screen got worse (`0.333 -> 0.167`, final min margin effectively zero). Final signed weight telemetry shows why: the score-gated path mostly performs positive target potentiation while score-gated nontarget depression is too weak/biased (`final_signed_weight_sums_by_class_v ~= [0.520, 0.340, 0.579]`). So the existing low-common score cap is not strong enough as a direct MOS gate for softmax-like negative pressure. Next target: restore/amplify score-derived error rails or use a real competitive/normalizing error primitive before applying class-local writes.
- Added two opt-in transistor/passive multiclass regularization experiments to the continuous split-rail block sequence. `error_mode=amplified-score-competitive` reuses the class-local low-gain score preamps and adds target-side competitive branches: when the current class is the label target, physically amplified opponent score rails add extra positive write pressure and directly pull the restored positive write gate lower. A low-level ngspice primitive now proves that a high opponent score gate increases `gvp`, lowers `rgp`, and produces a stronger signed positive readout update; the focused multiclass block suite passes under the shared `ngspice_path` fixture. Integrated evidence was active but not yet a breakthrough: `mnist3fixed8_6` with target-only class bias stayed at `0.333 -> 0.667`, while `mnist3fixed8_12` stayed at `0.333 -> 0.500` with essentially the same negative worst margin (`~-0.582 mV`) as amplified-score nontarget plus target-only bias. Also added an opt-in passive readout-center leak (`--readout-center-resistance`, `--readout-center-voltage`) and a low-level ngspice test proving it pulls `vwp/vwn` toward neutral and reduces signed state. In 12-sample screens, 10 MOhm and 5 MOhm leaks damped learned signed weights but did not improve accuracy or margin (`0.333 -> 0.500`, worst margin `~-0.589/-0.597 mV`). Conclusion: these knobs are useful physical probes/regularizers, but simple target boost or always-on leakage is not enough; the next multiclass move should be class-conditional centering/normalization or a duplicated low-common-mode decision/error path rather than more unconditional damping.
- Added a class-local `restored-score-binary-descent` mode to the continuous multiclass split-rail block runner. This duplicates the low-common-mode score decision per class and uses the physical `decision/decisionn` latch outputs as error gates: target classes receive positive write pressure only through the `decisionn`/miss rail, while nontarget classes receive negative write pressure only through the `decision`/false-positive rail. Low-level ngspice coverage proves the intended signs (`targetp*decisionn` increases signed readout state, `targetn*decision` decreases it), and the integrated one-hot multiclass regression still learns all three classes with positive diagonal and negative off-diagonal signed weights. On real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only class bias, the new mode remained active but did not break the plateau: `0.333 -> 0.500`, worst final margin `~-0.582 mV`, matching the current amplified-score/target-bias rung. Also added `class_bias_mode=label-descent` as a physical signed bias-row option. Its primitive behavior is correct (target bias positive, nontarget biases negative), but the balanced 12-sample MNIST3 run regressed to baseline (`0.333 -> 0.333`, worst margin `~-19.6 mV`) because all class bias rows ended negative by the final eval. A quick `nontarget_scale=0.5` screen on the amplified target-bias path hit an ngspice timestep failure at a write stack, so it is not counted as evidence. Conclusion: class-local decision-conditioned errors are now scaffolded and tested, but the current fixed per-class threshold is not enough to solve MNIST3 centering. The next useful topology should compare classes against each other or against a physical shared/common-mode score reference, not just each class against its own score/scoren latch.
- Added `pairwise-binary-descent` as a more literal class-competitive error mode in the continuous multiclass split-rail block runner. The deck reuses the pairwise score-decision latches, then gates positive target writes with opponent-beats-class rails and negative nontarget writes with class-beats-opponent rails. Low-level ngspice proves the isolated primitive signs: target-losing rails strengthen positive signed writes, and nontarget-winning rails strengthen negative signed writes. The integrated one-hot control keeps correct final class predictions, but the signed matrix is intentionally sparse because the rule only writes on pairwise losses/wins instead of forcing every diagonal positive. On real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only bias, the mode was not viable yet: final accuracy dropped to `0.167`, although the worst final margin magnitude improved to `~-0.335 mV` from the current `~-0.582 mV` plateau. That suggests pairwise comparison is directionally useful for centering but too conditional when it replaces the base write path. Next target: blend pairwise competition with the stronger amplified-score/target-bias path, rather than using pairwise decisions as the only gradient source.
- Added `amplified-score-pairwise` as that blended multiclass error mode. It keeps the `amplified-score-nontarget` base path, so target and score-gated nontarget writes still get continuous learning pressure, then adds physical pairwise correction branches driven by the class-vs-class decision rails. The first implementation tried to make the correction too small and restore-gate-only; ngspice showed why that was wrong: tiny parallel correction stacks were convergence-sensitive, while restore-gate-only branches had almost no effect because the base path already pulled the restored gates close to ground. The committed correction uses a `16u` pairwise branch into both gradient storage and restored gates, with regression coverage proving that the base target write remains active and a nontarget-winning pairwise rail adds a much stronger negative signed update. The one-hot integrated control still learns all three classes. Real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only bias remained at the current plateau (`0.333 -> 0.500`, worst final margin `~-0.582 mV`), and a reduced `nontarget_scale=0.5` variant hit an ngspice timestep failure at `c1_f0_gvn_a`, so it is not counted as learning evidence. Conclusion: branch blending is now scaffolded and tested, but it is not the missing multiclass centering mechanism. The next useful move should be a physical shared/common-mode class reference or normalization stage for class scores/errors, not more additive write branches.
- Added `common-ref-score-nontarget` as the first physical shared class-score reference experiment. The deck passively averages the class-local `score_amp` rails onto a `score_common` capacitor through equal resistors, then each class builds a transistor `score_common_gate` by competing its own `score_amp` against that shared rail. A low-level ngspice primitive proves the intended local behavior on forced score-amp rails (`score_common ~=0.45 V`; high/mid/low class gates separate monotonically), and the one-hot integrated control still reaches correct final predictions. The real `mnist3fixed8_12` target-bias run regressed to baseline accuracy (`0.333 -> 0.333`) while slightly improving worst margin magnitude (`~-0.523 mV` versus the `~-0.582 mV` plateau). The one-hot telemetry also exposed a weakness: in small integrated decks the class `score_amp` rails are nearly identical, so the common gate can become a mostly common-mode nontarget suppressor instead of a strong class-contrast signal. Conclusion: a passive common reference is useful scaffolding, but using the existing weak score preamp as its input is not enough. The next multiclass centering attempt should generate a stronger class-contrast signal before the common reference, likely from raw score-net/low-common decision primitives or a shared differential current normalizer rather than simply averaging `score_amp`.
- Added `raw-common-ref-score-nontarget` to drive the same physical class-common reference directly from raw class `score` rails instead of the compressed `score_amp` rails. The raw scores were too low for nominal-threshold common-gate devices, so this mode uses the existing low-threshold `NSENSE` devices only in the score-vs-common gate; the shared rail itself is still just passive resistors plus a capacitor. Low-level ngspice now proves raw rail tracking at the actual low-common scale (`score_common ~=60 mV` for `120/60/0 mV` rails, with monotonic gates), and the one-hot integrated control remains correct. The real `mnist3fixed8_12` target-bias result was effectively unchanged from the preamp-common version (`0.333 -> 0.333`, worst final margin `~-0.523 mV`). This rules out "the PMOS preamp average compressed away all useful contrast" as the only explanation. The common-reference family is useful infrastructure, but the current multiclass block still needs a stronger physical class-contrast/error-generation primitive, not just a different tap point for averaging.
- Added `amplified-score-binary-descent` as a blended multiclass error mode. It keeps the amplified score-gated nontarget base path and adds class-local low-common score-decision correction branches: target misses (`decisionn`) add positive write pressure, while nontarget false positives (`decision`) add negative write pressure. Structural coverage proves the mode emits both the score preamp/decision latch and the base `score_amp` nontarget path without falling back to behavioral sources. Low-level ngspice coverage proves the base target/nontarget writes remain active and the binary correction is directionally aligned; the target-miss correction is tiny because the base target path already nearly saturates the positive gradient node, while the false-positive nontarget correction is strong. The one-hot integrated ngspice control still reaches final predictions `[0, 1, 2]`. Real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only bias matched the current plateau (`0.333 -> 0.500`, worst final margin `~-0.582 mV`) in `~49.3 s`; compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_mnist3fixed8_12_t6e6_amplified_binary_bias_scorecap5_summary.json`. Conclusion: another additive correction branch is not enough. The next multiclass direction should move to a stronger physical class-contrast/error primitive, likely current-mode/virtual-ground score normalization or a more direct winner/runner-up error rail, instead of stacking more write-side branches.
- Added `target-ref-score-nontarget` as a label-selected class-contrast experiment. During training the physical `targetp` rail selects the labeled class `score_amp` onto a shared `target_score_ref` capacitor through MOS pass devices; each class then builds a transistor `score_target_gate` by comparing its own `score_amp` against that selected target-score rail. Low-level ngspice proves the selected reference tracks the labeled class score (`target_score_ref ~=0.45 V` for forced `0.75/0.45/0.15 V` scores with class 1 labeled) and the gates order high/target/low. The one-hot integrated control still reaches final predictions `[0, 1, 2]`. Real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only bias regressed to baseline accuracy (`0.333 -> 0.333`, worst final margin `~-0.523 mV`) in `~27.9 s`; compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_mnist3fixed8_12_t6e6_targetref_bias_scorecap5_summary.json`. Conclusion: selecting the target score as a physical reference is valid infrastructure, but using it as the only nontarget depression gate is too conditional. The next error primitive should keep continuous nontarget pressure while deriving a stronger normalized/current-mode class contrast, not simply replace the base pressure with target-relative gating.
- Tried an uncommitted `amplified-score-target-ref` variant that kept the continuous amplified-score nontarget base path and added a target-relative correction branch. The direct `gvn` correction branch was locally effective at moderate analog gate levels, but full-rail/high-score cases repeatedly produced ngspice timestep failures in the integrated `mnist3fixed8_12` deck around `gvn_tcorr_*` stack nodes, even after adding high-value DC leakage. A restore-gate-only version was convergence-stable but had no measurable effect on `rgn` or signed readout movement in the isolated primitive. I removed the mode instead of committing unstable or inert code. This reinforces the current conclusion: the next useful multiclass step should be a proper normalized/current-mode score-error primitive, not another parallel write-stack correction.
- Added a standalone multiclass score-error primitive to `run_score_error_primitive.py`. This tests a physical approximation to `y - p`: first accumulate a shared `nontarget_mass` rail from the labeled nontarget score rails using MOS stacks, then drive the target class positive error from that mass while each nontarget class gets negative error from its own score. This is still transistor/passive LEVEL=1 SPICE: no behavioral sources, no Python-derived error values, and no Python weight updates. Ngspice-backed tests prove the intended signs for target and nontarget classes, score-tracking nontarget magnitude, smaller positive target pressure for a clear target, and a no-label dead zone. The script-level multiclass run passed all 5 cases with `5 mV` classification margin (`aligned=12`, `dead_zone=3` across the three class error rails). Representative measured rails: for `target1_high_wrong0`, `err0=-711 mV`, `err1=+673 mV`, `err2=-126 mV`, `nontarget_mass=711 mV`; for `target0_clear`, target positive pressure dropped to `+197 mV`. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_multiclass_score_error_mass_summary.json` and the matching CSV. Next step is to map this primitive into the multiclass block writer path carefully, likely replacing fixed target-positive pressure and score-gated nontarget pressure with these `dp_i/dn_i` rails rather than adding another parallel correction stack.
- Integrated that multiclass score-error mass primitive into the continuous multiclass split-rail block runner as `error_mode=score-mass-descent`. The new mode builds physical `score_nontarget_mass`, `cX_errp`, and `cX_errn` rails from class score preamp nodes and one-hot target rails, then routes the readout writer through those error rails directly rather than adding another correction branch. Structural tests check the emitted transistor/passive topology has no behavioral sources, and ngspice-backed low-level tests prove target-positive/nontarget-negative write signs, target pressure tracking nontarget mass, and one-hot integrated predictions. Focused suite: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_score_error_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` -> `83 passed`. On real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only class bias, the default 32u mass/error mode matched the plateau (`0.333 -> 0.500`, worst final margin `~-0.582 mV`, `~26.4 s`), and a 64u/64u width screen was essentially identical (`0.333 -> 0.500`, same worst margin, `~24.1 s`). This is useful negative evidence: the physically normalized `y-p`-like error rails are active and tested, but rail strength alone is not the missing multiclass centering mechanism. The next useful move is likely score/error centering or winner-runner normalization at the score/current-sense stage, not more write-side branch strength.
- Added `common-score-mass-descent`, which feeds the score-mass error primitive from physical class-vs-common score gates instead of raw `score_amp` rails. This keeps the same direct `errp/errn` writer interface but gives it a centered class-contrast input. The first implementation exposed two useful circuit facts: the mass block was accidentally skipped behind an `elif` when the common reference was emitted, leaving `cX_err*` nodes floating; and the default common-gate rail (`~0.17-0.25 V`) was below the bounded writer threshold. The committed version emits both the common-reference/gate network and the mass/error rails, uses a stronger centered sense ratio plus smaller local mass/error capacitors for this mode, and preserves the older common-ref/target-ref modes unchanged. Low-level ngspice proves centered gate ordering and error polarity, and the integrated one-hot control returns `[0, 1, 2]`. Focused suite: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_score_error_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` -> `86 passed`. On real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only class bias, the centered mode still plateaued at `0.333 -> 0.500`, but improved worst final margin from the raw score-mass `~-0.582 mV` to `~-0.498 mV` in `~25.6 s`; compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_mnist3fixed8_12_t6e6_common_scoremass_bias_scorecap5_summary.json`. Bias screens with raw score-mass and no/label-descent bias both regressed to baseline (`0.333 -> 0.333`, worst margin `~-19.6 mV`). Conclusion: score centering helps the margin but does not solve multiclass separation; next target should be a stronger winner/runner-up or current-normalized decision/error stage, not just common-average centering.
- Added `target-contrast-score-mass-descent`, which physically selects the labeled class `score_amp` onto `target_score_ref`, builds per-class score-above-target gates, and feeds those gates into the same direct `score_nontarget_mass` / `errp` / `errn` writer path. This is a more target-aware variant of the centered mass idea: target positive pressure comes from nontargets above the labeled class, and nontarget negative pressure comes from each class's target-relative gate. Low-level ngspice proves the label reference ordering and error polarity, and the one-hot integrated control remains correct. Focused suite: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_score_error_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` -> `89 passed`. On real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only bias, this did not improve the plateau (`0.333 -> 0.500`, worst final margin `~-0.581 mV`, `~27.8 s`), with compact evidence in `results/tables/codex_mnist3fixed8_12_t6e6_target_contrast_scoremass_bias_scorecap5_summary.json`. Conclusion: target-relative error rails are directionally correct but too conditional/weak for this multiclass block; the best margin among recent modes remains common-average score mass (`~-0.498 mV`). The next useful step should likely leave the error writer alone and improve score readout/normalization itself, e.g. a stronger current-mode class competition or winner-runner score sensor before error generation.
- Added a standalone pairwise score-competition primitive (`spice/run_multiclass_score_competition_primitive.py`) with ngspice-backed tests. The first version usefully failed: the old pairwise latch made only tens of millivolts of differential and left the losing decision node high enough to leak false error current. The committed primitive uses low-common-mode PMOS-input pairwise preamps, a symmetric regenerative pair latch, and PMOS supply-blocking devices controlled by the opposite decision rail before target/opponent error stacks. Low-level tests now prove clear pairwise polarity, target-below-runner-up correction, clear-target dead-zone behavior, near-tie margin-building pressure, and increasing pairwise margin with score gap. The script-level sweep passes all 5 cases (`aligned=8`, `dead_zone=7` across class error rails), with compact evidence in `results/tables/codex_multiclass_score_competition_primitive_summary.json`.
- Promoted the tested pairwise score-competition topology into the continuous multiclass block as `error_mode=pairwise-score-competition-descent`. This mode uses the same low-gain pairwise comparators and `errp/errn` rails as the primitive, then routes the existing readout writer through those rails directly. Focused regression including the new primitive and integrated mode passed (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_score_competition_primitive.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_score_decision_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` -> `89 passed`). The one-hot continuous transient is strong (`0.333 -> 1.000`, final min margin `+0.210 mV`), proving the mode can train class-local state in one uninterrupted run. On real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only bias and the same 6-train/6-eval comparison setup, it did not improve (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final min margin `~-0.523 mV`). This is useful negative evidence: winner/runner-up style gating fixes the weak-pairwise leakage and works on separable one-hot data, but fixed8 MNIST3 still needs better feature/readout centering or a less sparse multiclass error signal rather than another purely conditional competition gate.
- Added `common-score-mass-pairwise-descent`, a blended multiclass error mode that keeps the continuous common-score-mass `errp/errn` capacitors and injects small pairwise score-competition correction current into those same rails instead of adding another writer family. The pairwise branch strength is now explicit as `--score-mass-pairwise-error-scale` / `score_mass_pairwise_error_scale` (default `0.0625`, preserving the original `2u` pairwise branches at the default `32u` mass-error width). Low-level ngspice coverage proves the hybrid adds mistake pressure relative to common-score-mass alone, and the one-hot integrated control still reaches final predictions `[0, 1, 2]`. Focused SPICE-backed regression passed after the change (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_multiclass_score_competition_primitive.py tests/test_score_error_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` -> `103 passed`). On real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only bias, the hybrid was valid but not better: `0.333 -> 0.500`, final min margin `~-0.588 mV`, worse than the current common-score-mass baseline `~-0.498 mV` and close to target-contrast score-mass `~-0.581 mV`. Conclusion: pairwise correction remains useful infrastructure and works on separable one-hot data, but blending more write-side pressure is not the multiclass breakthrough; the next aligned target is a stronger physical class-contrast/current-normalized score-error stage before the writer.
- Screened stronger pairwise injection in the same hybrid mode. `--score-mass-pairwise-error-scale 0.125` converged and produced essentially the same MNIST3 result as the default hybrid (`0.333 -> 0.500`, final min margin `~-0.587 mV`, `~28.3 s`), with compact evidence in `results/tables/codex_mnist3fixed8_12_t6e6_common_scoremass_pairwise0125_bias_scorecap5_summary.json`. Increasing to `0.25` did not produce usable learning evidence: ngspice hit a timestep failure at `c1_f2_gvn_a` around `140.8 ns`. I also tried a low-level common-reference decision primitive as the next class-contrast candidate, but rejected it before commit because the restored `above_common` rails did not separate cleanly from the complementary rails under forced score cases. Current conclusion is sharper: the multiclass blocker is not weak pairwise gain; it is the lack of a robust physical score-normalization/error-generation primitive that produces clean class-contrast rails before the writer.
- Added a standalone analog multiclass score-contrast primitive (`spice/run_multiclass_score_contrast_primitive.py`) plus ngspice-backed tests. The circuit passively averages class score rails onto `score_common`, then each class stores a continuous contrast value on a capacitor using a score-controlled charge path and a common-score-controlled discharge path. It is transistor/passive only and is intended as a pre-writer normalization probe, not a learning rule by itself. Low-level SPICE tests prove that score-amp-scale rails are ordered (`0.75/0.45/0.15 V` gives contrast spread `~246 mV`), equal scores collapse to a dead zone, and larger score gaps produce larger spread; they also document an important limitation: raw millivolt-scale score rails remain quiet (`~0` spread), so this topology needs amplified score rails or different devices before it can replace raw-score logic. Focused regression passed (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_score_contrast_primitive.py tests/test_multiclass_score_competition_primitive.py tests/test_score_error_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` -> `49 passed`). Script-level sweep passed all five cases with compact evidence in `results/tables/codex_multiclass_score_contrast_primitive_summary.json`. Next useful step is to feed this continuous contrast rail into the score-mass/error writer path and compare against `common-score-mass-descent` on one-hot and `mnist3fixed8_12`.
- Fed the analog score-contrast rail into the multiclass score-mass writer as `contrast-score-mass-descent` and added an experimental `contrast-gated-score-mass-descent` variant that converts each contrast cap through a physical score-vs-reference gate before the mass/error rail. The raw contrast mode passes low-level and one-hot integrated tests, but on real `mnist3fixed8_12` it failed at baseline (`0.333 -> 0.333`) and collapsed final predictions to class 2, showing that raw contrast common-mode can still masquerade as class evidence. The gated variant was structurally valid and completed the same strict continuous real-data run, but it over-suppressed the feature writes: final accuracy stayed `0.333 -> 0.333`, all non-bias readout signed deltas were effectively zero, and only the target-only bias feature moved (`~44.7 mV`). Low-level probes explain both failures: fixed-reference gating keeps the right ordering but reduces error rails to `~60 mV`, below the existing restored-gradient/write threshold; passive contrast-common centering either leaves flat cases with common-mode gates or loads the contrast caps; and a quick low-gain contrast comparator produced millivolt-scale/inverted decisions. Conclusion: contrast extraction is useful observability, but the next aligned multiclass circuit target should be a stronger physical class-score normalization/current-sense stage before error generation, not another weak post-contrast gate.
- Extended the standalone multiclass score-contrast primitive with an opt-in `input_stage=low-gain` path. This reuses the PMOS-input low-common-mode score amplifier pattern before the passive class-common / contrast-cap normalizer, so raw millivolt score rails are no longer fed directly into nominal-threshold contrast devices. Ngspice-backed tests now prove the previous raw `low_common` case remains quiet in direct mode, while low-gain mode recovers ordered normalized score caps and an ordered contrast spread (`~4.86 mV`) from `7.5/4.5/1.5 mV` score rails; flat low-common rails stay in the dead zone. A script-level sweep with `--input-stage low-gain --min-margin 0.001` passed all five cases (`ordered=4`, `dead_zone=1`), with compact evidence in `results/tables/codex_multiclass_score_contrast_low_gain_primitive_m1mv_summary.json`. Important limitation: the recovered low-common contrast is still only millivolt scale, so this is a better pre-writer normalization primitive but not yet a strong direct writer rail for `mnist3fixed8_12`.
- Added an opt-in `error_stage=score-mass` to the same low-gain multiclass score-contrast primitive, wiring the normalized contrast caps into the physical score-mass error fragment used by the block runner. The new ngspice test proves that even the low-common recovered contrast case (`contrast_spread ~=4.86 mV`) can produce writer-scale error rails through the transistor/passive mass stage: with target class 1, `err1_diff ~= +361 mV`, `err0_diff ~= -316 mV`, and `err2_diff ~= -310 mV`. The script-level sweep with `--input-stage low-gain --error-stage score-mass --target-class 1 --min-margin 0.001` passed all five contrast cases, and focused regressions including writer/error primitives passed (`66 passed`). Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_multiclass_score_contrast_low_gain_scoremass_primitive_summary.json`. Caveat: this strengthens the pre-writer rail but still applies softmax-like nontarget pressure on flat/equal cases; it needs an integrated block run before it can be counted as multiclass MNIST progress.
- Promoted the low-gain contrast score-mass path into the continuous multiclass block runner as `error_mode=low-gain-contrast-score-mass-descent`. The structural test verifies the intended topology: PMOS low-common score preamps feed high-impedance (`10 MOhm`) score-common averaging, analog contrast caps, and the existing physical score-mass `errp/errn` writer rails. Focused regressions passed after integration (`111 passed`). The one-hot control still learns (`0.333 -> 1.000`), but the real `mnist3fixed8_12` comparison run with target-only bias and scorecap 5 failed at baseline (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final min margin `~-0.523 mV`). This confirms the user's diagnosis: the primitive can create writer-scale rails, but the integrated block still lacks the right target-vs-impostor margin/error alignment and class-evidence centering. I also added `derive_class_evidence_normalizer_sizing()` to `parameter_theory.py` and documented the policy in `docs/parameter_derivation.md`: common-reference impedance, mass/error caps, and target/non-target width ratios are now derived from writer-domain requirements and class count, leaving `error_drive_scale` and future margin/dead-zone scales as the tuning surface rather than broad local knob sweeps.
- Added a standalone target-vs-impostor margin-correction primitive (`spice/run_multiclass_margin_correction_primitive.py`) plus ngspice-backed tests. The primitive shifts the target compare rail down by an analytical margin reference, uses the existing physical low-gain pairwise score comparators, and drives only the target-positive and offending-opponent-negative writer rails. This is still a primitive abstraction for the margin offset, but it gives a low-level contract for the missing handoff: clear target wins stay quiet, ties/barely-winning targets build margin, class permutations select the offending class, and common-mode-shifted score cases preserve direction. Initial full-rail error clocks produced `~1.15 V` rails; width and cap scaling did not fix that because the stack still charged to the rail. The useful analytical correction is to bound the error clock itself as `target_writer_rail + sense_threshold`, which gives writer-domain rails around `77.5 mV` for a target of `80 mV`. Tests passed (`26 passed`), and script-level evidence in `results/tables/codex_multiclass_margin_correction_primitive_summary.json` passes all 7 cases with active rails in writer range rather than full restoration.
- Reworked the margin-correction primitive to remove the source-shift abstraction: the margin is now a weak physical discharge branch on the target-wins pairwise decision node, gated by `targetp` and `scoredec`. The analytical size is `pairwise_pulldown_width * target_margin / score_window`, giving `0.25 um` for a 1 mV margin against the existing 64 um low-gain pairwise family. Integrated this into the continuous block as `error_mode=pairwise-margin-correction-descent`; its `scoreerr` clock is bounded at `0.45 V` and error caps are `0.5 fF`, because lower `~80-320 mV` rails were directionally correct but did not move the existing readout writer. New ngspice tests prove one-sample strict correction in a single continuous transient: a class-1 sample starts tied, receives physical `errp/errn` rails, moves `c1_f0_signed_final` above `10 mV`, and improves the class-1 score margin by more than `10 mV`. Focused regression passed (`106 passed in 346 s`). Evidence runs: one-hot remains `0.333 -> 1.000`; real `mnist3fixed8_12` with target-only bias improves `0.333 -> 0.500` but keeps a negative final min margin (`~-0.576 mV`), so this is progress on the score-to-writer handoff rather than a solved multiclass MNIST path.
- Added a standalone normalization subcircuit library (`spice/run_normalization_subcircuits.py`) with ten candidate score-to-writer approaches: current-sum, common-mode, pairwise-margin, fixed-total-feedback, soft-WTA, charge-share, time-domain, pulse-density, log-domain, and learned-calibration. The first ngspice run usefully failed because all candidates tried to use `1-8 mV` score rails directly as MOS gates, producing effectively zero writer rails. The fix was to put the score-scale conversion inside the subcircuit contract with a low-gain PMOS score-lift stage, then let each candidate normalize readable internal score nodes. A second failure exposed a sequencing issue from using the same pulse to settle the score lift and fire the error stacks; the lift now settles continuously from static score rails, and the testbench normalization pulse stays high through measurement to avoid artificial falling-edge convergence failures. Low-level ngspice coverage now passes for all ten candidates (`tests/test_normalization_subcircuits.py`: `33 passed`), and the script sweep passed all 40 approach/case combinations (`aligned=30`, `dense_bootstrap=10`) with compact evidence in `results/tables/codex_normalization_subcircuits_summary.json`. This is primitive evidence only; none of the ten has been plugged into end-to-end training yet.
- Integrated the normalization subcircuit library into the continuous multiclass block as `error_mode=normalizer-<approach>-descent` for all ten primitive approaches, so the trainer instantiates the same tested `.subckt norm_*` rather than duplicating score/error logic. Structural regression now checks `normalizer-current-sum-descent` emits the subcircuit, routes raw class score caps and target rails through `Xscore_normalizer`, and feeds `c<i>_errp/c<i>_errn` into the existing local writer. Focused regression passed with the new mode included (`tests/test_normalization_subcircuits.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py`: `102 passed`).
