Two scoped end-to-end probes were run after the low-level tests: `normalizer-current-sum-descent` and `normalizer-soft-wta-descent` both learned the one-hot control (`0.333 -> 1.000`, final min margin `~0.496 mV`), but both matched the existing real `mnist3fixed8_12` plateau with target-only bias and scorecap 5 (`0.333 -> 0.500`, final min margin `~-0.582 mV`). Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_onehot_normalizer_current_sum_summary.json`, `codex_onehot_normalizer_soft_wta_summary.json`, and the matching fixed8 summaries. Conclusion: the reusable normalizer handoff is wired and valid, but these two candidates do not solve fixed8 multiclass separation.
- Added `run_normalizer_block_screen.py`, an aggregate runner for `normalizer-<approach>-descent` block modes, plus compact train-time error-rail diagnostics in the multiclass block summary. The reducer now reports `train_target_errdiff_*`, `train_nontarget_errdiff_*`, and raw train `errdiff` rows when a mode exposes `c<i>_errp/c<i>_errn`, so screens can distinguish "same result because the rails are identical" from "same result despite different writer pressure." The strict `mnist3fixed8_12`, 6-train/6-eval, target-only bias, scorecap-5 screen over all ten normalizer modes produced useful negative evidence: six candidates converged and all landed on the same plateau (`0.333 -> 0.500`, final min margin `~-0.582 mV`), while four candidates failed in ngspice in the integrated deck (`pairwise-margin`, `fixed-total-feedback`, `charge-share`, `pulse-density`). The converged modes had substantially different train error magnitudes, for example current-sum target/nontarget means were about `+1.04 V/-1.07 V`, common-mode `+0.48 V/-0.51 V`, and learned-calibration `+0.56 V/-0.59 V`, yet final accuracy/margin were unchanged. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_normalizer_all_mnist3fixed8_12_t6e6_bias_scorecap5_summary.json` and its CSV. This argues the fixed8 blocker is no longer merely writer-domain error amplitude; the next aligned target should inspect score/readout feature evidence and class separability before the normalizer, or redesign the score/current-sense representation rather than adding more normalization variants.
- Added a bounded `--normalizer-error-clock-high` control and screened current-sum at lower writer-clock amplitudes. This tests whether the normalizer plateau was just full-rail saturation. It was partly saturating, but not in a useful smooth way: at `1.2 V`, target/nontarget train errdiff means were about `+1.04/-1.07 V` and fixed8 plateaued at `0.500`; at `0.45 V`, the rails dropped to `+0.405/-0.428 V` but the same plateau and nearly the same final margin remained; at `0.35 V` and `0.30 V`, the rails were `~+0.306/-0.329 V` and `~+0.256/-0.279 V`, and learning collapsed to the `0.333` baseline with near-zero final margin; `0.40 V` hit an integrated ngspice timestep failure. The final readout matrices from the six converged full-rail normalizer modes were also essentially identical, differing by only `~1e-6 V` despite different error amplitudes. Conclusion: the existing error-rail writer handoff is behaving like a narrow thresholded update, not a healthy analog gradient transport. The next circuit target should be a non-saturating writer/gradient-storage path or a better readout evidence representation, not Bayesian tuning of the normalizer clock.
- Corrected the writer direction back toward the intended "gradient flow" architecture. The existing readout writer primitives use `gvp/gvn` and `rgp/rgn` as sampled gradient proxies followed by a separate `apply` phase, which is useful debug scaffolding but not the user's target of live on-device weight updates. Added `generate_live_update_netlist()` as a direct coincident update primitive: eligibility and descent rails overlap, and transistor/passive stacks move the persistent `vwp/vwn` capacitors during that same window, with no gradient-storage caps and no `Vapply` phase. Ngspice regressions prove sign correctness (`positive` increases signed weight by `>200 mV`, `negative` decreases by `<-200 mV`, `none` stays quiet), require coincident eligibility, and show monotonic positive update versus usable descent amplitude (`0.30 V < 0.45 V < 0.60 V`). Focused writer tests pass (`tests/test_readout_writer_primitive.py`: `27 passed`). This is still a primitive, not integrated MNIST learning, and it retains expected MOS threshold behavior at very small rails; the next aligned step is to use this live-update style for readout/hidden updates instead of further tuning sampled gradient storage.
- Integrated that live update style into the continuous class-local multiclass output-head sequence as `writer_mode=live`. In this mode the deck emits no `Vacc`, `Vapply`, `Vapplyn`, `Cgvp/Cgvn`, or `Crgp/Crgn`; feature eligibility and label-descent rails overlap and directly move class/feature `vwp/vwn` capacitor pairs. Structural and ngspice-backed sequence regressions pass (`tests/test_multiclass_output_head_sequence.py`: `12 passed`), including a one-hot continuous train/eval control that still reaches perfect final accuracy without gradient-storage nodes. Real fixed8 output-only probes remain negative: `mnist3fixed8_6`, 3-train/3-eval, label descent with nontarget scale `0.5` stays `0.333 -> 0.333` with final min margin `~-64.0 mV`; removing nontarget depression also stays `0.333 -> 0.333` but improves the min margin to `~-1.05 mV`. Compact evidence lives in `results/tables/codex_live_writer_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_live_writer_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_no_nontarget_summary.json`. Conclusion: the live writer integration is correct and more aligned with gradient-flow hardware, but raw one-vs-rest label rails on fixed features are still not enough; the next aligned path should feed live writers from score-normalized/descent rails rather than reintroducing sampled gradient storage.
- Added score-normalized descent rails to the live output-head sequence: `error_mode=normalizer-<approach>-descent` now instantiates the tested normalization subcircuit library and feeds `c<i>_errp/c<i>_errn` directly into `writer_mode=live` update devices, still with no `Vapply` phase and no `gvp/gvn` or `rgp/rgn` storage. Ngspice coverage proves the normalizer-fed live path preserves the one-hot continuous train/eval control (`tests/test_multiclass_output_head_sequence.py tests/test_normalization_subcircuits.py`: `47 passed`). Real fixed8 output-only evidence remains negative but diagnostic: `normalizer-current-sum-descent`, `mnist3fixed8_6`, 3-train/3-eval stays `0.333 -> 0.333` with final min margin `~-62.8 mV`, essentially matching raw live label descent; with `nontarget_scale=0`, the normalizer has no nontarget mass, all signed readout weights remain at the neutral `~20 mV`, and scores remain tied. Compact evidence lives in `results/tables/codex_live_norm_current_sum_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_live_norm_current_sum_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_no_nontarget_summary.json`. This keeps the architecture aligned with live gradient flow, but shows the current output-only fixed-feature objective still lacks useful class-evidence shaping; the next circuit target should be target-vs-impostor/margin descent or better feature/readout evidence, not a return to sampled gradient storage.
- Added `error_mode=live-pairwise-margin-descent` to the same live output-head sequence. This reuses a local copy of the low-gain pairwise score comparator to compare early class score caps, applies the analytical 1 mV target-margin penalty, builds class `errp/errn` rails only for target-vs-offending-opponent corrections, and feeds those rails directly into the live readout writers; there is still no `Vapply` phase and no per-feature gradient storage. The initial attempt imported the pairwise helpers from the block runner and exposed a circular import, so the small comparator emitter was localized in the output-head sequence. Focused regressions pass (`tests/test_multiclass_output_head_sequence.py tests/test_multiclass_margin_correction_primitive.py`: `25 passed`). The one-hot control still learns perfectly. On `mnist3fixed8_6`, 3-train/3-eval, the mode stays at `0.333 -> 0.333` but improves the final min margin to `~-33.8 mV` versus `~-62--64 mV` for dense live label/normalizer descent. On `mnist3fixed8_12`, 6-train/6-eval, it again stays at `0.333 -> 0.333` with final min margin `~-56.3 mV` and a class-2 score collapse. Compact evidence lives in `results/tables/codex_live_pairwise_margin_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_live_pairwise_margin_mnist3fixed8_12_t6e6_summary.json`. This is a more aligned live objective and has a margin benefit on the tiny slice, but output-only fixed8 still needs stronger feature/readout evidence or class balancing; the live plumbing is no longer the main blocker.
- Added an opt-in passive readout-centering leak to the live multiclass output-head runner: a `Vreadout_center` reference plus large resistors from every `vwp/vwn` persistent readout capacitor. This keeps the gradient-flow contract intact: live modes still emit no `Vapply`, `Vacc`, `Cgvp/Cgvn`, or `Crgp/Crgn`, and the leak is only weak device-state anchoring rather than gradient storage. Focused ngspice regressions pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_output_head_sequence.py tests/test_multiclass_margin_correction_primitive.py -q` -> `26 passed`). The fixed8 probe was negative: `live-pairwise-margin-descent` on `mnist3fixed8_6`, 3-train/3-eval with `5 MOhm` centering stayed `0.333 -> 0.333` and final min margin `~-37.5 mV`; a weaker `20 MOhm` leak also stayed `0.333 -> 0.333` with margin `~-34.5 mV`, roughly matching but not beating the uncentered `~-33.8 mV` run. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_live_pairwise_margin_center5m_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_live_pairwise_margin_center20m_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_summary.json`. Conclusion: passive readout centering is now a controlled live-runner knob, but it does not solve output-only fixed8; the next aligned work should move away from output-only fixed features toward stronger feature/readout evidence or live hidden/backward credit, not more static readout leak tuning.
- Added `readout_update_mode=live` to the continuous multiclass split-rail block runner. In this mode the block emits no `Vacc`, `Vapply`, `Vapplyn`, `Cgvp/Cgvn`, or `Crgp/Crgn`; stored eligibility and label/error descent rails directly move persistent `vwp/vwn` readout capacitors through the same live writer motif used in the output-head runner. The sampled-gradient mode remains available as a control, but the new option is the aligned gradient-flow path. Supported live modes are `label-descent` plus the existing error-rail descent families (`score-mass`, `pairwise-margin-correction`, normalizers, etc.); unsupported score-gated modes are rejected instead of silently emitting dangling `acc`-based storage. Focused regressions pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_emits_single_continuous_deck tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_can_use_live_readout_update_without_gradient_storage tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_validation tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_ngspice_one_hot_multiclass_learning tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_ngspice_live_readout_update_moves_one_hot_matrix -q` -> `5 passed`). Live one-hot block training moves the readout matrix in the correct direction (`diagonal ~= +140 mV`, off-diagonal `~= -140 mV`) without gradient buckets, but final score accuracy remains tied/chance, which separates live writing from score evidence generation. A compact strict MNIST3 fixed8 block probe with `readout_update_mode=live`, `pairwise-margin-correction-descent`, target-only bias, and `5 fF` score caps also stayed at `0.333 -> 0.333` with final margin `~-28.4 mV`, while train error rails were directionally sensible (`target mean ~= +336 mV`, nontarget mean `~= -244 mV`) and weights moved substantially. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_live_block_onehot_label_descent_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_live_block_pairwise_margin_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_summary.json`. Conclusion: the fuller block now has a live no-gradient-storage readout update path, but the next blocker is still hidden/readout score evidence and class centering, not the existence of a write phase.
- Made block score sampling time explicit with `score_measure_ns` after debugging the live one-hot failure. The live readout matrix was correct, but the existing late `base+8.5 ns` score measurement occurred after the long `out` phase had equalized the passive conductance readout rails. Direct timing probes showed the correct class has a useful early score margin (`~28 mV` at `base+5.0 ns`) that collapses to microvolts by the old late sample. The runner now records the score sample point in summaries and supports earlier score diagnostics without changing circuit state or Python-owned weights. Ngspice regression proves the same live no-gradient-storage one-hot block becomes class-readable when sampled during the early differential window (`score_measure_ns=5.2`): final predictions are `[0,1,2]` with worst margin `~11.9 mV`; focused tests pass (`4 passed`). The compact MNIST3 fixed8 live pairwise-margin probe with early score diagnostics still fails (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-25.8 mV`), so the next aligned target is to move the training-time score/error generation to the same early differential evidence window, not just final diagnostics. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_live_block_onehot_label_descent_earlyscore_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_live_block_pairwise_margin_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_earlyscore_summary.json`.
- Added `score_timing_mode=early` to move the physical score/error sequencer itself into the early differential window: the readout `out` pulse now ends at `5.35 ns`, `scorepre/scoreamp/scoredec/scoreerr` move to `5.45--8.10 ns`, target rails overlap the early pairwise decision/error clocks, and the reducer measures pairwise decisions, score amps, common/contrast gates, and error rails at timing-derived points rather than hard-coded late times. This keeps the live gradient-flow path free of `acc/apply` and gradient-storage nodes. Focused tests pass (`5 passed`). The compact fixed8 live pairwise-margin probe with early score timing still did not learn (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-31.8 mV`), despite directionally sensible train error rails (`target mean ~= +336 mV`, nontarget mean `~= -259 mV`). This separates two issues: early score sampling is required for readable live one-hot output, but MNIST3 fixed8 still needs better feature/readout class evidence or a different score/error rule. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_live_block_pairwise_margin_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_earlytiming_summary.json`.
- Added train-time eligibility overlap diagnostics to the multiclass block summary. The reducer now records train eligibility rows, active feature counts at `25/250/500 mV`, and train pairwise cosine overlap, so dense update overwrite can be tracked directly instead of inferred from final readout weights. Focused reducer tests pass. Regenerating the compact strict fixed8 live pairwise-margin early-timing probe preserves the prior failure (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-31.8 mV`) and exposes the underlying update density: every train sample has all `9/9` eligibility rails above `25 mV`, an average of `6.67/9` above `250 mV`, `5/9` above `500 mV`, and mean pairwise train-eligibility cosine `~0.785`. This explains the observed class-2 overwrite: the writer receives broad, highly overlapping feature eligibility, so later class updates move shared readout weights. I tried an uncommitted common-gated eligibility primitive, but rejected it because the analog gate was too weak for the live writer and the restored gate leaked or suppressed the one-hot control. Next aligned circuit target should be a stronger, tested feature-contrast/competition primitive before integrating it into the live writer. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_live_block_pairwise_margin_mnist3fixed8_6_t3e3_earlytiming_eligdiag_summary.json`.
- Added a standalone feature eligibility competition primitive before touching the live MNIST block integration. The primitive uses pairwise transistor/passive discharge comparators on forced eligibility rails, then precharges one writer gate per feature and discharges any feature that loses to another feature through a high-threshold loss device. The first low-common-mode score-preamp version failed the ngspice tests because that comparator is tuned for millivolt class-score rails and became non-monotonic around the `0.3--0.6 V` eligibility common mode; replacing it with direct pairwise discharge fixed the issue. New ngspice coverage proves unique one-hot and dense maxima select the right feature, rotated maxima are not index-special, flat dense rows do not enable all writers, and the observed fixed8-like dense rows are sparsified to at most two writer-readable gates. Focused regressions pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_feature_eligibility_competition_primitive.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_can_use_live_readout_update_without_gradient_storage tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_summarizes_train_eligibility_overlap -q` -> `13 passed`). This is not integrated yet; the next aligned step is to feed live readout writers from this tested competition gate and verify that it reduces the dense-overwrite failure without reintroducing gradient storage.
- Integrated the feature eligibility competition primitive as opt-in `eligibility_gate_mode=competition` for the live multiclass block writer. The first integration attempt was misleading: `egate` diagnostics showed all gates stayed high because the block reset was active-low during the compare window, keeping PMOS prechargers on. A dedicated `eligpre` phase now precharges before comparison and stays high through the live writer interval. With that fix, compact `mnist3fixed8_6` improves from the prior raw-eligibility baseline (`0.333 -> 0.333`, margin `~-31.8 mV`) to `0.333 -> 0.667`, while measured writer gates are sparse (`2.33/8` active over `600 mV` on average). The larger `mnist3fixed8_12` probe is still negative (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-19.9 mV`) even though gates are sparser (`1.5/8` active on average), so this is not a solved path. The evidence says competition reduces dense overwrite but is too winner-take-all/leaky to carry enough feature evidence by itself. Focused regressions pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_feature_eligibility_competition_primitive.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_can_gate_live_writer_with_feature_competition tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_eligibility_gate_mode_validation tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_summarizes_train_eligibility_gate_activity tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_can_use_live_readout_update_without_gradient_storage tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_ngspice_live_readout_is_class_readable_when_sampled_early -q` -> `16 passed`). Next direction should be soft/top-k or contrast-preserving eligibility, not hard WTA alone.
- Added a rank-preserving variant of the eligibility gate as `eligibility_gate_mode=rank`. Instead of discharging a feature gate completely on the first pairwise loss, each loss removes a bounded amount of charge from a larger gate capacitor (`50 fF`, `0.5u` loss devices), giving an approximate top-k/rank-coded writer gate. Primitive ngspice tests prove this keeps one-hot/unique maxima readable while preserving mid-level gates for runner-up evidence and suppressing weak rails. Integrated results are useful but still negative on the real objective: compact `mnist3fixed8_6` again reaches `0.333 -> 0.667`, with rank gates averaging `4.33/8` active features over `600 mV`; `mnist3fixed8_12` remains `0.333 -> 0.333`, with `4.5/8` active gates and final margin `~-31.4 mV`. This rules out the simple story that "hard WTA threw away too much evidence"; even a softer top-k writer gate does not fix the class-2/readout-evidence collapse. Focused regressions pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_feature_eligibility_competition_primitive.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_can_rank_gate_live_writer_with_feature_competition tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_can_gate_live_writer_with_feature_competition tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_summarizes_train_eligibility_gate_activity tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_can_use_live_readout_update_without_gradient_storage tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py::test_multiclass_block_sequence_ngspice_live_readout_is_class_readable_when_sampled_early -q` -> `19 passed`). The next circuit target should move upstream/downstream of writer sparsity: either improve hidden feature separability/contrast before eligibility, or improve readout score evidence/normalization so sparse or rank-coded updates produce class-specific margins.
- Added a common-mode contrast eligibility gate as an opt-in third writer-gating primitive. The standalone primitive builds a passive `elig_common` reference from the forced eligibility rails and charges each `egate` only when its local eligibility exceeds that common reference; ngspice tests prove it selects above-common fixed8-like features and rejects flat dense rows without behavioral sources. In the integrated block, the passive common reference must be high impedance: the original `20 kOhm / 8 fF` common net visibly loaded and equalized stored eligibility caps, while `1 MOhm / 1 fF` preserves the eligibility rows. The block runner now exposes those common-reference values and records `250 mV`, `600 mV`, and max gate diagnostics. The high-Z integrated compact fixed8 probe is still negative (`mnist3fixed8_6`, 3-train/3-eval, live pairwise-margin, early score timing, target-only bias: `0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-0.76 mV`); contrast gates peak at only `~0.325 V` and never cross `600 mV`, despite sensible score/error rails. Focused regressions pass (`22 passed`). Conclusion: common-mode contrast gating is a useful tested diagnostic and preserves more analog evidence than hard/rank WTA, but its current gate amplitude is too weak to be the missing fixed8 learning mechanism. The next aligned target remains feature/readout evidence and score-to-writer scale/centering, not more eligibility-gate sparsity alone.
- Added an ideal signed-readout projection diagnostic to the multiclass block reducer. It uses ngspice-measured final activation capacitors and final readout capacitor differences after the transient to compute a diagnostic-only signed dot product; it is not fed back into training. This separates "the physical score rails lost a good weight solution" from "the learned capacitor state itself encodes bad class evidence." Re-running the high-Z contrast gate compact fixed8 probe shows the latter: the physical score path still reports `0.333 -> 0.333`, and the ideal signed projection of the final weights is also `0.333` with class 1 winning all final eval samples (`final_eval_signed_projection_min_margin_v2 ~= -98 mV`). A quick `nontarget_scale=0.5` check produced the same signed matrix/projection, so the pairwise-margin block is not being controlled by that old label-descent scale. The isolated live writer can move weights with rails in the observed contrast/error range (`elig ~=325 mV`, `err ~=335 mV` gives `~100 mV` signed delta), so the immediate blocker is the update rule/feature evidence driving the matrix toward class-1 dominance, not readout physics or a dead live writer.
- Added a measured-activation prototype diagnostic to separate hidden feature separability from the live readout update rule. It builds per-class prototypes from train-cycle activation capacitors after ngspice, then scores final eval activations by raw dot product and cosine. On the same high-Z contrast fixed8 probe, raw prototypes reach `0.667` final eval accuracy but still miss class 1 (`min margin ~= -0.40 V^2`), while cosine-normalized prototypes fall to `0.333` and the train prototype pairwise cosine is high (`~0.659`). This says the measured feature space has a weak magnitude-based class cue but not clean separability, and the current live readout update degrades that weak cue further to class-1 dominance. The next circuit direction should improve feature contrast/centering or create a physical prototype-like update that preserves the measured magnitude cue, rather than adding another final score latch or tuning old nontarget scale knobs.
- Added a conductance-proxy projection diagnostic and an opt-in `score_sense_mode=current-clamp` for the multiclass block readout. The target-only live label update (`nontarget_scale=0`) is now a useful prototype-like control: on `mnist3fixed8_6`, 3-train/3-eval, raw voltage/conductance projections both reach `0.667`, and the physical floating-score readout is also `0.667` only in the first `~100 ps` of the readout window (`5.05--5.10 ns`) before collapsing to `0.333`. On `mnist3fixed8_12`, 6-train/6-eval, the same target-only stored weights project to `0.667` under voltage and conductance proxies, but floating score rails stay `0.333` for `5.005--5.10 ns`. The new current-clamp diagnostic improves that 12-sample physical readout to `0.5`, confirming virtual-ground/current-mode sensing helps, but it still does not fully recover the projected `0.667` because margins are tens of nA and one class-2 sample flips to class 0. Conclusion: target-only live updates can preserve a weak prototype-like capacitor state; the next circuit target should be a real low-impedance/current-mode score sensing and centering stage, plus better feature contrast for the remaining class overlap, not more floating-score-cap timing.
- Added an opt-in diode-isolated class-local readout forward topology to the multiclass block runner. The default direct path is unchanged, while `readout_forward_mode=diode` inserts per-feature `midp/midn` nodes plus diode-connected `NSENSE` devices between each actrow/readout conductance branch and the shared score/scoren rails. Structural tests cover the emitter and block integration, and the existing low-level diode-readout ngspice regressions still pass. On the compact strict fixed8 target-only control (`mnist3fixed8_12`, 6-train/6-eval, live label descent, early timing, `nontarget_scale=0`), diode isolation improved physical voltage-score readout from the prior direct `0.333 -> 0.333` and current-clamp diagnostic `0.333 -> 0.5` to `0.333 -> 0.667`, matching the signed/conductance projection accuracy. The remaining margin is still weak (`final_eval_min_margin_v ~= -22.1 mV`) and class-1 samples are still the hard failures, so this is not an end-to-end multiclass solution. It is a real circuit lever for the score-readout handoff, and the next aligned experiment should combine diode isolation with the live pairwise/normalizer error modes or a stronger current-mode/centering score stage rather than returning to Python-side thresholds.
- Checked whether diode-isolated readout fixes the live gradient/error modes. It does not. With `readout_forward_mode=diode`, `pairwise-margin-correction-descent`, early timing, target-only bias, and raw eligibility, compact `mnist3fixed8_6` and `mnist3fixed8_12` both remain at `0.333 -> 0.333`. More importantly, the diagnostic signed/conductance projections also fall to `0.333`: the learned readout state itself becomes class-2 dominated, so this is not a score-rail readability failure. `normalizer-current-sum-descent` with the default full `1.2 V` error clock hit an ngspice timestep failure; reducing the already-exposed analytical clock rail to `0.45 V` made it converge, but it also stayed at `0.333 -> 0.333` with a class-2 dominated signed matrix. Its error rails were strongly symmetric (`target errdiff ~= +405 mV`, `nontarget errdiff ~= -428 mV`), which makes the failure look like dense one-vs-rest overwrite through highly overlapping eligibility, not missing gradient generation. I also swept the diode target-only control on `mnist3fixed8_6` with score loads derived from the RC scale (`30 kOhm` gives `0.15 ns`, `100 kOhm` gives `0.5 ns` for `5 fF`) and neither recovered the `0.667` projection in the physical score rails. Current conclusion: diode isolation is useful when the stored state is already prototype-like, but the live normalized/pairwise descent paths need a different update direction or feature-contrast mechanism; simple score-load retuning and dense nontarget pressure are not the next main path.
- Tried to formulate a stronger feature-common contrast gate before integrating it, but the first two-stage restore idea failed its low-level SPICE contract and was not kept: fixed8-like above-common contrast rails only restored to about `0.21 V`, while flat dense rows were already around `0.16 V`, too little separation for a writer-domain gate. I removed the failed mode and re-ran the existing contrast and hidden-credit alignment regressions (`5 passed`). This reinforces the current direction: the output-only fixed8 path has weak class-1 feature evidence, and the existing `readout-weighted` hidden-credit primitive already proves latch-free backward credit and hidden-writer motion in isolation. The next aligned implementation step should be a small multiclass block rung that actually updates hidden conductance weights from readout-weighted hidden credit, rather than another output-readout or eligibility-gate retune.
- Integrated an opt-in live hidden-weight update path into the continuous multiclass block runner as `--hidden-update-mode readout-weighted`. The path samples row eligibility into `xelig*` during the forward/sample window, builds latch-free hidden credit from the class error rails and readout conductances (`hdp += errp*vwp + errn*vwn`, `hdn += errp*vwn + errn*vwp` with activation gating), and connects those rails directly to the hidden weight writer; there is no gradient-storage capacitor or separate Python-mediated writeback. Added CLI/validation for hidden credit/update sizing and rejected hidden updates for modes without physical `errp/errn` rails. New ngspice coverage proves the low-level helper in both polarities: with `vwp/vwn=1.0/0.05`, `hdp ~=0.956 V`, `hdn ~=29 mV`, and `whp-whn` moves to `+0.748 V`; reversing the readout weight reverses the hidden update to `-0.747 V`. Focused regressions pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "hidden_credit_drives_live_hidden_writer or live_update_hidden_weights or validation"` -> `5 passed`; `python3 -m pytest tests/test_row_conductance_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` -> `31 passed`). A strict one-hot integrated smoke with live readout plus live hidden update converged but did not learn (`0.333 -> 0.333`): hidden signed weights moved slightly (`~0.8 -> ~0.776 V`), error rails were directional, and activation prototypes stayed perfectly separable, but the learned readout matrix collapsed toward class 2. The next blocker is still update-rule/class-evidence alignment, not the existence of a live hidden gradient-flow writer.
- Fixed a live-gradient-flow contract bug in the multiclass block: physical `errp/errn` rails were reset only during train-cycle score-error phases, so stale error capacitors from the last training sample could keep driving the live writer during final eval as eligibility/activation refreshed. For live readout or live hidden update modes, `scoregaterst` now pulses in the cycle reset window on every cycle; score/error generation remains train-only. A new ngspice regression proves the fix on a strict 2-class one-hot deck with live readout plus readout-weighted hidden update: final predictions are `[0, 1]`, final readout weights match the last train measurements within `2 uV`, and diagonals/off-diagonals stay above `+/-100 mV`. The prior 3-class one-hot pairwise-margin hidden-update smoke now learns cleanly (`0.333 -> 1.000`, final min margin `+27.7 mV`, signed/conductance projections both `1.000`), with compact evidence in `results/tables/codex_fix2_onehot_pairwise_margin_hidden_summary.json`. The same corrected path on compact `mnist3fixed8_6` with target-only bias still fails (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-20.7 mV`); it preserves the known dense-overlap diagnosis (`9/9` features above `25 mV`, mean train eligibility cosine `~0.785`) and class-2-dominated readout state. Focused regressions pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "hidden_credit_drives_live_hidden_writer or live_error_rails_reset_before_eval_writer or live_update_hidden_weights or live_readout_is_class_readable_when_sampled_early or live_readout_update_moves_one_hot_matrix or validation"` -> `7 passed`; `python3 -m pytest tests/test_row_conductance_primitive.py tests/test_ngspice_availability.py -q` -> `31 passed`).
- Added `error_mode=label-rail-descent` as a physical label-to-descent-rail control for the live gradient-flow path. This mode creates real `cX_errp/cX_errn` capacitors from `targetp/targetn` through `scoreerr`, resets them with the same live-writer-safe `scoregaterst`, and routes both live readout and readout-weighted hidden writers through those rails; it does not add gradient storage or Python weight intervention. Ngspice regression on a strict 2-class one-hot deck proves the rails (`+/-1.07 V`), final predictions `[0, 1]`, and no eval-time weight drift. The 3-class one-hot integrated smoke learns cleanly (`0.333 -> 1.000`, final physical margin `~58 mV`, signed/conductance projections both `1.000`). On compact `mnist3fixed8_6`, full one-vs-rest label rails fail (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-45.2 mV`, signed/conductance projections also `0.333`), confirming dense nontarget pressure corrupts the stored matrix rather than merely hiding a good state. A target-only physical rail control (`nontarget_scale=0`) preserves a prototype-like stored state (`signed/conductance projection accuracy 0.667`) but physical floating-score readout still reports `0.333` with `~-23.6 mV` worst margin. Current conclusion: the best next circuit target is low-impedance/current-mode score sensing and centering for prototype-like states plus better feature contrast for class-1 overlap, not more full one-vs-rest write pressure.
- Promoted a transistor/passive diode-mirror score sensor into the multiclass live block as `score_sense_mode=diode-mirror`. The conductance-readout primitive now has ngspice coverage for the same sensor: diode-loaded low-score nodes feed mirror capacitors, with a physical active-low PMOS mirror reset; the primitive is monotone and cancellation-safe, though two active branches are sublinear (`~1.53x` a single branch) rather than ideal-current additive. The first integrated attempt failed because an NMOS source-follower reset left mirror caps drifting around the reference; replacing it with a real `rstn` PMOS precharge fixed the reset contract. With `score_measure_ns=5.30` inside the early readout window, a 3-class one-hot live label-rail + hidden-update smoke again learns (`0.333 -> 1.000`, final physical diode-mirror margin `~3.75 mV`). More importantly, the compact `mnist3fixed8_6` target-only physical rail control now exposes the previously hidden prototype-like state as real circuit readout: diode-mirror final accuracy is `0.667`, matching signed/conductance projections, while the older floating-score readout stayed `0.333`. This does not solve full one-vs-rest or class-1 overlap, but it is a concrete score-handoff improvement: a fully circuit-owned current-derived score sensor can recover weak target-only learned state without Python thresholds or current-clamp voltage sources.
- Extended the diode-mirror target-only control to the larger compact `mnist3fixed8_12` strict transient with live readout plus readout-weighted hidden update. It now reaches `0.333 -> 0.667` physical diode-mirror accuracy, matching signed/conductance projection diagnostics, but the worst margin remains slightly negative (`~-1.15 mV`) and train eligibility is still broad (`9/9` features above `25 mV`, mean pairwise cosine `~0.827`). This says diode-mirror sensing fixed an important score-readout handoff for prototype-like states, but it did not solve feature overlap or give a robust gradient/error rule. I also hardened the 10-candidate normalization primitive suite: all normalizer subcircuits are now ngspice-tested not only for basic bootstrap signs, but also class permutation symmetry and score common-mode direction before they are trusted as writer-domain error rails (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_normalization_subcircuits.py -q` -> `53 passed`; focused diode/live-gradient regressions -> `11 passed`).
- Made the normalizer block screen usable for the strict live-gradient path by exposing `readout_update_mode`, `hidden_update_mode`, hidden writer sizing, score timing, and readout-forward topology instead of only screening the older sampled writer. A strict live current-sum normalizer run with readout-weighted hidden update, early timing, diode readout, and `0.45 V` normalizer clock learns the one-hot control cleanly (`0.333 -> 1.000`, final margin `~44 mV`) and reaches `0.667` on compact `mnist3fixed8_6`, but the larger `mnist3fixed8_12` run stays at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`, margin `~-22.7 mV`). The failing 12-sample run has strong symmetric error rails (`target errdiff mean ~399 mV`, nontarget mean `~-425 mV`) and again dense overlapping eligibility (`9/9` features above `25 mV`, cosine `~0.827`), while signed/conductance projections are also chance. This confirms the live normalizer can drive the hardware writer, but normalized one-vs-rest pressure still overwrites through nonseparable/dense features. I also added projection/prototype/eligibility diagnostics to the normalizer screen CSV so future screens show this failure mode directly. Regressions: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_normalizer_block_screen.py tests/test_normalization_subcircuits.py -q` -> `56 passed`.
- Added `eligibility_source_mode` to the continuous multiclass block so readout writer eligibility can be sampled from the old positive precharge rail (`pre-p`, default), the raw differential activation (`act-raw`), or the stored activation capacitor (`act`). This is still circuit-owned state sampled by MOS switches; Python only selects the topology. The stored-activation mode keeps the strict one-hot live current-sum normalizer with readout-weighted hidden update working (`0.333 -> 1.000`, final margin `~44 mV`) and, in readout-only mode on `mnist3fixed8_12`, reduces eligibility overlap (`active >25 mV: 9.0 -> 7.33`, cosine `0.827 -> 0.696`) and improves physical accuracy from chance to `0.5`. It does not solve the full path: combining activation-derived eligibility with dense MNIST hidden updates still hits ngspice timestep failures in the hidden-credit path, so the next circuit target is stabilizing/bounding hidden credit or delaying/decoupling activation-derived eligibility rather than more score/error tuning. Focused regressions pass (`9 passed`).
- Added explicit hidden-credit storage/damping controls and made the activation device in the hidden-credit stack selectable (`NREL`, `NMOS`, `NSENSE`). This is aimed at the dense-MNIST timestep failure, which localized to the activation-gated hidden-credit transistor rather than the score/error rail. With `50 fF` hidden-credit caps, `250 kOhm` shunts, `NMOS` activation gates, `0.1u` hidden writers, stored-activation eligibility, diode readout, and `0.35 V` normalizer clock, the strict `mnist3fixed8_12` live-hidden current-sum run now completes instead of aborting, but remains at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`) and hidden weights barely move (`~0.8 V` signed). The same damped path still learns one-hot (`0.333 -> 1.000`, margin `~22 mV`). Conclusion: the circuit now has a stable knob set for full-path diagnostics, but the damped hidden-credit setting is too conservative; the next useful step is an analytical sweep of hidden-credit RC/update strength, not another score normalizer. Focused regressions pass (`8 passed`).
- Added train hidden-credit amplitude diagnostics to the continuous block and normalizer screens, plus a small `run_hidden_credit_rc_screen.py` wrapper for repeatable RC/update variants. The measured stable `NMOS` hidden-credit path on `mnist3fixed8_12` confirmed the real problem: strong score/error rails (`target errdiff ~+0.40 V`, nontarget `~-0.43 V`) and usable stored activation eligibility coexist with hidden-credit rails of only `~2.4 uV` mean / `20 uV` max, so the `hdp/hdn` intermediate capacitor is attenuating the live gradient before it reaches the writer. To align better with the user's gradient-flow target, I added `hidden_update_mode=direct-readout-weighted`, which removes the intermediate hidden-credit storage and directly gates hidden `whp/whn` updates from `xelig`, stored activation, class error rails, and readout conductance pairs during the live window. Low-level ngspice proves the direct writer preserves readout-credit sign in both polarities, and explicit `0.05 fF` internal stack parasitics fix the timestep failures from otherwise ideal floating series nodes. Integrated evidence: one-hot still learns (`0.333 -> 1.000`, final margin `~44 mV`), `mnist3fixed8_6` reaches `0.667` with hidden weights moving by `~15-20 mV`, and `mnist3fixed8_12` now completes with direct hidden updates and hidden weights moving by `~36-57 mV`, but remains only `0.500` with final margin `~-3.6 mV`. This is progress on the gradient-flow circuit contract, not a solved MNIST path; the next blocker is still dense feature overlap / update direction, now with a live hidden writer that actually moves. Focused regressions pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_hidden_credit_rc_screen.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_normalizer_block_screen.py -q -k "direct_hidden or can_directly or hidden_credit or summary_runner or rc_screen"` -> `9 passed`).
- Added hidden-weight progress diagnostics so every continuous block summary can report `hidden_signed_after_each_train_v` and final hidden signed deltas relative to the initial hidden state. This immediately exposed a second direct-writer issue: the first direct writer used both "raise desired rail" and "lower opposite rail" branches, so common-mode readout credit could collapse hidden signed state even when the readout pair was neutral. I changed the direct hidden writer to an up-only differential conductance update: positive credit raises `whp`, negative credit raises `whn`, and the opposite rail is not actively discharged in the same branch. Low-level ngspice now proves strong positive/negative readout signs still move hidden signed weight in the correct direction, while a neutral readout pair preserves the local signed state in the isolated primitive. On integrated `mnist3fixed8_12`, this reduces direct-hidden shrink substantially: full current-sum normalized pressure remains `0.333 -> 0.500` with final margin `~-3.60 mV`, but hidden signed delta improves from about `-51 mV` mean to `-18.9 mV`; target-only label-rail with diode-mirror score sensing remains `0.333 -> 0.667` with final margin `~-60 uV` and hidden delta `~-18.7 mV`. A short delayed hidden-update phase was tested and discarded because it worsened shrink to `~-25 mV`. The remaining blocker is not a dead hidden writer anymore; it is a true differential/dead-zone hidden-credit gate or better feature/update rule so neutral readout common-mode does not slowly raise the hidden negative rail. Focused regressions pass (`7 passed` for direct hidden, hidden progress, and screen summaries).
- Added an explicit `hidden_direct_readout_gate_mode` for the direct hidden-gradient-flow writer. The old `raw` mode uses absolute `vwp/vwn` gates; the new default `differential-excess` mode first builds transistor/passive `vdiff_p/vdiff_n` gates by charging from the stronger readout rail and bleeding with the opposite rail, so neutral readout common-mode does not masquerade as hidden credit. Low-level ngspice tests now check the gate voltages directly, prove strong positive/negative readout signs still move hidden weights in the right direction, and add a high-signed-state neutral-readout regression (`whp/whn=1.0/0.2`, `vwp=vwn=0.4`) that preserves `whp-whn >0.75 V`. Integrated `mnist3fixed8_12` evidence is a circuit-health improvement, not a learning breakthrough: target-only label rails plus diode-mirror still reach `0.333 -> 0.667` with the same `~-60 uV` final physical margin, while hidden signed shrink drops from `~-18.7 mV` mean to `~-0.29 mV`; current-sum normalized pressure remains `0.333 -> 0.500` with final margin `~-3.59 mV`, while hidden shrink drops from `~-18.9 mV` to `~-0.36 mV`. This confirms the previous hidden drift was readout common-mode leakage. It also shows the differential-excess gate is conservative, so future progress needs either a stronger differential credit amplifier or better feature/update direction rather than reintroducing raw common-mode gates. Regressions: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_normalizer_block_screen.py tests/test_hidden_credit_rc_screen.py -q -k "direct_hidden or can_directly or raw_direct or validation or summary_runner or rc_screen"` -> `11 passed`; `python3 -m pytest tests/test_normalizer_block_screen.py tests/test_hidden_credit_rc_screen.py -q` -> `6 passed`.
- Tested the next hidden-writer output-stage hypothesis under TDD before promoting it. The direct hidden writer now has opt-in `hidden_direct_readout_gate_mode=restored-excess`, which adds a second transistor/passive excess stage (`4x` pulldown) after `vdiff_p/vdiff_n`, and `hidden_direct_output_stage=pmos-pullup`, which uses the low-voltage credit rail to discharge a dynamic PMOS write gate instead of using it as the final NMOS pass gate into an already-high weight capacitor. Low-level ngspice shows why this looked promising: `vwp/vwn=0.40/0.28` is dead in the NMOS-pass path (`signed_after ~=50 mV`, unchanged), while restored-excess plus PMOS at only `0.125u` moves the isolated hidden signed state to `~346 mV` and preserves a neutral high-signed state (`~0.800 V`). The integrated strict `mnist3fixed8_12` target-only diode-mirror result is negative: `8u` and `0.125u` PMOS both collapse hidden signed state by about `-0.42 V/-0.40 V`, flatten the learned readout matrix, and fall back to `0.333 -> 0.333`. This rules out unbounded PMOS pull-up as the next main path. The useful circuit lesson is narrower: low-voltage credit rails need an inverted/bounded writer target, not a raw rail pull-up. Keep the option experimental/off by default; the next aligned design should bound the PMOS writer toward a finite reference or update common-mode explicitly. Regressions: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_normalizer_block_screen.py tests/test_hidden_credit_rc_screen.py -q -k "direct_hidden or can_directly or raw_direct or validation or summary_runner or rc_screen"` -> `12 passed`.
- Added an explicit hidden-domain bounded PMOS writer target as `hidden_direct_output_stage=pmos-bounded` plus `--hidden-direct-high-ref`. This fixes a testbench/integration mismatch from the previous PMOS experiment: the isolated primitive had been using `vwhi_ref=1.05 V`, while the integrated block's readout writer reference is `0.42 V`, so `pmos-pullup` was pulling hidden weights toward the wrong domain. The new low-level ngspice regression forces readout-like refs (`0.42/0.28 V`) while sourcing the PMOS hidden writer from `hidden_whi_ref=1.05 V`; it recovers moderate readout credit (`signed_after >300 mV`) and preserves a neutral high-signed hidden state. The integrated strict `mnist3fixed8_12` target-only diode-mirror run is still negative: `pmos-bounded` prevents the old `0.42 V` collapse but exposes the next bug, an up-only writer raises both hidden rails across changing class labels, so final hidden signed weights collapse from `~0.8 V` to `~0 V` and physical accuracy drops to `0.167` (`final_eval_signed_projection_accuracy=0.5`, conductance projection `0.333`). I briefly tried a differential-bounded anti-rail pulldown, but low-level ngspice showed the simple NMOS add-on either had negligible pull at moderate restored-excess gates or over-corrected the signed state, so it was not kept as a selectable topology. The next aligned design needs a proper common-mode-preserving hidden writer/update pair, not another PMOS high-ref tweak. Regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py spice/run_normalizer_block_screen.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_normalizer_block_screen.py tests/test_hidden_credit_rc_screen.py -q -k "direct_hidden or can_directly or raw_direct or validation or summary_runner or rc_screen"` -> `13 passed`.
- Added an experimental `hidden_direct_output_stage=pmos-complementary` that tries to preserve hidden signed state by raising the credited rail and weakly pulling the opposite rail toward a hidden low reference. The ngspice primitive is a two-update continuous transient with no Python intervention: a first positive update followed by an opposing update must flip `whp-whn` instead of leaving both rails high. The first implementation was too hard because the anti-rail inverter was sourced from `vdd`; measured weak opposite-credit gates still charged the anti gate. Sourcing that inverter from `hidden_whi_ref` and separating `--hidden-direct-complement-width-scale` from the high-write width produced a passing primitive at scale `0.0625` (`signed_after_first ~=+0.55 V`, final `signed_after ~=-0.14 V`). The integrated strict `mnist3fixed8_12` target-only diode-mirror result remains negative: scale `0.25` erases feature eligibility almost immediately, and the weaker `0.0625` path still collapses final hidden signed state to about zero/negative (`final_hidden_signed_delta_mean ~= -0.800 V`) and stays at/below chance (`0.333 -> 0.167` for the scored run, signed/conductance projections `0.333`). This rules out a simple local anti-rail pull as the next main solution. The useful lesson is that common-mode preservation has to be tied to a better hidden update objective or bounded differential state controller; merely adding a complementary pulldown around the PMOS writer can destroy the hidden feature representation. Regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py spice/run_normalizer_block_screen.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_normalizer_block_screen.py tests/test_hidden_credit_rc_screen.py -q -k "direct_hidden or can_directly or raw_direct or validation or summary_runner or rc_screen"` -> `13 passed`.
- Added a real sampled feature-gate handoff for direct hidden gradient-flow updates. The first `hxelig` implementation was only structurally gated: a continuously connected pass device let `xelig` charge `hxelig` before the competition gate resolved, so low-level ngspice showed a losing feature with `egate_f1_1 ~=0` still had `hxelig_f1_1 ~=0.25 V`. The fix adds a post-decision `hxsamp` phase and a two-device gated sampler (`xelig -> hxsamp -> egate -> hxelig`), plus `hxelig` measurements and summary stats. The new ngspice regression proves the contract directly: raw losing eligibility remains present (`xelig_f1_1 >0.20 V`) while hidden-update eligibility is suppressed (`hxelig_f1_1 <0.05 V`). Re-running strict `mnist3fixed8_12` target-only diode-mirror probes with direct differential-excess hidden updates shows the feature gate is now electrically real but not sufficient: competition has one hidden-update feature above `250 mV` on average and stays `0.333 -> 0.333`; rank preserves more features (`~2.67` above `250 mV`) and also stays `0.333 -> 0.333`; contrast gives softer writer-visible analog gates (`max hxelig ~=0.305 V`, `~6` features above `25 mV`) but remains `0.333 -> 0.333` and biases readout toward class 1. The hidden-state collapse is avoided (`final_hidden_signed_delta_mean` about `-0.62 mV`, `-0.58 mV`, and `-0.077 mV` for competition/rank/contrast), so the next blocker is not hidden writer destruction; it is the class evidence/descent interface producing target-only or class-biased readout updates instead of score-normalized discriminative gradients. Focused regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_normalizer_block_screen.py tests/test_hidden_credit_rc_screen.py -q -k "direct_hidden or can_directly or raw_direct or validation or summary_runner or rc_screen or gate_direct_hidden or gates_direct_hidden_update_eligibility"` -> `15 passed`.
- Checked the same corrected rank-gated direct-hidden path with an existing score-derived normalizer instead of target-only label rails: `normalizer-current-sum-descent`, `normalizer_error_clock_high=0.45`, native voltage score rails, early diode readout, and strict `mnist3fixed8_12` continuous transient. The run still stayed at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final physical margin `~-16.7 mV`), but it changed the failure signature. The normalizer generated strong writer-range descent rails (`target errdiff mean ~+0.405 V`, nontarget mean `~-0.428 V`) and rank-gated hidden-update eligibility remained writer-visible (`~2.67` features above `250 mV`), while hidden signed drift stayed small (`~-0.945 mV`). The learned readout was no longer class-uniform; it became class-2 biased: class 0/1 signed weights around `-0.11 V`, class 2 around `+0.088 V`. This rules out "normalized rails are absent or too weak" as the sole blocker. The next aligned circuit target is update centering/alignment under repeated multiclass normalized pressure: constrain common-mode and class bias in the live writer, or build a one-sample margin-correction primitive that proves `score_target - score_wrong` improves before retrying full `mnist3fixed8_12`.
- Added a stricter live-gradient one-sample margin-correction regression. Unlike the older one-sample pairwise-margin test, this uses `readout_update_mode=live` and initializes actual readout capacitor state so class 0 physically wins before training (`initial scores [~114 mV, ~0.14 mV, ~-43 mV]` for target class 1). One continuous SPICE train/eval transient then drives class-1 `errdiff ~=+0.426 V`, class-0 `errdiff ~=-0.427 V`, updates the stored readout weights (`c1_f0_signed_final ~=+120 mV`, `c0_f0_signed_final ~=-105 mV`), and improves the target-vs-wrong margin from `~-114 mV` to `~+73 mV`. That proves the live writer can correct a controlled wrong winner with no gradient storage or Python intervention. The same mechanism still does not solve real fixed8 integration: corrected rank-gated direct-hidden `pairwise-margin-correction-descent` on strict `mnist3fixed8_12` remains chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final physical margin `~-80.9 uV`) and, like current-sum normalization, becomes class-2 biased: class 0/1 signed weights around `-80 mV`, class 2 around `+108 mV`. So the remaining problem is no longer a missing local margin-correction primitive; it is update centering/alignment under a repeated nonseparable multiclass sample stream. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "validation or live_pairwise_margin_corrects_wrong_winner or pairwise_margin_correction_improves_one_sample_margin or live_readout_update_without_gradient_storage"` -> `5 passed`.
- Tested the existing passive readout-center leak as the first common-mode/order-bias control on the same corrected rank-gated direct-hidden pairwise-margin run. I used `5 MOhm` to `0.40 V`, which gives roughly a `100 ns` time constant against the `20 fF` readout weight capacitors, comparable to the compact 6-sample training transient rather than a negligible leak. It reduced absolute signed readout magnitudes and shrank the class-2 signed feature mean from about `+108 mV` to about `+42 mV`, but it did not fix class order bias or accuracy: strict `mnist3fixed8_12` stayed `0.333 -> 0.333` and physical margin worsened to `~-3.26 mV`. This rules out a simple always-on same-rail center leak as the next main solution; the next circuit target needs class-relative/update-window centering or a writer rule that subtracts common class pressure without erasing useful differential state. Added future-run diagnostics for final readout class bias: per-class feature signed means/spread and per-class feature common-mode means/spread are now emitted in block summaries so this failure does not require manual matrix inspection. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "readout_matrix_class_bias or validation or live_pairwise_margin_corrects_wrong_winner"` -> `4 passed`.
- Exposed the live readout writer device width as `--readout-update-width` instead of leaving `class_local_live_label_descent_update_lines` hardcoded at `0.5u`. This keeps the live gradient-flow contract unchanged while making the per-update charge scale explicit enough for analytical sizing and small screens. Structural/validation regressions pass, including a live ngspice margin-correction test (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "parameterizes_live_readout_update_width or validation or live_pairwise_margin_corrects_wrong_winner"` -> `4 passed`). A strict corrected rank-gated direct-hidden `mnist3fixed8_12` run with a `0.125u` live readout writer still stayed at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`), but tightened the final physical margin to about `-7.6 uV` while preserving hidden state (`final_hidden_signed_delta_mean ~= -0.48 mV`). The readout matrix remains class-2 biased (`final_readout_signed_feature_class_means_v ~= [-76.3, -65.2, +98.7] mV`), so the blocker is not simply an overlarge live writer; the next aligned circuit target is class-relative/update-window centering or a writer rule that subtracts common class pressure during the live update.
- Added `pairwise-margin-centered-descent`, an opt-in class-relative error rail mode. The new transistor/passive primitive first generates raw pairwise margin rails, then builds physical common positive/negative error nodes and cross-couples them into the writer rails so each class sees an approximate `e_i - mean(e)` pressure instead of raw absolute pressure. Low-level ngspice tests prove the intended contracts: uniform positive pressure mostly cancels, while a target-positive/nontarget-negative pattern preserves target/nontarget signs and class symmetry. Integrated strict `mnist3fixed8_12` with corrected rank-gated direct hidden updates still stayed at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`), but the failure signature changed sharply: train target/nontarget error rails shrank to about `+24.0 mV / -40.6 mV`, readout class mean spread dropped from the prior `~175 mV` to `~30.1 mV`, and hidden drift was nearly zero (`~-70 uV`). This rules out simple common/order pressure as sufficient by itself: centering removes most overwrite but under-drives discriminative learning. Next direction should be a gain-restored centered rail or a balanced zero-sum writer that preserves stronger target-vs-impostor pressure without reintroducing class-common bias. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "pairwise_margin_centered or centered_error_rails or validation or live_pairwise_margin_corrects_wrong_winner"` -> `6 passed`.
- Added `pairwise-margin-centered-gain-descent`, a second-stage transistor/passive restore after the centered rails. The restore charges final writer rails from the centered pre-rails and cross-discharges each rail with the opposite pre-rail, giving modest differential gain without Python-computed gradients or behavioral equations. Low-level ngspice proves three contracts: uniform common pressure still mostly cancels, target/nontarget signs are preserved and boosted, and a controlled live wrong-winner case is corrected in one continuous transient (`score_1-score_0` improves from about `-114 mV` to `+41.8 mV`, with class-1 signed readout ending at `~117 mV`). On strict `mnist3fixed8_12`, the mode remains negative (`0.333 -> 0.333`), but it lands between raw and plain-centered behavior: train target/nontarget error means increase to about `+38.6 mV / -52.1 mV`, readout class mean spread rises to `~65.2 mV` (still far below raw pairwise's `~175 mV`), and hidden drift stays negligible (`~-70 uV`). This says the missing mechanism is not merely restoring centered rail amplitude. The persistent class-2 signed projection points back to feature/update-rule alignment: the circuit needs stronger target-vs-impostor discrimination tied to actual score evidence or more separable hidden features, not just class-common cancellation. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "centered_gain or centered_error_rails or pairwise_margin_centered or validation"` -> `8 passed`.
- Added an explicit MNIST sample-order diagnostic (`--sample-order grouped|round-robin`) because the existing balanced split is class grouped (`0,0,1,1,2,2` for six train samples). This does not change the circuit contract: Python still only supplies the sample stream, labels, clocks, and diagnostics; weights remain live circuit state. The round-robin strict `mnist3fixed8_12` run with the same gain-restored centered pairwise rails also stayed at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`) and kept nearly the same class-skew signature as grouped order: final readout class feature means moved from about `[-29.6, -27.2, +35.6] mV` grouped to `[-27.3, -24.9, +35.2] mV` round-robin, with hidden drift unchanged at `~-70 uV`. This rules out simple last-class recency from the grouped stream as the primary failure. The next useful direction is not sample ordering; it is changing the feature/update objective so score-derived pressure actually distinguishes class 0/1 from class 2 in the learned readout state. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "round_robin_order or mnist_scenario_uses_counted_records or validation"` -> `4 passed`.
- Added class-conditioned readout progress diagnostics for continuous block summaries. The runner now reduces the already-measured `signed_after_each_train_v` state into per-train class feature means, per-train mean deltas, target-vs-nontarget delta rows, and final within-class feature-centered readout magnitude. This is diagnostic only; no Python-side training or weight intervention was added. The strict round-robin `mnist3fixed8_12` rerun with gain-restored centered pairwise rails again stayed at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`), but the new rows sharpened the failure: every sample applies aligned target-positive/nontarget-negative live pressure (`target_minus_nontarget_delta` stays positive, minimum about `92 mV`), yet the learned readout is dominated by class-common shifts after each label rather than feature-specific evidence. Final class feature means were about `[-27.3, -24.9, +35.2] mV`, while demeaning each class left only about `0.29 mV` mean feature-specific magnitude in the manual check. Hidden drift remained negligible (`~-70 uV`). This points away from "writer direction is wrong" and toward a live update rule/objective problem: the circuit needs target-vs-impostor pressure that accumulates feature-discriminative evidence without class-common overwrite, or stronger hidden features before the readout. Focused regression: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "readout_train_progress or summarizes_readout_matrix_class_bias or mnist_scenario_uses_counted_records"` -> `3 passed`.
- Added a writer-visible readout eligibility handoff for gated live readout updates. The previous `competition`/`rank`/`contrast` path used the class-agnostic `egate` directly as the live readout writer activation, which selected features but discarded the feature's analog eligibility amplitude and produced mostly class-common readout movement. The new `relig*` nodes are sampled after eligibility-gate resolution with a transistor/passive PMOS-restored handoff (`elig & egate & relsamp -> relig`) and are measured in summaries. Low-level ngspice now proves the selected feature reaches writer range while the suppressed feature stays off, and the structural tests prove the live readout writer uses `relig` rather than `egate`. The strict round-robin `mnist3fixed8_12` gain-restored centered pairwise run moves from the old chance signature to nontrivial physical learning (`0.333 -> 0.667`), with feature-specific readout structure restored (`final_readout_signed_feature_centered_abs_mean_v ~=36.4 mV`, class-mean/feature-centered ratio `~2.05`, `~4.83` features above `500 mV` readout-update eligibility). The smaller `mnist3fixed8_6` reaches `0.667` as well. This is aligned with live gradient flow: Python still only supplies sample/label/phase rails, and no per-weight gradient is stored or written back. It is not solved: physical worst margins are still not robustly positive, conductance projection lags signed projection on `mnist3fixed8_12`, and the PMOS-restored `relig` is mostly binary rather than magnitude-preserving. A strongest-impostor-only pairwise rail was also tested and is negative evidence (`0.333 -> 0.333`), so the next useful direction is preserving analog eligibility magnitude or fixing readout common-mode/conductance readability, not hardening pairwise winner selection. Focused regression: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py && python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "sampled_gated_readout_eligibility or gate_direct_hidden or readout_update_eligibility or pairwise_winner_error or validation"` -> `6 passed`.
- Parameterized the PMOS-restored readout eligibility ceiling as `--readout-update-eligibility-ref`, sourced from a real voltage rail `relig_ref` rather than hard `vdd`. This keeps the live-gradient contract unchanged while making the writer-domain eligibility amplitude explicit enough to size analytically. A low-level ngspice regression with `relig_ref=0.65 V` proves the selected feature is bounded near the reference and the suppressed feature remains off. The integrated strict `mnist3fixed8_12` rank-gated centered-gain run at `0.65 V` is negative-but-informative: accuracy is still nontrivial (`0.333 -> 0.500`) but worse than full-rail `relig` (`0.667`), final physical margin is still negative (`~-22.7 mV`), and conductance projection remains chance. Readout feature structure remains present (`centered abs mean ~40.2 mV`) and class-common spread is modest (`~74.6 mV`), but fewer features are writer-visible (`~4.5` above `500 mV`) and the path appears underdriven. This rules out "just lower the restored eligibility rail" as the next main fix; the next aligned direction is a magnitude-preserving or gain-shaped eligibility handoff that keeps writer range for strong features without collapsing all selected features to one binary value, plus the existing conductance-readability/common-mode diagnostics. Focused regression: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py && python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "sampled_gated_readout_eligibility or bounds_gated_readout_eligibility_ref or gate_direct_hidden or readout_update_eligibility or validation"` -> `6 passed`.
- Added opt-in `readout_update_eligibility_mode=hybrid` to test a magnitude-preserving readout eligibility handoff. The circuit first pass-samples the analog `elig` value through the resolved feature gate, then uses a separate local boost phase to discharge a PMOS restore gate for strong selected features. Low-level ngspice proves the intended primitive behavior with analytical sizing knobs: low/mid eligibility remains analog (`~0.15/0.30 V`), while stronger eligibility can be boosted to writer range. Integrated strict `mnist3fixed8_12` evidence is negative: both simultaneous pass/boost and split-phase boost variants underdrive the real deck (`train_readout_update_eligibility_max_v ~=0.213 V`), readout signed weights remain at numerical noise, and final accuracy stays `0.333 -> 0.333`. This means the isolated monotone/boosted primitive is not enough under the block's actual timing, gate amplitudes, and writer loading. The full-restored binary `relig` remains the best current path (`0.667` on fixed8_12), while the next useful direction is diagnosing the integrated PMOS-gate discharge/hold directly or changing the writer to accept the stored analog eligibility scale, not continuing blind hybrid sizing. Focused regression: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py && python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "hybrid_readout_eligibility or sampled_gated_readout_eligibility or bounds_gated_readout_eligibility_ref or gate_direct_hidden or readout_update_eligibility or validation"` -> `8 passed`.
- Added writer-time and PMOS-gate diagnostics for readout-update eligibility so the hybrid failure can be separated from measurement timing. The strict round-robin `mnist3fixed8_12` hybrid rerun still stays at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`), with `relig` at the writer only about `0.213 V` and `relig_pgate_min_v ~=1.078 V` (`relig_pgate_max_v=1.2 V`). That means the integrated boost path never pulls the PMOS gate low enough to restore strong selected features into writer range. The latest evidence therefore keeps full-restored binary `relig` as the best current learning path, and narrows the next circuit target to either an integrated discharge/hold fix for the hybrid PMOS gate or a writer that intentionally accepts the analog `~0.2--0.45 V` eligibility scale.
- Fixed the integrated hybrid readout-eligibility underdrive by making the `relig` sample capacitance explicit and analytically defaulting hybrid mode to `5 fF`. The issue was charge sharing: the old `20 fF` `relig` cap was the same size as the stored `elig` cap, so the pass phase cut a `~0.42 V` eligibility to `~0.21 V` before the PMOS-gate boost phase. The new default follows `Csample <= Celig * droop / (1 - droop)` with a 20% droop target (`20 fF * 0.2 / 0.8 = 5 fF`), and a focused ngspice regression now proves the active feature reaches writer range while the suppressed feature remains off. On strict round-robin `mnist3fixed8_12`, the same hybrid run improves from the prior dead `0.333 -> 0.333` signature to `0.333 -> 0.500`; writer-time `relig` reaches `~1.199 V`, `relig_pgate_min_v ~=0.721 V`, and the feature-centered readout magnitude rises to `~66.7 mV`. This is still not the main solution: signed/conductance projections remain chance and the physical worst margin is negative (`~-21.0 mV`), so full-restored binary `relig` remains the stronger current path while hybrid is now a usable probe for magnitude/threshold shaping. Focused regression: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py && python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "hybrid_readout_eligibility or readout_update_eligibility or validation"` -> `6 passed`.
- Made the score measurement default timing-mode aware. The early score schedule was still using the late `8.5 ns` default unless every run explicitly passed `--score-measure-ns 5.3`, which can falsely report chance accuracy after early score rails have decayed. The netlist generator and CLI now default to `5.30 ns` for `score_timing_mode=early` and `8.50 ns` for late timing, while explicit `--score-measure-ns` still overrides both. A current-code restored-eligibility target-bias compact run demonstrates the effect: with the old late measurement it reported `0.333 -> 0.333`; with the corrected early default it reports `0.333 -> 0.667` again (`score_measure_ns=5.3`, final margin still negative at `~-9.73 mV`). The same corrected measurement does not rescue the hybrid target-bias compact run (`0.333 -> 0.333`), and a small hybrid discharge-width screen (`0.5u/1u/2u`) also stayed at chance, so the hybrid issue is not just the score read timing. Next useful path remains the hard-restored eligibility baseline plus better conductance/readability/hidden-feature alignment. Focused regression: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py && python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "score_measure or validation or hybrid_readout_eligibility or readout_update_eligibility"` -> `7 passed`.
- Added physical-vs-projection alignment diagnostics for continuous multiclass block summaries. The runner now compares final physical score predictions/margins against both signed-weight and conductance-threshold projection rows and emits prediction agreement plus mean score correlation. A compact restored-eligibility, target-bias, early-score run on `mnist3fixed8_6` still reports physical learning (`0.333 -> 0.667`, worst margin `~-9.73 mV`). The new diagnostic shows projections are directionally related but not equivalent to the physical early score behavior: signed projection stays at chance with `2/3` prediction agreement and mean score correlation `~0.820`; conductance projection also stays at chance with `2/3` agreement and correlation `~0.962`. This keeps hard-restored `relig` as the current best path, but the mismatch says the next debugging target should be physical readout replay/surrogate alignment and conductance readability, not just looking at final signed weights. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_religrest_alignment_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260527_summary.json`.
- Added an opt-in physical readout replay diagnostic. After a continuous training transient finishes, the runner can now instantiate separate ngspice replay decks that load the measured final readout capacitor voltages and measured final-eval activation voltages, then re-run the same transistor/passive readout topology. This does not checkpoint or intervene in training; it is post-run evidence for whether final state explains the continuous physical score rows. The first compact evidence used a late-aligned replay measurement and was superseded by the corrected timing entry below.
- Corrected the physical readout replay diagnostic timing. The first replay reused the old primitive's `4.5 ns` measurement point, which is only aligned with late score timing; for early timing the continuous deck measures `0.3 ns` after output-readout start, so the replay deck must measure at `1.3 ns` after its local `1.0 ns` output pulse start. With the corrected timing, the same compact restored-eligibility target-bias `mnist3fixed8_6` run has continuous physical accuracy `0.667` and replay accuracy `0.667`, with `3/3` prediction agreement and mean score correlation `~0.982`. That rules out score-node residual/lifecycle state as the source of the compact replay mismatch; the remaining blocker is still weak/non-robust readout margins and projection/readability alignment, not hidden Python-side state. The stale late-timed replay summary was removed, and corrected evidence is in `results/tables/codex_religrest_physical_replay_timingfix_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260527_summary.json`.
- Added analytical replay-margin sizing. The helper uses the first-order `Vmargin ~ Wreadout / Cscore` relationship to compute the W/C scale needed for a target observable margin, but only from rows whose class sign is already correct; wrong-sign rows are reported separately because scaling cannot fix their decision. On the compact restored-eligibility target-bias `mnist3fixed8_6` replay, two rows are correct and one remains wrong-sign. The weakest correct row has only `~0.293 mV` margin, so reaching a `10 mV` target by W/C alone would require `~34.1x` signal scale: about `2182 u` readout width from the current `64 u`, or `~0.293 fF` score capacitance from the current `10 fF`. Both are outside the default feasibility bounds, and the wrong-sign class-1 row remains. This rules out simple readout-strength/capacitance scaling as the main path; the next circuit target should change the learning signal/features/readout common-mode so the class-1 row becomes sign-correct before margin amplification. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_religrest_replay_sizing_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260527_summary.json`.
- Added wrong-row replay contribution diagnostics for the compact physical readout replay. The summary now uses the measured final activation vector and final readout capacitor voltages to rank which features make the predicted class beat the target class under the same first-order conductance approximation used by the projection diagnostic. On the restored-eligibility target-bias `mnist3fixed8_6` replay, the only wrong final row is cycle 7 (`label=1`, `prediction=2`, replay margin `~-2.89 mV`). The error is dominated by feature 6: activation `~0.424 V`, class-1 effective readout weight `~-37.5 mV-equivalent`, class-2 effective readout weight `~48.1 mV-equivalent`, contribution delta `~36.3 mV^2`. Features 1/2/7 contribute much less and mostly because class 1 is negative while class 2 is neutral. This sharpens the next target from generic readout scaling to feature-specific update alignment: the live gradient-flow path must correct the class-1/class-2 feature-6 readout state instead of simply amplifying the final score rails. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_religrest_replay_contrib_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260527_summary.json`.
- Added a focused ngspice regression for that feature-specific correction contract. A one-sample, eight-feature continuous deck initializes feature 6 so class 2 strongly beats the target class 1, then runs the current live `pairwise-margin-centered-gain-descent` writer with rank-gated restored eligibility. The netlist contains no accumulator/apply clocks and no `gvp/rgp` gradient-storage capacitors. The active feature-6 eligibility is restored to writer range (`relig_f6 > 1 V`), inactive feature 0 stays off, class-1 error is positive, class-2 error is negative, class-1 feature-6 signed state increases by more than `100 mV`, class-2 feature-6 signed state decreases by more than `100 mV`, and inactive feature-0 weights stay near zero. This proves the primitive can correct the exact class/feature shape exposed by the replay diagnostic in isolation; the remaining integration failure is therefore about when/where that pressure is generated over real MNIST sequences, not a total inability of the live writer to move the offending feature in the right direction.
- Added wrong-row contributor update-history diagnostics and reran the compact restored-eligibility replay trace. The update trace explains how the bad feature-6 state is created in the real sequence: after train sample 1, class 1 and class 2 feature-6 signed states are both essentially zero; the class-1 train sample barely changes feature 6 (`target_delta ~= -16 nV`, predicted-class delta `~56 nV`); then the class-2 train sample drives class-1 feature 6 down by `~-113 mV` and class-2 feature 6 up by `~118 mV`.
