Similar but smaller histories appear for features 1 and 2. So the primitive can correct feature 6 when it receives the right local pressure, but the integrated sequence does not generate that pressure on the class-1 example and later overwrites the shared feature on the class-2 example. The next circuit target should be reducing shared-feature interference or making the class-1 sample produce writer-visible feature-6 target pressure, not W/C scaling. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_religrest_replay_update_trace_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260527_summary.json`.
- Added an analytical nontarget-width scale for pairwise-margin error rails. The default is now derived as `1/(class_count - 1)`, which matches the normalized-gradient intuition that target correction mass should be split across nontarget classes rather than giving each nontarget a full target-strength depression path. Low-level/structural coverage verifies that the default 3-class pairwise path keeps the target error branch at `128u` while reducing raw nontarget branches to `64u`, and an explicit `0.25` override scales them to `16u` when the target branch is `64u`. The compact restored-eligibility replay run is negative evidence: the summary reports the analytical scale `0.5`, but final accuracy/replay accuracy stay `0.667`, the wrong class-1 row remains, and feature 6 is still overwritten by the class-2 train sample (`class1 feature6 ~-113 mV`, class2 feature6 `~+118 mV`). This means the current centered-gain restoration stage mostly erases the raw nontarget magnitude scaling. The next aligned fix should be a bounded or magnitude-preserving writer-range error handoff, not just smaller raw nontarget devices before a hard restore. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_pairwise_ntscale_analytic_replay_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Checked the same compact path with `pairwise-margin-centered-descent`, which removes the final gain-restoration stage and therefore should preserve more rail magnitude. It is also negative evidence: final accuracy falls to chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`), final physical margin is still negative (`~-4.37 mV`), target error rails are only `~23-25 mV`, and feature-centered readout structure drops to `~39 mV` average. This brackets the next design space: raw centered rails are too weak for the live writer, while hard centered-gain restoration reaches writer range but erases useful magnitude/scaling. The next circuit experiment should be a bounded/gain-shaped error handoff between those extremes.
- Added `pairwise-margin-centered-bounded-gain-descent` as that intermediate error handoff. The new stage takes centered `errp_ctr/errn_ctr` rails and drives live writer rails through a larger-cap, weaker-cross-discharge NSENSE source-follower stage (`48u`, `4 fF`, weak `4u` cross discharge) instead of the hard `128u/64u/1 fF` restore. Structural coverage proves the integrated live block emits no `Cgvp` gradient-storage caps for this mode, and a low-level ngspice probe proves the bounded stage preserves rail magnitude monotonically (`0.10/0.20/0.32 V` inputs produce increasing output diffs while staying below `0.40 V`). The compact strict restored-eligibility target-bias `mnist3fixed8_6` runs are negative: default analytical nontarget scale `0.5` stays `0.333 -> 0.333` with replay accuracy `0.333` and final replay margin `~-2.40 mV`; explicit `0.25` is essentially unchanged. The target error rails are writer-visible (`~+30 mV` target, `~-57..-99 mV` nontarget), but the same integration signature remains: the class-1 training sample has no writer-visible feature-6 eligibility, so the later class-2 sample overwrites feature 6. This rules out "hard restore erased nontarget scale" as the sole blocker; the next aligned target is feature/eligibility coverage or shared-feature interference, not another scalar score-to-writer gain tweak. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_pairwise_centered_bounded_gain_rank_hxsamp_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_pairwise_centered_bounded_gain_nt025_rank_hxsamp_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added an opt-in support-gated live nontarget writer (`--readout-nontarget-guard-mode support`). Each class-feature cell gets a persistent transistor-charged support capacitor that charges only from positive descent pressure and eligibility; the negative live writer branches then require that support node before they can raise `vwn` or lower `vwp`. This preserves gradient-flow semantics: there are no `gvp/gvn` gradient buckets, no `Vapply`, and no Python writeback. New low-level ngspice contracts prove the guard blocks unsupported nontarget depression on a neutral class-feature while still allowing nontarget depression after the same class-feature has first received positive support. A compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` restored-eligibility target-bias run with centered-gain pairwise error improves to `0.333 -> 0.667` physical accuracy and `0.667` replay accuracy, but the class-1 row still loses to class 2 with replay margin `~-1.79 mV`. The failure signature changed: class-1 feature 6 is no longer driven strongly negative, but it also never receives meaningful positive support (`~0.3 uV` final signed), while class 2 learns features 1/6 at `~118 mV`. So the support guard reduces destructive overwrite but does not create missing target eligibility/support; the next target should make class-1 shared features writer-visible on the class-1 sample or add a less winner-take-all positive support path. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_supportguard_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added `--readout-support-source-mode` so the support memory can charge from the actual writer eligibility (`writer`, default), stored `elig`, stored `act`, or raw `act_raw`, while the live weight writer itself remains gated by the restored writer eligibility. A low-level ngspice primitive proves why this is useful: with writer eligibility held off during positive target pressure, `support_source=act` charges the support capacitor without moving the weight, then permits a later nontarget depression pulse; tying support to the writer node leaves support uncharged and blocks that later update. On the real compact `mnist3fixed8_6` failure, however, the class-1 sample has feature 6 electrically absent at all these local sources (`act`, `act_raw`, `elig`, and `relig` are all near zero), so `support_source=act` stays at `0.667` physical/replay accuracy and the same wrong class-1 row remains. This rules out "rank suppressed a valid class-1 feature" as the main explanation for feature 6; with one class-1 training sample the local circuit simply has no evidence that this eval-time feature belongs to class 1. The next aligned step is either a more representative strict sample rung or a hidden-feature alignment/update mechanism that changes the representation, not another local support source on the readout writer. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_supportguard_actsource_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Ran the more representative strict `mnist3fixed8_12` rung with two train and two eval examples per class, support-gated live readout, direct readout-weighted hidden update, hard-restored rank eligibility, and physical replay diagnostics. It regresses to chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`) and predicts class 2 for most final rows despite target-class readout updates remaining positive on each training step. The decisive new evidence is that hidden weights are effectively fixed: final hidden signed deltas are only `~-70` to `-84 uV`, so the representation stays broad and the readout has to memorize overlapping features. A realistic low-level probe of the direct hidden writer using integrated-like rails (`errp~40 mV`, `act~0.42 V`, `xelig~0.46 V`, `vwp/vwn=0.40/0.28`, hidden `whp/whn=0.8/0`) shows why width tuning is not a clean fix: the nmos-pass stage saturates near only `~-14 mV` signed change because high-side `whp` has no headroom, while PMOS bounded/complementary stages raise both hidden rails and collapse the differential state. The next circuit target should be a hidden representation/update topology with usable differential headroom or a separate suppressive hidden-weight path, not more readout support or a blind hidden-width multiplier. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_supportguard_rank_hxsamp_pairwise_centered_gain_voltage_targetbias_mnist3fixed8_12_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Exposed the direct hidden writer's internal dynamic-node capacitance as `--hidden-direct-internal-capacitance-f` so low-voltage hidden writer strength can be sized by charge sharing/timing instead of only by transistor width. Added an ngspice regression using integrated-like rails (`errp=40 mV`, `act=0.42 V`, `hxelig=0.46 V`, readout refs `0.42/0.28 V`, hidden state `0.8/0`) that makes the hidden-writer contract explicit: `differential-excess`/`nmos-pass` barely distinguishes positive and negative readout signs (`signed_after` remains near `0.79 V` for both), while `restored-excess`/`pmos-complementary` has low-rail authority in isolation (`~+0.91 V` for positive readout sign and `<-0.10 V` for negative sign). The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` run with damped `pmos-complementary` (`0.2 fF` internal cap, support guard, restored rank eligibility, centered-gain pairwise rails) is negative evidence: it stays `0.333 -> 0.333` and still collapses hidden signed state by about `-0.800 V`. This rules out a simple hidden-writer capacitance damping knob as the fix; the next aligned topology needs bounded differential hidden-state control or a different hidden-feature objective, while preserving live gradient flow and no gradient buckets. Regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "direct_hidden_writer_realistic_low_rails or can_directly_update_hidden_weights or validation"` -> `4 passed`. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmoscomp_cap02_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added two focused diagnostics around the `pmos-complementary` direct hidden writer. First, `--hidden-direct-nontarget-guard-mode=support` can now gate negative-error hidden feedback through the same class-feature support capacitors used by the live readout nontarget guard; a low-level ngspice primitive proves that unsupported immature nontarget rails no longer erase a hidden feature, while supported nontarget feedback is still allowed. Second, `--hidden-direct-error-source-mode=centered` lets the hidden path consume `errp_ctr/errn_ctr` before the writer-range gain stage, and summaries now record train `errp`/`errn` rows as well as `errp-errn`. Both compact `mnist3fixed8_6` probes are negative: support-gated hidden feedback and centered hidden-error sourcing each stay `0.333 -> 0.333`, and both still collapse hidden signed state by about `-0.798 V`. The new error diagnostics show the final writer rails have large common-mode (`errp/errn ~=0.31--0.35 V` with only `~40--100 mV` differential), but centered sourcing did not change the integrated collapse, so the blocker is now localized to the `pmos-complementary` hidden writer/state-control topology rather than only nontarget support or score-error common-mode. Focused regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "direct_hidden_writer_support_guard or hidden_direct_support_guard or centered_hidden_direct or direct_hidden_writer_realistic_low_rails or validation"` -> `7 passed`. Compact evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmoscomp_hguard_w005_cap005_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmoscomp_hguard_centererr_w005_cap005_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added `--hidden-direct-output-stage=pmos-suppressive` as a low-side-only direct hidden writer experiment. It uses restored readout excess to suppress the opposite hidden rail rather than adding a high-side PMOS pull-up, so the primitive avoids the obvious rail race seen in `pmos-complementary`: with integrated-like rails (`errp=40 mV`, `act=0.42 V`, `xelig=0.46 V`, `vwp/vwn=0.40/0.28`, hidden `whp/whn=0.80/0.25`) the positive case preserves `whp` and pulls `whn` to about `0.16 V`, the negative case pulls `whp` to about `0.245 V`, and neutral readout preserves the initial signed state near `0.55 V`. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` run is negative evidence: it regresses to `0.333 -> 0.0`, train error rails remain directionally sensible (`target errdiff ~=+39.4 mV`, nontarget `~-77.6 mV`), but hidden signed state collapses by about `-0.792 V` and all but one hidden feature end near zero signed weight. This says suppressive-only direct hidden updates have useful low-level headroom but are too destructive as the integrated hidden-learning objective. The next aligned target should be bounded differential hidden-state control or sparse/dead-zone hidden updates while preserving live gradient flow, not another simple output-stage polarity tweak.
- Added `--hidden-direct-output-stage=pmos-balanced`, which keeps the same live direct hidden writer contract but weakens the credited-rail PMOS recovery path to `hidden_direct_complement_width_scale * hidden_update_width` while retaining the low-side suppressive path. Low-level ngspice with integrated-like rails and `complement_width_scale=0.25` proves the intended single-pulse behavior: positive credit drives `whp/whn` to about `0.98/0.15 V`, negative credit to about `0.15/0.58 V`, and neutral readout preserves `~0.55 V` signed state. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` integration is still negative and slow (`~200 s`): accuracy regresses `0.333 -> 0.0`, hidden signed state collapses by about `-0.800 V`, and the final hidden signed vector is only millivolt-scale. This rules out a weak recovery pull as the missing bounded hidden-state controller. The next hidden-update work should test explicit dead-zone/sparsity or objective-level constraints before another full integrated run.
- Added `hidden_direct_error_source_mode=differential-excess`, a transistor/passive hidden-error prefilter that builds per-class `herrp/herrn` rails from `errp-errn` before the direct hidden writer. The low-level ngspice contract passes: equal `340/340 mV` error common-mode produces matched `~2.2 mV` rails and zero differential, `350/310 mV` produces only `~3.4 mV` differential, and a clear `450/250 mV` case produces about `94.6 mV` one-sided differential. This proves the extractor can reject common-mode and create a physical dead zone. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` integration with `pmos-complementary` still fails (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-1.62 mV`) and hidden signed state collapses by essentially the full `-0.800 V`. So raw error common-mode is not the sole cause; the current direct hidden writer/objective interaction can still erase the representation when score-derived differential pressure is present. Next useful hidden work should add a writer-level state limiter or sparse objective guard and test the combined writer, not just the error prefilter.
- Clarified the learning-state contract after reviewing the "gradient storage" wording. The intended main path is live gradient flow: eligibility/activity and descent/error rails overlap in time and directly move persistent weight capacitors through MOS/passive writers. It is acceptable to store local physical state that exists in the circuit, such as activation, eligibility, support, score, hidden credit, or bounded error rails, when sequencing requires it. It is not acceptable for headline training to store per-weight gradient buckets and later apply them through a separate `Vacc`/`Vapply` phase. Older sampled-gradient writer modes remain useful debug controls, but the aligned path is `readout_update_mode=live` plus live hidden update modes with no `Cgvp/Cgvn`, `Crgp/Crgn`, or Python-side gradient/writeback.
- Added an opt-in physical hidden-state anchor probe to the continuous multiclass block. When `--hidden-state-anchor-resistance` is set, each hidden `whp/whn` capacitor gets a real resistor to its initial positive/negative hidden reference; by default no anchor sources or resistors are emitted. This is a diagnostic passive state limiter, not the final hidden-learning architecture. A focused ngspice primitive now checks that a `1 MOhm` anchor measurably limits the same `pmos-complementary` negative writer drift under integrated-like low rails. In the compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` run that previously collapsed hidden signed state by about `-0.8 V`, the same `1 MOhm` anchor with live readout, support guard, restored rank eligibility, and direct hidden update improved physical accuracy from `0.333 -> 0.667`, limited hidden signed drift to `~-57.8 mV`, and left the worst margin slightly negative (`~-1.64 mV`). The result shows bounded hidden-state control can rescue the integration signature, but a hard passive anchor should be replaced by an active/common-mode-preserving hidden state controller or a sparser hidden objective before treating this as scalable.
- Added `--hidden-direct-state-guard-mode=signed-support`, a local same-sign support guard for the direct hidden writer. The positive hidden writer path gets an extra MOS/passive series gate controlled by `whp`, while the negative path is gated by `whn`; the same guard also gates the complementary anti-rail pulldown path, so the guard cannot be bypassed by the `pmos-complementary` low-side correction. There are still no gradient buckets or apply phase. The focused ngspice primitive proves the intended local behavior under integrated-like low rails: a weak opposite-sign `pmos-complementary` update that would erase a strong positive hidden feature is blocked, while same-sign positive reinforcement remains active. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` integration is negative evidence: with support-gated live readout, restored rank eligibility, centered-gain pairwise rails, and no passive hidden anchor, accuracy stays `0.333 -> 0.333` and hidden signed state still collapses by about `-0.799 V`. The guard is therefore a useful tested diagnostic, but it is not the missing hidden controller; the integrated failure is driven by repeated writer/objective interactions that can still erase the representation even when isolated opposite-sign erasure is blocked.
- Added `--hidden-state-keeper-mode=differential`, an opt-in circuit-owned differential keeper for hidden `whp/whn` state. The keeper uses cross-controlled MOS devices to reinforce the currently larger hidden rail toward `hidden_whi_ref` and the smaller rail toward `hidden_wlo_ref`; it stores no gradient buckets and does not need Python intervention. A focused ngspice test proves the useful contract under integrated-like weak opposing update: without the keeper, a strong positive hidden state flips negative; with a `0.25 u` keeper, the signed hidden state remains above `0.50 V` and the rails stay near the intended high/low references. In the compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` run with live readout, support guard, restored rank eligibility, direct hidden update, and no passive anchor, the keeper improves physical accuracy from `0.333 -> 0.667`. The limitation is clear: this width drives every hidden feature to `whp=1.05 V`, `whn=0.15 V` (`signed=0.90 V`) and leaves the worst margin negative (`~-9.33 mV`). So the result validates bounded differential hidden-state control as the right failure mode to attack, but the always-on keeper is too strong/broad; the next aligned version should be dead-zone, adaptive, or objective-gated so it prevents collapse without saturating all hidden features.
- Added `--hidden-state-keeper-mode=differential-threshold`, a thresholded hidden keeper that first detects whether `whp` or `whn` is already above a high-state threshold using an `NHIGH` device, then only reinforces that established sign. The low-level ngspice contract is better than the always-on keeper: established positive and negative states survive weak opposing hidden updates, while a neutral `0.45/0.40 V` pair remains unchanged instead of being latched to a rail. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` integration is negative: accuracy stays `0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin is `~-2.41 mV`, and all hidden features end with both rails near `1.0276 V`, giving essentially zero signed hidden state. This says a static thresholded keeper avoids the broad positive latch failure, but it does not solve the integrated objective: the live hidden writer can still equalize both rails over the training sequence. The next hidden-state controller needs a common-mode/signed-state discipline or update objective change, not just a stronger keeper threshold.
- Added `--hidden-state-common-clamp-width`, a separate opt-in hidden common-mode clamp that can be combined with the thresholded keeper. The final primitive uses a high-threshold two-device detector to sense `whp` and `whn` both high, then briefly enables NSENSE pulldowns to the hidden low reference; the control nodes have fast RC recovery so the clamp is transient rather than a persistent erase path. Low-level ngspice proves the intended local contract: a double-high `1.0/1.0 V` hidden state is pulled below `0.80 V` common-mode, while valid `0.80/0.0 V` and `0.0/0.80 V` signed states keep their signs. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` integration with thresholded keeper plus `4 u` common clamp is negative: accuracy stays `0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin is only `~-1.6 uV`, and hidden rails collapse near the low reference (`~0.150-0.158 V` on both rails, signed state near zero). This brackets the hidden-controller problem: always-on retention over-saturates positive, threshold retention permits high-high equalization, and common-mode clamping converts that to low-low erasure. The next aligned design should change the hidden update objective/topology so only one rail is credited per feature, or add a true differential/common-mode servo that preserves signed magnitude rather than merely clipping common-mode.
- Added `--hidden-direct-output-stage=pmos-differential`, a direct hidden writer stage that removes the stale dynamic inverter used by `pmos-balanced`/`pmos-complementary` for the anti-rail path. The anti-rail PMOS pull now uses the same restored readout event gate as the credited-rail pull, so the primitive keeps live gradient-flow semantics without per-weight gradient buckets or an `apply` phase. Low-level ngspice with integrated-like rails proves the intended direction contract: positive readout sign increases the hidden signed state, negative readout sign decreases it, and neutral readout preserves the initial sign. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` run with live readout, support guards, restored rank eligibility, and centered-gain pairwise rails improves physical accuracy from `0.333 -> 0.667` and avoids the high-high/low-low hidden collapse seen in the keeper/clamp probes. The limitation remains substantial: final hidden signed state shrinks from the initial `~0.80 V` to `~0.30-0.41 V`, and the final physical margin is `0.0 V`, so this is promising hidden-state control evidence rather than a scalable solution. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Ran the stricter `mnist3fixed8_12` rung with the same `pmos-differential` hidden writer and physical readout replay enabled. The continuous physical score path remains at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `0.0 V`), while signed/conductance projections of the measured final state reach `0.667` but still fail class 2 (`min margins ~-1.29 mV` signed, `~-0.39 mV` conductance). Physical replay agrees with the continuous physical predictions and gives equal class score nets (`~21.00267 mV`) to numerical precision, so the score/readout deck is faithfully showing the trained state rather than a transient measurement artifact. Inspecting the replay decks revealed the real scale issue: final eval hidden activations are mostly microvolts, with only the bias row around `0.427 V` and a weak class-1 feature-6 activation around `10.9 mV`. The readout weights have learned some signed structure, but the physical readout cannot see the collapsed activations. I added summary-level activation row statistics so future runs report this directly instead of requiring generated-deck inspection. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_12_replay_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added an opt-in `--hidden-activation-model` so the hidden forward activation source can use `NREL`, `NMOS`, or low-threshold `NSENSE`, and added summary-level `pre_margin` rows. A low-level ngspice primitive proves why `NSENSE` looked tempting: for a weak `pre_p/pre_n=80/40 mV` case, the old `NREL` activation is below `5 mV` while `NSENSE` raises the activation above `25 mV`. The integrated compact result is negative: with the otherwise-best `pmos-differential` setup, `NSENSE` makes every train/final-eval feature activation exceed `25 mV`, collapses class contrast, and regresses `mnist3fixed8_6` from the baseline `0.333 -> 0.667` to `0.333 -> 0.333` with a negative margin. The new pre-margin diagnostics show the baseline has positive pre margins on all features, so a simple lower activation threshold converts common positive evidence into false support rather than solving class-2. I also reran the existing `hybrid` readout-eligibility handoff in the same setup: it gives the class-2 sample analog writer eligibility on features 4/6 (`~48/96 mV`) instead of zero, but leaves readout updates too small and stays at chance; scaling the live writer width by the analytically implied `~4x--12x` range caused ngspice timestep failures in the live writer stack. The next aligned target is therefore not a blunt low-threshold activation or width multiplier; it is a differential/magnitude-preserving eligibility or activation handoff that suppresses common positive pre-margin while keeping class-specific features writer-visible. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_premargin_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`, `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_actnsense_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`, and `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_hybridrelig_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added `--hidden-activation-negative-width-scale` as an opt-in differential activation shunt diagnostic. The low-level ngspice primitive proves that `NSENSE` with a `4x` negative shunt can preserve a strong `pre_p-pre_n` margin (`~117 mV` activation), suppress common high `pre_p/pre_n` evidence below `50 mV`, reject reverse margins below `1 mV`, and keep a weak true margin above a weak common-mode case. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` integration is negative: accuracy stays `0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin is `~-2.56 mV`, all train/final-eval activation rows still exceed `25 mV`, and class 2 receives essentially no readout update (`~0.14 uV` target delta). This rules out a simple stronger negative activation shunt as the missing class-selective handoff; the integrated pre-margin distribution is too broad/common-mode-heavy. The next aligned target should build a real differential/competitive feature objective or writer-visible eligibility handoff rather than just resizing the hidden activation source follower. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_actnsense_nshunt4_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added `--readout-update-eligibility-sense-model` so the readout eligibility handoff can use a low-threshold sensing device where the feature gate is intentionally analog and small. A focused ngspice primitive captures the actual scale problem from `contrast` gating: with `NREL`, a `~120 mV` eligibility and `~150 mV` contrast gate only produces `~120 mV` writer rail, while `NSENSE` at `8 u` restores the same case above `0.8 V` and keeps the off case below `1 mV`. A compact contrast/hybrid control with the default NREL sense path similarly sparsifies but leaves every writer-visible feature below `250 mV`. In the compact `pmos-differential` hidden-update run, the `NSENSE` handoff fixes the readout handoff itself: writer-visible eligibility becomes sparse and full-range (`3/8` features above `250 mV` on average), and the class-2 training step now gives a real positive readout delta (`~14.8 mV` instead of microvolts). The integrated result is still negative (`0.333 -> 0.333`): final physical activations collapse to mostly the bias row with hidden update enabled, while a readout-only isolation run over-activates feature support on the third sample (`6/8` writer-visible features on average) and class 2 dominates. This separates the blockers cleanly: the feature-to-writer scale handoff now has a tested transistor-level fix, but the next aligned target is hidden feature state/control that preserves activation scale without turning sparse support into all-feature overwrite. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_contrast_hybridrelig_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`, `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`, and `results/tables/codex_readout_only_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Fixed an integration mismatch in the `pmos-differential` direct hidden writer: the low-level primitive had been validated with `hidden_whi_ref` as the PMOS recovery source, but the generated end-to-end netlist was still using the readout writer's `vwhi_ref=0.42 V` for this stage. A regression now checks that both positive and negative credited-rail PMOS devices use `hidden_whi_ref`; focused primitive coverage still passes. Re-running the compact contrast/hybrid/NSENSE handoff case with the corrected reference is negative for learning (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-21.15 mV`), but it changes the hidden failure signature from low activation collapse to high-high common-mode collapse: final hidden rails average about `0.998/0.994 V` with only `~1.6--5.5 mV` signed state. Existing mitigations do not solve it: `hidden_direct_error_source_mode=differential-excess` drives exact high-high equality (`~1.021/1.021 V`, signed near zero), and `hidden_direct_state_guard_mode=signed-support` still ends near `0.992/0.991 V` with only `~0.8--1.4 mV` signed state. This corrects the hidden-writer reference bug and sharpens the next blocker: the direct hidden update objective/writer needs common-mode discipline or mutually exclusive differential rail credit, not another readout handoff tweak. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_hiddenref_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`, `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_hiddenref_herrdiff_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`, and `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_hiddenref_stateguard_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added `--hidden-direct-output-stage=pmos-differential-sink`, an event-restored anti-rail sink variant of the direct hidden writer. It keeps the live gradient-flow contract: no per-weight gradient storage, no `Vapply` phase, and no Python intervention; the credited rail is still recovered from `hidden_whi_ref`, while the opposite hidden rail is discharged through a restored event gate and an `NSENSE` path to `hidden_wlo_ref`. Low-level ngspice with integrated-like rails proves the static sink can remove common-mode in a single update: at `hidden_direct_complement_width_scale=2.0`, positive credit drives roughly `whp/whn=1.049/0.150 V`, negative credit drives `0.150/1.038 V`, and a repeated opposite-polarity primitive keeps common-mode below `0.60 V`. A scale sweep shows why this is only a bracket, not a solution: scales `0.125--0.5` leave the negative-credit anti-rail nearly unchanged (`neg_signed ~= -238 mV` from an initial `+550 mV` state), `0.75` begins to act but remains asymmetric, and `1.0--2.0` becomes rail-exclusive in isolation. Compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` runs bracket the integrated failure: scale `0.75` still collapses to high-high hidden common-mode (`~0.99/0.98 V`, signed `~4--10 mV`) and stays `0.333 -> 0.333`; scale `1.0` avoids full high-high but shrinks hidden signed state to only `~32--71 mV` and stays at chance; scale `2.0` over-suppresses into low-low hidden state and also stays at chance. This rules out a static anti-rail sink width as the missing hidden controller. The next aligned circuit target should be state-aware differential/common-mode control or an update objective that credits only one hidden rail without erasing signed magnitude. Focused regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "differential_sink or pmos_differential or direct_hidden_writer_pmos_differential or validation"` -> `6 passed`. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiffsink_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_hguard_w0125_c075_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`, `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiffsink_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_hguard_w0125_c1_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`, and `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiffsink_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_hguard_w0125_c2_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added `--hidden-state-common-clamp-mode=differential`, a sign-preserving double-high hidden common-mode clamp. Unlike the earlier equal clamp, it detects `whp` and `whn` both high and then uses cross-controlled pulldowns, so the lower/weaker rail is discharged more strongly than the higher rail. The primitive is transistor/passive only and stays in the live gradient-flow contract. Low-level ngspice proves the intended local behavior: double-high positive and negative states are pulled below `0.90 V` common-mode while retaining `>40 mV` signed margin in the correct direction, and a valid `0.80/0.0 V` signed state remains above `0.75 V` signed. In the active direct-hidden-writer low-rail probe, `0.5 u` is the useful small setting: positive readout credit keeps about `+0.799 V` signed state and negative credit reaches about `-0.775 V`; larger clamp widths disturb the negative case. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` integration with `pmos-differential`, contrast/hybrid/NSENSE readout eligibility, support guards, and differential common clamp remains at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-21.64 mV`), but the failure signature is materially better: final hidden signed states are now `~0.34--0.78 V` instead of millivolts or high-high/low-low collapse. The remaining issue is broad activation/readout class-2 dominance, not loss of hidden signed state. Focused regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "differential_common_clamp or common_mode_clamp or hidden_state_keeper or validation"` -> `9 passed`. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_hdirect_pmosdiff_diffcmclamp05_contrast_hybridrelig_nsense8_hguard_w0125_c025_supportguard_mnist3fixed8_6_roundrobin_20260528_summary.json`.
- Added `readout_live_high_side_topology=pmos-differential`, a live readout writer high-side that uses weak PMOS control keepers plus cross-coupled PMOS control devices so common-mode error rails do not turn both update directions on. The primitive is still a gradient-flow writer: eligibility and `errp/errn` overlap and directly move the persistent `vwp/vwn` capacitors, with no gradient bucket or apply phase. A new low-level ngspice test uses measured compact-run rails (`elig ~=129 mV`, target `errp/errn ~=351/313 mV`, nontarget `235/340 mV`) and proves target positive movement, nontarget negative movement, and off negligible movement. Writer-time error rail diagnostics now record `errp`, `errn`, and `errp-errn` at the actual readout update sample point, and the live writer references are exposed as `--readout-high-ref/--readout-low-ref` so headroom is explicit rather than hard-coded. Compact integration evidence is mixed but useful: with support guards enabled, `mnist3fixed8_6` remains `0.333 -> 0.333`; with widened `0.48/0.22 V` refs and both readout/hidden nontarget guards disabled, the same strict continuous run reaches `0.333 -> 1.0` with final physical margin `~0.44 mV`. Diagnosis: the differential writer handoff itself can produce class-selective movement, but the existing support guard suppresses the needed nontarget negative branch and leaves a class-common positive path. Next aligned step is to replace or redesign support gating so it gates the differential writer symmetrically rather than disabling only one branch. Focused regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py spice/run_multiclass_output_head_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "pmos_differential or rejects_error_common_mode or live_readout_writer_aligns or validation or reduces_train_error_rail_stats"` -> `10 passed`.
- Added `readout_nontarget_guard_mode=support-symmetric` as a principled alternative to the old support guard. The old `support` mode only guards the nontarget branch, which is incompatible with the common-mode-rejecting `pmos-differential` selector because it can suppress the negative branch while leaving common positive pressure. The new mode still stores class/feature support locally, but it gates both positive and negative live writer paths before the selector, so unsupported cells are quiet and supported cells let the differential `errp/errn` decision choose direction. A low-level ngspice primitive proves the local contract under realistic writer rails: supported target moves positive, supported nontarget moves negative, and unsupported nontarget stays quiet. Compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` evidence is negative: with raw eligibility, early pairwise centered-gain error, `pmos-differential`, `0.48/0.22 V` refs, target-only bias, and no hidden update, the no-guard control stays `0.333 -> 0.333` with final margin `~-0.68 mV`, while `support-symmetric` also stays `0.333 -> 0.333` but worsens the final margin to `~-104 mV` by over-selecting class 2. Conclusion: symmetric support fixes the branch-asymmetry bug as a primitive, but support memory is too coarse to be the next default integration path; the better direction remains score/feature normalization and hidden-state control rather than more support gating. Focused regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py spice/run_multiclass_output_head_sequence.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "support or symmetric_support or pmos_differential or rejects_error_common_mode or validation"` -> `20 passed`.
- Added a diagnostic-only class-mean-centered signed readout projection. It subtracts each class's final mean signed feature weight before applying the measured final activations, while leaving the optional bias feature untouched; it is summary-only and does not feed back into the transient. This tests whether the observed class-2 dominance could be solved by a physical class-mean readout centering servo. The compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` no-guard `pmos-differential` probe says no: raw signed projection stays `0.333` with min margin `~-157 mV`, while class-mean-centered signed projection also stays `0.333` with min margin `~-17.3 mV`; it changes the wrong winners for the first two eval samples but does not recover the labels. A `hidden_activation_contrast_mode=common-gate` rerun reduced dense activations above `250 mV` from about `5.67` to `2.33--2.67` active features, but also stayed at chance with the same physical margin signature. Conclusion: class-level readout centering and broad activation common-mode suppression are not sufficient by themselves; the next useful circuit target is feature-specific evidence alignment, likely a better objective/eligibility handoff or hidden-state controller rather than a simple readout mean servo. Focused reducer regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "class_centered_signed_projection or final_signed_projection or projection_alignment or readout_weight_matrix"` -> `3 passed`.
- Screened existing feature-gating primitives against the newer no-guard `pmos-differential` live readout writer on compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6`. `eligibility_gate_mode=rank` with analog-pass readout eligibility makes the integration worse (`0.333 -> 0.0`, final margin `~-2.48 mV`): rank gates still leave about `4.33/8` features over `600 mV`, writer eligibility peaks around `0.43 V`, and final physical/projection predictions disagree because the physical score path is dominated by marginal rail effects. `eligibility_gate_mode=contrast` with `NSENSE` analog-pass handoff also stays at chance (`0.333 -> 0.333`, final margin `~-1.08 mV`): writer-visible eligibility is lower and sparser (`~4.67/8` over `250 mV`, max `~0.29 V`), but the readout update still creates a strong class-2 signed/common-mode bias. Together with the class-centered diagnostic, this argues against another local feature-gate sizing sweep as the main path. The next useful primitive should change the objective or credit assignment so feature-specific evidence is aligned before writing, rather than only gating the same broad descent rails.
- Added a low-level feature-specific margin-correction primitive. It computes per-feature target and wrong-class contribution rails through the actual stored readout conductances, compares those rails with a transistor latch, derives a local correction gate, and uses the existing live `pmos-differential` writer to move the target and wrong readout capacitors during the same update window. This keeps the gradient-flow contract: no `Vapply`, no `gvp/gvn` gradient buckets, no behavioral sources, and no Python weight intervention. The first latch attempt exposed an important circuit detail: a half-high losing decision node around `0.57 V` is still enough to leak through `NSENSE` writer gates, so the primitive now uses a separate stacked correction gate plus target-side discharge. Ngspice tests prove the useful local contract: wrong-contributing active features increase the target signed readout and decrease the wrong signed readout, target-already-strong and inactive features stay quiet, and a rotated class case behaves the same. Focused regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_feature_margin_correction_primitive.py spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py spice/run_multiclass_output_head_sequence.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_multiclass_margin_correction_primitive.py tests/test_feature_margin_correction_primitive.py -q` -> `15 passed`.
- Integrated feature-specific margin correction as opt-in `readout_live_objective_mode=feature-margin` in the continuous block runner. The path builds per-feature class contribution rails from stored readout conductances, compares target vs wrong contribution rails, creates live correction rails, and feeds the existing live writer without `Vapply`, `gvp/gvn` buckets, behavioral learning sources, or Python weight updates. A controlled one-sample ngspice block test proves the integration contract: a wrong class-0 feature winning against target class 1 drives `c1_f0_fcorrp` and `c0_f0_fcorrn`, raises the target signed readout, lowers the wrong signed readout, and improves the target-vs-wrong margin in one continuous transient. Compact `mnist3fixed8_6` screens are negative but diagnostic. Raw eligibility with `1.0u` writer stays `0.333 -> 0.333` and collapses into almost pure class-2/common class overwrite (`final margin ~-119 mV`, feature-centered abs mean only `~0.16 mV`). Rank/restored eligibility improves feature structure but still stays chance: `1.0u` gives final margin `~-15.3 mV`, and an analytical `0.25u` width reduction avoids some saturation but still stays chance with margin `~-50.7 mV`. This rules out "feature-specific contribution gate exists" and "writer width only" as the missing pieces; the next useful circuit target is centering/normalizing the feature-correction pressure itself, or making the feature objective zero-sum across classes before the live writer. Focused regressions: `python3 -m py_compile spice/run_multiclass_block_sequence.py spice/run_feature_margin_correction_primitive.py` and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_feature_margin_correction_primitive.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "feature_margin or live_pairwise_margin_corrects_wrong_winner or validation"` -> `11 passed`.
- Added `readout_live_objective_mode=feature-margin-centered`, which turns the feature-specific correction objective into a local class-centered writer-pressure primitive. The first raw-centered version was electrically wrong: it drove both `fcorr_p` and `fcorr_n` rails near midscale and scrubbed readout common-mode. The kept version writes raw per-feature correction rails, centers them into `*_fcorr_{p,n}_ctr`, then uses a transistor restore/dead-zone stage to create writer-range `*_fcorr_{p,n}` rails. Instance names now include the per-feature common prefix so multiple feature normalizers can coexist in one deck. New ngspice block coverage proves the useful one-sample contract: class 1 target pressure exceeds negative pressure by `>25 mV`, class 0 wrong negative pressure exceeds positive pressure by `>25 mV`, class 2 stays balanced within `5 mV`, target signed readout rises by `>100 mV`, wrong signed readout falls by `>100 mV`, and the target-vs-wrong margin improves by `>100 mV`. Focused regressions pass: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_feature_margin_correction_primitive.py tests/test_multiclass_block_sequence.py -q -k "feature_margin or live_pairwise_margin_corrects_wrong_winner or validation"` -> `13 passed`. Compact strict `mnist3fixed8_6` remains unsolved: voltage-score training with rank/restored eligibility and `0.25u` writer stays physical `0.333 -> 0.333`, but signed/conductance projections reach `0.667`, so the live writer learned a useful capacitor state that the physical score rails do not read faithfully. A `score_sense_mode=current-clamp` variant also stays `0.333 -> 0.333` and degrades projection quality, so it is not a drop-in fix for this objective. Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_feature_margin_centered_restore_rankrelig_w025_pmosdiff_mnist3fixed8_6_20260528_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_feature_margin_centered_restore_currentclamp_rankrelig_w025_pmosdiff_mnist3fixed8_6_20260528_summary.json`. Current conclusion: the feature-specific writer primitive is good enough locally; the next main path is a physical score/readout sensing stage that preserves the learned signed/conductance evidence during final evaluation and during the next training sample's score/error phase.
- Added `--physical-readout-replay-sweep`, an opt-in diagnostic that replays the same final measured activation and readout-capacitor state through several transistor/passive readout topologies after the continuous transient. This keeps Python out of the training loop; it only diagnoses whether the final state is physically readable by any existing score topology. A unit test now proves the sweep dispatches the expected physical modes and picks the best mode by accuracy then margin. Directly integrating diode-isolated readout into the centered feature-margin branch did not help (`0.333 -> 0.333`) and degraded the learned projections to `0.333`. Recomputing the compact centered feature-margin replay sweep with the missing direct current modes gives the sharper diagnosis: direct-voltage replay is still `0.333` with class-2 bias (`min margin ~-12.9 mV`), diode-voltage/current-clamp/mirror are all `0.333`, but `direct-current-clamp` and `direct-diode-mirror` both recover `0.667` from the same final capacitor state. The margins are weak (`direct-current-clamp` min margin reported as `0 V` because two replay rows tie), but the sign/readability direction is clear. This changes the next aligned target from "invent any new sensor" to "make a real on-chip direct current-sense/readout path that can coexist with the voltage-domain feature-margin training path without clamping and corrupting the writer dynamics." Evidence is in `results/tables/codex_feature_margin_centered_restore_diode_rankrelig_w025_pmosdiff_mnist3fixed8_6_20260528_summary.json` and `results/tables/codex_feature_margin_centered_restore_replaysweep_rankrelig_w025_pmosdiff_mnist3fixed8_6_20260528_summary.json`.
